# Core-loop design notes

These are the design calls agents made while building this prototype, each with the principle it serves. The principles are Andrew's, from historical playtest entry 6 (`docs/playtests/PLAYTEST-LOG.md`, archived in git history). The coordinator brings these to Andrew and asks him only when a call would change the direction of the deliverable.

Each writer appends only inside its own section, so the four module branches merge cleanly.

## Spine (wave 1)

1. **Superseded by call 20 (the Warden gate, DEMO-SPEC).** This was: the beacon charges as you roll into its ring, with no key and no stop; leaving pauses the charge. It now applies only on the unsealed path (calm tests, or a stage whose spawner has no Warden). Serves momentum is sacred (1).
2. **Reward gates are arches with no collider.** They stand 38 m ahead on the ball's line of travel, 18 m apart. Rolling through one takes its part. If you roll past both, the stub puts them ahead of you again instead of waiting behind you. Serves 1 ("reward choices are gates you roll through, not a menu"). The run test measures speed through a gate: 20.93 m/s in, 21.11 m/s after.
3. **The launch keeps horizontal speed exactly.** Flow turns that speed onto the next stage's entry heading and keeps the vertical part. The world stub adds 20 m/s of lift and never launches slower than cruise, because raising a slow ball is a gift, not a cost. Serves 1 ("the stage exit is a launch into the next stage"). The run test measured 20.99 m/s before the launch and 20.98 m/s after.
4. **The next stage is entered from 14 m above its spawn,** so a launch lands rather than pops in. Serves 1 and the snappy, fluid half of 8.
5. **Stage 1 drops you in already rolling,** at 80% of cruise down the entry heading. Serves quick to start (2) and 1.
6. **A retry restores the entry velocity as well as the stage-start state:** scrap, HP, parts, kills and position. You re-enter the stage the way the launch delivered you. It is one synchronous call, measured at about 1 ms. Serves 2 and 1.
7. **The death screen takes one key.** A death ends the run (no free stage retry; the earned save item catches one lethal hit per run). N starts a new run as the same class. On the summary, Space goes to class select (the storeless death screen of tests and bots offers C). In play, holding R for 0.5 s restarts the run as the same pilot from world 1 (not during the victory lap). The hold stops a stray tap from throwing away a good run. Serves 2.
8. **Falling through the geometry costs nothing.** The rescue puts you back on the ground with your full horizontal speed and no damage, because the map failed, not the player. Serves 1 ("only the player's own performance costs momentum").
9. **The pylon banks the speed you plant with.** Space slingshots you off along the face toward where you look. The eject speed is the banked speed times 0.85, plus 6 m/s, and never less than a plain jump. E drops off in place, a stop the player chose. Both numbers are F1 sliders (`Slingshot keep`, `Slingshot boost`). Serves 8 ("the pylon keeps momentum"). The movement test measured a plant at 11.89 m/s and an eject at 16.10 m/s, against a 13 m/s jump.
10. **The camera moves to the shoulder while mounted.** The arm shortens to 4.2 m and the ball shifts left of the crosshair. The camera owns the hook, and combat decides when to use it. Serves entry 6 ("snap to a more shoulder view") and 5.
11. **Both classes can fire while rolling.** v2 allowed the zap only while mounted. The runner-gunner runs and guns, so the stub weapon fires any time for both classes. Serves 5.
12. **Collisions have weight on both sides of the seam.** The ball applies knockback itself, so a ram always shoves. Combat turns the same contact into damage once, through `strike`. Serves 1 ("collisions have weight") and TADR-008 (resolve once, publish the result).
13. **Run state changes only through mutators, and each mutator publishes its own fact.** The HUD, the summary and audio cannot disagree with the numbers. Serves fast without debt (7) and TADR-008.
14. **Every fact has a shape as well as a colour** in `event_kinds.gd`, so colour never carries meaning alone. The objective line, the HUD toasts and the field-notes legend all read their wording from the same row. Serves form mirrors function.
15. **Hit-stop is a spine API.** It is capped at 60 ms, never stacks, and runs only in play, so juice cannot become an obstruction. Serves 8 ("as weight and not as obstruction") and 1.
16. **Parts change tuning through multipliers, not by moving the sliders.** The recompute from the part list is idempotent, so a retry restores the build exactly, and F1 still shows the base values. Serves 7.
17. **Rigid physics only.** The kinematic body and the F2 toggle are gone. Serves entry 6 and TADR-006.
18. **Superseded by D's notes 1 and 3 (three distinct maps).** This was: the terrain belongs to world (D) behind a frozen API. For now all three stages share the Spillway map, read three ways: stage 1 runs the top shelf and its chute to a mid-shelf beacon, stage 2 drops into the basin the flood fills, and stage 3 takes the bypass deck to the basin floor. Serves 3 (the flood is a layer on stage 2's beacon), and D can replace the stages.
19. **The flood is announced before it rises.** Its klaxon runs for 5 s before the water moves (`event_announced`, then `event_started`). Serves 1 ("nothing the game does may stop or inhibit the player in a way they didn't see coming") and TADR-010.

### Correction wave (DEMO-SPEC, 2026-09-26)

Each call names the stage demand it serves and the principle it strains.

20. **The Warden seals every beacon in play.** Sitting in the ring adds nothing; the charge shown is how much of the Warden is broken; its death opens the gates wherever the ball is. Calm stages, or a spawner that returns no Warden, keep the presence path, so a missing module never locks an exit. Demand: the Warden. Serves 3 (one MUST, the rest opt-in) and strains 1: circling cannot clear a stage.
21. **The ground probe now points at the world's down.** The v2 probe turned with the spin: rolling on flat ground it read grounded on 38% of ticks, so drive, rolling resistance, air control and jump coyote all followed the spin phase, and repeat drives from rest took 191 to 266 ticks to reach 90% of cruise. Fixed, it reads grounded on every tick and every drive takes 109 ticks. This changes the approved feel (faster pickup on the ground, jumps that always fire), so F1's "Legacy spin probe (v2)" toggle brings the old probe back for comparison. Demand: every stage. Serves 1 and 2; it strains the entry-6 verdict until Andrew plays it.
22. **Heft lives in the run state and never touches the body.** Mass is separate from the wallet, so spending compacts scrap and keeps growth. A class change publishes once, and a restore changes class silently. Demand: the Warden (punch against plating). Serves 4 and holds TADR-008 line 40: heft never changes mass, radius, torque or air control, and the handling test measures it.
23. **Crush pass-through.** From the cruise tier up, a bow sensor a metre ahead asks combat whether the hit would kill. On yes, the ball ignores that enemy while it dies: no knock, no shockwave, no hit-stop. At heft 3 the ball kept 18.21 of 18.57 m/s. Demand: the fall line and events (fodder). Serves 1 and 4 (the Katamari moment).
24. **Bonus cores are stage-scoped and shown as two pips beside the objective,** one per gate tier they can buy, with a count past two. Demand: events and vaults. Serves 3 (opt-in layers pay visibly) and form mirrors function.
25. **The objective line names the fight: punch against plating, plus the stage hint.** Without a spawner reading, it shows the stage's own plating rather than a misleading 0. Demand: the Warden. Serves 2 (readable at a glance).
26. **The death screen names the gap.** It shows "Punch n / plating m · Heft X" when a Warden or the boss stood in the way. The T replay of stage 3 waits on wave 4's boss state, and the seam is already there. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 2. (Superseded by call 7: storeless flows only. Since 10 November 2026 that screen says what RUN OVER says, the cause line and the next-shift line, which read the build as it met the fight; `gap_line()` is gone.)
27. **The treasure burst lives in the spine,** so every codex category pays the same: 20 scrap times the stage, once per entry per run, at the spot. Demand: events, vaults and the world tricks. Serves 3 and 4 (exploring feeds growth).
28. **The run log records only what the bus and the run state say,** one cumulative line per death and per run end, so the balance report reads the same facts the player saw. Demand: the design feedback loop. Serves 7 (fast without debt).
29. **warden_engaged fires once per stage attempt,** on the first sealed ring entry or the first strike on the Warden, so the music's Warden theme enters on the player's move. Demand: the Warden. Serves 6.

30. **The sealed charge stops at 99%, and only this stage's Warden unseals it.** A Warden that vanishes without a kill opens the stage the normal way and is logged as an anomaly, rather than falling back to the ring. Demand: the Warden. Serves 1: a seam fault never locks or silently changes the exit.
31. **A heft drop is its own fact** (`heft_class_dropped`, sound `class_down`), so a theft never plays the class-up stinger. Demand: the boss's crane. Serves form mirrors function.
32. **A retry rewinds the codex's per-run memory for that attempt,** so a discovery made again after a retry fires and pays again. The retry already took its burst scrap back. Demand: events, vaults and world tricks. Serves 2 and 4.

33. **The boss is a flow state behind one module seam.** After stage 3's launch the run enters `boss` and calls `enter_arena` with the ball's velocity. The arena-entry checkpoint makes R retry the fight, not the stage. `boss_defeated` gives a 2.5 s victory beat before the summary. Without a boss module the run ends as before. Demand: the boss phase. Serves 1 (the launch carries into the arena) and 2. (Superseded by call 7. In storeless flows only: the dead screen carries only N, C and T; R restarts the fight only by holding it in play.)

34. **The arena is not a stage.** Flow's `in_stage()` is false in the arena, and `stage_content_cleared` tells stage modules to stand down on arena entry and on every arena retry. The flood cannot push, the director cannot spawn, and stage-3 chasers do not follow the ball into the fight. The arena has its own fall rescue, owned by the boss module. Demand: the boss phase. Serves 1: nothing the player cannot see pushes or blocks them.

35. **Contacts are read from the motion before the bounce.** The rigid ball keeps the last four velocities it handed to the solver, and a contact uses the one closing fastest on the enemy. A drop lands as a stomp with the speed it fell at: 17.62 m/s after 6 m, where the live velocity had already bounced to a ram at 0. A roll rams with the speed it rolled at: 13.30 m/s, not the 3.39 left after the hit. Demand: the Warden's vent caps (stomp and slam), the boss's caps and scooter. Serves 1 ("collisions have weight").

Closed: the flood's drag in the basin is allowed (R-21). It is announced, visible and avoidable, a foreseen cost from where the player chooses to roll.

## Combat and classes (writer A)

The contract other modules read is [src/combat/README.md](src/combat/README.md). The numbers below come from the `tests/combat_*.gd` runs on this branch.

1. **Weapons fire bolts, and one aim query sends them.** The reticle's centre ray finds the point, and the centre bolt of every volley flies straight at it and never corkscrews. The ray starts level with the ball, so nothing behind the shooter catches the aim. There is no aim magnetism anywhere. Serves TADR-008 (the reticle shows what the shot hits) and 5.
2. **The runner-gunner is laser-true.** It fires 12 bolts a second at 7 damage, with a thin 0.25 m field and no spread. A chaser 16 m out died 0.66 s into held fire. Serves 5 ("rapid tight laser shots").
3. **Overdrive is the runner's escape hatch.** F blasts a 12 m ring for 30 damage with a heavy shove, then gives 3 s of 1.6 times the fire rate, on a 6 s cooldown. It clears space when you are surrounded, so you keep rolling instead of braking. Serves 5 (the area alternative) and 1.
4. **The arc caster forgives with a wide field and a chain, not a magnet.** It fires 5 bolts a second at 12 damage, with a 0.55 m field and one chain jump within 9 m. In the test the chain reached the chaser beside the target 4 times. Serves 5 and TADR-008, since a homing bolt would make the reticle lie.
5. **The pylon turret is the caster, planted.** While mounted, held fire fires 9 volleys a second, chains two jumps within 16 m, and reaches 95 m. The test measured 10 turret volleys in 1 s. Serves 5 and 8.
6. **The engineer's special is one discharge with two triggers.** Every plant fires it free, powered by the banked speed: that is the opening burst. F fires it any time, powered by your current speed, or by the bank while mounted, on a 5 s cooldown. Speed buys arcs, reach and damage. A plant at 5.0 m/s arced 2 enemies for 20.9 each within 14.2 m. A plant at 25.8 m/s arced 7 for 50.1 each within 23.6 m. Serves 8 ("a faster mount gives a stronger opening burst") and 1, since speed becomes power rather than something the wall takes away.
7. **The shoulder view follows the mount state every tick,** not only the mount and dismount signals. A retry, a death or an E drop can never leave the camera stuck at the shoulder. In the test the blend reached 1.00, and 10 of 10 turret shots sat on the shoulder reticle's ray and struck the chaser under it. Serves entry 6 ("snap to a more shoulder view") and 5.
8. **Patterns are data, and transformers are rows.** A row adds, then multiplies, then floors pattern fields, once per stack. Cross split adds three arms around the aim line, making a cross and then a seven-point star. Spiral wave turns the pattern 23 degrees per volley and corkscrews the off-centre bolts. Parts reach these through `register_part(part_id, ids)` or by using a transformer id as the part id. Combat recomputes from `run.parts`, so a retry restores the build. Serves 4 (Isaac and Gunfire Reborn synergies) and 7.
9. **Stacking can multiply damage without limit, but never what is on screen.** A volley has at most 16 bolts and 8 arms, fire rate stops at 20 a second, and at most 64 player bolts are live. Past the cap a new bolt retires the oldest, so the trigger never stalls and only the far end of the stream thins. Bolts fade from 0.9 to 0.4 alpha as the count climbs. A spiral plus two crosses plus every rate and range tier would put about 103 bolts up; the test peaked at 64 and kept 20 volleys a second. Serves 4 ("cap projectile counts") and 1.
10. **Enemy telegraphs always draw on top.** They use `HAZARD`, with no depth test, at render priority 40. Player bolts draw at 0 and combat effects at 1, and no player weapon uses `HAZARD`. Serves 4 ("never let our own effects hide an enemy").
11. **An enemy hurts you only through the geometry it showed you.** Each attack commits a ring or a lane once, and the damage asks that same node whether the ball is inside. Touching an enemy that is not attacking costs nothing but the bump. Rings and lanes reach 3.5 to 4 m above the ground, so a jump or a glide clears them. In the test a charge lane committed on the ball missed once the ball was moved 12.6 m off it. Serves 1 (nothing stops you unforeseen) and AGENTS.md (damage and telegraphs consume the same committed geometry).
12. **The chaser lunges down a lane; the lobber shells a ring.** The chaser lays an 11 m lane with a 0.6 s wind-up. A knock of 10 m/s or more during the wind-up breaks the lunge, so a ram or overdrive can interrupt it. The lobber commits its landing ring the moment it fires, leading a moving ball by half its travel, and shows the ring for the whole 1.9 s flight. A shell already in the air still lands if its lobber dies, because what was shown is what happens. Serves 1 and TADR-008.
13. **A hit taken shoves the ball** by 5 to 10 m/s along the attack. Serves 1: collisions have weight, and a hit taken is the player's own performance. It uses `add_external_accel`, which CONTRACTS now lists for a committed, telegraphed hit, the Warden pulse and the tug.
14. **Weight divides every knock.** A chaser weighs 1, a lobber 3 and the miniboss 5. The lobber used to be immovable and now slides when rammed. Serves 1 ("collisions have weight").
15. **The miniboss fights in a readable rhythm.** It alternates a leap slam (a 7 m ring) and a charge (a 30 m lane, 1 s brace). After each attack comes a 2.4 s weak window, and the window reads three ways: the armour plates swing open, the core swells and glows, and a halo spins overhead. Since the demo spec it speaks the plating grammar: its plating is 1.25 times the stage Warden's (38, 75, 119), and the weak window sets it to 0, so a small punch lands in full there and chips 20% outside. It has 600 health times stage threat. The base laser killed it 12.4 s into held fire at stage 1, starting at its second window. Serves 3 (the boss half of an event), 8 ("feel like a badass": read, dodge, punish) and 4 (readable threats).
16. **An enemy that falls through the map is put back on the ground, not killed,** so nobody gets scrap they did not earn. Serves 7.
17. **One function decides every hit against plating.** `resolve()` computes punch (a contact's force times heft, or a shot's damage), compares it with the target's plating for that hit and weak point, and returns full or a 20% chip. `strike()`, `punch_now()`, `plating_of()`, `would_crush()` and the amber/slate tint all read it, so the tint, the objective's "Punch 48 / plating 60" and the damage cannot disagree. Measured: a ram at force 14 dealt 32.2 in full to plating 30, a 14-damage bolt chipped 2.8, and `would_crush` matched 20 of 20 contacts. Serves the spec's demand (punch against plating) and TADR-008.
18. **The Warden is a lock, not a fight you can lose to.** It never moves (0.0000 m over 30 s), its pulse only lifts (planar speed across a pulse changed by at most 0.18%, lift about +10 m/s), and every pulse follows a telegraph of at least 2.0 s. The lift and the 10 damage only reach a ball inside the rings it showed. Base runner fire chipped Warden I down in 24.1 s and Warden III in 59.7 s, against 60 s and 90 s limits. Serves 1 (it never takes your line) and the spec's one MUST per stage.
19. **Each Warden lesson is data and reads on the body.** Vent caps are bright discs on top: a stomp or slam on one meets plating 0 for 1.5 times its punch (61.5 from a punch of 41 against plating 60). The magnet arm swings up over the clamp and burns bright during its tug; a bolt on it drops the plating to 0 for 3 s (measured 269 ticks). The arm rides 3.6 m high because at 1 m it sat in the line of body fire and got shot by accident; with that fixed, Warden III took 0 accidental stuns. Serves the spec's lessons and 8 (earn the break).
20. **The tug is 3.5 m/s², not the 6 in the change request.** At 6, a ball driving away through a tug fell to 10.4 m/s against the request's own 0.8 x cruise floor of 11.2, while the same run with no tug bottomed at 12.9. At 3.5 the floor holds, at 11.66. Serves 1 (you can always drive out).
21. **Vaults are plated at the next stage's Warden,** 80, 140 and 168 since the tuning pass (note 36), with 400 health per stage, and never move or attack. Cracking one early says your build is ahead of the curve. Serves 3 (opt-in reward) and the spec's SHOULD tier.
22. **Shells can be batted home.** A bolt, or a rolling ball at CRUISE or faster, that reaches a lobber's shell in flight sends it back at its lobber. The return commits a new ring in amber, because a player-owned blast must not read as a threat, and hits every enemy inside it for 40. Serves the codex treasure hunt ("a deflected lobber shell kills its own lobber") and 1, since the counterplay happens at speed.
23. **Six enemy codex tricks, detected by combat:** Return to Sender, Counter-ram, Rolling Tide (three crushes in a second), Parting Shot (a kill within 1 s of a slingshot), Pulse Return (a cap stomp within 1.5 s of a pulse) and Demagnetized (break the Warden while the magnet has it unplated). Each card's clue names one ingredient and hints at the other. A frozen-chaser shatter waits for build's frost state. Serves the spec's synergies ("shown, discoverable, and accidental").
24. **Chasers have 60 health, not 40.** At heft 1 a ram at 20 m/s has punch 40 and bounces off one; from heft 1.5 it rolls straight through. Measured with the real spawner: heft 3 kept 18.21 of 18.67 m/s through the crush and killed it; heft 1.2 rammed, knocked it and it lived. Serves the spec's Katamari moment (growth turns fodder from obstacle into pickup) and 1.
25. **A kill is recorded on the tick health reaches 0, and the body stops colliding on that tick.** The Warden's `enemy_killed` arrives with it still in the tree, collision layer 0 and integrity 0, so the gate opens from the kill path and a crush exception never ends inside a live collider. Serves 1 (no unforeseen depenetration stop) and TADR-008.
26. **Combat reads build's weapon tuning keys live, at the moment it fires.** Handler order on `part_gained` can never leave a stale multiplier. Serves 7.
27. **What cannot die is never crushed.** An enemy whose `crushable()` is false (Brindle held at a phase floor) makes `would_crush` answer false whatever the punch, so the ball bounces off it rather than ending a 0.3 s pass-through inside a live hull. Measured: a ram of punch 274 against plating 0 and 240 HP crushed a plain enemy and was vetoed on the double. Serves 1 (no unforeseen depenetration stop) and keeps note 25 true for the boss.
28. **A trick found in a retried attempt pays again.** Combat calls the codex every time a trick fires and leaves the dedupe to the codex, which rewinds per attempt. Measured: counter-ram paid a 20-scrap burst, R restored the scrap, and a second counter-ram published a second fact and paid 20 again. Serves the codex treasure hunt and the run log's discovery counts (CONTRACTS Codex, over build call 21 by coordinator ruling).
29. **A clash is previewed by the ram alone.** A target whose `tint_verb()` is `"ram"` (Brindle charging) is tinted against `ram_punch_now()`, which has no weapon term. Measured: 160-damage bolts below cruise read amber on a plating-150 hull at rest and slate on the same hull charging. Serves TADR-008 (the tint never promises a clash the commit refuses) and 1.
30. **A fact handler reads the plating the hit meets.** While `enemy_struck` and `strike_deflected` go out, `resolve()` with that strike's enemy, kind and force answers with its weak point, including one the caller named. Measured: a stomp that named a Warden II vent cap read plating 0 inside the handler, a body stomp 60. Presentation's hit-stop gate reads this, so a cap landing no longer freezes. Serves presentation call 21 and 4.
31. **In the arena, threats stand on the floor you roll on.** Enemies, telegraphs and shells read their ground from `ground.gd`: the terrain on a stage, the boss's `ground_height_at(point)` in the arena. Measured with a floor 30 m above the terrain: the shell's ring centred on the floor (69.82, terrain 39.82), contained the ball resting there and hit it, and the drone lobber aimed at 69.82. Serves 4 (a ring shows where it hits) and AGENTS.md (damage and telegraph share geometry).
32. **The vault follows stage_defs.** It reads the next stage's `warden_plating` from `StageDefs.of(stage + 1)`; the spec fallback covers only a missing field. Measured: stage 2's plating edited to 77 gave a stage-1 vault 77, and a def without the field gave 60. Serves note 21 under tuning.
33. **An arena retry resets the class kit like a stage retry.** The fire and special cooldowns and the overdrive reset on `stage_content_cleared`, whether the reason is `arena` or `arena_retry`. Measured: overdrive fired in the arena, the arena was retried at once, and F fired on the first tick of the new fight. Serves 1 (quick restart) and Andrew's momentum principle.
34. **The magnet pulls inside its own wedge.** Warden III's magnet telegraphs as a 35-degree wedge from the arm toward the ball, with a dashed rim and chevrons pointing home, never a second copy of the solid pulse ring; the tug pulls only a ball the wedge contains. The magnet is a weak point only in the tug, so a shot during the telegraph meets the body. Measured: with both charging, the pulse read RING and the magnet WEDGE, the wedge contained the ball, and `weak_points()` was empty until the tug. Driving out down the wedge, the ball sat inside it for 133 of 133 tug ticks and held 12.53 m/s against the 11.20 floor. Serves 4 (a lift and a pull read differently at a glance) and 8 (earn the break).
35. **A synergy never lands on a weak point.** Kind `synergy` resolves at its force and always against body plating; `resolve` drops any part named with it. Measured on a double that opens any named part: a synergy naming one met plating 60 on the body. Serves the spec's plating rule and build's I3 call.
36. **Shred: a landed hit strips plating for a while.** Andrew chose it on 2026-09-27 because every gun chipped at 20% whatever the build, so a completionist broke Warden III only 1.6 to 2.9 times faster than a skipper (Feedback loop note 13). Every landed hit, a chip included, strips 0.0184 times its punch off that enemy's plating, capped at its plating. Held fire bleeds the strip at 0.15 a second, so the strip settles in proportion to damage per second: doubling your fire doubles it. From 0.5 s after the last hit the plate knits back at 1.0 a second. It is kept per enemy instance and subtracted after `plating_for`, so Wardens, the miniboss, vaults and Brindle's pieces shed alike, and a weak point already at 0 stays 0. The objective's plating number, the tint and the damage all read it, and Warden III's jaws crack with amber seams past 25% stripped. Measured with held runner fire on Warden III's body from 14 m (`combat_shred_test`): the base kit settled at 9.8 (plating 130.2, still slate against its 7-damage bolt). The power curve's engaged build (16.9 damage, 5 bolts, 20 a second) stripped it to 16.8, under its own bolt, in 6.04 s, and the objective went from "Punch 16 / plating 140" to "Punch 16 / plating 16". A full strip was gone 5.92 s after the fire stopped. The top vault moved from a fixed 120, under Warden III's 140, to 1.2 times its own Warden (168). Demand: the Warden. Serves 4 (a grown ball visibly routs the lock) and keeps the skipper on rams and weak points. The rates are starting numbers for the balance loop.

Open questions for Andrew:

- F on the engineer fires the pylon discharge from your speed. Would you rather F did something with the pylon itself, such as planting one without a wall?
- A hit taken shoves you 5 to 10 m/s. Is that weight, or does it read as the game taking your line?
- Base fire chips Warden III down in about a minute without its lesson. Does that feel like a lock you broke, or a chore (the spec's rival question)?

## Build and growth (writer B)

1. **The scrap magnet reaches further the faster you roll.** Its reach is 7 m plus 0.3 m per m/s of speed, so about 16 m at 30 m/s. A piece it catches homes in faster than the ball can outrun it and stays caught. Serves momentum is sacred (1): you never slow down or turn back for scrap.
2. **Scrap sticks to the ball, and so do parts.** Held scrap shows as chunks on the ball's surface, growing with the square root of the total and capped at 36. Each part is a piece of hardware: its shape says its family (barrel, cross, coil, rod, vane, capacitor, gem) and its colour says its rarity. Pieces cap at 24 and stick out no more than 0.7 m. Spending at a fabricator takes chunks off and bolts a part on, so the purchase reads on the ball. Serves 1 ("building momentum and rolling and growing"), 4 (visual clarity) and form mirrors function.
3. **Fabricators are hoops you roll through.** They stand across the scrap lines, away from the beacon and the spawn: 2, 3 and 4 hoops in stages 1 to 3. There is no collider. Roll through with enough scrap and the hoop spends it and grants the part. Short of scrap, nothing happens, and the hoop reads "need N scrap (have M)". After a sale the hoop restocks with a new part 2.5 s later. The build test measured 19.34 m/s before a paid pass and 19.86 m/s after, and 18.79 then 18.54 m/s through an unpaid one. Serves 1 ("fabricators are drive-through").
4. **Price depends only on the part's tier and how many parts you hold.** It is 10 scrap times 1, 1.6 or 2.6 by tier, times 1.15 for each part held. A retry restores the part list, so it restores prices too. Serves quick restart (2) and fast without debt (7).
5. **A gate says what it gives while you approach.** It shows the part name large, its effect in one line, and "* completes Storm Lattice *" when taking it would complete a synergy. Rarity shows twice: in the arch's colour and in the topper's shape (a bar for common, a diamond for rare, a star for epic). The offer records carry `name`, `text`, `rarity`, `tier` and `synergy` for presentation. Serves form mirrors function and 1 ("reward choices are gates you roll through").
6. **Offers climb in tier across the run.** (Superseded by 23: tier now follows bonus cores, not the stage.) The test measured a mean offered tier of 1.25, 1.59 and 1.97 in stages 1 to 3. When the run is one part short of a synergy, half the time one offer slot shows that part. Serves 4 (Isaac-style "one more piece").
7. **The scaling curve runs from modest to absurd on purpose.** Tier 1 gives +10 to 15%, tier 2 +25% and tier 3 +50%. Copies multiply, and pattern levels add. The build sheet's output estimate puts one Hot Rivets at 1.12x a bare weapon and a ten-part late build at about 324x. With punch added in phase 1, the same builds measure 1.44x and about 1,444x. That number is a model for shaping the curve. What is actually drawn and how hard it hits is combat's (A), under the visual caps. Serves 4 ("the ability to scale insanely").
8. **Synergies are derived, never held.** (Superseded in part by 17 and 18: synergies are now tag pairs, and Storm Lattice lost its +25%.) Storm Lattice (Cross Split and Chain Arc) makes every split bolt chain, adds a chain level and +25% damage, and puts a gyroscope cage of arc rings on the ball. Thermal Shock (Frost Vane and Arc Coil) makes the slam's arc shatter nearby ice into a shard nova at double damage, then refreezes the water around you. It puts a frosted coil ring on the ball. A retry that drops a part drops its synergy. Serves 4 and form mirrors function.
9. **Frost on water makes real ground.** Ice is a StaticBody3D on the world layer, so the ball, the grapple and the camera all treat it as terrain. It has the same grip as the ground, so there is no handling penalty (BIBLE line 481). It lasts 9 s and blinks for its last 1.5 s, so losing the ground is never a surprise. The test rolled a ball over 3 m of water: with no ice it sank to the ground, and on ice it stayed at the surface and kept 12.4 m/s. Serves 1 ("nothing ... in a way they didn't see coming") and TADR-009.
10. **Ice never forms inside the ball.** Where the ball already sits below the new surface, the slab forms just under it. The test measured 0.000 m of displacement. Serves 1: creating a surface never pops or blocks the ball.
11. **Frost on ice does nothing.** The table has no frost x ice row, so slabs never stack and land about one radius apart in an overlapping road. Serves TADR-009 (default-deny).
12. **Electrified water hurts enemies only.** The arc is the player's own weapon, so it never costs the player HP or momentum. Serves 8 ("feel like a badass") and 1.
13. **Dash and slam elements resolve in build's own physics step.** The `dashed` and `slammed` handlers only queue them. The frost trail lasts 0.7 s after a Frost Vane dash, with a visible frost puff every third tick, even over dry ground, so the part always shows it is working. Serves TADR-008 (a signal applies nothing) and form mirrors function.
14. **A won event pays out in the same frame.** The milestone handler grants the part at once, so the milestone stinger and "New part" land as one beat. This is the one place build mutates inside a handler. Serves 3 ("win great rewards like key milestone rewards").
15. **Events award named milestone parts.** Surge Run awards Rime Heart: you rode the flood, now you freeze it. Hold the Relay awards Tempest Core. Any other event awards the first milestone part the run does not hold yet. Fabricators and vaults never offer milestone parts, and a gate offers them only when overcharged by two or more bonus cores (23). Class mods (Pylon Capacitor, Runner Overclock) are offered only to their class. Serves 3 and 5.
16. **Build owns the tuning mods.** Weapon numbers travel as tuning mods (`weapon_damage`, `weapon_rate`, `weapon_range`, `turret_damage`), so combat reads them with `tuning.mod(key)` and needs no spine change. Pattern levels, transformers and the element table are behind the build API; [src/build/SEAMS.md](src/build/SEAMS.md) lists every id. Serves 7.

17. **Tags make synergies legible.** Every part carries one or two of nine tags: frost, arc, kinetic, split, spiral, chain, anchor, speed and mass. A named synergy is a tag pair. It goes live when two different held parts carry its two tags, so "something that splits" is a clue a player can act on. Serves Andrew's synergy request ("coherent sense for the player to be willing to experiment") and 4.
18. **Eight named synergies, each a behaviour, never a bare number.**
    - Storm Lattice (chain + split): split bolts chain.
    - Thermal Shock (frost + arc): the slam's arc shatters ice at double damage, then refreezes.
    - Lightning Rod (arc + mass): each pickup sparks an arc at the nearest enemy.
    - Tesla Pylon (anchor + arc): arcs leap to two enemies while mounted or hooked.
    - Wrecking Ball (speed + kinetic): rams send out a shockwave.
    - Shrapnel Bloom (split + kinetic): kills burst into shrapnel.
    - Glacier Hull (mass + frost): hard landings crack out a frost ring.
    - Pinwheel (spiral + speed): at fast speed and above, a blade ring cuts the enemies you pass.

    Storm Lattice lost its +25% damage. Serves 4 ("a synergy changes behaviour, never just a percentage").
19. **Every synergy effect is capped.**
    - Shrapnel: 6 bursts a second, 3 targets each.
    - Lightning Rod: 6 zaps a second.
    - Tesla Pylon: 2 targets every 0.5 s.
    - Pinwheel: up to 4 targets, each cut at most once per 0.3 s.
    - Zap lines on screen: at most 12.
    - Rings: one per live synergy, 8 at most, each at its own tilt and in its first tag's colour.

    The behaviour test sent 20 kills in one tick and measured 6 shrapnel strikes. Serves 4 (visual clarity is the balance rule).
20. **Hint cards set goals.** An undiscovered card names one ingredient and gives a clue from the other's tag: "Chain Arc + something that splits." Offers carry a star line:
    - "* completes Storm Lattice *" when taking the part brings a synergy live;
    - "* ingredient: ??? *" when it is a card's named ingredient;
    - "* ingredient: Storm Lattice *" once that synergy is discovered.

    Serves Andrew's "goal setting and creating some excitement" and 1 (you choose by reading a gate at speed).
21. **Superseded by integration call 15 (I3): owners report every firing and the codex applies the once-per-attempt rule.** This was: **A synergy is discovered once per run.** Its first activation is the discovery. A retry that drops it and a later reactivation do not repeat it. From phase 2 the lasting record and the scrap burst belong to the codex registry. Serves 2 and 3.
22. **Accidental finds come from the systems, unnamed.** Each element-table row and the spiral + chain tag rule has a plain description, logged the first time it fires in a run. Every slam carries kinetic, so slamming your own ice shatters it: the spec's "frost followed by kinetic". Serves Andrew's "accidental ones" and TADR-009.
23. **Rarity follows source.**
    - Gates: common or uncommon at 0 bonus cores, uncommon or rare at 1, rare or rare-plus at 2 or more. At 2 or more, a gate may offer a milestone part.
    - Fabricators: uncommon.
    - Vaults: rare.
    - Won events: rare-plus.

    Only common parts may be plain percentages, and every gate pair holds a part that raises punch or transforms a pattern. The measured mean offered tier is 1.43, 2.38 and 3.00 at 0, 1 and 2 cores. Serves the demo's one demand (punch against plating) and 4.
24. **Labels state the demand.** An offer reads "{name}: {verb}. Punch +{n}", so what a part does for the plating check is on the arch. Serves form mirrors function.
25. **The capped behaviours start full.** Their per-second budgets refill at each stage entry, so the first shrapnel burst of a stage is never swallowed; the behaviour test caught that bug. Serves 8 ("feel like a badass" from the first kill).

26. **Spending compacts; only theft sheds.** The shell is drawn from `run.mass` and the parts, never from the wallet, so a fabricator purchase bolts on a part and keeps every chunk. On `scrap_lost`, the chunks the lost mass carried fly off toward the thief. Measured: 24 chunks before and after a 40-scrap purchase with heft 1.8 to 2.0; `lose_scrap(30)` took 24 chunks down to 20, and 4 flew off. Demand: the Warden, since mass is heft and heft is punch against plating. Serves 1 (Katamari growth). Strains 4 (visual clarity): chunks stay capped at 36.
27. **The shell grows a step per heft class; the collider never does.** Each class above I scales the shell by 7%, 1.28x at class V. The collider stays 1 m, as Andrew chose. Demand: the Warden and the boss, where heft is the number that matters, so the ball shows it. Strains 4: at class V the hardware stands about 1.1 m off the collider.
28. **The magnet grows with heft and still reaches further at speed.** Its heft reach is min(18, 6 * sqrt(heft)) m, measured from 6.0 m at heft 1 up to the 18 m cap, with the pull scaled by the same factor. I kept the 0.3 m per m/s speed term on top of the cap, so a fast ball still catches what it passes. A pickup changed planar speed by 0.000% across 6 pickups. Demand: the Warden (mass feeds heft). Serves 1. Strains 4: pieces pulled from 18 m away cross more of the screen.
29. **A Warden's scrap is thrown down the line.** Its drop lands in a 20 m line along the ball's heading, toward the gates that just opened, so you roll through your payout on the way out. Measured: 12 pieces reaching 18.1 m. Demand: the Warden. Serves 1.
30. **Cores buy gate tier.** A vault kill adds a rare part and a vault core, and a won event adds an event core. The gates read `run.gate_tier_bonus()`. Measured with 3 cores: rare and rare-plus offers; at 0 cores, common and uncommon. Both D and B were briefed to grant the event core, so build waits 0.1 s and skips its grant when world's `core_gained("event")` has already arrived. The build test measured no double core. Demand: events and vaults. Serves 3 (opt-in layers pay out). Strains 7 (fast without debt): two owners for one fact, so the handoff asks the coordinator to settle it.
31. **A hint card is read literally.** A named synergy needs its shown part plus another part with the second tag: "Chain Arc + something that splits" means Chain Arc, not any chain part. Under the looser tag-pair rule from call 17, the power curve's 9-part reference build had all 8 synergies live, which empties the cards as goals. Under this rule it has 4 of 8, and the skipper has 1. Unnamed tag rules keep the loose rule, because they are the accidents. Demand: gates and vaults, whose offers now complete specific cards. Serves Andrew's "goal setting and creating some excitement". Strains 4 less than before.
32. **Build's hint cards are declared on the first tick of play, not in setup.** `spine_codex_test` counts exact codex entries right after class select, and any module that declares at setup breaks it. The Tab codex matters only in play, and a repeat declare is harmless. The handoff asks the spine to make that test count only its own ids, after which the declare can move back to setup. Demand: none (plumbing). Strains 7.
33. **The power curve passes every band, and the modelled weapon output runs hot.** Ram punch at cruise measures 64, 144, 213 and 308 for the skipper, reference, engaged and absurd builds (heft 2.0, 4.5, 6.3 and 9.1); the skipper reaches 98.5 at blazing speed. The modelled DPS is 82x the skipper's for the engaged build and 422x for the absurd one, against floors of 3x and 6x. Patterns and synergy shot factors compound. The real numbers and caps are combat's, and tuning is a human-approved edit to the catalogue, so I left it and flagged it. Demand: the Warden and the boss. Strains 4.

34. **Gates open on flow's gates state, never on the charge reading.** A sealed beacon's shown charge can reach 1.0 a tick before the Warden's kill is recorded. Gates opened then would be ignored by flow and soft-lock the stage (spine review finding B1). Measured: `beacon_charge_changed(1.0)` while flow was in "beacon" opened no gates, and flow's own "gates" state opened them on all three stages. Demand: the Warden (breaking it must always lead out). Serves 1 and 2.
35. **Gates stand downhill of the ball.** Writer D's integration run measured gates 38 m ahead landing on the beacon bowl's 24-degree bank, and the climb cost 20 to 13 m/s. Build now tries headings nearest the ball's line first, at 38, 30, 24 and 18 m and spreads of 9 and 6 m. The first candidate whose gate spots and approach lines stay within 0.75 m of the ground under the ball wins; otherwise the least-climbing candidate is used. On the stub Spillway terrain on this branch the worst gate spot sat 0.73 m above the ground under the ball and always below the ball itself, and gate speed losses were 1.2 to 2.7%. Not yet measured on D's bowl terrain. Demand: the Warden, since the gates follow its break. Serves 1. Strains 4 slightly: at a wide turn the gates stand off the ball's line, so the player turns to them.
36. **Speed-derived numbers are measured on the fixed ground probe.** That is the default; the legacy spin probe is an F1 comparison only. The power-curve and magnet tests assert it. Serves 7.
37. **Stage 2's gates guarantee a pattern part to a build without one (B4).** The balance loop found skippers in two groups at stage 3: with Spiral Wave, Cross Split or Chain Arc they broke Warden III in about 19 to 81 s, and without one in 117 to 204 s. HP tuning moved both groups and could not merge them. Now, when stage 2's gate pair opens and nothing held transforms a pattern (Tempest Core counts), one of the two offers is one of those three parts (`Parts.roll_gate`). It replaces the second slot, so the synergy bait in the first slot survives, and a pattern part raises the pair's punch-or-pattern floor. At 0 or 1 bonus cores the pattern parts are already in the gate's rarities. At 2 or more none is, so only that slot drops to uncommon. The other gate is still a free choice, and the gates are still drive-through. Over 50 seeds, `build_catalogue_test` found a pattern part in every stage-2 pair for a pattern-less build; 42 pairs needed the swap. A build that already held a pattern, and every other stage, got exactly `roll()`'s pair for the same seed. Twenty seeded stage-2 openings through build offered one every time. Demand: the Warden III fight and Brindle. Serves 4 (a floor under insane scaling) and 1 (no stop). Strains choice slightly: a pattern-less build sees one fewer random offer on stage 2.
38. **Kinetic is off Arc Coil and Runner Overclock (B4).** Wrecking Ball (Double Feed + something that hits hard) was found in 33 of 48 bot runs (69%), over the 60% cap. Double Feed was held in 32 of those 48 runs, and 29 of the 32 also held a kinetic part. Kinetic was not the identity of Arc Coil (an arc slam) or of the Overclock (fire rate on the run), so both now carry one tag: Arc Coil `arc` and Runner Overclock `speed`. Parts per tag across the 28 parts:

    | Tag | frost | arc | kinetic | split | spiral | chain | anchor | speed | mass |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | Before | 3 | 4 | 8 | 2 | 3 | 3 | 5 | 8 | 7 |
    | After | 3 | 4 | 6 | 2 | 3 | 3 | 5 | 8 | 7 |

    Only kinetic changed, and all nine tags are still in use. Kinetic is the hidden tag of Wrecking Ball and Shrapnel Bloom alone, so only those two can move. Replaying the 48 t7 bot part lists under the new tags, with the same choices, moves Wrecking Ball from 29 to 21 runs (60% to 44%; the replay reads 4 runs lower than the codex's 33) and Shrapnel Bloom from 15 to 13 (31% to 27%). The other six are unchanged at 12 to 38%. Scaled to the measured shares, that is roughly 50% and 30%. A carrier count alone predicts less, since 8 to 6 of 28 parts lowers the odds of holding a kinetic part only a little. The replay says more because the Overclock and Arc Coil were the only kinetic part in 8 of the 29 Double Feed runs. Bots choose differently once the offers change, so the balance loop's rerun is the measurement. Demand: the Warden and vault, where the hint card is read. Serves 3 (a find should feel like a find).
39. **Gates never open across a static prop's approach.** Call 35's climb check only sampled terrain height, so a kerb, bench or ice slab standing on the fall line was invisible to it. Traced repro: stage 1's chute-2 kerb (map_spillway.gd build_props, a 2 x 2.4 x 16 m box at x -34, z 9 to 25) sat 2 m before an arch a straight roll-in landed beside, an unforeseen stop (momentum is sacred). `_candidate` now sweeps a ball-sized sphere (radius 1.0 m) from the ball to each gate spot, and a further 6 m past it along facing, on the world physics layer terrain and props share; the terrain's own "Ground" body (the only StaticBody3D always named that) is excluded so only real obstacles count. A blocked candidate is heavily penalised so `choose_placement` keeps searching; if every candidate is blocked, the least-blocked one is used and a warning is pushed once, never a failure. `build_gate_props_test` reproduced the kerb block on the unfixed code (red), passed after the fix (green), and swept 20 seeded ball positions and headings across all three stage maps, catching similar blocks on stages 2 and 3 as well. `build_gate_seam_test`'s momentum checks (climb <= 0.75 m, speed loss <= 15%) stayed green. Demand: the Warden, since the gates follow its break. Serves 1 (nothing the player did not choose may stop the ball).

Open for integration: in live play, frost and arc find water only once world answers `ctx.terrain.material_at(point)` (now in CONTRACTS) or registers a water provider. Until then the element table works in the tests, and in play the dash shows only its frost puffs.

## Audio and feel (writer C)

1. **What the player does sounds now; what the world announces lands on the beat.** A jump, a hit, a pickup, a death or the gate you rolled through plays the moment it happens. A stage, the gates, the klaxon, a milestone or the stage clear waits for the next beat and plays through the band, at most 349 ms later. Serves 1 (feedback never lags your own move) and 6 ("stingers land on the beat").
2. **A burst of flow facts becomes one phrase.** Flow stingers queue in one lane. A run start folds stage 1's hit into its fanfare. A stage clear plays its jingle, and the next stage's hits come exactly two bars later, as the cadence resolves. The last clear folds into the run-end finale. Serves 6 and form mirrors function (one event, one sound).
3. **The stage clear is the rice cooker.** The band cadences A7b9 to D major, a Picardy lift out of the minor key. Over it a mallet-and-beep melody climbs the A7 chord, turns, lands on D, and two "done" beeps ring on high D. Serves Andrew's end-of-cycle-melody reference and 6.
4. **You hear which stage you entered.** Stage N plays N stabs an eighth apart. Serves form mirrors function.
5. **One musical source.** `harmony.gd` holds the tempo, key and scales, and both the band and every pitched cue read it. Rewards climb D minor pentatonic (D F G A C), whose notes sit inside every chord of the loop, so rapid scrap pickups, kill streaks and the beacon charge climb in key. At 100% the beacon lands on home, two octaves up. Serves form mirrors function and 4 (growth you can hear).
6. **Danger is low and minor.** Getting hurt is a buzzy F falling to D. An enemy arriving is a low E-F semitone growl, and a miniboss is pitched a fourth lower and louder. The klaxon is two falling minor-third "WAH"s on the beat. Death is a falling minor line into a low D that sags a fourth. Measured: danger cues average 291 zero crossings a second against 3,617 for rewards. Serves form mirrors function.
7. **The band is 172 BPM drum and bass in D Dorian.** The eight-bar loop is Dm9, G13, Dm9, Cmaj9, Bbmaj9, G13, then Em7b5 and A7b9 as the ii-V turnaround. Calm leaves space: electric-piano chords, a sub and a light hat. Pressure brings the two-step kick up, then snare, ghost notes and a jazz ride, then the reese bass, then the lead, then a bell arpeggio. A new layer enters on the next downbeat behind a one-beat riser. Serves 6 ("layers rise with director pressure").
8. **The lead is a synth saxophone.** It scoops into each note and uses reedy FM and delayed vibrato. It plays a two-bar call and an answer, sequences the call up, and ends on C#, the leading tone back to D. The answer changes every loop (inversion, retrograde or a displaced echo), and a few chromatic approach notes are added. The lead is seeded, so a run replays the same. Serves 6 ("jazzy melodic invention, call and response").
9. **Death never stops the music.** On the next beat the band drops to the keys and waits with you. A retry cancels any pending announcement, and the phrase restarts from bar 1 with the stage hit on the next beat. Serves 2 (quick restart, no dead air).
10. **Hit-stop is weight, never obstruction.** It lasts 30-50 ms and is used only for big contact hits: a ram at 20 m/s or more, a stomp falling at 12 m/s or more, strong slam knockback, or a full slam of strength 0.9 or more. Stops are at least 220 ms apart, so a chain of rams never stutters. Weapon fire and damage taken never freeze. Measured on the real `Engine.time_scale`: the longest dip was 41 ms. Serves 8 and 1.
11. **Squash and stretch is an underdamped spring.** A landing squashes the ball along the contact normal, overshoots into a stretch and settles in about a quarter second, with the contact point planted. The ball also stretches along its travel line with speed. Serves 8 ("snappy and fluid").
12. **Camera punches follow your moves and stay short.** The FOV widens 7 degrees on a dash, 5 on a boost, 16 on the stage launch, and 4 to 10 on a slingshot, scaled by throw speed. A slam kicks the camera down and zooms in 4 degrees, for a thump. Big contact hits kick the camera toward the enemy. Serves 8 ("feel like a badass").
13. **No effect crosses the line ahead.** The trail is the ball's past. Speed lines run in a sleeve 1.7-3.4 m around the ball, from beside it backwards. Glyphs are additive, small (1.8 m at most, apart from flat ground rings) and last 0.7 s or less. Serves 4 ("never let our own effects hide an enemy") and 8.
14. **Rings show real reach.** The slam ring grows to the slam's radius (9 m) and the plant ring to the pylon's repel radius (8 m), read from `augments.gd`. Serves form mirrors function.
15. **Every glyph takes its shape and colour from its event_kinds row.** Examples: the square scrap sparkle, the copper kill burst, the amber slam ring and the bar muzzle flash. Speed effects use the speed tier's own colours. Serves form mirrors function.
16. **Classes sound like themselves.** The arc caster fires three semitones low and the rapid laser four semitones high, each with slight random detune. The slingshot sings higher the faster it throws you, and the pylon plant rises with the speed you banked. Serves 5 and 8 ("the pylon keeps momentum", heard).
17. **It never turns to mush.** Each cue has a voice limit and a minimum gap. For example, a held trigger plays one shot per 45 ms and at most four overlapping. Priority 2 and 3 cues duck the band. The SFX and master buses have limiters, and the band soft-clips below full scale. Serves 4 (clarity) and 6.
18. **Mixer keys.** `-` and `=` set the music level, `[` and `]` set the effects, and `M` mutes everything. A corner readout shows the levels for 1.6 s. The music keeps playing at 45% while paused. Serves 2 and Andrew's request for sound feedback he can tune.
19. **The title never waits for the synth.** Baking the 32 cues and the band's notes takes about 2 s of CPU, so it runs on a worker thread at startup. Cues in that first window are skipped. The band then costs about 3-7% of one core on the main thread. Serves 2 (quick to start).
20. **Headless runs have no audio output.** On the dummy driver (the tests, or a machine with no sound device) no players start. The band runs on a real-time clock, so every cue decision and beat still happens. This also avoids the playback leak Godot prints when a test quits in the same frame it frees a playing player.

Demo spec, phase 1 (heft, plating, Warden, cores, codex):

21. **Hit-stop means "you broke through".** A stop is asked only when a hit lands on plating above 0 and is not deflected in the same frame, and when a Warden breaks. Stops are at least 0.4 s apart. Crushes, plating-0 kills, deflected hits and open-ground slams never freeze. Each strike is judged at the end of the frame, so the order in which combat publishes the strike and the deflection doesn't matter. Measured: 10 crushes in 1 s send 0 stops, and 5 break-throughs in 1 s send 2. Until combat exposes `plating_of`, the old big-contact thresholds are the fallback. Serves 1 (the roll-up never stalls) and the stage demand (punch against plating).
22. **The clank tells you how close you were.** A deflected hit rings bright plate metal an octave above the punch's thud. It rises in pitch as your punch nears the plating: 7 semitones low when far off, at pitch when nearly through. Measured: 324 Hz and 15% of its energy after 80 ms, against the punch's 192 Hz and 2%. Serves the stage demand and form mirrors function.
23. **Growth is visible and audible.** Each heft class swells the shell 10% (visual only: the collider radius is measured unchanged) with an overshoot and a squash-pop. A ground ripple goes out, and a three-note A-D-A climb plays on the next beat. Losing mass plays a torn-metal zip, never a stinger. Serves 4 (a growth trajectory you can see and hear) and 1.
24. **An overcharged beacon sounds warmer.** Each bonus core swells a warm pad under the band on the next bar: gain 0.7 for one core, full for two or more. The pad stays until the stage ends. The core-socket chime climbs the reward ladder with the core count. Serves 3 (opting in pays off, and you can hear it).
25. **The Warden break is the stage's drop.** `beacon_unsealed` plays a critical stinger on the beat: a clamp tearing loose, then the band hits and bells climb out. It is also the one non-strike hit-stop, gets a 9-degree FOV punch, and flashes a star over the beacon with a ring out to the beacon's edge. The gates fanfare is folded into it, so "gates open" plays once. Serves 3 and 8.
26. **Discovery is the most exciting short cue in the game.** The fanfare is a bebop run up the Dorian scale with a chromatic lean on C#, landing on high D over the home chord and a crash, on the beat. A banner drops in from the top edge (never over the crosshair), a burst in the category colour fires, and a ring in that colour stays on the ball for the run, up to 4. A repeat activation plays 6 dB softer, with a banner reading "Synergy" instead of "New synergy". Serves Andrew's synergy direction and form mirrors function (your build shows on the ball).
27. **Unnamed finds get a small, curious chime.** It is a rising F to D, lighter than the fanfare, so accidental discoveries feel noticed without competing with named ones. Serves the codex treasure hunt.
28. **Elements sound like what they did.** A freeze forms ice (a crystalline bell cluster), an electrified surface hums at 60 Hz with crackle, and a shatter is glass pings over a noise burst. The voice is picked from the behaviour string, so new materials reuse them. Serves form mirrors function.
29. **One codex look.** Each category has one colour, one silhouette and a procedural 64 px icon. The discovery burst, the ball ring, the banner and (through `presentation.codex_style()`) the codex page and the HUD all read it. The spine's `CODEX_CATEGORIES` table in event_kinds wins, so the colours and shapes are the spine's: build amber diamond, world arc wave, enemy rust burst, npc sandstone square, coop copper chevron. Presentation's own table is the fallback. Serves form mirrors function.
30. **Losing a heft class falls softly.** When a crane theft drops the class, the climb plays back down (A, F, D) on the mallet, 3 dB under the climb, with a small rattle. It lands on home rather than below it, because the spill trail pays the mass back. The ring falls inward in the torn-scrap colour, and the shell shrinks. The direction comes from the last class heard until the spine publishes one. Serves 1 (a setback, not a death) and form mirrors function.

Demo spec, phase 1b (level themes and boss tiers):

31. **One key, many rooms.** Every theme and boss track stays in D, with jazzy extended chords. So the reward ladder, the beacon rungs and every chime are in key wherever you are, and each level still has its own palette, groove and motif. Spillway is the D Dorian drum and bass. Sluice is funk: a clavinet riff, brass stabs answering it, a brass hook, and a mechanical groove with the kick on 1, the "a" of 1 and 3 and accents on the offbeat sixteenths. Weir is the halfpipe: gospel organ chops, a half-time roll, chords that climb to an A7sus4 and roll back, a bass ladder that goes up for four bars and down for four, and a talkbox lead whose answer is its call played backwards. Serves 6 and Andrew's level-theme brief.
32. **A theme changes on a downbeat.** A stage entry restarts the phrase on the stage stinger's beat, which becomes bar 1 of the new theme. A direct theme change waits for the next bar. Serves 6 (never mid-bar).
33. **The miniboss darkens its level.** It comes in on the next bar at 172. The major chords turn minor (G13 to Gm9, Cmaj9 to Cm9, Fmaj9 to Fm9 and so on), the lead doubles an octave up, a bell fills every sixteenth on top, and a riser leads into every other bar. It cadences home on its kill. Serves 3 (an event you opt into sounds like one) and Andrew's "intense variation".
34. **The Warden is the level as club music, at 129.** On `warden_engaged`, the next bar is a transition: its kick and open hat pulse half-note triplets, three to the bar, and that pulse becomes the new quarter at 129. That pivot always falls on a 172 beat (a 172 bar is exactly three 129 beats, 61,536 samples). The build is four on the floor with a funk octave bass, dark filtered chord stabs, claps, a sidechain pump on keys, bass, pads and lead, and a riser every four bars. `beacon_unsealed` hits the drop on the unseal stinger's beat: bright stabs, the level's lead and motif, bells, and a crash every four bars. After eight bars of drop it cadences home and returns to 172 on a beat. F6 switches to "four on the floor held at 172" so Andrew can choose by ear. Serves 6, 8 (the drop is the badass moment) and 1 (the music never stops).
35. **Brindle has his own set.** It is at 129: a boom and an anvil, industrial kicks, claps, bright brass stabs and a brass fanfare over D minor, the Neapolitan Eb and A7b9. Phase 1 quotes Weir's motif, phase 2 Sluice's and phase 3 Spillway's, so the fight replays the run in reverse. Organ is added from phase 2 and bells in phase 3. The victory runs Bbmaj9, C13 and A7b9, then holds D major while the fanfare climbs D, A and high D, landing on home, and settles to the idle band. It is driven by `boss_arena_entered`, `boss_phase_changed` (using its phase argument when present) and `boss_defeated`, each guarded for wave 4. Serves 5 and 6.
36. **Stingers keep the groove at 129.** Every grid stinger is baked on both grids, so the unseal roll and the class climb land in time over the club beat. Serves 6.
37. **The title waits for Spillway only.** The cues and the Spillway band bake first, in about 4.5 s on a worker. Sluice, Weir and the boss palettes bake on a second worker behind stage 1, in about 6 s, and anything needed before then renders on demand. The note cache is locked, so the two threads never race. Measured cost of the densest arrangement (miniboss at full pressure): 52-56 ms of main-thread time per second of music, 5.6% of one core. Serves 2.

Round 2, phase 1c (Andrew: "the bass should be kind of like looped throughout ... either rhythmic or drops at like the perfect moment ... electro dubstep, 2011-2012"). Items 38 to 47 amend 7 (calm no longer drops the bass), 25 and 34 (the unseal is now a brostep drop with a build).

38. **The bass never leaves.** Every theme at every pressure plays a rhythmic bass figure over a clean sub, including calm and the title. Spillway's calm figure is a root-and-fifth roll that opens into the reese line with an octave pop and a chromatic approach. Sluice's calm figure is a sparse slap that opens into the full funk octave line. Weir's calm figure swings between root and octave, then becomes the ladder that climbs for four bars and falls for four. Each figure note cuts the one before, so the riff stays tight. Measured over two bars, bass bus alone: calm rms 0.14-0.15 with 6-8 figure notes; full rms 0.21-0.24 with 10-18 notes. Serves Andrew's "looped throughout" and 6.
39. **The wobble is locked to the grid.** From mid pressure a wobble bass enters: detuned saws and a square sub-octave through a resonant low-pass that opens and closes once per LFO cycle. Each cycle is retriggered on the grid at 1/4, 1/8, 1/16, 1/8 triplet or 1/16 triplet, one rate per bar, switching bar to bar from a per-theme list, so the LFO never drifts off the beat at 172, 150 or 129. Below full pressure it answers the riff in the second half of the bar (call and response); at full it fills the bar. It plays chord roots, and on odd bars walks into the next root from a half step below, so it stays in key. Measured: 576 cycles over 48 bars across every tempo, all on subdivision points of their own rate, all 5 rates used. Serves Andrew's "LFO-synced wobble" and 6.
40. **Growls talk.** Two formant growls sit over the wobble: "yoi" glides o to i, "wub" glides u to a and back. They answer on the offbeats in drops. Sluice's wobble talks yoi, Spillway's and Weir's say wub. A reese sits under the drop and a clean sub under everything, so the weight never depends on the filter. Serves Andrew's "growl and talking formant bass".
41. **Every drop is built.** A riser, a snare roll that accelerates from quarters to sixteenths to thirty-seconds, and a high-pass sweep (20 Hz to 600 Hz) over the band. The riser and the roll skip the sweep, so the build climbs while the band thins. Then silence, and the drop on the downbeat: half-time drums, the wobble changing rate every bar, growls, the sub and a reese, and bright jazzy stabs. Serves Andrew's "drops at the perfect moment" and 8.
42. **The launch is the build; the landing is the drop.** `launched` starts the build over the rail flight, with the rice-cooker climb up A7 looping on a mallet. The clear jingle rings over it on the beat, and the cadence waits. `stage_entered` hands the build its stage. The first `landed` after that drops on the next beat in the new stage's theme, with silence from touchdown to the drop, never more than one beat later (measured 0.02-0.91 beats). If no landing comes within 2 s, it drops anyway. A retry, death, run start or arena entry cancels the build. Serves 1 (the flight feels like acceleration, not a loading screen) and 8.
43. **The Warden's last quarter is the build; the unseal is the heaviest drop in the stage.** Presentation reads the Warden's integrity through the spine's read-only `flow.warden_integrity()`. From three quarters broken, the build level follows the damage, so each hit tightens the roll and opens the sweep, and the riser retriggers each bar it runs out. `beacon_unsealed` drops on the next beat with the unseal stinger on the same downbeat: eight bars of brostep back in D with crashes, extra kicks and growls, the level's lead on top, then the kill cadence home to 172. Serves 3 (the opt-in fight pays off) and 8.
44. **Every Brindle phase change is a drop, and lifts the key.** The phase stinger plays on the next beat, and one bar of build later the drop lands on a downbeat. Phase 2 goes a half step up into Phrygian half time. Phase 3 goes a whole step up, with the tempo ramping to 150 beat by beat across the build. The chase returns to 172. Each phase adds a layer: the wobble answers in phase 1 and fills the bar from phase 2. The victory is the biggest drop: a two-bar build, a crash every bar, the bells on top, then the victory phrase resolving home to D. Serves BOSS-BRINDLE's "each phase moves the key up a step and adds a layer" and 8.
45. **A slam womps at once.** A slam is the player's own action, so its sub-bass womp plays the same audio frame, never quantized. It is a sine falling from 86 to 34 Hz with a wub on top, scaled by strength and flatness: the flat full plant is the loudest and a fourth lower. Measured: the womp starts on the slam's frame, and the flat full plant sits at pitch 0.75 against 0.93 for a half-strength tilted slam. Serves 1 and 8.
46. **Class-up gets a bass stab.** A growl on low D lands under the third note of the A-D-A climb. Serves 4 (growth you hear).
47. **Danger ducks the bass.** Hurt, enemy arrival, the boss telegraph, the klaxon, threat rising and death pull the bass bus (sub, figure, wobble) to 30% while they sound, and it comes back over a beat. So the low end is loud and felt but never masks a warning. The SFX and master limiters catch the stacked peaks of a slam and womp. Serves 4 (clarity).
48. **Cost.** Measured warm, the densest arrangement is 5.9% of one core (Brindle phase 3 at full pressure), and the miniboss is 5.6-6.5% depending on load. The test holds the band under 8%. Notes are warmed on a worker, and a note heard before its warm-up renders on demand the first time. Serves 2.
49. **Arena entry fades the stage.** `stage_content_cleared` plays a soft falling breath of air with a low D under it, 12 dB down, and cancels any launch build. Brindle's arrival is the event, not the fade. Serves form mirrors function (priority decides prominence).

Round 3, phase 1d (Andrew: "I love the gusto, but ... rein back the wubs. I think the underlying bass is great ... really well layered and complementary ... like Frank Ocean, Pyramids ... the melodies ... textured, laid on top of each other, thrown in and layered and remixed as the song progresses"). The style is distilled, not copied: no melody, progression or lyric from the song. Items 50 to 57 amend 39 and 40 (the wobble and growls leave the level themes), 41 and 43 (builds layer and open a low-pass instead of high-passing the band), 44 (Brindle's phases add layers, not wobble) and 46 (the class-up stab is the slap bass).

50. **The wubs are reined in.** No wobble and no talking growl in any level theme or the miniboss, at any pressure. The bass figures of item 38 stay exactly as they were. The figure's level rises 0.36 to 0.42 so it sits forward where the wobble was. Measured: zero wobble voices over 8 bars at full pressure in all three themes and the miniboss. Serves Andrew's "tailor back on the wubs" and "the underlying bass is great".
51. **The texture is additive.** Six melodic layers enter one by one as pressure rises, each staying once in:
    - a warm pad (from 0.1);
    - an arpeggiator shimmer (0.3);
    - a wordless formant choir on "ah" and "ooh" (0.45);
    - the lead (0.55);
    - a chopped echo of the lead, each note back a dotted eighth later, cut short and pitched up (0.7);
    - a counter-line (0.8).

    Measured, melodic layers sounding at pressure 0, 0.2 to 1: 0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6 in every theme. Serves Andrew's "laid on top of each other" and 6.
52. **Lines interlock and recombine.** The counter-line is the lead's own call, two bars late and a third lower, refitted to the chord it lands on, so the counter calls while the lead answers. In its "answer" rotation the choir sings the lead's note from two bars back, snapped to the current chord: one line's fragment answering another. Everything stays in D with the per-level harmony. Serves "thrown in and layered and remixed".
53. **The texture evolves every four bars.** Every four bars the lines trade voices:
    - the echo moves between bell, mallet and clav, and its interval between an octave, a fifth and an octave-and-a-fifth up;
    - the counter-line moves between electric piano, chopped "ooh" choir and bell;
    - the choir moves between guide tones and answers, and between "ah" and "ooh";
    - the arpeggiator changes shape and octave.

    Measured: 18 re-voicings over 24 bars of each theme at full pressure, at least one in every 8-bar cycle, and none repeating the one before. Serves "remixed as the song progresses" without new drops.
54. **The tone opens with intensity.** A gentle low-pass over the band (never touching the bass, which sits far below it) is darker when calm (2.8 kHz) and fully open from pressure 0.7. Calm reads as filtered keys; full pressure is bright. Serves the Pyramids-style slow reveal and 4 (the calm state is clear, not busy).
55. **Builds come from layering and the filter.** A build raises the band's intensity to its build level, so layers stack in as it rises. It closes the low-pass to 700 Hz and opens it back up into the drop. The riser and snare roll stay, but they are no longer the whole build, and the bass stays full through it. Measured: the Warden's build now holds rms 0.107 to 0.116, where the 1c high-pass build fell to 0.043. Serves Andrew's "builds from layering and filter opening".
56. **Drops pay off with the full texture plus the bass.**
    - A landing drop re-enters the stage's own theme with every layer in, a crash and a boom.
    - The Warden and Brindle drops go to half time with the full texture on top, a clean sub and reese, and a restrained wobble: half level (0.5), slow rates only (1/4, 1/8, 1/8 triplet), still locked to the grid.
    - The victory is the biggest.

    Measured: 62 wobble cycles in the Warden and Brindle drops, all on the grid, at level 0.5. Serves "the drops' energy, just less wub".
57. **Brindle's phases add layers.** Phase 1 sits at intensity 0.8 (pad, arp, choir, lead, half the echo), phase 2 at 0.9 (echo, half the counter) and phase 3 at 1.0 (everything), with the key lifts of item 44 unchanged. Cost, measured warm: the densest point is Brindle phase 3 at 7.2% of one core, and the levels run at 5.7-6.2%; the test cap stays at 8%. Serves BOSS-BRINDLE's "adds a layer" and 2.

Phase 1d consolidation (Andrew, listening back: "the dance hall funky ... Euro music hall ... the Warden and Spillway ... pulling all the gems to make something that really gleams"). The ledger is `src/presentation/MUSIC-GEMS.md`: every gem, where it lives, and whether it was kept, restored or elevated. `tests/presentation_gems_test.gd` guards them. Items 58 to 61 amend 34, 43, 56 and 42.

58. **The Warden is round 2's club again (G3, G4).** The build is four on the floor with claps, open hats, the funk octave bass, the sidechain pump and dark filtered stabs. The unseal drop is round 2's bright club drop (bright stabs, a crash every four bars, a clap with a snare, the lead), with the full layered texture on top and no wobble. Round 3's half-time brostep drop is gone from the Warden. The level's own calm bass figure (G8) runs softly under the funk bass on the reese, so each level's Warden still carries its bass. Measured: the pivot lands at 129 on a 172 beat, and the drop plays 9 kicks in 2 bars with the funk bass and the pump on. Serves Andrew's "love, love, love" and the Euro-club wave.
59. **The Warden escalates (G7).** At half broken, a pickup bar (a snare run over the new key's own dominant) lifts the key a half step; the stabs brighten, a snare joins the clap, and the lead comes in an octave up with the arp. At three quarters the tritone pickup opens the sinister break: half time, the bass walking the tritone, the lead leaning on the flat second, dark stabs and the choir. Its build (the roll, the riser, layers stacking and the low-pass closing and reopening) tightens with every hit. The unseal drops home to D. Serves Andrew's "sinister and intense ... so immersed" and 3 (the fight you opt into escalates).
60. **Brindle's drops are the funk club too (G4).** Four on the floor, clap and snare, his anvils, the funk octave bass and bright brass stabs, with the full texture. A restrained wobble (half level, slow rates, second half of the bar) is the only wobble left in the game. Serves the Euro-club wave and R2.
61. **The launch carries the rice-cooker cadence (G2).** From round 3 on, every real stage clear falls inside the launch build, so the band's A7b9-to-D cadence had stopped playing. The build now holds A7b9, the dominant, as its tension, and the landing drop resolves it to D, under the unchanged rice-cooker jingle that climbs A7 and rings two "done" beeps on high D. Serves G2 and form mirrors function (tension to rest is dominant to tonic).

Fixes from visual QA and the adversarial review (fix wave C3):

62. **The discovery banner never outlives its moment.** Visual QA frames 19 to 26 showed a "New synergy" card pinned top-centre across the arena, every Brindle phase, death, victory and the summary, because it ran on the paused tree's clock. It now runs on the real clock with process mode Always, about 3 s in all. It clears at once on `flow_changed`, `stage_content_cleared`, `player_died` and `run_ended`, queues quick discoveries one at a time, and never plays a queued one over a screen. It follows the UI regions rule: it sits in the toasts band, right of the HUD's centred toast column, and drops into the middle view on a screen too narrow for that; never in the top or prompts bands. Serves 1 (nothing lingers in the way) and 4 (clarity).
63. **An arena retry brings the band back.** A death in the arena left the band in DEAD mode (keys only) for the whole retried fight. Re-entering the arena now restarts the boss track on its first section, home key and play mode on the next beat; `flow_changed("boss")` also resyncs play mode. Arena entry clears the stage's overcharge pad and re-reads heft in audio, juice and VFX, so the shell and pad follow the restore. Serves 2 (quick restart) and item 24.
64. **Hit-stop reads the hit as combat resolved it.** Juice asks `ctx.spawner.resolve(enemy, kind, force)`, the pure read combat itself uses, for the plating the hit actually met, part included. A weak-point cap at plating 0 therefore never freezes, and a body break-through still does. `plating_of`, which reads body plating, remains the fallback. Serves item 21.

C4, "B everywhere" (Andrew, round 4: "MY MAN warden spill B is fire ... apply that to ... all the OST tracks and game sounds all throughout"). Warden Spillway B's grammar is gem G11 and the rule for every track and cue: keep the loved core; escalate on real game-state thresholds; add layers, never swap; land the payoff on the gameplay moment. Items 65 to 72 amend 42 to 44 (Brindle's changes now drop on the next beat), 33 (the miniboss escalates) and 5 (cues follow the live key).

65. **One B arc for every track.** `escalate(fraction)` is the Warden's arc, generalized. Half way, a pickup bar on the new key's dominant lifts the key a half step, and the lead rises an octave. At three quarters the sinister break starts where the track has one: half time, the tritone bass and the flat second, with a build (roll, riser, layers stacking, the low-pass closing and reopening) tightening with the fraction. The caller owns the payoff. The loved core keeps playing throughout; the test checks kick, bass and sub through every arc. Serves G11.
66. **The Warden in every level has the full arc.** The poll now follows the Warden through the lift. It had stopped at the build, so in play the break never fired. Sluice's Warden adds its clav on the offbeat sixteenths and Weir's adds gospel organ chops, over the same club. Serves G3, G4 and G5.
67. **The miniboss escalates on its integrity** (`ctx.spawner.integrity_of`, guarded). At 50% the key lifts; at 25% the sinister break starts, tightening with every hit. The kill drops home into the level on the next beat, in D, and the cadence follows. Serves 3 (the opt-in fight pays off).
68. **Events escalate on their own progress.** Half way lifts; three quarters builds, with no sinister turn, because an event is an opt-in reward, not a threat. A win drops home on the next beat with the milestone stinger on the drop. A pass or a loss settles gracefully: the build fades and the key walks home through a pickup bar, with no drop and no sting. Progress comes from `event_progress(event_id, fraction)` when the spine publishes it. Until then it is elapsed time over each event's usual length (Hold the Relay 18 s, Surge Run 22 s, Scrap Stampede 16 s); see REQUEST. Serves 3 and 1.
69. **Brindle builds inside each phase.** His HP falling toward the phase floor lifts the key half way. The floor held through the set piece is the sinister break, tightening with the set-piece clock. The phase change drops on the next beat into the next phase's key and tempo. In the chase, the heartbeat lifts and builds as the scooter's lead closes, and the catch drops the victory on the next beat, home in D at 172. The phase floors and set-piece lengths are read from the boss's own Rules table through the script's constant map, not copied here. Serves G6, G7 and G9.
70. **Heft brightens the band.** A class-up re-voices the next bar one step brighter (intensity +0.12, so one more layer is nearer), and a drop steps it back. The overcharge pad stays as it was. Serves 4 (growth you can hear).
71. **Every pitched cue follows the live key.** Reward and pickup chimes, the clank, the thwack and the other pitched "now" cues are raised by the live key. On-beat stingers (class-up, class-down, milestone, threat, boss phase) are baked in the live key, so after a Warden lift the chimes are in Eb. The synergy fanfare climbs into the current level's motif head. Warden hits and clanks climb the pentatonic as its integrity falls, and the unseal resolves them home. The vault's riser rises with its damage, and its break is a small drop on the moment. A synergy shockwave gets its own light chime, never the slam womp or a weak-point read. Serves form mirrors function.
72. **Death and retry are phrased.** A death breaks the band on the next beat (a boom and a crash, everything cut), then keys only. A retry fills into the groove: a snare pickup climbs into the restart on the beat after next. `stage_content_cleared("arena_retry")` restarts Brindle from phase 1 in D with the pickup. Serves 2 (quick restart that still feels composed).

## Events and stages (writer D)

1. **Three stages, each its own map, built once at setup.** Stage 1 keeps the wave-1 Spillway shape. Stage 2, the Sluice, and stage 3, the Weir, are new.
