The idle stages stay in the physics world, hidden and parked 5 km down, because re-adding a trimesh cost about 34 ms a swap and parking costs microseconds. The whole game is ready in about 1 s (measured 0.92 to 1.15 s headless). Serves 1 (no hitch in the launch).
2. **Height queries read the built grid and split each cell the way the collider does.** What the spine, combat and build ask about the ground is the ground the ball rolls on. Serves 7 and TADR-008 (one committed geometry).
3. **The momentum kit is a vocabulary of pure height shapes:** landing hills, banked berms, kickers, sluice chutes, a halfpipe weir, a relay mesa, flood bowls and dry causeways, plus grapple posts and pylon walls as props. The Sluice lands you on a hill, puts a kicker on the straight line between two berms, and drops two chutes into a bowl. The Weir lands you running east, cuts a halfpipe south through the middle, and puts the relay on a mesa with a berm round its foot. Serves 1.
4. **Chutes shallow out at their exits and stay open.** A carved channel that ends in a quarter-pipe wall would turn your speed straight up. Serves 1.
5. **The ground says what it is for.** Any face steep enough for a pylon to bite (over 30 degrees) is tinted verdigris concrete. Kickers, berms and landing hills are tinted amber, the movement colour. Grapple posts are copper with an amber cap, and pylon walls are verdigris stripes. Serves 5 and form mirrors function.
6. **The launch is a cannon rail.** Taking a gate fires the ball at 24 m/s of lift, and the rail holds its velocity to the arc for the whole flight, so nothing it clips on the way out can cost it speed. Rings are drawn on the exact arc it will fly. The launch test measured 21.93 m/s before the gate and 21.92 m/s through the flight and at the next stage's entry. Serves 1 ("the stage exit is a launch into the next stage").
7. **The next stage marks the landing and catches it on a hill.** Amber rings trace the fall onto a landing pad, and the ground falls away along the entry heading so the fall becomes speed. The test measured touchdown at 20.83 m/s, 28.36 m/s half a second later, and the pad 0.9 m from where the ball came down. Serves 1 and 8 (a drop from above that feels like a badass landing).
8. **The event director is pure, stepped and seeded (TADR-010).** A retry reuses the stage's seed, so it replays the same event plan and the same miniboss spots. A new entry draws a fresh plan from the run's stream. Serves 2 (a retry is the same stage, learnable and fair).
9. **Opt-in events are announced, then armed, then yours to take.** Each one is announced 4 s before its ring arms. A 70 m beam in the event colour stands over the ring, and a sign says "roll in: milestone reward". Rolling into the ring starts it. An offer you ignore waits armed and never ends or nags. A loss re-offers the event 12 s later. A win calls `run.record_milestone`, so `milestone_won` reaches build. Serves 3.
10. **No event can block the exit.** Rings, beams, gates, the surge front, the relay and the hauler have no colliders. The flow test ignores an armed Scrap Stampede and clears stage 1 through the beacon, a gate and the launch. Serves 3 ("skipping an event never blocks the exit") and 1.
11. **Surge Run: the water is the timer and the tailwind.** Rolling into the ring releases a wall of water down the route. Six gates must be taken in order before the front reaches each one. Riding just ahead of the front pushes you on at 14 m/s². One visible body carries both the threat and the help. On the Weir the gates alternate up the halfpipe walls. Serves 8 and 1.
12. **Hold the Relay charges while you are in its ring, and pauses when you leave without resetting.** Circling, ramming and turreting on one of its four walls all count. It needs 18 s inside a 60 s window. The spawn director spikes while it is live. Serves 5 (the engineer's turret spot, the runner's circle) and 1.
13. **A miniboss arrives mid-event at a seeded spot.** On the relay it comes at 35% charge, 32 to 46 m from the relay. On a stage with threat of 2 or more, which is stage 3, the Surge Run brings one at half its gates. Six seeds gave six distinct spots, and the same seed gives the same spot. Serves 3 (Andrew's "a miniboss arriving mid-surge") and TADR-010.
14. **Scrap Stampede is stage 1's own event.** A hauler bolts from the pump house down the east chute into the basin at cruise speed. Every ram knocks 8 scrap loose through `run.add_scrap`, and the hauler runs 8% faster after each hit. Three rams win it. Serves 1 (loot on the move, collisions have weight) and TADR-010 (an event unique to a map).
15. **The dust squall is seen before it is felt, and its wind never brakes.** A dust wall walks in from upwind over a 6 s lead. The haze is capped at a fog density of 0.006, so threats at combat range stay readable. The wind blows at 5 m/s². Serves 1, 4 (visual clarity is the balance rule) and 8.
16. **No event push may oppose the ball's motion.** The director drops that component from the summed squall and surge push in one place, so a headwind costs nothing and a tailwind is free speed. The events test found the worst component along the ball's motion is 0 over 36 headings. Serves 1 and 7.
17. **Every event that starts also ends.** When a stage ends under a live event, the world closes it with `passed`, so music and HUD layers never hang into the next stage. Serves 6.
18. **Pressure for the music is spawn pressure plus the events.** Each live opt-in event adds 0.25, and the squall and a rising flood add up to 0.1 each. The events' total is capped at 0.4. Serves 6 (layers rise with the chaos you chose).
19. **A combat module without a miniboss gets a chaser at the same spot.** If `spawn_enemy("miniboss", at)` returns null, the world asks for a chaser there. Serves 7.
20. **Each stage floods its own bowl on a seeded rhythm.** The stage 2 and 3 bowls sit below the ground around them, so the water plane never floats over dry land. The flood's drag is unchanged, and the spine's open question about drag near a beacon stays open for review. The Sluice's beacon ring is half in its bowl, so the dry half is always a choice. Serves 1 and TADR-010 (ebb and flow).
21. **`ctx.terrain.material_at(point)` answers "water" where the flood is, and "concrete" or "sand" elsewhere.** Build's element and material table can then look up what a hit touched without a private rule. Serves TADR-009 (one table) and 7.
22. **Each stage is a fall line (wave 3).** The spawn is high, and the beacon sits at the foot of the line where the drop can see its mast. Stage 1 lands on a drop deck, runs down the west chute and meets the Warden at the foot of the riser. Stage 2 runs straight down the Sluice's middle to a bowl on the dam above the basin. Stage 3's fall line is the weir's halfpipe, now at x = 30. `world_route_test` finds a clear sightline from the drop to the mast top on all three. Demand: the Warden. Serves 1 and the spec's "see the lock, roll the line".
23. **The beacon sits in a banked bowl.** A 28 m flat floor holds the Warden. A bank climbs at about 24 degrees out to 47 m, then blends back into the ground by 62 m. Each beacon sits at the foot of a slope, so the approach is always a descent into the bowl. The route test measured every sample on the 30 to 45 m ring banked at 20 degrees or more, on all three maps. Demand: the Warden. Serves 1 (a missed ram rolls back) and strains 1 (see 24).
24. **A slow ball is pushed up to the launch floor along the rail, not snapped to it.** Gates that open while the ball is in the bowl stand on its bank, and in the spine run test the ball climbs to them at about 13 m/s from 20. The rail now eases the horizontal speed from the ball's own up to cruise over 0.5 s, so the gate reads as no loss, and the entry and landing keep the gift. Demand: the Warden's exit. Serves 1 and 8.
25. **SHOULD content sits on the line, and the side content off it.** Every scrap line, and so every fabricator build stands on one, lies within 25 m of the spawn-to-beacon line; the worst is 20 m. The vault and every event ring stand 40 m or more off the line on a 3 m plinth. From each, the straight way back to the line never climbs more than 1.5 m. Demand: vault and event. Serves 3 (opt-in layers that cost no momentum).
26. **The sky holds at most three pillars:** the beacon's, one event's (the first open offer's) and the vault's while it stands. Each fades out as the ball closes within 20 m, so a pillar points the way and never hides the fight. Demand: event and vault. Serves 4 (visual clarity is the balance rule).
27. **The vault arrives on the first tick of play,** because combat clears enemies in its own `stage_entered` handler after the world's. It never spawns in calm, and a null return leaves the spot empty. Demand: vault. Serves 7.
28. **`material_at(point)` is a record:** `{"material", "surface_y"}`. It answers the flood's water at its live height wherever the water stands over the ground, the map's concrete or sand elsewhere, and `{}` off the map. Demand: stage 2's water lesson (TADR-009). Serves 7.
29. **The spawn director is honest.** HP scales with the stage threat only, and the clock's tier stops at +2. Every rise of the effective tier, the beacon spike and the relay spike included, is queued and announced with `threat_rose`. 60% of spawns land within 35 degrees ahead at 60 to 90 m, and the rest on the flanks at 45 to 70 m. None lands within 25 m of the ball, and each shows a ground ring in the enemy colour for 1 s first. The fodder cap rises by 2 per heft class, to a hard 24. `world_director_test` measured 59% ahead, 0 of 200 spawns inside 25 m, every body at least 1.011 s after its ring, and 10 rises announced out of 10. Demand: the Warden and growth. Serves 1 (nothing unforeseen) and 4 (a heavier ball rolls through a bigger crowd).
30. **An event win also grants a bonus core** through `run.add_core("event", at)` at the event's ring. Demand: event. Serves 3 (the milestone overcharges the beacon).
31. **The world's codex is five tricks, stated as data and detected by a pure detector.** Live Wire: electrify the flood under enemies. Surge Slingshot: take a surge gate at blazing speed. Sluice Hammer: slam after a kicker. Squall Sail: jump with the squall at your back. Ice Bridge: freeze the flood and roll across it. Each fires once per stage attempt, and near misses do not fire (`world_codex_test`). Demand: event, the flood and growth, since the codex burst feeds heft. Serves the treasure hunt and 8.
32. **The world wires straight into the landed spine.** It declares its five codex entries at setup and reports each trick through `codex.mark_discovered(id, at)`; it never emits `synergy_discovered` itself. It calls `run.add_core("event", at)`, reads `run.heft_class()` for the crowd cap, emits `threat_rose`, and hears `element_resolved` for Live Wire and Ice Bridge. The spine spawns each Warden at `terrain.beacon_point(stage)`, which is the centre of the bowl's flat floor on all three maps. `world_warden_flow_test` runs the whole Warden-gated run on the spine's spawner double. Demand: the Warden, event and growth. Serves 7.

## Integration (I1 to I4)

I1 is the module side of spine call 34, the arena stand-down (calls 1 to 5); I2 is the screen layout (calls 6 to 9); I3 is the adversarial review's spine, build and world findings (calls 10 to 16); I4 is the event-progress seam and the music CPU check (calls 17 and 18). Each call names the stage demand it serves and the principle it strains.

1. **The stage stands down at once when flow publishes `stage_content_cleared`.** Flow publishes it on arena entry and on every arena retry. Combat frees every enemy, shell and telegraph. Each one leaves its group and stops acting in the same call, so a count taken right after the clear reads zero. World closes any live event as `passed`. It then stops the director, the flood and the launch rail, clears the event beams and arrival rings, and parks every stage map. A parked map is hidden and its colliders are out of the physics world until the next `stage_entered` swaps it back in. Build clears the scrap field, the fabricators, any open gates, and the surfaces its elements made on the stage. From then on the stage work checks `ctx.flow.in_stage()`: the world's whole tick and its five codex tricks, plus build's pickups, fabricators, gates, kill drops and vault payout. The weapons, specials, synergies and elements keep checking `in_play()` and work in the arena. `int_arena_standdown_test` runs a real, non-calm run to stage 3 and holds it until 2 director enemies are alive, the flood stands 7 m deep and scrap and fabricators are laid out. Across 5 s in the arena, on entry and again after a death and R, it measured the following. The clear left 0 enemy, shell and telegraph nodes, and nothing spawned. The largest push on the ball was 0.000 m/s^2, with the ball held in the old flood basin and the flood clock forced high. There were 0 scrap pickups, 0 fabricators, 0 stage colliders in the physics space and 0 stage maps drawn. The weapon fired 28 times. The same run's ball in stage 3's flooded basin was pushed at 4.8 m/s^2, so the probe does see world pushes. Before this change the test failed 13 checks. The clear left 4 enemies on entry and 7 on the retry, and the flood pushed the ball at up to 569 m/s^2 after the retry. Demand: the boss phase. Serves 1.
2. **The launch rail stops at arena entry.** Before this change the world kept driving the ball's velocity along the stage-3 launch rail after flow had handed the ball to the boss module, so the rail overrode the velocity the arena entry set. The test saw the rail still flying 5 s into the arena. Demand: the boss phase. Serves 1 (the arena entry keeps the ball's own speed).
3. **Arena kills drop no scrap pickups.** The scrap field is stage content under the contract, so a kill in the arena drops nothing, Brindle's lobber drones included. If the fight should pay scrap, the boss module grants it through `run.add_scrap`. CONTRACTS now says so. Demand: the boss phase. Strains growth in the fight.
4. **Parking a map costs a trimesh rebuild when it comes back.** Taking the colliders out of the physics world means the next `stage_entered` puts them back, and world measured that at about 34 ms a map. That cost lands on a T replay or a new run from the death screen, never on a launch between stages. It has not been re-measured on this branch. The spine's boss double now lays its own world-layer floor at the stage ground's height under its arena spot and frees it on the next `stage_entered`, so the spine tests land on the double's arena and not on the fallback rescue. The real boss builds its own bowl 30 m above that ground. Demand: the boss phase. Strains 2 slightly (a replay hitch of a few frames).
5. **World alone grants an event's bonus core.** This settles build call 30, where build and world were both briefed to grant it. World grants it through `run.add_core("event", at)` at the event's ring, in the same call that records the milestone. Build grants only the milestone part and keeps the vault core. The 0.1 s wait and skip in build are gone. `build_codex_test` wins two events through world's publication path and measured exactly one event core per win. The count held 30 ticks after each win, well past the old 0.1 s window. Demand: event. Serves 7 (one owner for one fact).
6. **I2: one screen-region rule.** The first windowed visual QA pass showed three problems. Gate labels rose off the top of the screen and sat under the toasts. A fabricator's price sat mid-screen over the toasts. "Reward gates ahead" stacked three times. The screen is now four bands, and `src/ui/screen_regions.gd` names them for everything that draws text in play: the objective and status on top, toasts under them, world-attached prompts in the lower middle and the HUD at the bottom. `int_screen_layout_test` measured no overlap between the bands at 1600x900 and 1280x720, with every HUD piece inside its band at both sizes. Demand: the Warden, since gates follow its break. Serves 4 (readable threats: nothing covers the objective) and form mirrors function (where a thing sits says what kind of thing it is).
7. **Gate and fabricator words are screen prompts, not 3D text.** A `Label3D` above a gate grows as you close in and climbs off the top of the screen. A `WorldPrompt` keeps one type size and follows its anchor's column. The HUD clamps it into the prompts band, and it takes the free spot nearest its anchor when another prompt is there. In the test the two real gate prompts and a prompt anchored 40 m above the view all landed inside the band, apart from one another. A fabricator's prompt shows within 45 m, and a gate's while the gate stands. Demand: the Warden and growth. Serves 1 (a choice made at speed is readable at speed). 16 Nov 2026 (PF-22): a gate's name stays a screen prompt and is now attached to its own gate: the gate places it each frame at its crossbar point, so the band no longer clamps it away from the gate. A name whose crossbar point is in the top band drops straight down its own column to the band's lower edge, so it stays under its gate and clear of the HUD cards. A world-space plate was tried first and dropped for the reason above.
8. **A repeated toast is not repeated.** The gates republish their offers whenever they move, which is why "Reward gates ahead" stacked. A toast whose words are already up now stays up for another beat instead, and the band holds 2. Serves 4.
9. **The legend shows glyphs, and stages carry the map titles.** The field notes legend now reads each shape's glyph from the same table the codex pages use (★, ›, ×, ◆, ≈). Stages 2 and 3 are "The Sluice" and "The Weir", the world's own map titles, where they had been "Spillway: ...". Serves form mirrors function.
10. **I3: death drops the push that killed you.** A lethal hit resolves its damage before its shove, so the shove arrived after the death had frozen the ball and waited for the retry. A frozen ball now takes no push, and freezing or a reset drops any push already handed over. `spine_shove_retry_test` died to an 11 m/s shove, pressed R, and two ticks into the retry the ball moved at its entry velocity with a 0.00 m/s horizontal gap. Before the fix the gap was 21.63 m/s. Serves 2 (a retry is the stage start, exactly) and 1.
11. **The arena keeps its own attempt.** Flow now clears with `arena` on entry and `arena_retry` on a retry. The codex sets stage 3's attempt aside at entry and rewinds only the fight's discoveries on an arena retry, while T still rewinds both. The run log gives the arena its own record: deaths with the arena clock, the run clock and Brindle's phase, never filed under stage 3. `spine_arena_retry_test` measured an arena trick paying 60 scrap, then 60 again after R, while a stage-3 trick made before the arena stayed paid. After T the stage-3 trick fired again. The music's restart on an arena retry is presentation's to take from `arena_retry`. Demand: the boss phase. Serves 2 and 3.
12. **The arena objective reads the fight as it is.** Flow's objective line and death gap line read the boss's `live_plating()` and `objective_text()` through `boss_plating_now()`, and the gap line keeps the plating live at the moment of death. The boss module publishes them; until it does, flow falls back to the entry plating. With a boss reading 150, the objective read "hull plating 150", and the chase objective came through word for word. A death against 150 printed "plating 150" even after the boss read 0 (until 10 November 2026, when the death gap line gave way to the cause and next-shift lines; see the correction wave's note 26). Demand: the boss phase. Serves 4 (tint, objective and damage agree).
13. **One held R restarts once.** After any restart from a hold in play, the hold waits for R to come up before it counts again. The summary ignores any input held when it opens, until that input is released (an input latch, no time lock), so a Space still pressed from the victory beat cannot dismiss the run's only summary, and a fresh press after the release works at once. Serves 2 (quick restart, and no restart you did not ask for).
14. **Synergies strike as `synergy`, not `slam`.** Wrecking Ball, Shrapnel Bloom, Glacier Hull and Pinwheel borrowed the slam verb, so they broke vent caps and could catch the scooter with no landing. They now strike with their own kind, and the damage scales the way the slam row did (1.2 per unit of force, times heft). `build_behaviour_test` counts them under `synergy` and fails on any synergy `slam`; before the fix it counted -1 on all four. Combat's own README vocabulary and the boss's landing checks are those writers' seams. Demand: the boss phase and the stage-2 lesson. Serves 4 (a weak point answers only the verb it teaches).
15. **Build hands every synergy activation to the codex.** This supersedes build call 21's local once-per-run guard. After a retry takes a synergy back, reactivating it discovers it again, pays its burst again and stays in the run log, as the codex contract says. `build_codex_test` measured a second fact and a second 20-scrap burst after a retry; before the fix it measured one fact and no burst. On the arena clear, build also recomputes from the restored parts and mass. `build_arena_retry_shell_test` robbed the shell from class 2 to class 1 in the fight. After R it showed class 2 and 27 chunks, matching the restored run. Before the fix it stayed at class 1 with 6 chunks. Serves 3 and 2.
16. **Ctrl brakes on the ground and slams only from a press in the air.** A brake held from the ground stays a brake over a bump until Ctrl is released; a press that begins airborne slams. A retry or reset counts as held from the ground. `spine_brake_slam_test` rolled over a 16-degree kicker at 22 m/s with Ctrl held: the ball was airborne 31 ticks and did not slam. A fresh press in the air slammed. Before the fix the held brake slammed and planted the ball after 4 airborne ticks. Demand: the boss phase (the Slag Bowl bank, balance-loop L11). Serves 1 (no stop the player did not ask for).
17. **I4: an event tells the music how far along it is.** The bus carries `event_progress(event_id, fraction)`. The event director reads each live participatory model's `progress()`: Hold the Relay's share held, Surge Run's gates taken, Scrap Stampede's hits on the hauler. It publishes a value on the first live tick, again whenever the value has moved at least 0.01, and always on reaching 1, before `event_ended`. Presentation's escalation switches from its elapsed-time estimate to the published value on the first one. `world_event_progress_test` held stage 3's relay to a win: 97 values rose from 0.001 to 1.000, the win came after the last, and presentation was on its estimate before the first value and on the published value after. Before the seam the test saw no values. Boss `phase_progress()` and `chase_lead()` are left to the boss writer. Demand: event. Serves the music following the player's own play, and 3.
18. **The music CPU check is a ratio against a reference workload timed beside it.**
    - **Why the absolute check failed.** A full smoke on a machine busy with other agents' runs failed the 80 ms cap at 84 ms per second of music, while alone it measured 68 to 72 ms. Taking the least of three runs was not enough. Under sustained contention all three rose together, to 82 and then 94 ms, because contention slows this process's own CPU time and not only the wall clock.
    - **How it works now.** Every music measurement sits between two timings of a fixed reference workload: 8 fixed buffers mixed with a gain ramp into a 735-sample block, 1200 times. That is the shape of the engine's own mixing loop, run in the same process. The check is music ms per second of music over the mean reference ms, the least of three paired runs.
    - **Why a ratio holds.** Contention slows both sides of the ratio alike. A heavier arrangement moves only the music side.
    - **Calibration.** Three solo runs gave Brindle's densest arrangement 0.188, 0.180 and 0.180, with the reference at 395 to 410 ms. The cap is 0.21, which puts today's arrangement at 86 to 90% of it. That is the same headroom as 70 ms against the old 80.
    - **Under load.** With a load average of 17 to 19 from other agents, the absolute cost rose to 82 and 78 ms; the first is over the old cap. The ratio read 0.178 and 0.177, level with the solo runs, and the capped smoke passed.
    - **Regression proof.** In a scratch copy, not committed, every voice the engine started got a twin. The ratio rose to 0.333 (138 ms against 415 ms of reference) and the check failed.
    - **Output.** The absolute ms per second of music still prints as information, next to the old 80 ms line.
    - Demand: the boss phase (Brindle's densest arrangement). Serves honest measurement.

## Boss (writer E)

Admiral Brindle and the Requisition in the Slag Bowl (`src/boss/`). Every number is in `boss_rules.gd` and is a starting value for play to tune. Each call names the stage demand it serves and the principle it strains.

1. **Fixed HP with set-piece floors.** Brindle has 22000 HP whatever the build (`boss_rules.gd` MAX_HP, set by balance pass 8; L16). Phases end at 66%, 33% and 10%, and each phase also has to play its set piece (28, 24 and 22 s, then a 2.5 s eject). Damage past a floor is held at the floor until the set piece ends. With infinite damage every tick, the fight took 76.5 s. A grown ball still lands full hits and visibly routs him, but it cannot skip his set pieces. Demand: every boss phase. Serves 4 (insane-but-readable scaling, no rubber band). Strains the badass feel for a very grown ball, which has to wait out a set piece. Ask Andrew whether the held floor reads as a wall.
2. **The bowl floats 30 m above the highest ground under stage 3's beacon.** It is 124 m across: a flat floor, a banked ring from 44 to 60 m out that rises 10 m, and a 6 m rim wall. Everything is on the world layer, so the grapple, the pylon and the camera read it like terrain. Demand: the boss arena. Serves 1: the banked ring is out of every crane's reach and never floods, so the high-speed lane stays safe in phases 1 and 2.
3. **The entry keeps its speed.** The ball arrives on the floor 41 m out, heading along the ring, with its horizontal speed kept. A slower ball is raised to cruise, which is a gift. The momentum test entered at 18.00 m/s and measured 16.61 m/s a second later (7.7% loss). Demand: arena entry. Serves 1.
4. **A crane steals, then pays it back.** Each sector is drawn for 1.4 s, aimed where the ball was when it committed, then swept in 1.2 s. A catch takes 20% of mass (10 to 60) through `lose_scrap(..., "crane")`. It lifts the ball 7 m/s and pulls it 1.2 m/s toward the magnet, and never cancels its velocity. The magnet drips what it holds as a 12-pickup trail over 2.4 s along its swing. Rolling the trail at 18 m/s took back 30 of 30 in 2.3 s. Shooting the magnet while it still holds scrap drops the rest in one pile (codex: Requisition Denied). Demand: phase 1. Serves 1 and 4: a theft becomes a chase.
5. **Slag rings sit flush with the floor.** Two rings, 16 to 21 m and 30 to 36 m out. A klaxon and the drawn ring come 2.5 s before the slag flows. A hit costs 12 HP, bounces the ball up 14 m/s and out 10 m/s toward the nearer edge, then gives 0.8 s grace. The test ball left the channel within 0.07 s of the hit. Demand: phase 2. Serves 1: slag hurts and throws you out, it never holds you.
6. **Water vents quench flush crust, not raised platforms.** Rolling over one of the four rim vents turns a 50° sector of both rings to safe rock for 8 s (codex: Cold Crossing). The brief asked for rock platforms. A raised slab has an edge that would stop a rolling ball, so the crust is level with the floor. Demand: phase 2. Serves 1 and the lava row of the element table (TADR-009, a candidate). Ask Andrew whether flush crust reads as a platform.
7. **Vent caps take a landing.** In phase 2 the three deck caps open to a stomp or a slam with the ball on top of them, or a ram that came down on top of them, for 1.5 times the punch. The ball's position decides, so a strike that only borrows the "slam" kind (build's synergies) chips from the floor. Reach the deck from a deck ramp, a kicker or a crane-mast grapple. Demand: phase 2. Serves 3: a beeline ball wins through the weak points.
8. **The clash is the bow's plating.** While charging, the hull reads plating 150, so combat's amber or slate tint shows whether your punch out-clashes it. A ram at 150 or more stops the charge and stuns him for 2 s. A stun drops the plating to 0, and a full hit then publishes `cockpit`. Demand: phase 3. Serves form mirrors function: the threshold uses the same rule and the same tint as every plated thing.
9. **A charge at the rim is a bait, never a pin.** The lane is drawn 1.4 s ahead toward the ball. If it reaches the rim, the hull stops with its centre 49 m out, nose buried in the bank, every corner more than a ball's width short of the wall. It then sits stunned for 3 s with its cockpit open (codex: Rim Shot). A hit shoves the ball 12 m/s sideways and 9 m/s up, never along the lane. In the test, a ball resting at the rim was shoved to 15.1 m/s and was 11.6 m off the lane centre 1.5 s later. Demand: phase 3. Serves 1 and 5 (the engineer baits from a pylon, the runner strafes).
10. **The eject is a chase on the ring.** At the last floor Brindle ejects and `boss_phase_changed(4, "chase")` goes out. His scooter lands on the banked ring 2.5 s later and runs it at 11 m/s, under cruise, away from where the ball was. A ram, a stomp or a slam catches him. A shot slows him 25% for 1.5 s, which is how a gunner sets up the catch. A landing on him is Boarding Party. The win adds the part `admirals_crest` and hangs a brass badge on the ball's roll node. Demand: the finale. Serves 1 (the finale is a momentum chase) and scrap becomes your build.
11. **Brindle's name plate is a screen prompt.** A world prompt (`src/ui/world_prompt.gd`) anchored over the ship, and over the scooter in the chase, reads "BRINDLE · plating 120" (bow plating 150 while charging, 0 while stunned, "on the run" in the chase). The HUD keeps it in the prompts band, so it never covers the objective line and no mast can hide it. It is amber when your punch beats the live plating and slate when it does not, and the objective line carries the numbers. It replaced a large depth-tested Label3D that windowed QA caught drawing over the objective line and cut off by a mast. The plate's number is plating after combat's own shred, so a hull worn down by fire reads the same lower number the tint already answers to. Demand: every boss phase. Serves 2 (readable at a glance) under the UI-regions rule.
12. **Lobber drones are combat's lobbers.** They drop off the deck every 7 s in phase 2, at most three at a time, and none while `ctx.calm` is set. Demand: phase 2. Serves 3.
13. **Growth continues in the fight.** The scrap field idles in the arena, so the boss pays scrap itself, always as pickups you roll up. Each phase break, including the eject, spills a ring of 80 scrap in 16 pickups, 10 m around the ship. A lobber drone killed in the arena drops its scrap where it died. Demand: every boss phase. Serves 4 (heft and punch keep climbing through the fight) and scrap becomes your build. Strains 1 a little: a ring spilled into a channel before the slag flows is a telegraphed temptation, not a trap.

### Fix wave E2 (review findings, 2026-09-26)

14. **The ball stuck in the arena was the launch rail, not the bowl.** On the bot trial build (loop branch plus boss 9c9f250, before the stand-down), world ticked in the boss state and its launch rail kept setting a ballistic velocity every tick. Friction under that load pinned the ball to the floor while v.y fell to -499. `boss_ring_drive_test` enters the arena through a gate and stage 3's launch and drives 30 s around the seam at 40 to 59 m. It fails on the trial build (15 still half-seconds, |v.y| 499.9) and passes here, where the stand-down stops the rail. Demand: the boss arena. Serves 1.
15. **No piece of Brindle is crushable.** His HP is held at phase floors, so a crush exception on him would end with the ball inside a body that never died. Every piece answers `crushable()` false, and an open weak point on a piece that cannot die reads plating 0.01, so every hit lands in full but combat's crush rule never fires. Demand: phase 3. Serves 1.
16. **Live state for the HUD.** `objective_text()` names what to do now: magnets, caps, bait or clash, the stun window, the scooter. Flow shows it as the whole objective line, so fight states end with "Punch N / hull plating M" (a ram against bow plating 150 while charging). `live_plating()` is the plating of what to hit now: 120, 150 on a charging bow, 0 stunned or in the chase. `flow.boss_plating` follows it every tick. While charging, the readout, `tint_verb()` ("ram") and the hull's tint all count ram punch only, so a heavy weapon never previews a clash a ram cannot make. Demand: every boss phase. Serves 2 (the objective line cannot disagree with the tint or the damage).
17. **Rescues keep the ball's way round.** A ball flying out over the rim comes back on the ring moving the way it was going, clockwise or not. Demand: the chase. Serves 1.
18. **Drones arrive on a marked pad.** A pad ring is drawn 1 s before each drone lands, 9 m off the parked hull's side away from the ball. The drone waits while the ball is within 8 m. Drones read the bowl's ground (`ground_height_at()`, and a ground query on the drone until combat reads the seam), so their shells land on the arena floor. Drones killed in the chase still pay scrap. Demand: phase 2. Serves 1 (nothing appears on the ball unannounced).
19. **Nothing the boss does ends in a stop the player did not choose.** A charge hit ghosts the ball through the hull until the shove has cleared it. A mid-lane ball moved 0 m/s along the lane and was 9.2 m off it 0.8 s later. A slag bounce takes the first of four directions whose arc clears the ramps, masts and hull. At the ramp bearing it no longer meets the ramp's end face. The crust draws under every telegraph (priority 39), and live slag leaves the quenched span undrawn. Demand: phases 2 and 3. Serves 1.
20. **Only a stomp or a slam lands, and only contact catches.** Build's synergy strikes arrive with kind "synergy". They chip a cap against the hull's plating and slow the scooter, but never open a cap or catch Brindle. An arena retry (`stage_content_cleared("arena_retry")`) rebuilds the fight from nothing: full HP, phase 1, no hazards, pickups or drone pads from the attempt that died. Demand: phase 2 and the chase. Serves 3 (the lesson is the landing) and 2 (quick restart is a clean restart).

## Feedback loop (writer L)

1. **The bots play the real game through a player's hands.** `tools/bot_driver.gd` fills an `Intent.Scripted` source and turns the camera through `camera.look()`, the mouse's own door, and starts the run with the flow's title and class calls. It never calls a run mutator, strikes an enemy or moves the ball, so `runs.ndjson` and `codex-log.ndjson` record play rather than a test's shortcuts. Every fight is one loop: look at the target (its weak point when one shows), hold fire, and make ram laps; on a caps Warden it jumps on the approach and slams from above. Demand: the Warden, event and vault, which the bots measure. Serves 7 (fast without debt) and the "test-passing equals fun" counter, since the bots feed a report, never a verdict.
2. **Three policies bracket a player.** The skipper beelines at the Warden and takes the first gate. The completionist rolls every scrap line end to end (so it rolls through the fabricator hoops on them), joins each armed event once, spends up to 45 s on the vault, then breaks the Warden, and prefers a gate that completes a synergy. The random driver wanders on a seeded heading biased toward the beacon and commits to the Warden within 45 m or after 45 s. Steering aims the velocity, not the ball, because a ball steered at a point orbits it at speed. It never brake-carves (see 11). Demand: the Warden, event and vault. Serves 3 (opt-in layers pay visibly, which only a policy that skips them can show).
3. **Each seed reseeds the run's stream,** as `ctx.rng.seed = 20260926 + 7919 * seed`, and the global RNG. With the spine's fixed seed, every run of a policy drew the same stage plans. A seed replays the same run on the same machine and build, and the class alternates with the seed's parity. Demand: event (the event plan and the miniboss spots vary by seed). Serves 7.
4. **A run the spine cannot log still leaves a trace.** The run log writes only at a death or a run end, so a stage the bot cannot break within 240 s ends the run as `stalled`, and a flow state named `boss` (wave 4) ends it as `boss`. Both have only their `bots.ndjson` line: policy, seed, class, outcome, stage reached, ticks, game and wall seconds, and per-stage notes (Warden engaged and broken times, events started, cores, parts by source, detours). Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 7.
5. **The report reads logs and reports; it never tunes.** `tools/balance_report.gd` prints per-stage bands per policy and writes `artifacts/local/balance-<label>.md`. Its heft band reads the power-curve test's `BUILDS` table from the script itself, and scales the skipper and absurd builds to s/3 of their parts and mass at a stage-s exit. That interpolation is an assumption, stated in the report. The break time is flagged on the run log's measure, time from stage entry, and each flag also names the bot's fight time from `warden_engaged`, so a detour-heavy run can be told from a slow fight. Demand: the Warden (heft is punch against plating). Serves the DEMO-SPEC rule that tuning stays a human-approved edit.
6. **The coherence check holds old notes to a baseline instead of failing them on style.** Lines that predate the `Demand:` marker, and one whose marker names something outside Warden, event, vault and boss, are printed as NOTE lines; each module section's count may not grow. A new line without a demand fails the build, and "Demand: none" reads as declared plumbing. Demand: the Warden, event, vault and boss phase, the four demands it checks every mechanic against. Serves 7.
7. **The first loop at base 17a9437 reads growth outrunning the Warden.** Twelve runs, four per policy, all cleared stage 3 with no deaths (`artifacts/local/balance-base.md`, local only). The skipper's median fight from engagement to break fell from 22.5 s on stage 1 (plating 30) to 10.7 s on stage 3 (plating 95), because it left stage 3 at heft 4.9 to 5.6 against the power-curve table's skipper at 2.0. Every completionist left stage 3 above the absurd build's 9.07 (10.5 to 12.1). Pinwheel was found in 8 of 12 runs. Tesla Pylon, Wrecking Ball and Glacier Hull were found only by the skipper or not at all: their shown parts are common, and a gate overcharged by bonus cores stops offering commons. These are readings for the coordinator and Andrew; nothing was tuned. Demand: the Warden, event and vault. Serves 4 (insane but readable scaling needs a floor as well as a ceiling) and 3 (opt-in layers must not lock a card away).
8. **The bots read telegraphs with a human's delay.** A committed ring or lane (combat's `telegraph.gd`, the boss's hazards) counts only after it has stood for 0.25 s. From then on, a ball inside it, or inside it 0.35 s ahead on its current velocity, steers to the nearest spot outside every threat that keeps its line. Rings wider than 20 m (the Warden's magnet reach) are fields, not dodgeable hits. HP lost per stage now reflects pressure a reading player meets, not bot blindness. Demand: the Warden, event and boss phase. Serves 1 (a hit taken is the player's own performance) and the loop's honesty.
9. **The bots use their class and the lock's weak points.** The pylon engineer plants on a steep face within 22 m of a fight (the plant fires the opening burst), turrets for 3.5 s, then slingshots off toward the target; both classes fire their special into a crowd or a close fight. When the best punch seen since the Warden woke (fading 4 a second) is below its plating, a caps Warden gets slams beside the cap nearest the ball, taking its own pulse for height, and a magnet Warden gets the gun on its magnet while it tugs. At or above plating the bot rams, as a player would. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 5 (class identities) and 6 (weak points teach the lesson).
10. **The bots fight Brindle through the boss seams, and end at `boss` until the module lands.** Phase 1: guns on the live crane magnets, chasing any spilled scrap within 30 m, ramming the hull when punch reaches it. Phase 2: up the ramp the ship is passing and a slam onto a vent cap, or ram laps when punch reaches 120. Phase 3: bait charges from the rim (r 47) so they end in the wall, ram the stunned ship, clash head-on at punch 150. The chase: run the scooter down. The runner records phase start times, weak-point hits by point, scrap stolen, wall stuns and HP lost. A trial merge of `proto/core-loop-boss` 9c9f250 onto this branch, in a scratch clone, reached the chase phase in 74 to 94 s on gunfire alone, then stalled: from the first seconds the ball held near r 44 to 50 while its body velocity fell freely (to -217 m/s) and its position did not change, which reads as an integration fault for step 2, not a bot limit. Demand: the boss phase. Serves 8 (the finale reads as a fight a player can plan).
11. **Brake is also the slam key, so the bots never brake to carve.** A carve held over a bump turned into a slam that planted the ball, repeatedly on the Slag Bowl's bank. A player holding Ctrl through a turn meets the same trap; this is a feel question for the playtest, not a bot fix. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase, where carving at speed matters. Serves 1 (nothing the player did not intend may stop the ball).
12. **The tuning pass changed data only, one family at a time, with the bots rerun after each.** The heft per mass went from 1/150 to 1/450 (`run_state.gd` `HEFT_PER_MASS`). Warden plating went from 30 / 60 / 95 to 45 / 80 / 140, and Warden HP from 400 / 700 / 1000 to 800 / 2200 / 7000 (`stage_defs.gd`). Stage 2's threat went from 1.5 to 1.25. Brindle's `MAX_HP` went from 2400 to 14400 (`boss_rules.gd`). Three hint cards now show uncommon parts: Tesla Pylon shows Focus Lens, Wrecking Ball Double Feed, and Glacier Hull Slag Rounds (`synergies.gd`). After seven passes, 48 runs put the skipper's Warden fights at a median 34.5 / 39.6 / 65.5 s and its stage-3 exit heft at 3.0 to 3.9 (median 3.2). Its HP loss sits at a median 21 / 43 / 47% per stage. On stage 3 it chips with the gun and breaks the Warden in magnet windows (2 to 18 stuns a run). Every synergy is found in 13 to 54% of runs except Wrecking Ball (69%); the pass started at 8 to 63%. The before and after tables are in `artifacts/local/balance-before.md` and `balance-t7-heft.md`. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 4 (growth stays readable against a lock that grows with it) and 3 (every hint card can be reached at any core count).
13. **The completionist's 10 to 25 s rout of Warden III is out of reach by data alone.** Guns deal 70 to 90% of Warden damage, and a shot's own damage is always below plating, so it chips at 20% for skipper and completionist alike. Warden HP then scales both fights together. The completionist's fight ran 1.6 to 2.9 times shorter than the skipper's across passes (median 39.8 s against 65.5 s at pass 7), and the two bands need at least 2 and ideally 3 to 5. Reaching them needs a decision beyond this lease: a wider band, or a build whose weapon mods or ram carry more of the growth. Demand: the Warden. Serves 4 (a grown ball should visibly rout the lock).
14. **A pattern part splits the skipper in two, on Warden III and on Brindle alike.** A skipper holding Spiral Wave, Cross Split or Chain Arc at stage 3 (10 of 16 runs at pass 7) broke Warden III in 19 to 81 s in 9 of those runs (the tenth took 140 s) and beat Brindle in 80 to 138 s, most at the set-piece floor. The six runs without one took 117 to 204 s and 270 to 414 s, losing most of their Brindle HP to slag. The gun deals the damage and a pattern multiplies its hits, so the pattern's presence sets the fight length more than heft does. The skipper bands (Warden III 50 to 90 s, Brindle 120 to 240 s) sit between the two groups, so Warden or Brindle HP can move both groups but cannot merge them. Merging them needs pattern damage (combat or build), or a skipper who is always offered a pattern part. `MAX_HP` 14400 moved the median, not the split (pass 8 later set it to 22000; see L16 to L18). Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 4 (growth should be a curve, not a switch) and 5 (a class without its pattern should still have a plan).
15. **Wrecking Ball stays over 60% because its hidden tag is common.** Eight of 28 parts carry kinetic, so holding Double Feed nearly always completes it. The other uncommon speed parts are Thermal Shock's shown Frost Vane and the runner-only Overclock. The common speed parts are percentage-only and so cannot be uncommon. The fix is a tag, which is B's call, not a shown part. Demand: the Warden and vault, where the hint card is read. Serves 3 (a find should feel like a find).
16. **Pass 8 retuned shred against the bands.** The bots now read plating through `combat.plating_of`, so they see shred and ram a stripped Warden. Five 48-run loops moved `combat.gd`'s shred rates from A4's 0.0184 per punch, 0.15 hold decay and 0.5 s grace to 0.017, 0.05 and 1.5 s. Warden III HP went from 7000 to 7500 and Brindle's `MAX_HP` from 14400 to 22000. The longer grace keeps a strip through a ram lap. The lower hold decay lets more builds reach a full strip, and `combat_shred_test` still holds: the engaged build strips Warden III in 4.9 s, the base kit peaks at 18. The completionist now breaks Warden III in a median 21.5 s, 9 of 15 runs in its 10 to 25 s band, against 46.6 s and 3 of 14 at pass 7. Wrecking Ball fell to 43% after B4 took kinetic off two parts. Thermal Shock sat at 8 to 14% in every pass-8 loop, around the 10% floor. The tables are in `artifacts/local/balance-p8-before.md` and `balance-p8e-final.md`. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 4 (a grown ball visibly routs the lock).
17. **Shred splits the skipper into groups; it does not merge it.** A fight is short once the build strips the Warden past its own bolt damage, and long if it never does. At pass 8's final rates, 7 of 15 skippers stripped Warden III and broke it in 22 to 54 s. Three got it about 60% stripped and took 103 to 110 s. Five never passed 50% and took 159 to 203 s. None landed in the 50 to 90 s band, which sits between the groups. The peak strips of skipper and completionist builds overlap, so no shred rate separates the two policies; each loop moved skippers from one group to another. At 0.05 hold decay, 13 of 16 skippers stripped (median 37 s). At 0.08, only 5 of 15 did (median 112 s). The skipper's Warden III band needs Andrew's call: a band that accepts the fast group, or shred that scales with the gap between punch and plating rather than punch alone (A's code). Brindle shows the same split: six of the seven stripping skippers won at the set-piece floor (81 to 95 s), and six of the other seven that finished took 115 to 394 s. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 4 (growth should be a curve, not a switch).
18. **The report read the wrong run log for four seeds in every 16-seed loop.** `run_id` is a second stamp plus a draw from the seeded stream, so seeds 1, 5, 9 and 13 started together by the three policy processes shared ids, and the report's merged join kept the skipper's line. The completionist and random rows for those seeds showed the skipper's Warden times, exit heft and codex. The report now joins inside each process folder. Pass 7's completionist Warden III reads 46.6 s median, not 39.8. Separately, the random bot's seed-5 process hung in stage 1 in three straight loops, at the same point (just after its second codex find, stage tick 14161), yet ran clean alone. A shared `user://` written by twelve processes at once is one guess, not a finding. Demand: none (the loop's own plumbing). Serves 7 (fast without debt).

## v2 W1 (UI readability)

Andrew's entry-7 verdict was "so much words and I can't really tell what to press". Every call below serves principle 11 (readability): say at a glance what to press, what hurts and how to grow, with icons over words and information timed to its moment. Each line also names any other principle it serves. The look follows `VISUAL-LANGUAGE.md` sections 5 and 7 on `proto/core-loop-v2` where that was cheap.

1. **The budget is measured, not estimated.** `src/core/word_budget.gd` counts every shown Label, Button and RichTextLabel, every control's `drawn_words()`, and Label3Ds in view. A word is any token with a letter or a digit, keycap labels included. That is stricter than the visual language's letters-only count. `ui_word_budget_test` stages a real stage-1 fight: the runner, gun held, three chasers close, a part and a threat just landed. On base 2792144 it counted 92 HUD words. The always-on key hints alone were 45 of them. Here it counts 13. Demand: the Warden (the fight the words crowded).
2. **Keys show only when they work.** The always-on hint line is gone; Tab's field notes keep the full list. Each key is drawn where its action is:
   - A keycap sits beside the ball or the grapple target while the action is available: E beside a mountable face, right mouse beside a grapple target, Space while mounted.
   - A glyph retires for good after 3 successful uses on this profile (`ui-profile.json`).
   - The ability bar keeps each ability's key under its slot.

   Demand: the Warden and the boss phase, where the kit is used. Serves 1: nothing stops the player to read.
3. **Status became instruments.**
   - Health is a tank gauge with quarter ticks. Its edge turns iron oxide and pulses at 1 Hz under 25%.
   - Heft is a pressure dial. Its needle swings to the class plus progress, and it flashes on a rise or a theft.
   - Scrap sits right beside the dial, because scrap is heft (form mirrors function).
   - The top left holds the stage pips, the Fleet's tag for the arena, and the run clock in mono digits with the difficulty as a notched bar under it (since 10 Nov 2026 a ramp of rising steps with the word THREAT, `src/ui/run_status.gd`). Andrew plays against the clock.

   Demand: the Warden (heft against plating). Serves 4: growth reads at a glance.
4. **The objective is an icon and at most five words, and it changes with distance.**
   - Far from the Warden it reads "Break the Warden". Within 40 m it becomes that stage's lesson ("Ram it hard", "Slam its vent caps", "Shoot its magnet while pulled").
   - Punch against plating is two bars: punch over hatched plating, with a white notch at the plating on both. Winning reads as the punch bar passing the notch, and its tip turns mint.
   - A ring round the icon fills with the share of the Warden broken.
   - The wording lives in event_kinds' `OBJECTIVE_SHORT` and `LESSON_SHORT`, so flow's long objective line is unchanged for the tests that read it.

   Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 10: the demand is punch against plating.
5. **One toast at a time, at most four words, beside its row's shape.** The newest replaces the one showing. It enters in 0.12 s, holds 2.4 s for a critical fact or 1.6 s for a notable one, and leaves in 0.2 s.
   - **What toasts.** Only critical (priority 3) rows toast, plus the facts the player acts on: a part gained outside a gate ("+ Boost Coil"), scrap torn loose ("Chase your scrap") and a lost event ("Surge Run lost").
   - **What moved elsewhere.** "Threat rising" is now the difficulty bar. A heft drop flashes the dial. "The Warden wakes" became a callout and the lesson objective. The stage, launch and arena facts belong to the stage card.
   - **Conflict.** The visual language allows two toasts; the W1 packet says one. This build keeps one; it is flagged for the coordinator.

   Demand: event (its loss) and the Warden. Serves 3: an opt-in event that is lost still says so.
6. **The stage card rides the launch.** Taking a gate launches you. The next stage's beacon icon and name fade in over the flight and out after the drop. Brindle's arena shows the Fleet's tag and "Admiral Brindle". A toast that lands meanwhile waits for the card to clear. The card takes no input and pauses nothing. Demand: the boss phase and every stage entry. Serves 1: a launch is momentum carried, with no load screen.
7. **The ability bar speaks by shape before colour.**
   - It follows the visual language's order: weapon (LMB), class movement (RMB), special (F), smash (Ctrl).
   - `ready` has a solid bone frame. `cooling` has a petrol wedge uncovering a dimmed icon clockwise from twelve. `active` has a doubled frame in the class colour and a lifted slot. `unavailable` is faded and struck through.
   - Only a cooldown ending makes a slot swell (1.12 over 180 ms, with a class-colour rim). Leaving the ground or finding a grapple target does not, so the swell always means "it's back".
   - Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 7: timing a cooldown is snappy when you can see it return.
8. **The slot array is a contract, and W3 publishes it.** CONTRACTS "Ability slots" defines the records. The ball's ability state publishes them (`player.abilities.slots()`), and `ability_slots.gd` only renames what it publishes to the Icons ids: `ab_<pilot>_<role>` and `key_lmb`. The first adapter read combat's private cooldown fields; it is gone. A record that fails validation empties the bar rather than drawing a wrong state. Demand: none (the seam W3 builds on). Serves 11: standardized systems, no hidden bugs.
9. **Icons are the visual language's 70 ids.** `Icons.texture(id)` loads the look track's SVG when it lands, else a signed-distance placeholder in the fact's own colour. `Icons.tint` keeps an SVG's own colours. The event_kinds shapes are drawn, never files, so a toast wears its row's silhouette. Colours for UI-only facts (slot states, the key glyph, the clock, callouts) are `UI_ROWS` in event_kinds' v2 W1 block. They sit beside ROWS because ROWS must hold one row per bus signal. Demand: none (the look track's seam). Serves 11.
10. **Edge markers point, and say nothing.** When the Warden, the beacon or an open gate is off screen, a chevron sits at the rim of the play view with the target's objective icon behind it, in that target's event colour. It never enters the top, toast or bottom bands. It grows from 13 to 30 px as the target nears from 180 m to 25 m. Demand: the Warden and the gates. Serves 1: you steer while rolling, not after stopping to look. Since 10 Nov 2026 a thing the goal row names is the one exception to "say nothing": in view it wears brackets and a tag ([LEGIBILITY](design-v3/LEGIBILITY.md), the objective line).
11. **Four first-run callouts, each once per profile.** They are "Scrap = heft" with the scrap and heft icons on the first scrap, "Break it" when the Warden first wakes, "Roll through" when the gates open, and "Hold Tab: notes" after 60 s, with Tab drawn as its keycap in place. Each lasts 2.5 s in the toast band, never over the reticle, and gives way to the hold-R meter. Demand: the Warden and the gates. Serves 9: the gating spine taught at the moment it matters.
12. **World prompts get shorter at range.** At range a prompt shows an icon and a title of at most three words (gate, fabricator or vault). Within 12 m of the ball it adds the detail lines. Owners' multi-line text is parsed until they pass `set_parts()`, and the Label keeps the owner's text, so their code and tests are untouched. Demand: vault and event (their prompts). Serves 1: prices and offers read while rolling past.
13. **Menus take the mouse, and a click is a key press.**
    - Every button presses its key through `game._screen_key`, so a click and a key can never disagree. Keys still act at once (principle 2).
    - Hover brightens a button's edge to bone, focus thickens it, and a press edges it amber.
    - The death screen ignores clicks for its first 0.5 s, so a fire button still mashed as the ball dies cannot pick "New run". The summary's input latch covers clicks too.
    - Class cards show the class emblem, name and the pitch's first sentence. They lay out for N classes (the width fits three), and keys 1 to N pick them.
    - Demand: none (menus). Serves 2: quick to restart, and never by accident.
14. **Transitions fade; they never wait.** Screens fade in over 0.3 s (cubic ease-out, content rising 14 px) and out over 0.25 s (ease-in). Play starts at once under the fading veil. No tween holds input, the tree or the ball. Demand: none. Serves 1.
15. **Type.** The HUD uses Noto Sans Bold for words and Noto Sans Mono Bold for numbers, so the clock and counts do not jitter. Both are vendored in `assets/fonts/` with the OFL text and a provenance line, and load from `res://`; Godot's default font stands in only if a file is missing. Demand: none. Serves 11.
16. **The reticle carries no words.** "In reach 34 m" is gone. The reticle's shape and colour already say it, and the right-mouse glyph beside the target says what to press. Demand: the Warden (grapple targets on it). Serves 1.
17. **The rows themselves are short now (SOUND-STORY section 7, W1 must).** Every `hud` string the HUD shows fits four words ("{event} lost", "Chase your scrap", "+ {part}", "{event} won"). Every `objective` string the HUD shows fits five ("Charge the beacon", "Roll through a gate", "Hold your line"). The three exceptions are the `stage_entered`, `beacon_charge_changed` and `boss_arena_entered` objectives. They stay flow's full readout with punch and plating as numbers, because `combat_seams_test`, `combat_shred_test` and `spine_arena_retry_test` read them. They are never drawn: the HUD shows `OBJECTIVE_SHORT` ("Break the Warden", "Break Brindle's Requisition") over the two bars. `TOAST_SHORT` is gone, since the rows carry the words. Demand: the Warden and events. Serves 11.
18. **The HUD is the first place a class's tune is heard.** The ready swell and the charge pips wear the class row's shape: Sable's ring, Rivet's chevron, Kest's starburst. They also wear its colour. The `ability_ready` UI row's sound is `class_motif`, meaning the class row's own sound, for presentation to play. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 5: class identity.
19. **A hold shows at the reticle.** A charging gauge fills a ring round the reticle, clockwise from twelve and linearly, because it measures time (VISUAL-LANGUAGE section 8). It is drawn in the class colour, since the shot is yours, and it closes when full. The eye stays where the shot will go. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 7: skill shots with forgiveness, snappy to read.
20. **Class cards are pilots.** Each card shows the pilot's emblem (`cls_sable`, `cls_rivet`, `cls_kest`), name, role and species from the class row, and one fantasy line from DESIGN-V2 section 2 cut to fit. The lines are "Plant a beachhead, then launch like a cannonball.", "An unstoppable sprinter. Crowd control is fuel." and "Vanish in smoke, then drop a line of fire." Demand: none (menus). Serves 5.
21. **Instrument plates.** The run status and the objective sit on chamfered charcoal plates with a petrol inner line and bone bolt dots (VISUAL-LANGUAGE section 7), so the top band reads as workshop gauges rather than floating text. Demand: none. Serves 11 (a look with soul, from the look track).


## v2 W2 threat grammar

Playtest entry 7: Andrew could not tell an instant area from a travelling wave from a projectile, and asked for traced outlines that show what each thing will hit. Every enemy threat now carries one of four attack types, chosen at commit and drawn by `src/combat/threat_grammar.gd` for both combat's telegraphs and the boss's hazards.

1. **One grammar, four types, and no default.** INSTANT, WAVE, PROJECTILE and LANE differ by shape, fill, motion and sound; colour stays in the HAZARD family (AMBER for a threat the player owns), and the brighter pieces only raise value and lower saturation, so the hue never changes. Every factory (`Telegraph.ring`, `lane`, `wedge`, `Hazard.commit_*`, `enemy._commit_ring`, `_commit_lane`) takes the type as a required argument, and a node committed without one pushes an error the smoke run fails on. `threat_sites_test` scans every creation site and checks the live game against the audit in note 9. Demand: the Warden, event (the miniboss) and the boss phase. Serves form mirrors function and TH-03 AO-08: the node builds its descriptor from the fields its contains() reads.
2. **Superseded by note 13 (the striped inward fill).** This was: **An instant area has no motion.** A sharp edge, then an even fill whose alpha climbs from 0.06 to 0.6 across the warning (measured 0.19 at a quarter, 0.47 at three quarters), then a solid flash that outlives the telegraph for 0.22 s after it lands. The old ring grew its fill outward from the centre, which is exactly what a wave looks like; motion now belongs only to things that travel. Demand: the Warden (its pulse). Serves Andrew's entry-7 ask ("is it an AOE or a wave").
3. **A wave hurts only on its front, and the front is drawn where it is.** The whole reach shows as a faint dashed outline. A bright front stands at speed x time since it left, 1 m either side (`WAVE_EDGE`), with chevrons the way it travels and a curtain as tall as its height band, topped by a bright rail, so a jump's clearance is drawn (RR-02). `contains()` tests the same front band. `threat_grammar_test` compared the drawn front with the damage front at five times, 0.26 to 2.26 s after it left (2.04 to 18.04 m at 8 m/s); each matched within the test's 0.0001 m tolerance. Demand: the boss phase (the crane sweep). Serves 1 (nothing hits you that you could not see coming) and RR-01.
4. **A shell shows its whole flight.** A dotted trace runs from the launcher to the landing point along the path the shell actually flies. `lob_bump` turns the lob's fixed-time solve into the same parabola; over a 1.9 s flight the shell stayed within 0.00001 m of the drawn trace. Dots behind the shell disappear. A reticle's four brackets close from 1.6 times the blast radius to its edge as the shell falls (2.76 m of a 3.6 m blast, 1.8 s in), over a thin blast ring and a centre pip. This replaces the lobber's plain ring, which looked like the boss's instant slam. Demand: the Warden and events (lobbers), and the boss phase (drones). Serves Andrew's ask for "trace outlines to show objects and where they're intended to hit".
5. **The miniboss's leap slam is a projectile.** The miniboss is the projectile, so its ring gets the reticle and a trace from its feet along its real leap arc (4 x `SLAM_HEIGHT`). This is the one creation site that passes more than the type (`set_flight` after the commit); it changes no behaviour. Demand: event (the miniboss). Serves 8 (read, dodge, punish).
6. **A lane fills from its source.** The fill runs from the attacker's end to the far cap over the wind-up, then holds solid while the body runs. The Warden's magnet is a lane-type wedge: a pull shaped like a beam from the arm. It keeps its dashed rim and its chevrons pointing home (combat note 34), because a pull and a push must still read apart. Demand: the Warden (the magnet), event and the boss phase (charges). Serves 1.
7. **The crane sweep is the wave in play.** Its danger is the magnet head crossing your bearing, so it is a travelling front, not an area. Its spoke stands at `a_from + span x live_fraction()`, the same offset `boss.gd`'s `_swept` tests; the test matched them at four times across the 1.2 s sweep (0.117 to 1.283 rad). No enemy attack travels outward from a point today, so the ring wave (`Telegraph.wave`, stepped by its owner) is ready for Wave 3's cave-in and crater but has no caller yet. Demand: the boss phase. Serves 1.
8. **Superseded by note 20 (the presentation hook is built).** This was: **The warn sounds are in the rows but not yet audible.** `event_kinds.gd` holds `THREAT_ROWS` in the `ROWS` schema: a sharp double tick (instant), a rising whoosh (wave), a falling whistle (projectile) and a rising charge hum (lane), each with its own recipe in `sfx_bank.gd`. The rows sit outside `ROWS` because `ROWS` is keyed by bus signal and `spine_event_kinds_test` rejects a row that names no signal. Every telegraph and hazard joins the group `threat` and answers `threat_kind()`, so presentation can play the row's sound on `node_added`. That hook is presentation's file and is a requested change. Demand: the Warden, event and the boss phase. Serves form mirrors function (each layer reads one row).
9. **The audit: every creation site and its type.** Each row names the attack's code path and the type it now passes.

   | Site | Attack | Type | Why |
   | --- | --- | --- | --- |
   | `projectile.gd` setup, from `combat._on_lobber_fired` | lobber shell, and Brindle's drone lobbers | PROJECTILE | a shell flies to a committed landing point |
   | `projectile.gd` `bat()` | batted shell (amber, hits enemies) | PROJECTILE | the same flight, owned by the player |
   | `chaser.gd` `_begin_windup` | chaser lunge | LANE | the body runs down the strip |
   | `miniboss.gd` `_begin_attack`, slam | leap slam | PROJECTILE | the miniboss itself flies to the ring |
   | `miniboss.gd` `_begin_attack`, charge | charge | LANE | the body runs down the strip |
   | `warden.gd` `_step_pulse`, `_commit_ring` | pulse reach (lift, no damage) | INSTANT, bone tint: a harmless field (note 15) | the whole ring lifts on one tick |
   | `warden.gd` `_step_pulse`, core ring | pulse core (10 damage) | INSTANT | the whole ring hits on one tick |
   | `warden.gd` `_step_magnet` | magnet pull | LANE | a beam-shaped pull from the arm, held 1.5 s |
   | `boss.gd` `_commit_crane` | crane sweep (phase 1) | WAVE | only the magnet's swept front catches; converted from a pulsing area |
   | `boss.gd` `_step_furnace` | slag rings A and B (phase 2) | INSTANT | the whole band goes molten at once and stays |
   | `boss.gd` `_launch_lobber` | drone pad (amber, no damage) | INSTANT | marks a spot that fills at a known moment |
   | `boss.gd` `_commit_charge` | charge and wall charge (phase 3) | LANE | the hull runs down the lane |
   | `enemy.gd` `_commit_ring`, `_commit_lane` | helpers | passes `threat_type` through | the caller names it |

   Not telegraphs: the eject's scooter flight commits no geometry and hurts nobody, and the world events (flood, dust squall, scrap stampede, surge run, hold relay) push or score but commit no damage geometry. Demand: the Warden, event, vault and the boss phase. Serves AGENTS.md (damage and telegraphs consume the same committed geometry).
10. **The shared grammar crosses a module seam on purpose.** `hazard.gd` preloads `combat/threat_grammar.gd`, the one boss-to-combat preload, because the packet asked for one grammar in combat used by both. A later spine pass could move it under `src/core/`. Demand: the boss phase. Serves one grammar over two copies that drift.
11. **Superseded by note 17 (every ground type draws its band).** This was: **Only a wave draws its height band so far.** RR-02 asks every attack to draw the band a jump clears. The wave draws it because timing a jump over a front is the whole read. Rings, lanes and reticles still show reach only in their numbers. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves RR-02; the rest is a follow-up.
12. **Static pieces are drawn once; moving pieces redraw each tick.** Edges, areas and outlines paint at commit (and again when a quench opens a slag span). The front, brackets, trace and running fill repaint every step. The instant fill changes only a material tint. Every layer draws at priority 40, with underlays at 39 and no depth test, as combat note 10 requires. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase. Serves 7 (fast without debt).

### Harmonize round (VISUAL-LANGUAGE.md sections 3 and 4, SOUND-STORY.md sections 3 and 7)

13. **An instant fills striped from the edge inward.** The striped fill's inner edge closes from the rim to the centre across the warning (4.5 m at a quarter and 1.5 m at three quarters of a 6 m ring, measured), and a solid flash marks the landing. This follows VISUAL-LANGUAGE section 4 over note 2: the inward fill is a clock, and the edge never moves, so it still cannot read as a front leaving the centre. The flash stays 0.22 s, fading, rather than the sheet's one frame: one frame at 60 Hz is 17 ms, too short to register on a 30 m area at speed, and a fading solid still leaves the escape visible (RR-05). Demand: the Warden and the boss phase (slag). Serves Andrew's entry-7 ask.
14. **Hostile areas carry 45-degree stripes; the player's own and harmless fields never do.** Stripes are a world-space triplanar texture (HAZARD and charcoal, 0.5 m pitch) on the stripe layers, so every footprint shape takes them without clipping geometry. The tone decides the stance (`Threat.stance`): HAZARD is hostile (stripes, warn sound, group `threat`), AMBER is the player's own (clean fills, silent), bone is a harmless field (a dashed ring only). The test counted 432 striped vertices on a 6 m instant ring and none on an amber shell ring. Demand: the Warden, event and the boss phase. Serves the colour-blind rule: to a protanope Rivet's copper and HAZARD match, so the stripes carry hostility.
15. **The Warden's 30 m lift ring is a harmless field.** It lifts and never damages, so it draws as a dashed bone ring with no fill, no stripes, no flash and no warn sound, and it still answers contains() for the lift. Only the 6 m core ring, which does damage, stays an INSTANT threat. `warden.gd` passes the bone tint through `enemy._commit_ring`'s new optional `tint`. Demand: the Warden. Serves Andrew's entry-7 confusion: a harmless lift no longer reads as a hit coming.
16. **A boss telegraph keeps HAZARD and marks its owner in brass.** Brindle's hazards arrive tinted BRASS through the `boss_attack_telegraphed` row. `hazard.gd` now draws their danger in HAZARD and turns the brass into a tick every 30 degrees on the rim, or every 4 m along a lane's rails. The amber drone pad stays an owned marker. Demand: the boss phase. Serves VISUAL-LANGUAGE's conflict ruling on boss colour (danger is always HAZARD).
17. **Every ground type draws its height band.** Instant, projectile and lane put posts every 4 m along their outer boundary and a keylined rail at `reach`, broken where a quench opens a slag span. The wave keeps its curtain on the front. The test read the rail at 3.5 m on a 3.5 m reach. Demand: the Warden, event and the boss phase. Serves RR-02 (a jump's clearance is drawn, not implied).
18. **Keylines.** Every line already sat on a charcoal underlay. The flight trace's dots now get one too, and the rail has its own. The underlay stays 0.35 m wide against the sheet's 0.08 m, and edges keep their distance-scaled width (at least 0.35 m) against the sheet's 0.12 m. The telegraph's earlier readability fix set those widths so HAZARD reads on the Spillway's orange checker and a 30 m Warden ring still reads from 60 m; 0.12 m is the sheet's 4 px only at about 20 m. Demand: the Warden. Serves VISUAL-LANGUAGE's 1.3:1 rule by value, at the distances the fights happen.
19. **A wave shows the ground it has already swept, faintly striped (15%).** Its front stays 1 m either side (2 m), not the sheet's 0.4 m band, because the drawn band must be the band contains() tests (RR-01) and 1 m is the ball's radius; the crane's damage margin in `boss.gd` is the same 1 m. Demand: the boss phase. Serves RR-01.
20. **The warn sounds are live, in the threat register.** `audio.gd` hooks `node_added`: at the end of the frame a node joins the group `threat`, it plays the type's row sound at once, never on the beat (SOUND-STORY section 7), then the node's `owner_voice` and `mechanic_tag` when set. There is no fifth type sound. The recipes are retuned to SOUND-STORY section 3: accelerating ratchet clicks to a clang on Eb (instant), a whoosh rising Eb to Bb (wave), a launch thunk and a whistle falling Bb to Eb (projectile), a hum held on Ab to a tearing buzz (lane). Cues here are not positional, so a far threat is only trimmed, 9 dB down from 60 m. `threat_sound_test` committed six threats and logged exactly one row sound per hostile type, the lane's owner voice, and nothing for the amber and bone rings, all played at once. Demand: the Warden, event and the boss phase. Serves form mirrors function (each type sounds like what it does).

## v2 W3 characters (writer W)

W3 builds DESIGN-V2 sections 1 to 6: three characters on one input grammar, the shared hold-gauge-release shot, charges, and the bowling-ball contact. Numbers are starting points for Andrew's play. Principles are entry 6's: 1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing.

1. **Three rows, one source.** `class_def.gd` carries Sable (fox, pylon engineer), Rivet (mole machinist, runner-gunner) and Kest (bird, demolitions caster). Each row names its weapon, movement, special and smash ability ids, its colour (field mint, copper, safety amber), shape (ring, chevron, burst) and sound (a stand-in synth id plus the `sound_next` id W5 is to bake). Class-owned event rows read the same colour constants, so a class looks alike in the HUD, VFX and audio. Demand: the Warden and the boss phase, where the class is who fights. Serves 5.
2. **Each class fits only its own movement.** `Augments.KITS` maps Sable to grapple, pylon and vault; Rivet to the overdrive sprint; Kest to the smoke blink and the glide. The smash is everyone's, and jump, double jump and boost are base rules. The shared air dash is deleted, not hidden. Airborne Shift is now the plain boost along your velocity: it no longer redirects, because that job moved into each class's movement. Demand: the Warden and events, which each class now reaches its own way. Serves 5 and 1.
3. **`dashed` is each class's movement burst.** The vault kick, the sprint's start and the blink publish `dashed`, so Frost Vane's ice trail and the camera punch still work for every class. Dash Vane now lengthens the blink and raises its speed floor (`dash_impulse`, relabelled "Blink speed floor"). Demand: the flood event, where Frost Vane paves water. Serves 7.
4. **RMB cancels a charging gauge.** DESIGN-V2 says pressing the special again cancels, but a key held for the gauge cannot be pressed again. So an RMB press during any charge cancels it and moves nothing, as right click cancels a cast in League. A second F press still cancels for scripted sources and any rebinding. Sable's grapple now rides RMB through the intent like the other classes' movement, so `game._mouse_button` lost its RMB branch and `fire_grapple()` is gone. Demand: the Warden and boss fights, where a wasted charge costs the window. Serves 1.
5. **One gauge rule.** `AbilityState.Gauge` fills linearly while the key is down, and only down ticks count, so a tap fires at exactly the floor. Letting go fires at the fraction reached. A full gauge held 0.5 s longer fires by itself. A charge is spent only at release. A cancel, an RMB press or a knock leaves nothing spent, nothing fired and no cooldown. Demand: the Warden, whose windows the gauge is timed against. Serves 1.
6. **The indicator draws exactly what can hit; the forgiveness lives in the hit test.** This follows the coordinator's seam change (RR-01, TH-03), which overrides DESIGN-V2 section 4's padded drawing. The player-owned `indicator.gd` draws the ring, lane, lobbed arc or cone in the class colour, never hazard orange, over the world with no depth test. A release resolves the same record, and a hull up to 18% past the drawn edge still counts. `char_combat_test` checks both the record and the edge. Demand: the Warden and Brindle, where a near miss at the edge used to read as a lie. Serves 1.
7. **Sable's detonation gauge feeds the old discharge curve.** The gauge stands in for speed at `f x 40 m/s`, so a tap is the 12 m, 14 damage, two-target floor and a full gauge is 30 m, 70 damage and eight targets. Mounted, the banked speed counts when it is bigger. Planting still fires the free discharge. Cooldown 5 s, charge time 1.0 s. Demand: the Warden and events with crowds. Serves 7 (the pylon banks speed rather than stopping it).
8. **Rivet's tap is the old blast.** A tapped overdrive blast is exactly the old 12 m, 30 damage, 34 knock ring, because Andrew asked to keep the runner. The gauge scales radius, damage and knock up to 1.5 times, then the 3 s fire-rate boost follows. Cooldown 6 s, charge time 1.0 s. Demand: the Warden and crowded events. Serves 5.
9. **Kest's detonation line.** The line runs 6 to 14 m along the flat aim, 2.5 m wide, and deals 20 to 30 damage. Every live charge inside it goes off early and adds 25%, counted up to three. Five caught charges all detonate, but the bonus stops at 75% (DESIGN-V2 risk 3, boss plating). Cooldown 6 s, charge time 0.8 s. Demand: the Warden's plating and Brindle. Serves 4.
10. **Sticky charges land where the arc says.** LMB is a hold-release throw over 0.6 s with a reach of 10 to 30 m. The charge flies the drawn arc and sticks on the drawn landing ring. It does not inherit the ball's velocity: readability wins over realism, and a throw from 40 m/s would otherwise land far from its ring. Each charge deals 16 damage over 3 m and goes off after 1.2 s or on contact. The cadence is the weapon slot's 0.6 s cooldown. A fourth throw sets off the oldest rather than refusing the key. Held down, the throw fires by itself at full reach and starts again. Demand: events and the Warden's adds. Serves 1.
11. **The rocket jump is opt-in.** A blast only throws Kest when she holds Space inside 4.5 m of it: 22 to 30 m/s by proximity, away and up, with a 1.0 s self-launch cooldown. Without the hold, a demo slam's mini-charges at her feet would launch her unasked, a stop she did not choose in reverse. Chaining is bounded by that cooldown, which floors at 0.4 s, and by the throw cadence. Demand: vault climbs and the events' high ground. Serves 1.
12. **The cloak fools fodder, not set pieces.** For 1.5 s after an own detonation or a blink, chasers, lobbers and the miniboss see the player through a sight proxy (`combat/sight.gd`). One farther than 60% of its reach sees only the smoke where the cloak began and fights that. The Warden and the vault always see the ball. The first sticky or line out of the cloak deals double and ends it. Demand: events, where the crowd is. Serves 5 (hiding by moving, never by standing still).
13. **The blink redirects instead of stopping.** Kest moves 8 m along the flat aim and stops short of the world with a shape cast, passing through enemies. Her planar speed turns onto the aim at no less than 16 m/s. A climb is kept and a fall is caught. Cooldown 4 s. Demand: events. Serves 1.
14. **One door for crowd control.** Every stop an enemy imposes goes through `player.crowd_control(kind, shove)`, which force-cancels charging gauges (the slingshot's too) and applies the shove. Rivet's sprint shuts the door for every kind, publishes `cc_ignored` and never blocks damage. Combat's committed-hit knock uses the door today. Wave 3's pits, tethers, nets and bolas, and its tenacity rule, belong behind it. The Warden's pulse and magnet and Brindle's shoves still push through `add_external_accel` directly, because they are those fights' lessons, and they are outside W3's files. Demand: the Warden and Brindle. Serves 1 and 7.
