15. **Rivet's sprint.** Holding RMB spends a charge and runs up to 2.0 s, with a 5 s recharge. The drive aims at 1.25 times cruise through `BallState.cruise_boost`; speed tiers keep reading plain cruise, so the ram and crush thresholds do not drift. While sprinting, a contact's punch is 1.25 times, which puts the crush bar 20% lower, and the rebound bar drops to one times heft as DESIGN-V2 says. Every contact with something your weight or more then pays a refresh instead of a dead physics bounce. Demand: the Warden's adds and Brindle's charges. Serves 1.
16. **The vault kick.** In the air at a steep face, Space kicks off along the slingshot's eject line at `max(jump, incoming x 1.8)`, capped at 45 m/s. A face is its plane: the normal to about 17 degrees and the offset to 3 m, so one long wall is one face. It locks for 1.5 s, so two faces cannot ping-pong the speed up. An aim cone in Sable's colour shows while a kick is available. A stomp or rebound refresh unlocks the last face kicked. Demand: vaults and the vertical stage's towers. Serves 1.
17. **The slingshot is a gauge.** A tapped Space while mounted is the old eject (banked x 0.85 + 6, never below a jump). A 0.8 s hold adds up to 30%, and the aim cone grows with the fill. Demand: the Warden, fought from a pylon. Serves 7.
18. **Smash flavours on one slam.** Sable's arc slam marks steep faces within 12 m for 2 s, as small mint rings for a vault. Rivet's shockwave skid never plants: the fall's normal part times 0.6 goes forward along the ground, and a 10 m cone ahead is knocked, each hit refunding 0.5 s of sprint. Kest's demo slam lays three mini-charges 3.5 m out (5.3 damage, 1.5 m, armed after 0.3 s, 1.0 s fuse). Their spread follows her heading, not a draw from the run's seeded stream, so bot seeds replay. Demand: the Warden's caps and events. Serves 5 and 1.
19. **The bowling-ball contact.** An enemy weighing at least 1.15 times your heft bounces you. The incoming velocity is reflected at restitution 0.65, the bounce is capped at 1.4 jumps high, and you get the stomp's single refresh: one air jump and one movement charge. The ram still lands if you were at cruise, so rebounding off the Warden still hurts it. Resting against a body (closing under 2 m/s) is not a hit. A body with no weight, such as Brindle's pieces, keeps the plain ram. Lighter enemies are crushed or rammed as before. Demand: the Warden and Brindle. Serves 1 (a bounce you choose keeps the line going).
20. **Pins.** A knocked enemy flying faster than 6 m/s at another within 1.2 m hull to hull hits it once for `8 + 0.5 x speed` (cap 40). The key is the carrier's instance and knock count plus the victim, so one knock never pays twice. The carrier must be heading at the victim, and it hands on 60% of its speed, so a chain runs down below the bar by construction. Demand: events and the Warden's adds. Serves 4.
21. **Charges and floors.** `AbilityState` holds the four slots. Each has `max_charges` (1 by default), recharges one charge at a time and stockpiles. No modifier takes a cooldown under 0.4 s; a base of 0 stays 0. Stage entry and the arena refill the slots. `slots()` is W1's ability-slot record: id, icon, key, cooldown_frac, charges, max_charges and state, plus the gauge fraction for the reticle ring. Demand: the Warden and the boss, where timing the next charge is the skill. Serves 4.
22. **Sixteen facts on the bus, heard now.** The new signals each have an event row with a stand-in sound, and `audio.gd` plays them until W5 bakes the `sound_next` ids. The presentation test requires every signal to be heard. Adding them meant two appends outside W3's listed files, `game_events.gd` and `audio.gd`, each in a block marked "v2 W3 characters". Demand: none.
23. **The harness covers three classes.** The bot picks its class by seed modulo three, and Kest's bot lets go of LMB once the throw reaches its aim. `spine_run_test` takes `--class=N`, and `char_full_run_test` runs it for Rivet and Kest. The frost test's dash is now Kest's blink. Demand: none.
24. **Harmonize round: the class row carries a motif id.** `motif` (`sable`, `rivet`, `kest`) replaces the row's `sound_next`. The notes, rhythm and instrument belong to W5's motif table in `harmony.gd` (SOUND-STORY section 7: W3 owns the id, W5 the table). `sound` stays as the stand-in. Demand: the Warden and boss fights, where the class is heard. Serves 6.
25. **Rivet's bolts are copper.** Class colour means yours (VISUAL-LANGUAGE section 2), and amber is now Kest's. The rope, the pylon plate and the glide vane also take the class colour when the class is fitted. The ball's checker material is a material, not an effect, and stays. Demand: the Warden and events, where the bolts are read against amber plating tints. Serves 5.
26. **The indicator follows VISUAL-LANGUAGE sections 3 and 4.** It draws triangles, not lines: a 15% clean fill, a 0.12 m committed edge over a 0.08 m charcoal keyline, rounded caps and the class's rim ticks (Sable's pips, Rivet's chevrons, Kest's starburst spikes). A dashed outline shows the full-charge extent, and the fill breathes on a 0.5 s period at full through the grace. A cancel, an RMB or a knock collapses the shape into its origin in 120 ms. The world ring stands in for the reticle's gauge ring until W1's HUD reads `slots()[i]["gauge"]`. Demand: the Warden and Brindle, where yours must never read as a hazard. Serves 1.
27. **A throw over the void is blocked.** Kest's landing point needs ground under it. Over a void the arc and ring draw in bone, dashed, with a charcoal cross (RR-03), and a release throws nothing, spends nothing and publishes `gauge_cancelled(sticky_charge, blocked)`. It is the one blocked aim this round: a ring or a line always has somewhere to land. Demand: events and vaults on the vertical stage's sky layer, where void is common. Serves 1.
28. **The ability slots use the canonical icon ids.** `slots()` returns `ab_sable_*`, `ab_rivet_*` and `ab_kest_*` (weapon, move, special, smash), each with a file in `assets/icons`. Demand: the Warden, where Andrew reads the bar at a glance. Serves 5.
29. **Two Sable discharges sound different today.** The gauge release rides `gauge_released`, whose stand-in is now `found_chime`, a bright chime toward the arpeggio resolving onto a chord. The free plant discharge rides `mounted`, the plant clack. Both still publish `special_used`. The sixteen rows' `sound_next` ids now name SOUND-STORY section 3's material (for example `rebound_spring_snap`, `gauge_dry_click`, `sable_octave_leap`), and `gauge_cancelled` stands in with the dry `ui_tick`. The vault cue is pitched up to the octave at the 45 m/s cap. Demand: the Warden, fought from the pylon. Serves 6.
30. **Every stop an enemy imposes goes through the crowd-control door.** Each of the Warden's pulse and magnet and Brindle's tug, slag bounce and charge shove was a one-line change, from `add_external_accel` to `crowd_control`. So the sprint ignores all of them and the damage still lands. The magnet and the tug are steady pulls, so they pass `interrupts = false` and never wipe a charge every tick. The pulse, the slag and the charge are knocks and do. A sprint ignoring the magnet means Rivet can cross it for up to 2 s; that is what "unstoppable" buys, and play decides if it is too much. Demand: the Warden's magnet lesson and Brindle's phases. Serves 1 and strains the magnet lesson.


## v2 W5 audio board

W5's first task is the primer's audio board (the plan's Pacing addendum, TRACKING EV-0588): fourteen demos rendered offline from the sketch's own synth and band, with every new sound written where the build will reuse it. Principles are entry 6's: 1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing.

1. **One motif table.** `harmony.gd` `MOTIFS` holds sable, rivet, kest, fleet, reactor, swarm and mind, each a list of `[step, midi, steps]` cells with its voice. Every class cue, faction cue and band layer reads it, and `Harmony.mind_quote` derives the Mind's line from the class row it answers. The class rows' `sound_next` ids (`sable_arpeggio`, `rivet_hihat_roll`, `kest_low_thump`) now bake from the table. Demand: every fight, where the class is who acts. Serves 6 and 5.
2. **The Fleet's rhythm.** SOUND-STORY gives the pitches (D Eb D) and "stamp, stamp, decree". The cells are an anvil stamp with brass D on beat 1 and again on beat 2, the brass leaning up to Eb on the "and" of 2, then D held. Demand: every Fleet callout. Serves 6.
3. **Kest's boom.** Kest's motif is four eighths on the tuned tom, D A F D. The last thump is doubled on an 808 an octave down, so the tune reads "tick, tick, tick, boom". Demand: Kest's releases. Serves 5.
4. **The ready pulse.** Each class's ready pulse is its motif's first note, 120 ms. Sable's and Rivet's sit an octave above the motif so they read over the band; Rivet's adds a closed-hat tick. Kest's is the first thump, soft, at pitch. Demand: every cooldown. Serves 5.
5. **The class motif in the band.** It takes rotation 2 of the four re-voicings (the bell counter). It plays once per two-bar call and ends just before the next call. Each time it moves to the chord tone nearest its first note and snaps into that chord's scale, so its shape survives and it never clashes. It sits 3 dB under the lead (measured -25.9 against -22.8 dB) and never replaces it. Rivet and Kest take their band voice an octave up. Demand: stage themes at full pressure. Serves 3 and 6.
6. **The Fleet layer.** The band gained a fifteenth layer, `fleet`, gated by the difficulty ticks (`set_fleet_ticks`, from the next bar line). Tick 1 adds the anvil on beats 2 and 4. Tick 2 adds the brass semitone lean (root, a half step up, root) on steps 8, 10 and 12, answering the lead. Tick 3 puts the anvil on every beat. Each adds and none swaps. Demand: the clock after the Warden break. Serves 3.
7. **The reactor pedal.** While a vent charges, the bar's held sub plays Ab (44), plus its octave 10 dB down, under the unchanged bass figure. The first Geiger tick of a vent ducks the band. Later ticks do not duck, because a click every 35 ms would hold the band down. Demand: the stage 1 reactor Warden. Serves 3.
8. **Moss's lounge.** The lounge is a layer mask (`LOUNGE_GAINS`) with its own trigger: brushes and a ride, the snare on beat 3 only, the calm bass figure on the clean sub, keys on beats 1 and 3, the pad, and `sax_lounge` at half speed. `sax_lounge` is the gem sax with a 6% scoop and a 4.3 Hz vibrato after 0.22 s. The sax answers itself by inversion or retrograde. The whole sits about 7 LU under the full band, so Moss's babble at the spec's -8 dB is 4.3 LU above it (measured -16.4 against -20.7 LUFS). The tone filter holds 2.8 kHz. A drop, restart or track change ends it. A launch build over the lounge sits on A7b9, and the landing drop resolves it (G2). Demand: the bay. Serves 1, 6 and 3.
9. **The holds assemble the sinister set.** The pit sits on D, the bola on Bb, the tether on Eb (rising up to two semitones with strain) and the net on Ab. Each is trimmed to about -20 LUFS, so four stacked holds match the band (-13.3 LUFS) instead of burying it. The "about to be over" riser is a rising sine fifth from Ab to D with an accelerating shimmer, kept apart from the build riser's noise. Demand: Fleet crowd control. Serves 1.
10. **Breaking free.** Each `<class>_break` is a bright burst, then the class's first interval an octave up; Rivet's is the fourth A to D, because his first interval is a unison. Tenacity plays the choke, then the same cue. `free_bass()` brings the bass back from that frame over a beat. Demand: every hold. Serves 1 and 5.
11. **Babble details SOUND-STORY leaves open.** Words are 30 ms apart, times the beat's speed. The grumble sits an octave under the beat root. Beat 6's last word is 1.6 times slower. The relent's last blip glides down a fifth over 320 ms on a triangle through 800 Hz. Demand: the bay. Serves 6.
12. **The callout chime is a stand-in.** No source defines it (PROTOCOL trigger 3, flagged in the hand-back). The stand-in is two dry mallet pings, A5 then D6, 70 ms apart, on the UI bus. Demand: pack callouts. Serves 7.
13. **The shop bell** is a counter bell, then the rice cooker's two "done" beeps on high D (STORY-THREADS callback C9). The bench clank is a low FM strike on a thud. Demand: the bay. Serves 6.
14. **The swarm and the Mind.** The locked buzz sounds for 62% of each sixteenth. Scattering detunes each note and smears the gate. The Mind's quote is an octave up on the pure sine chime. Each of its notes rings two sixteenths longer than the class's, with the 3-cent bend over 1.8 s. Demand: the stage 2 and 3 Wardens. Serves 6.
15. **The board.** `tools/audio_board.gd` drives `render()` between calls on one timeline, as `audio.gd` does. It mixes at `audio.gd`'s bus levels (Music -3, SFX 0, UI -4 dB) with its priority trims and ducks, and reads sounds from the event rows and W2's threat rows. It matches each file to -14 LUFS (BS.1770) under a 1.5 ms look-ahead limiter at -1 dBFS. On file 01 its loudness agreed with ffmpeg's ebur128 (-14.0 LUFS, sample peak -1.04 dBFS). File 08 runs 24 bars (34 s) rather than 30 s, so the motif's second rotation arrives after the lift, in Eb. Demand: the primer gate. Serves 7.
16. **Not on the board.** The gorge's layers by height (SOUND-STORY W5 must). The ancients' own arrival cue. The gauge-charge and charge-pip cues. W3's other `sound_next` ids. The wiring of these layers to game facts in `audio.gd` (threat_rose to `set_fleet_ticks`, the bay to `set_lounge`, a vent to `set_reactor_pedal`, the class row to `set_class_motif`). Demand: the Wave 3 build. Serves 7.

## v2 W4 loot and the Moss shop economy

W4 answers entry 7's "how could I get more powerful" and "what am I searching for" (DESIGN-V2 sections 5, 7 and 12; I2; PROGRESSION section 1). The numbers are starting values for Andrew's play. The principles are entry 6's (1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing) and 11 (readability and standardized systems).

1. **One closed trigger vocabulary, read from facts already on the bus.** The twelve kinds live in `triggers.gd`. Every source is a published signal or build's own pickup and speed-tier read, so W4 changed no combat or movement code. Demand: the Warden and events, where the procs land. Serves 11: no hidden second pipeline.
2. **A kind fires at most once per commitment.** The first guard only refused a kind that was resolving. `item_trigger_test`'s storm case (every consumer posts all twelve kinds) resolved 67 commitments, with one kind 11 times. It ended, but that broke I2's "no trigger fires twice per commitment". The guard now refuses a kind already queued or resolved in the pass. One burst that kills five is one `on_kill` in that pass. Demand: events, where crowds die together. Serves 11 and Andrew's "no unforeseen bugs or hidden glitches".
3. **Build never feeds itself.** Its strikes (synergies, elements, items) never post `on_hit`, and the `synergy` strike kind has proc 0. That is two guards, because one would be luck. Demand: none. Serves 11.
4. **A generated item's id is its recipe.** `gen:<base>:<rarity>:<element>:<trigger>` rebuilds the same part everywhere: a retry, the run log, the fuzzer. Nothing about a generated part is hidden state. A quarter of offer slots are generated recipes; the rest are authored rows. Demand: the gates after each Warden. Serves 4 and Andrew's "procedurally generated loot".
5. **One stack-cap unit per rule.** I2's cap column mixed copies ("3") with values ("40%"). For linear and capped rows the cap counts copies; for hyperbolic rows it is the value ceiling below 1. `PartDef.Stacking.value` is the one function. Demand: none. Serves 11.
6. **Charges are parts.** Cap Relay, Sprint Cell and the rest raise `AbilityState.max_charges` through `set_max_charges`. So do Moss's cells, which are shop-only parts, so a retry restores them and each adds heft like any bolted part. A slot's ceiling is 4. A raised cap arrives stocked, which is what "Second cell's in" promises. The 0.4 s floor is AbilityState's own. Demand: the Warden and Brindle, where a stockpiled special is the plan. Serves 4 and Andrew's "store charges ... and start stockpiling".
7. **A part that needs another unit's seam is never offered.** Five rows wait: `fan_spread` and `helix_drill` (combat has no transformer rows for them), `feedback_loop` (no `extend_cloak`), and `anchor_spring` and `vault_battery` (the vault kick is not an AbilityState slot). Each names its seam in `needs`, and `Parts.seams` opens it the moment the module answers. The alternative was a part that does nothing. Demand: none. Serves 11. The seams are in the handback's requested changes.
8. **I2 adaptations, kept to I2's intent with a build-owned effect.**
   - `quick_spool` is passive (a timed cooldown cut is just a cooldown cut).
   - `slipstream` is a speed surge on tier-up plus 15% air control. Turn rate is not a tuning key.
   - `crush_guard` takes about 10% less damage, hyperbolic to 30%. The would-crush threshold is combat's.
   - `brace_frame`, `rebound_skin` and `deflect_plate` heal back part of a hit already taken, so none can undo a killing blow.
   - `ballast_drum` bolts on 40 scrap of mass once, on pickup.
   - `beacon_lamp` pings the beacon every 6 s and `echo_locator` the nearest vault every 12 s, because world's event positions are not a build seam.
   - `ring_wave` is a build-drawn pulse on each hit.
   - `glide_vane` carries 3 punch, like the older class rows, so no uncommon row is plain.
   - `sprint_cell`, `cloak_flask`, `grapple_coil` and `vault_battery` are class-gated, like `glide_vane`, `anchor_spring` and `jet_vent`, because they do nothing for another class.

   Demand: as each row's trigger. Serves 5 and 11.
9. **Rarity is weighted, and a stage rarely repeats itself.** Each slot draws its rarity 60/27/10/3 within the source's allowed rarities, then down-weights a part already offered this stage. The pattern guarantee and "every pair hits harder" hold. `build_catalogue_test` checks both over 50 seeds. Demand: the gates. Serves 4.
10. **Red is only for what hurts you.** Rare-plus gates, fabricator gems and accreted chunks were HAZARD red. They are now amber, and a rare-plus topper has a mint heart (DESIGN-V2 section 11). Demand: the gates. Serves 11.
11. **A gate reads without words.** Beside the topper it wears three billboarded glyphs from the Icons ids: family (`cat_`), trigger (`trg_`) and element (`el_`). It pulses when it completes a synergy. The prompt passes `set_parts(name, verb and effect and star line, obj_gate)`, so at range it shows only the name. Fabricators do the same with `obj_fabricator`. Demand: the gates and fabricators. Serves 11 and entry 7's "what am I searching for".
12. **The difficulty clock is data W1 draws, on the director's schedule.** `clock_record()` carries the run clock, the stage's filling bar with its tier and pause flag, and the par ghost. Build may not load world's files, so `build_clock_test` compares the two schedules and fails if they drift. The bar pauses in Moss's bay; world's director still needs to read `clock_paused()` for the spawns to pause too (requested). Demand: every stage. Serves 1 (the calm is real) and 4.
13. **Par and Scrapless are opt-in and tagged.** `set_modifiers` applies at run start. The tags go on `ctx.run` meta and `run_tags()`; the run log line is a requested spine change. The par ghost reads the best Warden break from `runs.ndjson`, so it needs no new save file. Scrapless takes nothing from gates, vaults or events, but a gate still launches you. Discovery bursts still grant scrap through flow, which is the spine's. Demand: the Warden (the par split) and every gate (Scrapless). Serves 2 (self-challenge without menus mid-run).
14. **Moss's shop is an economy with verbs, not a scene.** W6's bay calls `enter()` and `leave()`, and W1's shop UI reads records and calls verbs. Every verb answers with a result record carrying Moss's bark, so the UI, the babble and the tests read one answer. Prices are the fabricator's price rule times Fast Hands. The reroll is once per visit, rising 1.5 times per visit. Cells are at most two per slot, and each costs 1.6 times the last. Sell-back returns half the current price and lowers heft by the part's 0.2. Demand: the lull after each Warden. Serves 4 and Andrew's "some player decision be made".
15. **The heft lock is the growth goal.** The hook's lock is the heft class at entry plus one, drawn with `res_heft`. Buying from the shelf adds heft, so a player can buy their way to the relent inside one visit. Selling back can put them under it again. Demand: the lull. Serves 4.
16. **The conversation loop is a state machine over SOUND-STORY section 8's lines.** Its states are six beats, then the restart line, then fresh variations. A visit never repeats a variation until it has used them all, and class lines are extra choices. Each beat record carries the babble root and grumble from section 8's table, so W5 reads one source. `shop_lines_test` fails if any line is not in SOUND-STORY word for word, runs past 12 words, or names a potion, a traveller, a wizard or a weaker seller. Demand: the lull. Serves 6 and 7.
17. **Sell-back without a run mutator.** `run_state` has no `remove_part`. Until the spine adds one, the shop removes the part the way that mutator would. It never sells a pattern part (`welded`), because combat rebuilds patterns only on `part_gained` and would keep firing the sold pattern until the next stage. Demand: the lull. Serves 11 (no hidden glitch). The mutator is requested.
18. **Shop and item facts are rows, not new signals.** `LOOT_ROWS` in event_kinds' v2 W4 block gives each shop and item fact its colour, shape, stand-in sound and the `sound_next` W5 is to bake. A new bus signal would fail `presentation_sfx_test` ("every signal heard") until presentation connects it, and presentation is not W4's. Entering and leaving the bay reuse the mount and slingshot cues. A refusal is one dry bone-coloured interruption, never rust. Demand: the lull. Serves 6 and 7.
19. **Callback: Strike, Spare, Turkey.** This is bowling scoring as `build` codex entries (PROGRESSION section 6, callback 2), riding the pin rule and the rebound. A Strike is three enemies a pin chain takes down within 1.2 s. A Spare is an enemy that bounced you, taken down within 2 s. A Turkey is three Strikes within 4 s, Strike Pocket's window. `lanes.gd` scores them and the codex names them only after they happen. Test 1: three down from one knock is a readable feat without the name. Test 2: found in play, named in the codex. Test 3: pins are momentum. Demand: events and the Warden's adds.
20. **Callback: "Too heavy for you".** Moss's heft lock (DESIGN-V2 section 12, STORY-THREADS C3) rides the heft class. The relent writes the `build` codex entry. The joke and the growth goal are one mechanic. Demand: the lull.
21. **Callback: Moss notices what you neglect (C14, in-run).** If you leave the bay without buying and then die holding a shelf part's worth of scrap, his next greeting in this run is the `neglected` line. It rides the run's own death and the last visit's prices, with no save file. Test 1: a fair nudge to spend. Test 2: heard only by a player who hoarded and died. Test 3: it never stops anything. Demand: the lull.
22. **The bowling rows are named for the lane.** Pin Setter, Ten Pin, Gutter Guard, Re-Rack, Strike Pocket and Domino Wire keep I2's names. The effects match the words: a smash opens a pin window, a rebound off a heavy is the gutter bounce, landing near a pin re-racks it. Demand: events. Serves 4.
23. **The standardization is structural.** `build_catalogue_test` checks I2 section 4's invariants on all 86 authored rows and 240 generated recipes. `item_fuzz_smoke_test` runs 8 seeds x 20 s of the fuzzer in a child process at `--fixed-fps 90` (about 40 s). `tools/item_fuzz.gd` is the 200 x 90 s run; its anomalies go to HAPPY-ACCIDENTS.md for KEEP or KILL. Demand: none. Serves 11 and Andrew's "exploits can be fun too if they're accidental in a fun way".


## v2 W3 trees

The upgrade trees Andrew chose at the primer gate (DESIGN-V2 §15). Four picks per run and 16 end builds per pilot. Principles are entry 6's: 1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing.

1. **Each tree's two halves are the pilot's two identities.** Every build keeps one of them whole.
   - Sable: Cannonball (boom and bounce) against Beachhead (take point and turret).
   - Rivet: Gunline (run and gun) against Juggernaut (unstoppable).
   - Kest: Trapper (set up and burst) against Ghost (hide by moving).
   - Every tree touches all four keys.
   - Every depth-4 node changes how a key plays. There are 3 slot swaps (Crater Slam, Point Blank, Sticky Rain), proc chains and new modes such as Air Turret, Nightfall, Perpetual Motion, Sentry Post and Hitchhiker.
   - Demand: every fight. Serves 5 and 4.
2. **A node is data; variants plug in by table.**
   - A node carries ops (swap, knob, proc, pattern, charges, cooldown).
   - A variant is one `ABILITIES` row, plus one entry in its slot's dispatch table (`_specials`, `_actions`, `_smashes`) when it plays differently.
   - Movement and combat no longer branch on a literal ability id. This closes the primer board's flag about fourteen places that did.
   - Demand: mastery's alternate branches later. Serves 7.
3. **A pick lands on the live kit.**
   - A swap keeps the slot's stockpile cap and cooldown scale, and a stockpile pick adds one charge on top of items.
   - The saved WIP re-fitted the whole ability state on every pick, which would have erased a charge upgrade bought at Moss's shelf in the same visit.
   - Build's charge upgrades must add too (CONTRACTS, Upgrade trees).
   - Demand: the workshop. Serves 4.
4. **No build can chain itself.**
   - Every proc has a gap, floored at 0.4 s.
   - `UpgradeTree.problems` walks each of the 48 builds' procs, from trigger to the facts its action causes, and fails any loop.
   - Timed knobs (Anchor Storm, Fortress, Carpet Bomb, the special echo) share the floor.
   - Across all 48 builds the lowest repeat is 0.40 s (Rampage's and Dust Devil's gaps). DESIGN-V2 §6 risk 4.
   - Demand: dense fights. Serves 1.
5. **Balance against the tuned difficulty.** Three nodes were pulled back from the WIP:
   - Ghost leaves enemies 30% of their sight (the base cloak leaves 60%). The WIP's 0% hid Kest from depth 1, because every detonation refreshes the cloak and she throws every 0.6 s.
   - Anchor Storm pulses every 1.5 s at a 0.2 discharge: up to 3 targets for 25 each. At 1 s and 0.25 it added up to 112 DPS on top of the caster's 60.
   - Bounce Battery waits 3 s between refills.
   - Left for Andrew's play, the sharpest of the rest:
     - Perpetual Motion makes crowd control slide off whenever Rivet is fast.
     - Air Turret gives Sable the turret's 99 DPS instead of the caster's 60 while airborne.
     - The special echo (Twin Discharge, Vortex) repeats a release at 60%.
   - Demand: every Warden. Serves 1 and 4. Strains the tuned curve until the bot's balance report bands the builds.
6. **The tree's sound and colour.**
   - `tree_offered` and `tree_picked` are bone diamonds, because every pilot picks. Amber is Kest's and the rare rarity. The pick UI tints them with the class row.
   - Their cues for W5 are `motif_fork` (the class motif's first note answered by two notes, one per branch) and `motif_pick` (the chosen note resolving onto the motif head).
   - The shop bell stays purchases only (primer ruling 3). The WIP's `tree_offer_bell` was renamed for that reason.
   - This was PROTOCOL trigger 3 (a sound family no source covers), flagged in the hand-back.
   - Demand: class select and the bay. Serves 6.
7. **A pick never blocks.**
   - The start pick opens with the run and each stage clear opens the next.
   - An untaken pick simply stays open, and the run plays the kit it has.
   - Taking a skipped pick offers the next depth at once, so a player who skips the start pick loses no depth.
   - Demand: the drop and the bay. Serves 1 and 2.
8. **Callback: Octave Kick is a real octave.** It sets the vault gain to exactly 2. Speed doubles, and since the vault cue already pitches by exit speed toward the cap, the kick's top note leaps a true octave (C4 made literal). Test 1: a faster vault is readable alone. Test 2: heard, never announced. Test 3: it is momentum. Demand: Sable's vault chain. Serves 6.
9. **Bot runs at a fixed 90 fps.**
   - `tree_play_test` and `tree_bot_test` run their children with `--fixed-fps 90` in parallel. That is the same 90 Hz ticks, without the wall clock.
   - Kest's `char_bot_test` gave the identical result (6448 ticks to the Warden break) in 18 s instead of 76 s.
   - The four new tree tests add about 45 s to the smoke.
   - Demand: none. Serves the loop.

## v2 W6 stage and world

1. **Stage 2 is the Cable Reach, a gorge in three heights** (`src/world/map_gorge.gd`, I3).
   - Play rect 300 x 336 m. Sky: the rim deck, the barges and the crown dish, 44 to 70 m. Shelf: 24 to 32 m either side. Undercroft: the canal trough, 14 m down to 0 at the Warden bowl, and the wall tunnels.
   - The dam-works landmark is a 92 m cast tower at (-88, 14) with three satellites, lit only by Cherenkov-blue seams in charcoal grooves and one lamp at the crown. It is the one vertical line that reads from every layer.
   - Heights connect by five rides that keep speed (glide drop off the rim, grapple line, skylight halfpipe, geyser to the dish, marble-run chutes) plus the cannon, the trebuchet and two steam lifts. `stage_transitions_test` holds each to 85% of its entry speed; `stage_layers_test` walks every class through all three heights.
   - Demand: stage 2 of every run. Serves momentum and readability.
2. **Launchers ride the launch rail's own maths.** `ride_rail.gd` reuses `Launch.arc_point`; a ride hands back velocity each tick and lets go 0.04 s before the end, and a release caps the fall at 10 m/s so a landing keeps its planar speed. Cycles (`steam_cycle.gd`) charge 2.5 to 4 s on a dial and fire only when ready; the lift rests 3.2 s and rises in 0.85 s (a faster rise threw the ball 38 m).
3. **The barge carries the ball; it does not tilt it.** A rolling sphere on an accelerating deck lags by 5/7 of the change and rolls off (42 m of drift in the first try). The world applies a carry acceleration that matches the deck plus station keeping (gain 4) when the player is not steering, and the lean is visual only (3 degrees on the deck mesh). Measured drift at the far dock 0.55 m, after the round trip 0.00 m.
4. **Terrain events swap finished colliders.** Cave-in (3 s lead, first at 30 to 45 s) and impact (4 s lead, 55 to 75 s) pre-build the after collider and move it in: 26 to 40 us against the ~34 ms of a trimesh re-add. The change holds while the ball is inside its footprint (held 1.5 s in the test) and riding the new feature within 90 s pays the milestone. Live heights come from overlays on `terrain.height()` so the rules and the physics agree. A moved body reaches the physics space at the next transform flush, so the swap lands on the tick after the call.
5. **Moss's bay is a workshop barge docked behind and above the gate row.** A bay far away would make build's gates hop to it (they re-place beyond 110 m), so it docks within reach: 30 m back, 46 m up. The cradle catches the ball, banks its speed and throws it aboard (3 s); the exit pad throws it back in front of the gates at the banked speed (3.6 s). The shop opens on the catch and closes on the pad; the difficulty and event clocks stop while aboard. The practice yard waives cooldowns only inside its lines. The deck carries a marble toy, a bell, a jukebox and a timed lap (par 9 s).
   - The bay opens the upgrade tree's next pick on the catch; a skipped bay's pick opens at the stage's clear, once per stage per run (coordinator seam note; the stage_cleared hook in player_ball.gd is gone).
   - Demand: after every Warden break. Serves opt-in layers and quick restart.
6. **Gate sweep across the gorge.** `stage_gate_sweep_test` extends #39 to towers, the cable tower, barges, lifts, tunnel roofs, bridges, valve doors, the cracked wall and the swapped terrain, with a ground-following sweep beside build's chord. It found the tunnel roofs standing up to 2.3 m proud of the shelf beside them (they followed the shelf's nominal height); each end now sits 0.3 m under the shelf-side ground. Barges that sail across a gate's line after placement are a moving hazard the placement cannot foresee.
7. **Performance (headless, shared machine).** Stage 2 adds 57 colliders and 374 mesh instances with 227 distinct materials and one light, against stage 1's 15, 15, 5 and 0. Frame wall time over 600 frames: stage 2 p99 8.7 ms, max 35.7 ms (stage 1 p99 8.3 ms); frames pace at about 6.9 ms headless. The material count is the obvious draw-call lever if a real-window profile needs one (most come from per-prop `Palette.solid` calls).
8. **Callback: Rivet's sprint goes through the cracked wall.** The e1 tunnel's cracked wall breaks only to the overdrive sprint above 8 m/s; any other class meets a wall (codex `sprint_through`).
9. **Callback: Kest rides steam vents as thermals.** A glide over a vent climbs (+3.1 m in 1.2 s against -4.4 m beside it; codex `steam_thermal`).
10. **Callback: C12, do not open until clear.** Smashing the valve by the dug-up reactor vent from outside its 6 m ring opens the e2 door and vents the reactor early (codex `do_not_open_until_clear`).
11. **Callback: the pit-stop lap.** A bay lap under par 9 s unlocks `pit_stop_lap`, whose text carries the nomads' marble-run lore.
12. **The gorge keeps the world's route rules** (world_route_test, world_warden_flow_test, world_flow_test). The Warden stays in sight from the drop: the first bridge at z 4 cut that line, so the bridges moved to z 30 to 38 and 46 to 54, under it. Scrap lines stay within 25 m of the fall line; the two shelf lures became lures toward the steam lifts. The Surge Run is a side trip 44 m off the line: it sweeps west across the rim deck and hands the ball into the marble run's mouth.

## v2 V3 hero assets

Recipes live in `tools/blender/` (one per asset, shared slots and helpers in `common.py` and `shell_base.py`), models in `assets/models/`, and the runtime hook in `src/presentation/hero_assets.gd`. Every call below was checked in a headless turntable at 1080p gameplay distance before it was kept.

1. **The shell splits the way it moves.** `Core` (the scrap ball) rolls under the ball's rolling mesh. `Pod` (the class kit, the dome and the pilot) rides the juice node, stays upright and yaws to the heading. A rolling kit would tumble Rivet's fin chevron and Kest's horizontal satchel bands out of the directions the section 9 "must read" column asks for, and the cockpit would roll under. It also matches the lore that the shells are gyro-stabilized (DESIGN-V2 "Why balls"). The motion treatment is unset under RP-23, so this is the sketch's answer, not a Bible fact. Serves readability first and form mirrors function.
2. **Sable's collar rides the pod, ringing the dome, not the ball's rolling equator.** The primer board's s1 and f02 frames place it there, and a collar on the rolling equator would tumble. The rope spool sits on the right flank of the pod with a fairlead and a `RopeExit` node. `player_ball.gd` still draws the rope from the ball's centre (a seam request below). Serves readability.
3. **Chunks tuck under the pod.** A chunk scales to nothing as it rolls under the saddle (between 0.52 and 0.74 cosine from straight up) instead of clipping through the glass and the pilot. At any instant the top cap is bare, but every chunk shows over a roll. Serves readability (AC-01: the cockpit is findable).
4. **Chunk size follows the accretion slot.** Slots 0 to 11 take small scrap (bolt plate, bracket, hinge strap), 12 to 23 medium (dented tank end, pipe elbow, flanged stub), and 24 to 35 large (panel sections joined by a weld seam). A young build looks like loose hardware, and a heavy one visibly grows bigger salvage. A thief takes the largest first, because `fling` pops the last slots. Serves growth and form mirrors function.
5. **One `paint` slot carries three colours as vertex colours.** Section 9 names one slot "varied per chunk", so each plate panel and chunk bakes petrol, iron oxide or bone, and no two neighbouring panels share one. `hero_assets_test` pins every paint vertex colour to those three palette roles. Serves one source (palette.gd).
6. **`class_accent` glows softly on every surface it paints.** One slot cannot light only the working device. So every class_accent surface was placed on a device (Sable's plate edges and mast tip, Rivet's fins, lens and visor, Kest's satchel bands, fuse tips and mortar mouth), and it emits at 0.5. Brightening it while an ability works needs an ability-active seam (requested below).
7. **Rivet's optical rail is a flat bed that runs forward and down to a ringed lens.** The board's s1 note says a straight rail reads as a gun barrel. Mounted level, it also hid the mole's face from the front. Serves readability.
8. **Kest's glide vane is two hinged blades.** Folded, they stand like a dorsal fin behind the dome. While `kit.gliding` they swing out flat as wings. The class-colour glide wings in `player_ball.gd` stay as the effect. Serves "inventive" (every ability shows its mechanism).
9. **The pilots are animals and nothing else.** The fox is iron-oxide fur with a bone muzzle, charcoal-tipped ears, big eyes and a petrol cap. The mole is slate with a pink star nose and digging paws, a copper visor and an instrument cable into the dash. The bird is bone with a swept iron-oxide crest, pale goggles and a sandy amber beak. There is no badger and no human anywhere. Each head is sized so its ears, visor or crest clears the saddle at gameplay distance, and every pilot faces forward. Serves "more soul and character" and SOUND-STORY section 7 (V3 must).
10. **Gate toppers follow the section 5 rarity frames.** Common is a capped bar, uncommon a diamond with a bold rim, rare a star with a double outline, and rare_plus a tall double-outlined star with four bolt notches and a mint heart. The lintel trim and keystone take `reward_gates.gd` `rarity_color()`, so W4's pending bone-for-common fix flows through with no change here. The glyph plates hang from a gantry behind the gate's own billboarded glyphs, placed from their positions, so the plate count always matches the glyph count. Serves readability and one source.
11. **The Warden's pivot is a tower on the housing rim, not the beacon.** Both C-jaws hinge there and close round the beacon, so the jaw path reads as a clamp. Below 2 m nothing passes the 2.8 m collider radius that `warden_look.gd` promises. The kit sits on the tower top, so each family's weak point faces outward, away from the beacon. The kits are modelled 1.35 times larger than first drafted, because at 30 m the reactor dome, hive mound and Mind's arms only told apart at that size. Charcoal grooves use the `rubber` slot, because section 9's ancient slots name none. Serves readability and section 11's no-reskins rule.
12. **Glass is clear white, never ICE.** Section 2 reserves ICE for frost, so the cockpit glass is untinted and Kest's goggle lenses are pale bone.

Known limits: at full growth (36 chunks, heft class III) the chunks cover most of Kest's truss cage, and the satchels, mortar and vane carry the class. The previews are Blender EEVEE renders with Godot-like materials, not captures of the running game. Feel and in-motion readability wait on Andrew's play.


## v2 W7 enemies

W7 builds DESIGN-V2 sections 10, 11 and 15's enemies (TRACKING EV-0588, light play lane). Principles are entry 6's: 1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing. `roster.gd` is the one source for what each enemy is.

1. **One roster row per enemy, one mechanic per row.** Every id owns a `mechanic` no other id has and a one-line `line` saying what the player must do; `enemy_roster_test` fails two rows that share one. Nineteen ids: the five from the first cut, eleven Fleet counters and three ancients. Demand: every stage's Warden run, where the packs meet you. Serves 5 and 7.
2. **Two timbre families.** A Fleet telegraph carries `owner_voice = clank`; an ancient carries its family's (`reactor_tick`, `swarm_buzz`, or the Mind's quote). Each row's `tag` is an existing baked sound used by no other row, standing in until W5 bakes `tag_next`. Demand: the Warden stages. Serves 6.
3. **Every hold goes through one node.** `holds.gd` is the only thing that holds the ball: `catch()` asks `player.crowd_control`, `pull()` applies the steady effect with `interrupts = false`. A hold the player breaks (jump, boost or any movement burst, smash, glide, or a tether's speed) ends that unit's attack for good, kicks the ball forward and refunds 0.6 s of the movement ability. Demand: events and the Warden's packs. Serves 1.
4. **Tenacity.** 1.8 s of any hold summed over a sliding 4 s cuts every hold at once and makes the ball immune for 2.5 s, drawn as the broken bracket in the class colour. One clock for every source, so a pack chaining holds is bounded exactly like one unit. Demand: the Warden's packs past the break. Serves 1.
5. **The counters, each a different verb.** Mag pit (heft at the disc's rating rolls through; jump or smash shatters it), tether (outrun 19 m/s, boost, or have the heft rating and it snaps and slings you), air net (only an airborne ball; double jump, glide or smash), bola (a slow projectile arc that stings and drags you down), plated brute (one extra breaking hit), deflector (restitution 0.9; jump inside 0.45 s for a x1.8 vault, any class), winch drone (hovers 5.5 m up, pulls you toward its pack), gyro jammer (a sideways drift across your line at 0.7 Hz; the camera never moves), swatter (a low wedge sweep that bats you along it; boost to ride it), ramp-flipper (a tilting plate that flings you sideways; jump on the tilt for lift), catch-trap (the one outright stop, 1.4 s, boost or smash out). Demand: events and the Warden run. Strains 1, bounded by 3 and 4.
6. **One catch per tool.** A cup and a net catch once per attack; a pit and a field may catch again only after tenacity lets go. Without it an expired cup re-caught the ball on the next tick. Demand: none.
7. **Units close to their reach before they circle.** A unit's roaming keeps its far distance under 0.9 of its attack range; the swatter and the brute otherwise circled forever out of reach. Demand: none.
8. **The reactor relic's crater stings on entry.** The vent charges 2.4 s (`vent_charging` carries the time, for W5's accelerating Geiger and the Ab pedal), erupts for 14 and a lift, then leaves the same ring burning 5 s. Rolling into it stings 4 once per entry and never slows you; a per-tick burn would cancel a charging gauge every half second. Demand: stage 1 and its reactor Warden. Serves 1.
9. **A swarm is one threat.** One spawn brings a rack of six drones; the rack locks, draws its dive lane, and dives. It stings once per dive and peels off, because six kinematic drones ploughing through the rigid ball flung it to 22 m/s. A rack recovering from a dive or scattered flies at 3.4 m, over the ball, because the ball rams anything it touches from about 8 m/s and a drone has 12 HP. Only a forming or locked rack scatters. Demand: stage 2 and its hive Warden. Serves 1.
10. **Callback: The rack.** The swarm's formation is a bowling rack, apex toward you: a 1-2-3 of six, and the hive Warden's guard is the full ten, 1-2-3-4. Bowling through it scatters it. It rides the formation and the scatter, not pins, so it leaves C19's bowling scores alone. Discovered by looking. Demand: stage 2's Warden.
11. **Callback: C11, Awaiting operator.** Scattered drones follow the ball for 2 s at its heels with their blue seams dark, and a follower that reaches another enemy launches into it as a pin carrier. The codex enemy entry "Awaiting Operator" quotes fragment 5. Demand: stage 2 and its Warden. Serves 1 and 7.
12. **Callback: C6, the Mind quotes you.** A Mind's hand commits its strike lane voiced as `mind_<class>` (the class motif inverted, last note flat) and publishes `mind_adapted(habit, icon)` in the same call, with the habit's trigger icon on its lens in HAZARD. Then it braces 5 s: that verb's hits meet 1.6 times its plating and its lens is open at plating 0. The stage 3 Warden does the same at 1.4 times, every 10 s in reach. Habits and the Mind's memory reset on `run_started`. Demand: stage 3 and its Warden. Serves 5 and 6.
13. **Callback: C13, What it learned (data only).** `fleet.mind_memory` keeps every adaptation of the run, and the codex entry "What It Learned" marks the first. The page listing your three most-fired triggers is W1's to draw from it. Demand: the stage 3 Warden.
14. **The family Wardens.** The clamp is one base; `warden_kit.gd` adds the family's shape and weak point, and the lessons (plate, caps, magnet) stay as they were. Reactor: the pulse is the reactor venting, and the core is open 2.5 s after each. Swarm hive: a ten-drone guard rack stands between hive and ball; scattering it opens the queen cell 4 s, and a guard that dies out comes back after 10 s. Mind: the lens opens while it braces. Plating, the 20% chip, the gate and break-anywhere are untouched. Demand: the Warden. Serves 3.
15. **Packs.** The director turns a share of spawn slots into packs: 0 at tick 0, 0.12 a tick, 0.08 more for each tick past the Warden break, capped at 0.6. A tagged pack is a controller and a punisher (grounded, strung, netted, rebound, reeled, wobble, flung, cupped); a quarter of packs are the stage's ancient. Counters unlock two a tick from a stage base of 2, 4 or 6. Demand: the Warden run past the break. Strains 1, bounded by 4.
16. **Packs test the build, fairly.** Weight is 1 plus how hard the build leans on the pack's axis (habits, the class's own axis, and W3's upgrade-tree `branch_tags()` mapped through the director's `TAG_AXES`), so the build's own axis comes up to twice as often and every open pack still comes. Measured over 3000 draws: an air build drew the air pack 583 times against 364 with no build read, and the rarest pack still 304. Demand: the Warden run. Serves 4.
17. **pack_arrived.** The director times a pack's members on one ring, so combat reads every reinforcement spawned in one frame as one pack and publishes `pack_arrived(faction, at, kinds)` once per faction. `EventKinds.faction_icon(faction)` is the edge marker's badge. Demand: the Warden run. Serves 3 and 6.
18. **The plating dress.** "Breaks now" is a class-colour rim with an open-crack glyph, "not yet" closed dark plates, and the clank ladder is unchanged (primer ruling 6). `fleet.gd` sets the rim colour from the run's class row every tick. Demand: the Warden, the brute and the vault. Serves 5.
19. **Eleven bus facts, not yet heard.** `hold_started`, `hold_broken`, `hold_ended`, `tenacity_gained`, `pack_arrived`, `shield_vaulted`, `vent_charging`, `vent_erupted`, `swarm_scattered`, `mind_adapted` and `weak_point_opened` each have an event row with a baked stand-in. `presentation_sfx_test` requires presentation to listen to every signal, and `src/presentation` is outside W7's files, so the hand-back requests the audio.gd block W3 wrote for its own facts. Demand: none.


## v2 W5b music layers

`music_engine.gd` already had four layer setters nobody called: `set_fleet_ticks`, `set_reactor_pedal`, `set_class_motif` and `set_lounge`. `audio.gd` now wires them to the bus, per DESIGN-V2 section 11's audio rulings and SOUND-STORY section 8.

1. **`threat_rose(tier)` raises the Fleet layer.** The existing `on_beat("threat_rose", ...)` stinger now also calls `set_fleet_ticks(tier)`, so the anvil and brass add one layer per difficulty tick instead of only stinging once. Serves 6.
2. **The reactor relic is the only ancient that colours the band.** `vent_charging` and `vent_erupted` moved out of the generic `ENEMY_KINDS` bind (which only played their stand-in cue) into their own listener, so each also sets the tritone pedal on and off. Only the reactor's vent touches `reactor_pedal`; swarms and the Mind stay effects-only, as ruled. Serves 6.
3. **`run_started(class_id)` seeds the counter-line.** The direct connect used `_class_id` for nothing but a flow-queue phrase; it now calls `set_class_motif(class_id)` too. Serves 6.
4. **Moss's lounge follows the bay.** `bay_entered` and `bay_left` had no listener at all; each now calls `set_lounge` and plays its row's stand-in cue (`pylon_plant` / `pylon_eject`), so the sax-led re-voicing masks the stage theme while the ball is banked and drops away when it leaves. Serves 6.
5. **A pedal never sticks.** `fleet_ticks` and `reactor_pedal` reset at `run_started` and at every `stage_entered` (alongside the existing beacon and combo resets), and `run_ended` also clears the pedal directly, so a run or stage change can never carry a stale layer into the next one. Serves 2 and 6.
6. **`presentation_sfx_test`'s merge seam.** W6's `world_fact(kind, at)` and W7's `bay_entered`/`bay_left` had no presentation listener at all, so the base commit's "every signal heard" check named all three unheard. `bay_entered`/`bay_left` are covered by item 4 above; `world_fact` now plays `EventKinds.WORLD_ROWS[kind]`'s sound, or stays quiet when the kind has no row (the same empty-override skip `play()` already does elsewhere). Demand: none, closes the seam.

No `music_engine.gd` setter needed a fix; the four already did what SOUND-STORY.md describes.

## v2 W8 workshop UI

W8 gives the tree and the shop a screen. Andrew's entry-7 verdict was "so much words and I can't really tell what to press", and he asked for mouse menus, transitions, information timed to its moment, and "clear how to get stronger and what to search for". Principles are entry 6's (1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing) and 11 (readability). The files are `src/ui/workshop.gd` (when each screen opens and what a choice does), `tree_pick.gd`, `tree_card.gd`, `tree_crumb.gd`, `shop_counter.gd`, `shop_tile.gd` and `moss_bubble.gd`.

1. **A pick card is the ability bar's own slot, changed.** The middle of each card is the HUD slot: the node's `ab_` glyph on a rounded square, one notch per depth on its top edge, and the key it changes drawn under it as on the bar. The name and the effect line sit below. The number key that takes it sits top left, as on the class cards. Demand: class select and every workshop visit. Serves 4 and 11. A player reads "this key gets this" without a sentence.
2. **The path is the tree itself.** Above the cards a fan of 2, 4, 8 and 16 dots draws the whole tree. The picks so far are lit and joined in the class colour, the pair on offer pulses, and what the pair can still reach is brighter than what the path closed off. No numbers and no words. Demand: every pick after the first. Serves 4. The doubling reads at a glance.
3. **The pick pauses; everything else keeps rolling.** A pick is a choice screen, so it pauses the tree while it is up, and the ball's speed is exactly where it was when it closes. It opens with the run, on the bay's catch, or as the catch-up at a stage clear. "Later" (Esc) leaves the pick open (W3 note 7). It comes back at the next checkpoint or from the pick plate on Moss's counter. Opened from that plate, the pick stands in for the counter until it closes, so the two screens never add their words together. Demand: class select and the drop. Serves 1 (momentum held, not lost) and 2.
4. **One input grammar for every menu I touch.** The mouse hovers and clicks. Hovering a card or plate also gives it keyboard focus, so the mouse and the keys never point at different things. On the pick, 1 and 2 take a card, and A and D or the arrows move focus, confirmed with Enter or Space. On the counter, WASD and the arrows move by rows. The pad's d-pad and confirm come free through Godot's `ui_` actions. Esc backs out of either screen. While a screen is up the workshop holds the mouse cursor and the ball's keys, so a click never recaptures the mouse and a key never jumps the ball. game.gd carries only two one-line hooks for it (CONTRACTS, Workshop UI). Demand: none (menus). Serves 2 and 11.
5. **Transitions never wait, and a cut transition leaves nothing behind.** The pick's cards drop in over 0.28 s (cubic ease-out, staggered 60 ms) and take input on their first frame. On close the chosen card pops to 1.12 with the back-out overshoot VISUAL-LANGUAGE section 8 keeps for good news, and the rest fade in 0.22 s. The counter slides 40 px and fades. Closing gives up focus and the mouse at once. A generation counter stops a stale fade from hiding a screen that has reopened. `ui_workshop_pick_test` cuts the drop-in with Esc and reopens in the middle of a fade-out. Demand: none. Serves 1.
6. **The counter opens when you pull up to it.** Rolling inside Moss's reach under 4 m/s opens the counter, like a pit stop. Rolling past faster does not. Once you leave, it reopens only after the ball has rolled out of reach and come back. The ball parks while you shop, which costs nothing because the cradle already banked its speed and the pad pays it back. The bay's clocks already stand still, so the counter pauses nothing, and the lounge keeps playing. Demand: the lull after each Warden. Serves 1 and 3 (opt-in: you have to steer to him).
7. **Plates, icons first.** Each shelf part is its `cat_` glyph inside its rarity frame (VISUAL-LANGUAGE section 5: thin bone, bold mint, double amber, notched amber and mint), with its `el_` and `trg_` glyphs on the frame's lower corners and its price as the scrap cog and mono digits. Too poor dims the plate and strikes the price through; nothing turns red, since red is only for harm. The locked hook wears the heft dial with an amber notch at the class Moss wants and the needle at yours, which is the growth goal in one glyph. A sold plate is an empty dashed outline. Cells show the slot's ability glyph, its key and diamond pips for cells held against the cap. The name and what the part does (`Parts.headline`, the gate's own first line) show only for the plate in focus. Demand: the lull. Serves 4 and 11. It answers "what to search for".
8. **Two faces, not one long list.** The counter shows Buy (shelf, reroll, a waiting pick, hook, cells) or Sell (every held part at its quote). The face buttons wear their verbs' row shapes from `LOOT_ROWS`: `shop_bought`'s diamond and `shop_sold`'s chevron. A welded part shows a cross and no quote. Demand: the lull. Serves 11.
9. **Every word on the counter is the shop's.** A plate press calls the verb, and the record goes out as the `LOOT_ROWS` fact it was (`shop_bought`, `shop_rerolled`, `shop_charge`, `shop_sold`, or `shop_refused` for any refusal). The bubble says the record's own bark and nothing else. Demand: the lull. Serves 7. The UI, the babble and the tests read one answer.
10. **Moss is a voice and hands.** His line is a speech bubble whose tail runs off the left edge, the hatch's side, at 30% of the screen height and well clear of the reticle. A mitt below it slides out to give on a sale, taps on a beat, and holds up a wagging palm on a refusal. The talk key (T) and a small bubble glyph sit by the hatch only while the bay is open and the ball is at his counter. Demand: the lull. Serves 5 (a character without a model) and 11.
11. **Exasperation is shape and motion.** Each beat's `exasperation` (0 to 5) tightens the bubble's corners, cuts square teeth into its straight edges and shakes it harder (13 Hz, decaying). A grumble beat lands with a squash. The restart rewinds: the bubble shrinks to 0.7, turns back 0.35 rad and comes round smooth. The relent is amber (`moss_relent`'s colour), rounded and pops with overshoot over that row's star. Each beat's babble root lifts the stand-in cue's pitch from D3, so he rises as he loses patience. Demand: the lull. Serves 6 and 7.
12. **Callback: the send-off bubble is a cog.** At full exasperation the bubble's square teeth make it the scrap cog (`res_scrap`), which is what Moss keeps telling you to bring ("Scrap adds weight"). It rides the heft lock. Test 1: a spiky, shaking bubble reads as irritation on its own. Test 2: found by looking, never named. Test 3: it takes no input and costs no motion. Demand: the lull.
13. **The calm-screen word budget is 40.** The fight keeps its 25. The pick and the counter are calm moments you open on purpose, so they get more, but still icon-first. The bound comes from the parts. The pick at most has two names (3 words each), two effects (8 each), two number keys, two slot keys and "Later", which is 27. The counter's fullest buy face has Moss's line (12, SOUND-STORY's cap), the focused part's headline (about 10), nine prices (three shelf, reroll, hook, four cells), two cell keys, scrap, the heft numeral, T and "Leave", which is 38. Measured by `word_budget.gd` on real screens: the pick 19, the buy face 26 with a 9-word bark up, the sell face 14. Demand: none. Serves 11.
14. **Sound through the rows that exist.** The pick's `tree_offered` and `tree_picked` are bus signals audio already hears. Shop facts are not signals, so the workshop plays each `LOOT_ROWS` fact's stand-in sound through the audio director's own `play()` on the UI bus. W5's baked `sound_next` cues (`shop_bell`, `workbench_clank`, `moss_grumble`, `moss_babble`, `moss_babble_sigh`) replace the stand-ins without a UI change. Demand: the lull. Serves 6.
15. **Armed only in play.** In test mode the workshop is off, so the bots and the older tests run exactly as before; `ui_workshop_pick_test`, `ui_workshop_shop_test` and `ui_workshop_bay_test` switch it on. The bay test runs world's own hooks: the catch opens the pick over the throw with Moss's greeting behind it, and pulling up to the hatch opens the counter by itself. Demand: none. Serves 11 (no hidden change to anyone else's checks).

## v2 W10 menus and wording

W10 answers Andrew's entry-7 asks for mouse menus and fewer words, and closes DESIGN-V2 sections 14.1 (settings and pause menus for the mouse and the keys) and 14.10 (a one-page how to play on the title). Principles are entry 6's (1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing) and 11 (readability). The files are `src/ui/pause_menu.gd`, `volume_row.gd`, `settings_store.gd` and `help_page.gd`, with small edits to `screens.gd`, `field_notes.gd`, `stage_defs.gd` and `event_kinds.gd` `LESSON_SHORT`.

1. **Pause is a menu, not a caption.** P, Esc or a pad's Start opens it in play and closes it again. It holds six rows: Resume (P), Restart run (N), Field notes (Tab), Music, Effects and Quit (Q). The old "PAUSED · P to resume" label and field notes' P handler are gone. It fades in over 0.16 s and takes input on its first frame, and Resume recaptures the mouse at once, so the ball is back under your hand in one press. Demand: any moment in play. Serves 1, 2 (Restart run is one key) and 11.
2. **W8's input grammar, extended to the pause menu.** The mouse hovers and clicks. W/S or the arrows move focus, A/D or left/right step a focused volume, and Enter or Space takes the focused row. Esc or the pad's cancel backs out. The pad's d-pad comes through Godot's `ui_` actions. Each choice row wears its key's cap, the same `ChoiceButton` the title and death screens use. Demand: none (menus). Serves 11.
3. **A volume is a row of rising segments.** The row sits on the ChoiceButton plate. Where a choice has its keycap, it has a drawn glyph (a beamed note for music, a speaker for effects), then one word, then a down arrow, ten segments that rise left to right, and an up arrow. A click on a segment sets the level there, the arrows and the wheel step a tenth, and a drag sweeps it. Each step plays the UI tick on the pentatonic rung the `- = [ ]` keys use, so the slider and the hotkeys sing one scale. Levels reach the mixer through `audio.set_volume(bus, level)`, one new setter beside `_apply_volumes()`. Mute stays on M. Demand: none. Serves 6 and 11.
4. **Settings persist per viewer.** `settings.json` sits in the game's storage folder beside `ui-profile.json`, on the same never-throw pattern. It stores only levels a player set, so an untouched game keeps the mixer's defaults, and `apply_saved()` puts them back at launch. The `- = [ ]` hotkeys still change the session only. Demand: none. Serves 2 (nothing to redo after a relaunch).
5. **It never stacks on the workshop.** The menu does not open while the workshop holds the player (`menu_held`). The pick already swallows P, and a P at Moss's counter now does nothing where it used to pause the tree. If a pick opens under the menu, Resume leaves the tree paused for the pick. Demand: the lull. Serves 7.
6. **Field notes are pinned from the menu.** The Field notes row holds the notes open until the next key or click, except Q, E and Tab, which still turn pages. The menu hides underneath so the two never add their words together. Demand: none. Serves 11.
7. **Only real settings get rows.** Reduced motion does not exist, and mouse sensitivity is a constant in `chase_camera.gd`, so neither has a row. Quit is a row because Esc in play now opens the menu instead of freeing the mouse and then quitting. Outside play, Esc still quits. Demand: none.
8. **Help is a page of the title.** A Help button (H) swaps the title's objective and actions for the how-to-play page, and H, Esc or its Back button swaps back. The top row is the goal: Grow, Break the Warden and Visit Moss, each on its icon (`res_heft`, `obj_warden`, `res_scrap`) in its event row's colour (`heft_class_changed`, `warden_engaged`, `bay_entered`), joined by chevrons. Under it are the eleven keys the field notes mark with `caps` and `short`, each as its keycaps and one or two words. FieldNotes.CONTROLS stays the one key list; the page adds no text of its own beyond the title and Back. Demand: the title. Serves 11.
9. **Word counts, measured by `word_budget.gd` on the real screens** (`ui_pause_menu_test`). The pause menu has 9 words (13 counting the P, N, Tab and Q caps the ChoiceButtons draw, which the counter does not read). The help page has 34 (35 with Back's H cap). Both are inside the calm-screen budget of 40 (W8 note 13), and the test fails either one past it. Demand: none. Serves 11.
10. **Each stage's objective names its own Warden.** Stage 2's "Slam its vent caps" put the reactor's word on the swarm hive. The words change and the lessons stay put, because DEMO-SPEC's ladder (plain plate, then caps, then magnet) and Brindle's phase 2 (the caps, quoting Sluice, the stage that taught them) both depend on caps staying on stage 2. Stage 1's reactor keeps the plate lesson and names its vent: near the Warden it reads "Ram it as it vents", and the hint is "Ram it hard, right after it vents." That is when the core stands open. Stage 2's hive keeps the caps and names its guard: "Bowl its guard, slam caps", and the hint is "Bowl through its guard rack and slam its caps." Scattering the rack opens the queen cell. Stage 3 is unchanged. `spine_objective_test` now fails any stage 2 wording that says "vent". This supersedes DEMO-SPEC's example objective line. Demand: every Warden. Serves 7 and 11.
11. **Stage 2's caps are brood cells, not vents** (closed by the Codex Style A pass, b996c27). `warden_look.gd` drew them as glowing domes with heat plumes, which read as a reactor on a hive. They are now shallow hexagonal brood membranes with enamel rims and raised open hex markers; the hit positions, multipliers and flash are unchanged. The hive kit's rubber hatch hinges open with the queen window, and the beacon mast opens around a live family weak point. Demand: stage 2's Warden.

## v2 W9 the ear

The vision keeper's alignment pass found W5's cues baked in `sfx_bank.gd` while the running game still played the event rows' stand-ins. W9 wires them: every cue resolves from the event, class and roster rows, with no table of its own. Principles are entry 6's (1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing).

1. **One resolution.** `Rows.sound(kind, context)` returns a row's `sound_next` when the bank has a recipe for it, else its stand-in `sound`. It reads ROWS, the UI rows, the loot rows and the world rows alike. Symbolic ids resolve by who is involved. `class_motif` is the class's ready pulse, `class_break` its break-free, `motif_release` its whole motif, `hold_by_kind` the holding unit's loop, `faction_arrival` the faction's roster arrival and `mind_quote` the Mind's inverted quote. A symbolic id the context cannot fill plays nothing rather than a blip. A W3 cue such as `sable_octave_leap` plays the day W5 bakes it, with no wiring. Serves 5 and 7.
2. **The band never knew the class.** `run_started` carries the class row's id (`pylon_engineer`), but `set_class_motif` was keyed by the motif id (`sable`), so the live game never seeded the counter-line. The music-layers test emitted `"rivet"` directly and passed. `audio.gd` now maps the id through `class_def.gd` (`motif_of`), and a motif id passes through as itself. Every class cue reads the same `_motif`. Serves 5 and 6.
3. **A class's release is found, not listed.** The class row has only `motif`, and `sable_arpeggio` cannot be spelled from `sable`. The release is the one bank recipe named for the motif that is neither `<motif>_ready`, `<motif>_break` nor a cue an event row names for itself. A second candidate plays the stand-in, and `audio_cue_resolution_test` fails on it. The cleaner source is a `sound_next` on the class row (W5's note 1 calls them "the class rows' `sound_next` ids"); `class_def.gd` was outside this unit. Serves 5.
4. **A hold is the unit's loop.** `hold_started`'s kind is the roster mechanic (`air_net`), not the loop's name. The loop is the unit's `hold` or `tag`, whichever is a baked `hold_*` recipe. The netcaster's `hold` still names the `zap_hum` stand-in and its tag is `hold_net`, so it sounds the net. Serves 1.
5. **The Mind quotes you once.** `mind_adapted` resolves `mind_quote` through `Roster.voiced` with the run's motif, so Kest hears Kest's tune come back wrong. The hand's telegraph also quotes (its voice and its tag are both `mind_quote`) on the same frame, and a stage 3 Warden's ring has no voice at all, so silencing `mind_adapted` would leave the Warden mute. The three `mind_*` cues take a 600 ms gap instead: whichever quote lands first sounds, once per adaptation. Before this, the hand played it twice at once. Serves 5 and 6.
6. **Ancients have their own voice.** The Fleet growl skips every ancient kind, including a Warden (roster faction). `pack_arrived` has its own listener and plays the faction's roster `arrival`: the Fleet stamp, the reactor's Geiger tick, the swarm lock, or the Mind quoting your motif. A swarm guard rack no longer growls once per drone. Serves 6.
7. **Moss babbles.** `audio.speak(kind, text, beat)` bakes `SfxBank.babble` in the live key, once per line, beat and key, and plays it on the SFX bus at the babble trim with one voice. A new line cuts the last. Talk presses climb beats 1 to 6. The relent is the sigh, and the restart line and every bark (greeting, buy, reroll, sell, refusal, send-off) are level. The babble replaces `moss_beat`'s and `moss_relent`'s stand-ins, as their `sound_next` rows say. A 12-word line babbles about 4 s, inside the bubble's 5.2 s hold. The first bake of a line costs up to 43 ms on the main thread (measured over all 38 lines), in the bay where nothing is moving. Serves 6.
8. **The bench and the bell.** The loot rows named `workbench_clank`, but the bank bakes `bench_clank`, so the row now says `bench_clank`. One name, and no map to keep. A purchase rings `shop_bell` through the same resolution. Serves 6.
9. **The HUD plays the class tune first.** The ability bar plays `ability_ready` once when a swell starts, and its row resolves to `<motif>_ready`, so the ready pulse is the class's first note. The first-run callouts play their row's `callout_chime` as they appear. Both reach the audio director through the game the HUD already holds. Serves 5 and 7.
10. **Baked up front.** The startup bake now covers every recipe in the bank, so a resolved cue never bakes mid-fight on the main thread. Serves 1.

`audio_cue_resolution_test` checks, from the cue log, that each class hears its own release, ready pulse, break-free and Mind quote (and the quote once), each hold kind its loop, the shop bell and the bench clank, no ancient growl and each faction's arrival, a non-silent cached babble, and the callout chime.

## v3 W11 juice

Playtest entry 8 found the loop flat: on the Warden Andrew "was just going around in circles and I don't know if I was dodging". W11 gives every loop the same universal feel before the redesign round judges fun: hits read, kills pop, pickups feel greedy, a dodge says so and a hit taken is unmistakable. Andrew's follow-up asked for knockback by weapon class and weight on his own actions ("visceral feedback"). How hard each fact lands is one table, `EventKinds.JUICE`, keyed by the fact's `ROWS` kind, so its stop, shake, particles and streak window sit beside its colour, shape and sound. Principles are entry 6's (1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing).

1. **Every landed hit stops, and the old break-through-only rule is retired.** Writer C's policy stopped only on a plated break-through, 30 to 50 ms, 400 ms apart, and never on a weak point or a crush. W11's brief asks for a stop on each landed hit, scaled by damage, with crits and weak points stronger. Stops are base 10 ms plus 0.4 ms per damage point, clamped to 12 to 40 ms. Measured bands (damage to ms): 0 to 5 is 12, 10 is 14, 20 is 18, 40 is 26, 60 is 34, and 80 or more is 40. A crit or a weak point (a part softer than the body) multiplies by 1.4. A ram, stomp or slam adds 10. Nothing passes the spine's 60 ms cap. A deflected hit clanks and never stops. `presentation_hitstop_test`, `presentation_juice_test` and `presentation_retry_test` now assert this policy in place of the old one. Serves 1 and 7.
2. **Rapid fire ticks, it never stutters.** The hits of one frame coalesce into one stop: the largest band plus 2 ms per extra hit, up to five. Every stop, whether from a hit, a dodge, a multi-kill, a weak point or the unseal, draws on one shared budget. A stop starts at least 90 ms after the previous one ended, at most 60 ms of stop falls in any 300 ms, and at most 100 ms in any second. A stop over a budget is trimmed, down to 8 ms, or dropped. Measured: 30 hits in a second send 5 stops totalling 100 ms. A 60 ms crit ram, a dodge tick 50 ms later and a multi-kill beat 100 ms later send one 60 ms stop. By that budget a Katamari crush through ten chasers freezes at most a tenth of every second. Serves 1.
3. **A stop never eats speed.** Hit-stop is `Engine.time_scale` through the spine, which scales time and never writes velocity. Measured on a ball rolling at 15 m/s: across a real 60 ms stop the planar velocity drifts 0.004 m/s, and only gravity moves y. Every kick below is camera and shell only. `combat_knockback_test` fires a shot, a throw, a blast 1.5 m away, a ram, a rebound, a dodge and a hit taken in one frame, and the ball's velocity is bit-identical before and after. The rocket jump stays the only feedback that moves the ball. Serves 1.
4. **Hits read.** Every landed hit bursts 8 sparks in the `enemy_struck` copper, and a crit bursts 16 in the weak-point brass with a bright `crit_ping` over the thwack. The enemy's own white flash (enemy.gd) stays the target read. A ram, stomp, slam or bowling rebound is the heavy thump: a camera kick of 0.45 m (0.55 for a rebound) along the ball's travel, 12 to 14 dust puffs at the contact and a shell squash into the hit. Serves 1 and 5.
5. **Kills pop, chains climb.** Each kill gets a pop of 14 and a debris burst of 12. The kill chain and the pickup streak are one record each (`streak.gd`, windows 1500 ms and 700 ms from the JUICE rows), which audio bumps as it plays the pentatonic rung and juice reads at the end of the frame. Hearing and seeing never count differently. From the second kill, pips orbit over the ball, one per kill up to 8, warming from copper to amber to white at the top rung, and they vanish when the window lapses. A group of kills inside 90 ms (one ram or one blast) gets an extra beat at 2, 4 and 8: a 30 ms stop, a zoom-in, a ring and the pop an octave up. Serves 6.
6. **Pickups feel greedy.** A caught piece of scrap now swings in on a curve. It moves sideways at 0.6 and upward at 0.3 of its homing speed while far, and straight in as it closes. The homing term is unchanged, so the pull should take about as long as it did (not timed here). Each piece's tick climbs the pentatonic across the streak (measured 0, 3, 5, 7, 10, 12 semitones for six pieces 60 ms apart). Each piece also kicks the shell's spring outward, harder as the streak climbs, which reads as a heft pulse. A part throws a fountain of 18 in its element's colour (frost blue, arc teal, kinetic bone), else its rarity's, so what it will do flares before its name is read. A core throws 24 in arc. Serves 4.
7. **The dodge says so.** Combat's watch (`near_miss.gd`) publishes `near_miss` when the ball stood in a committed threat while it warned and was outside when it landed, or left within 250 ms unhurt. It reads each threat's own `contains()`, the test its damage uses, so it cannot disagree with a hit. The answer is a 45 ms slow-mo tick, a 5-degree zoom-in, an amber chevron over the ball with a ring under it, and the `near_whoosh` (air rushing past, then a bell on the fifth). A combat WAVE telegraph's `contains()` is only its front, so jumping a wave front is not yet a near miss. Serves 1 and 3.
8. **A hit taken is unmistakable.** The screen edge toward the attacker flushes in HAZARD at 0.4 to 0.65 alpha for 0.4 s. The direction comes from `ctx.spawner.last_player_hit`, where combat records the shove it applied. A hit with no source lights every edge. The camera shakes at 0.25 to 0.6, and a scrap loss pops the shell inward. The edge flash draws four gradient bands with no texture or material. Serves 1.
9. **Knockback by weapon class, as data.** Guns nudge, bombs throw and rams push, from the rows that already hold each weapon and ability. Rapid laser knock went from 4 to 3, the arc caster from 7 to 5, the pylon turret from 9 to 6 and the sticky charge from 10 to 26. `BURST_KNOCK` went from 16 to 24, the detonation line has its own `LINE_KNOCK` of 28 (was half of overdrive, 17), overdrive stays 34, and the ram's knock is `Augments.RAM_KNOCK` 24 (was a literal 18). The enemy's `weight` still divides every shove, so plated and heavy units resist as before. Measured on chasers at 12 damage, in m/s: laser 2.9, arc 4.9, turret 5.8, sticky 25.2, discharge 23.3, line 27.2, overdrive 33.0, ram 23.3. Serves 5.
10. **Your own shots have weight.** Each weapon's shot kicks the camera back along the aim and squashes the shell with it. The kick and squash per weapon are laser 0.05 and 0.04, arc 0.12 and 0.08, turret 0.08 and none, sticky 0.16 and 0.12, in `JUICE.shot_fired`. A sticky throw adds a whoosh, and the charge biting in pops 8 sparks and a clink (the charge manager's own `stuck` signal). A detonation flashes, rings out to 9 m, throws 12 debris and 14 dust, shakes at 0.35, and punches the lens 3 degrees plus 1.5 per enemy caught, up to 10. A blast within 6 m of the ball kicks the camera 0.6 m away from it. The bomb's sound is `kest_kick_thump`, which the row already named for W5: a kick, Kest's tom landing on D (the motif's last note), a bright crack and a falling sub. It is now the heaviest cue in the kit. Serves 5 and 6.
11. **Comfort.** The pause menu gains Screen shake and Hit-stop rows (the volume row's pattern, their own glyphs), saved in `settings.json` under `comfort`. Screen shake scales every camera shake, kick and punch, the camera's own landing shake included. Hit-stop scales every freeze and the dodge tick. At 0 both are off. The menu is 60 px wider so "Screen shake" fits. Serves 3.
12. **Cost.** All particles come from pools built at setup, with one mesh and one material per burst kind. A burst sets only its emitter's colour, position and count, and no hit creates a material. The pools (emitters times the most particles each) are spark 10 x 16, pop 6 x 14, debris 6 x 12, dust 4 x 14 and fountain 3 x 24. That caps live burst particles at 444, on top of the 6 x 20 scrap sparkles. At most 6 bursts per kind start in one frame, whatever a blast catches. The chain pips are 8 meshes sharing one material. The p99 frame time at 1080p is not measured here, because headless tests do not render. Andrew's play is the check.

`presentation_hitstop_test`, `presentation_near_miss_test`, `presentation_chain_test`, `presentation_comfort_test` and `combat_knockback_test` cover notes 1 to 3, 5 to 7, 9 and 11.

## v3 L3 interact and interactables

L3 builds stage 1 of `design-v3/LOOP-REDESIGN.md` section 9: one key for travel, opt-in fights, treasure and risk bets. Andrew chose E everywhere (section 6). Principles are entry 6's (1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing). CONTRACTS "Interact seam (v3 L3)" is the reading guide.

1. **E asks the world first, but never steals a move.** Before Sable's mount reads a press, the player asks `interact.claims()`. The interactable E has locked takes the press; otherwise E plants the pylon as before. Three cases keep the mount (Codex review finding 5). A move already under way keeps E: the hook in, a slam plant, the slingshot drawing. A lock farther than 6 m yields to a face she could mount, so a device down the line never steals an escape off the wall beside you. Mounted, E still drops off. Only one glyph shows at a time: the pylon glyph shows only when E would mount. Demand: every family below. Serves 5, and 7 (the mount and the interact key share one key without a mode).
2. **Five families, one row each.** `interactables.gd` gives each family an icon, shape, colour, consent, verb and sound register. The prompt, the ground ring, the VFX glyph and the cue all read that row. Travel is amber `obj_launch`. Fights are HAZARD `thr_instant`. Secrets are verdigris `obj_vault`. Allies are ancient blue `cat_defense`. Bets are copper `trg_timed`. The ally row exists so the second sitting only adds a device. Demand: none on its own. Serves 7.
3. **Three consents, chosen by cost, locked early.** Travel arms on E in its lane and fires at the mouth. Anything with a cost or a risk takes a 0.35 s hold that fills the class ring round the reticle, so a tap never fires. A free chest takes a press. The first cut used a 6 m reach, which the Codex review (finding 5) showed demands a stop: at 40 m/s a 12 m pass lasts 0.3 s. Now E locks the nearest ready device within 18 m inside the 60 degree look cone. The lock holds until the device is 24 m away, facing or not, so a hold may finish after the ball has passed it. In the lab a hold probe locked 10 m ahead at 40 m/s fired with the ball 4.1 m past it, still at 39 m/s. Demand: the ambush, the cache and the bet. Serves 1: no consent asks the ball to stop.
4. **Roll-through is a model, not a device.** `RollThrough` tests the mouth crossing on the tick's segment, so a 40 m/s ball that skips past the plane between ticks still fires. Leaving the lane or crossing wide disarms it and costs nothing. `interact.travel(...)` wraps it around the owner's launch callable, and the lift adopts it that way (CONTRACTS).
