A lab lane at 30 m/s fired with the ball at 29.4 m/s. Demand: the return (the lift, writer L1). Serves 1.
5. **The prompt grammar is one function.** `device.show_prompt()` gives every device the same three states. From 30 m it is the glyph alone. Locked and ready, it is the E keycap, the glyph and at most five words, so a cost or a stake is on screen before any hold starts. When not ready it is the gauge round the glyph and no keycap. `Interactables.prompt()` cuts at five words, so no device can break the rule. Word counts: "Fight: core in 30 s" 5, "Open: three parts" 3, "Double: 2 cores or none" 5 (5 at every core count), "Ride: to the shelf" 4. The fight budget test still reads 12 of 25. Demand: anticipation (section 8). Serves 3.
6. **The ambush altar sits beside the east chute's head.** It is a copper post with a HAZARD-striped cap in a HAZARD ring. A held E brings a pack of two chasers and a lobber round the ring at once. Clearing it within 30 s pays one bonus core; a clock that runs out pays nothing. While it runs, its gauge is the clock. The post has no collider and the pack comes to you. It is spent after one run per stage. Demand: cores (the gate tier, and the window per core in option A). Serves 1 and 4.
7. **The cracked wall's gauge is your speed.** The pocket behind the vault plinth is verdigris concrete with a charcoal groove, and its open side is walled off by a cracked face. The wall crumbles the tick before contact at 18 m/s, or at 8 m/s while sprinting, or when a smash lands within 7 m of it. Slower, it is a wall. While sealed, the chest's gauge reads your approach speed over 18 m/s, so the ring filling tells you what the wall wants without a word of text. The plinth's slope runs down at the wall, so the vault's own mound is the run-up. Inside, E opens the chest, the one stop on the map. Three parts rise along the back wall, one from the chest itself, clear of the ball that stopped to open it. Rolling onto one takes it as a `cache` part and the others sink. A lab ball crumbled it at 24 m/s and kept 24 m/s; at 10 m/s it held. Demand: the vault. Serves 1 (the only stop is chosen) and 4.
8. **Double or nothing on the bowl's south bank.** Section 9 puts the crate "at the bay's cradle". The cradle docks only after the break, when the gate tier is already rolled and the stage's cores are spent. A bet there would stake cores that buy nothing. So the crate stands on the bank the loop leaves the bowl by, where the section 11 storyboard puts the gates and the cradle, and it is in play before the break. It is a copper crate with a HAZARD stripe and a dial on a post, half mint and half rust. A held E stakes every core you carry. The drum roll (armed) plays while the needle spins 0.9 s, the clunk (fired) lands as it stops on its half, and then, 0.35 s later, the crit crack of a doubling or the dry refusal (`womp`) of a burn (`bet_resolved`). It takes one committed wager per stage, then it is spent; the stake is on the locked prompt before the hold (Codex review finding 7). With no core it shows the empty dial and no keycap. Demand: cores. Serves 4, and 6 (the result lands as three beats). This is a spec call the coordinator may overrule; the crate is one `PLACEMENTS` row.
9. **Luck replays, and a restart cannot fish.** `game.gd` seeds every run with one constant, so luck drawn from the run's stream alone would land the same way on every run. `interact.luck(device_id)` seeds each device's one stream from the run's seed, the run's id, the stage and the device. A stage restart replays the same roll, so restarting to re-bet lands the same way and fishing pays nothing (Codex review finding 7). Another run rolls differently. The run log keeps `run_id`, so a replay that restores it matches. `ctx.rng` is never touched, so a bet cannot shift the gates' rolls. An earlier cut seeded from the retry count and the stage tick; that let a restart re-roll, so it went. Across 10 run ids the crate landed 8 heads and 2 tails; the test plays both in the game. Demand: none. Serves 2.
10. **Nothing new on the map files.** Placements live in `Interactables.PLACEMENTS`. `map_spillway.gd`, `stage_map.gd`, `flow.gd`, `warden.gd` and `tuning.gd` are untouched, and writer L1 owns the lift and the kicker lips. The spine gained a run mutator (`burn_cores`), two intent fields (`mount_held`, `interact_pressed`) and one player hook (`interact_claim`), and the pylon's face test moved from `key_glyphs.gd` onto the player (`face_in_reach()`) so the glyph and the routing share it. The field notes and help page are writer W12's, so their E row still names only the pylon; the README row names both. Demand: none. Serves 7.
11. **Costs never shrink the ball.** Each family row names its `cost`: none, cores, or wallet scrap. Allies charge scrap through `run.spend_scrap()`, which keeps mass, so paying for a drone never lowers heft (standing principle 10; Codex review finding 10, which caught section 9's "lowers heft" wording). A future "call the Warden" must keep a real cost when the clock is already at its cap. Travel devices give back banked speed through `RideRail`, never a fixed 85%. Demand: allies and the return. Serves 4 and 1.

**Checks, and what they do not cover.** `world_interact_test`, `world_ambush_test`, `world_cache_test` and `world_bet_test` are headless automated play in the real game on stage 1. They cannot say whether a 0.35 s hold feels right at speed, or whether the dial reads from the bank. That is Andrew's sitting. Presentation is stand-in cues and glyphs only; juice is W11's. Next sitting: the allies, which need the drone's brain; the challenge post and the Warden call (fights); the cursed cache and the timed trial (bets).

## v3 L2 laps mode

Option C of `design-v3/LOOP-REDESIGN.md` on stage 1, behind `--loop=laps` (or the title's L row, or the F1 panel then hold R). Base is L1's lab switch at 3c219ab. Demands served: the Warden (why stay) and the stage (the map as a course). Principles strained: momentum, at the landing turn, and the speedrun test, since the laps are a fixed tax.

1. **The bell and the course.** `src/world/lap_course.gd` is a pure model. It reads L1's loop fields and lays five checkpoint rings, which are the surge run's gate ring (`event_view.gd gate_ring`, now shared). The rings sit at the approach to the return lift in the bowl, 8 m past the lift's landing on the ride's own heading, the fast drop line's start, its first bend, and its kicker lip. A `route.gd` loop through them sets which way each ring faces. Rolling into the Warden's ring rings the bell and lights the course. The rings are taken in order and are roll-through: a 9 m reach, any speed, no stop. The rings and the beacon's three notch collars stand from the first frame, dim bone before the bell, so both read from the drop deck. Serves the stage.
2. **The gate.** Flow reads `LoopMode.runs_laps` once at stage entry and puts the Warden to sleep. `warden.gd` holds only the dormant gate and the strip. Asleep, it neither pulses nor opens, every hit meets 999 plating, and `take_damage` refuses the chip. `plating_now()` reads 0 while asleep, so combat's shred never builds on it and it wakes whole. It wakes on the third lap (`warden_engaged`, the v2 theme cue). Each lap past the third takes a third of the stage's plating off: 45, then 30, 15 and 0. Awake, it is the v2 Warden. Section 2's readable windows come from L1's drop-branch `warden.gd` after the merge; this gate sits in front of that code, so the laps fight gets those windows without any laps code of its own. Serves the Warden.
3. **The clock per lap.** `director.gd` gains `laps` and `step_lap()`. Each lap is one reinforcement step (a `PAST_BREAK_STEP` of pack share) and one announced rise. Every rise now reads the level (ticks plus laps), so a later clock tick never announces a lower number than a lap did. In v2, `laps` stays 0 and nothing changes.
4. **Facts.** There are four new bus signals with rows: `lap_bell_rung`, `lap_ring_passed`, `lap_completed` and `warden_stripped`. Audio and VFX each hear them in small separate listeners. Checkpoint pops climb the reward ladder one rung per ring. The ring that closes a lap, the beacon's notch and the director's `threat_rose` stinger all land on the same next beat; the test checks that each lap and its rise publish on the same tick. The objective reads `Ring the Warden's bell`, then `Lap the course: 1/3` on the HUD. The long line is "Lap the lit course: 1 of 3. The Warden wakes at 3."
5. **Bots.** The skipper is the camper: it rings the bell and rams the sleeping Warden. A new `lapper` policy laps (`laps_wanted`, default 3), then fights. The completionist laps once its loot is done. Writer L1 is adding a lapper for the drop lines, so the two need merging. `bot_run.gd` gains `--stages=N`.

**Measured.** Method: `godot --headless --fixed-fps 90 --path sketches/core-loop --script res://tools/bot_run.gd -- --out=<dir> --policy=<p> --seeds=1,2,3 --loop=laps --stages=1`, one seed per class, on commit 7844508. Runs are in `artifacts/local/l2-laps/head-7844508/` (git-ignored). These are bot runs, not play. A rerun reproduced the bell, lap and wake times; the break times moved by up to 5 s against the earlier pass, so read them as plus or minus 5 s.

| Policy | Class | Bell (s) | Lap times (s) | Warden wakes (s) | Warden breaks (s) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| camper (skipper) | runner_gunner | 5.0 | none | never, stalled at 240 | never |
| camper (skipper) | demolitions_caster | 5.0 | none | never, stalled at 240 | never |
| camper (skipper) | pylon_engineer | 5.0 | none | never, stalled at 240 | never |
| lapper | runner_gunner | 5.0 | 11.1, 12.3, 12.5 | 40.9 | 66.5 |
| lapper | demolitions_caster | 5.0 | 11.1, 14.7, 12.6 | 43.4 | 94.1 |
| lapper | pylon_engineer | 5.0 | 11.1, 12.5, 11.7 | 40.4 | 79.8 |
| completionist | runner_gunner | 35.3 | 37.1*, 12.2, 13.1 | 97.7 | 117.7 |
| completionist | demolitions_caster | 33.8 | 60.8*, 12.1, 12.5 | 119.2 | 153.5 |
| completionist | pylon_engineer | 31.2 | 45.2*, 12.5, 12.0 | 100.9 | 134.3 |

\* The first lap runs from the bell and includes the completionist's vault and event.

The lapper was re-measured at integration head ac0311e, after the L1 merge and the lip-aim fix, with the same method (seeds 1 to 3, runs in `artifacts/local/fix1-laps/`). The Warden breaks at 67.6 s (runner_gunner), 76.9 s (demolitions_caster) and 73.4 s (pylon_engineer). The last two are faster than the table above, so read the table as the pre-merge figures.

**Reads.**

- A lap takes 11 to 15 s for every class. That is half the 25 to 40 s the spec budgets. The three-lap tax is 35 to 38 s, and the classes do not separate on the course.
- The camper cannot win, and the completionist wakes it 2.4 to 2.7 times later than the lapper. The lapper breaks it at 66 to 94 s, inside section 4's 60 to 120 s band. The spec's own risk holds, though: the laps are a fixed tax the skilled player cannot shorten.
- The lift's landing throws the ball north at 30 m/s, and the course then turns about 90 degrees west toward the chute. That turn is where the lap loses its speed: in the traced lapper run the ball fell to 15 to 18 m/s there. It is L1's return geometry, and it is worth a look before the sitting.
- Ringing the bell puts flow in its beacon state, so v2's beacon spike (+2 tiers) is on for the whole of the lapping. That is faithful to Risk of Rain's charge zone and to v2, but it is a knob to question.
- Reading taken: the spec says both that extra laps "before waking" strip plating and that the Warden "wakes at full charge". Here it wakes on lap 3, and any later lap still strips. The course runs through the bowl, so it could not stay asleep for a fourth lap anyway. You fight it or leave it to lap again.
- Visible from the drop: before the bell, the five rings and three collars stand, and the terrain blocks no sight line from the stage's drop point to any ring's top or to the top collar; the farthest is 176 m. That is a height-field sight line, not a render. Fog, the camera's heading and whether dim bone reads against the sand still need Andrew's eye.

`tests/loop_laps_test.gd` covers the camper, the course standing before the bell with clear sight lines from the drop, the lift's banked speed, the wake, the strip, ring order, the director per lap and v2 untouched.

## v3 L1 fun lab and drop mode

The fun lab's `drop` mode on stage 1 (design-v3/LOOP-REDESIGN.md sections 1, 2 and 7, Andrew's section 6 answers), built against the independent review's findings 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 and 11 (research/CODEX-REVIEW-1.md on the design branch). The Warden takes damage only from a ball that comes off the map onto its open core, and the gun's part is the lab toggle. `v2` builds no drop rules, so the v2 fight and its tests are unchanged. Launch with `scrapkeep core-loop-v3 --loop=drop --gun=hold` or `--gun=full`, or flip the title row's L and G. Principles are entry 6's (1 momentum, 2 restart, 3 layers, 4 growth, 5 classes, 6 music, 7 harmonizing).

1. **One physical hit predicate.** `warden_drop.gd` judges every ball contact (stomp, ram, slam) once. A **drop** came off the map (beyond 50 m of the mast within the last 6 s), arrives descending at 2 m/s or faster with its centre no more than 1 m under the core's top and within 3.84 m of the mast (the drawn 3.2 m core plus 20%), at 20 m/s or more, into an open window that has not had its drop. A **miss** came off the map but failed any other test. It deals nothing, and the core throws the ball at the return lift. A **floor** contact is anything from inside the bowl, or within 1 s of the last drop or miss. It deals nothing and throws nothing. Pulse-lift slams, lift camping, nearest-lip repetition from the bowl floor and rebound chains all land on floor, so bowl shortcuts count for nothing (answer 2a). A slam's dive counts only its planar speed. Serves 1.
2. **A drop's damage is truthful.** A segment's HP (max HP over the stage's `warden_drops`, 4 on stage 1) times heft times arrival speed over 25 m/s. The badge draws whole chunks from the Warden's real HP (`segments_left()`), and the objective line promises no count ("Drop on the Warden from a chute."). A fast drop can take more than one chunk. Serves 4.
3. **A timing contract for the first drop.** In drop mode the pulse cycle runs from the stage's start whether or not the ball is in reach. There is no four-second residence. The next opening is a committed schedule (`next_pulse_in()`, `open_at(t)`), drawn as a 12-pip dial over the Warden that fills toward the next opening and gives way to the beam. On a drop line's approach and in the air off its lip, a landing ring stands on the core's top: amber if the schedule says the core will be open at the arc's eta, HAZARD if shut, none if the arc falls short. `warden_window_test` checks 169 of 169 schedule lookups made 0.5 to 6 s ahead against what then happened. A 32 m/s ball let go on the west run-up when the ring turned amber landed its first drop 1.02 s later. Serves 1.
4. **The lip catches a hopping ball.** The predecessor's lapper landed 1 or 2 drops against 15 to 17 misses in 240 s (3 seeds, hold). The ball hopped the chute's foot, struck the lip's ramp face in the air, lost a third of its planar speed (29.7 to 19 m/s) and fell short, because the lip aimed only a ball leaving the ground within 3 m of it. A live lip now also aims a ball crossing its whole 10 m run-up in the air, once per flight, while it skims within 3.5 m of the ramp. The same schedule-reading lapper now lands every descent (0 misses in 6 seeds per role). A ball too slow for any arc to reach the mast (about 22 m/s at gravity 26 over 18 m) is still not aimed and falls short. Serves 1.
5. **Miss recovery is one lift ride.** A miss throws the ball at the return lift's mouth. The blind lapper's miss-to-next-descent time is 8.4 s median (7.8 to 8.7 s over 137 misses). Most of it is the lift's 7.3 s arc to the top shelf. Serves 1 and 2.
6. **`hold`: the gun holds the door, and its share rides the drop.** Section 1 says weapon hits "deal a small share"; the same section says holding fire from the bowl "only extends windows nobody is dropping through", and section 4 requires the camper to deal 0. Both hold if the share is banked. A weapon hit on the open core adds 0.1 s to the window, up to 2 s shared by every weapon for that opening, and banks 2.5% of its punch. The bank lands with that window's drop and lapses when the window closes without one. A landed drop shuts the window. The camper's lapsed bank was about 88 HP over 240 s (87.8 and 88.7 in two runs). Serves 5 and 7.
7. **`full`: a drop staggers, then the gun is the hammer** (review finding 11). A landed drop deals 75% of its hold value, and it stands the core open for a 6 s stagger (plus 1 s per bonus core). Every weapon hit on the Warden deals its full punch during the stagger and nothing outside one, including in an ordinary pulse window. A ball that never drops cannot open a stagger. The rebound throws the ball up the bank a quarter-turn from the lift (36 m out), so the stagger is gunned from the bank before the ride. Serves 5.
8. **Three reads, three owners.** Open is the beam: a class-colour column standing on the core, 6 m tall per second left, so it only shrinks, and a held door or a bonus core reads as a taller beam. A dodge is bone and an arrow on the bus (`warden_pulse_dodged`), never the class colour or the core shape. A near-miss (in the core ring during the telegraph, out within 0.3 s of the clang) gives the air jump back and plays the rising whip with a streak. A safe resolution (out earlier) plays a quiet tick and flashes a hollow bone ring at the core ring's edge, with no bonus. Progress is whole chunks popping off the badge. A pulse throws and burns only a ball on the floor; a ball in the air is on its drop arc. Serves 3 and 7.
9. **The landing's juice.** 60 ms of hit-stop, an FOV punch scaled by speed (6 to 14), a slate chunk off the core tumbling to the floor, the badge's chunk popping outward, the reward ladder one pentatonic rung per chunk lost, and the rebound snap pitched by arrival speed over 25 m/s. In any loop mode, `game.gd` refuses a stop that would take more than 120 ms of stops inside one second (the wall clock in play, physics ticks under `tools/bot_run.gd`; see SimClock below). Four back-to-back 60 ms stops spend 120 ms, and two are refused. v2 keeps its per-stop cap alone. The break reward hangs caged over the mast, built from `reward_gates.gd`'s topper frames, one more frame per bonus core, and drops when the Warden breaks. Serves 1 and 6.
10. **States for the look.** `warden_state_changed` publishes closed, telegraph, open, recover (0.4 s after a window) and punish (a 5 s burning crater where a floor ball sat through the pulse, 6 damage once, audited INSTANT in `threat_sites_test`). `warden_look.gd` is the visual track's, so the dome halves opening and closing on these states are theirs to dress. Nothing in the look was touched. Serves 7.
11. **The rebound's collision exception.** The drop's throw lets the ball pass the core for 0.35 s. Reading the ball's exception list to check it hit freed enemies a crush had excepted (a Jolt "body is null" error). The rule now tracks its own exception with a flag.

**Measured, stage 1, 6 seeds a role (3 for the camper), `bot_run --until=1`.** Logs are under `artifacts/local/l1/measure/m5/`.

| Policy, role | Warden broken at | Drops landed | Descents missed | Windows before the break | Drop share of damage | Speed after a drop (0.25 s) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| lapper, hold | 33.5 to 47.3 s (5 of 6 at 33.5 to 33.9) | 3 (one seed 4) | 0 | 7 to 10 | 0.99 to 1.00 | 0.85x median, 0.75x min |
| lapper, full | 26.8 to 65.3 s | 2 to 4 | 0 | 4 to 13 | 0.55 to 0.93; all gun damage inside staggers | 0.84x median, 0.75x min |
| lapper_blind, hold | 100 to 182 s | 3 | 6 to 13 (25% landed) | 17 to 32 | 0.98 to 1.00 | 0.74x median, 0.71x min |
| lapper_blind, full | 167 to 202 s, 1 of 6 unbroken at 240 s | 2 to 3 | 12 to 17 (13% landed) | 27 to 41 | 0.60 to 0.92 | 0.76x median, 0.72x min |
| camper (skipper), hold | unbroken, 0 damage in 240 s (2 seeds; 1 died at 144 s, also 0) | 0 | | 23 to 39 | | |
| camper (skipper), full | unbroken, 0 damage, 0 staggers in 240 s (2 seeds; 1 died at 138 s, also 0) | 0 | | 22 to 39 | | |
| v2 skipper (control) | 25.8 to 66.9 s | | | | | |

`loop_camper_test` gives the camper HP to last and measures 240 s under hold and 80 s under full at 4x, with the physics tick raised to match (the smoke run's 120 s per-test limit; on a loaded machine the full span stops at 100 s of wall time once it has camped 30 s). Both deal 0.

**What the numbers say, not tuned.**
- A hold lapper breaks the Warden in about 34 s with 3 drops. That is under section 4's 60 to 120 s band and fewer than the badge's 4 chunks, because lap drops arrive at 33 m/s against the 25 m/s reference. Raising `DROP_REF_SPEED` to about 33 would make a typical drop one chunk. Review finding 4's "trial two successful drops first" points the other way. It is Andrew's call after play.
- A blind lapper's lap (about 12.7 s) is close to two 6 s cycles, so it arrives at nearly the same phase every lap and repeats its miss. A player who ignores the dial will feel this; one who reads it lands every time. Jittering the cycle or reading the dial in the lab sitting decides whether it matters.
- `full` is class-shaped. Rivet's gun took 42 to 45% of the Warden in the staggers, Sable's arc 36 to 41%, and Kest's sticky 8 to 10%.

**Left for later.** The `laps` mode. Drop lines on stages 2 and 3 (a drop-mode run meets v2 Wardens there). A shorter lift arc to cut the 8.4 s recovery. The completionist on the loop and `balance_report.gd` bands for the new policies. Baking the named cues (`held_door_tick`, `drop_segment_pop`, `near_miss_whip_or_safe_tick`), which play stand-ins now. The Warden's per-lap reinforcement step.

**Evidence limit.** Bots and headless tests measure structure: the predicate, the schedule, the reads as nodes and bus facts, the bot's lap times. They say nothing about whether the drop feels good, whether the dial and the landing ring read in moving footage with music, or whether Andrew leaves the bowl on purpose. That is the sitting.

## v3 W13 reward ceremony

Andrew asked on 2026-09-28 for "some sort of ... treasure find ceremony when you find an item", one that "could pause in the moment if ... the area has calmed down after a boss fight", with anticipation built up and paid off by an item that "changes the way they play". W13 presents every `part_gained` at a tier, so a big find feels big and a small one stays out of the way. Each tier's look, sound, timing and pause rule is a `reward_*` row in `EventKinds.LOOT_ROWS`. The tier comes from one accessor, `RewardCeremony.rarity_of(part_id)`, mapped through `EventKinds.REWARD_TIER_BY_RARITY`, so the rarity ladder the Codex design job is writing can remap parts in their rows without touching the ceremony. The ladder already has an `evolution` rung, which is a ceremony wherever it drops. Principles are entry 6's.

| Tier | When | Pauses | What you see | What you hear | Timing |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| common | a common or uncommon part found before | never | W11's fountain and glyph pop | `rarity_tick` on the part's rung | instant |
| rare | a rare part found before | never | a 4.5 m ring on the ground and a star over the ball, both in the rarity colour, plus a 45 ms stop from the shared budget | `rarity_bell` on rung 4 | 0.5 s flare |
| reveal | a rare-plus part, any first find, a completed synergy, or a ceremony that never found its calm | never | a salvaged plate card on the left of the toasts band, with the glyph socket, the name, at most 6 words of the verb, a stamped tag and, for a synergy, a wire to the partner. Rare and up also flare. | `reveal_clank` (a plate on the bench, then a fifth), plus `synergy_hit` when the wire snaps | 0.22 s in, 2.4 s hold, 0.25 s out, with the wire snapping over 0.18 s |
| ceremony | sources `gate` (the Warden-break reward), `boss`, `vault`, `cache`, `cage`, `warden` and `evolution`, or the evolution rung | only when calm | a veil, then a crate that drops in, shakes and cracks in the class colour over a growing halo. The lid bursts with a flash, 14 rays, 32 bits and the lid flying off. The card rises and the hull strip seats the part in the last slot. | `ceremony_build` (16 steps: toms closing in, a snare roll and bells climbing the pentatonic), then `ceremony_resolve` (a D chord, then A, B and C walking up to the home D) | 0.25 s in and 1.4 s build, so the burst is at 1.65 s. The reveal holds 1.8 s, and the world resumes at 3.45 s as a 0.25 s fade runs. |
| payoff | the first effect of a revealed or ceremonial part after it lands | never | the part's glyph pops over the ball in a socket, with a class-colour ring on screen and on the ground | `rarity_bell` on the part's own rung, the bell it arrived with | 0.7 s |

1. **Calm is measured, not guessed.** The tree pick has no calm test of its own, because it opens only at checkpoints, so the ceremony defines one. It needs play or the victory roll (not the launch), nothing already holding the tree (a pause, the notes, the workshop's `menu_held`), a ball that is not frozen, and the ball on the ground or moving vertically under 4 m/s. No committed threat may be within 30 m and no live enemy within 24 m. That calm must hold for 0.4 s. A find that is not calm waits and plays at the next calm. After 10 s it plays in the flow as a reveal card. A gate's part is gained mid-launch, so the Warden's reward plays on the landing in the next stage when that landing is calm. Brindle's crest may play in the victory roll. The pause should also hold back the summary's countdown, which is not measured here. Serves the Warden, vault and boss demands. It strains principle 1, and notes 2 to 4 are how it keeps momentum.
2. **Momentum is kept exactly.** The pause is the tree pause the pick uses. Velocity is captured when it begins and handed back if anything moved it. `presentation_ceremony_test` measures the body's and the state's velocity bit-identical across the pause (4.0, -2.4, 5.0 m/s on the slope). The world resumes as the overlay starts its fade, never after it. Serves 1.
3. **Skippable and short.** Any key, click or pad button during the build jumps to the burst, and a second one closes. The pause menu's `Ceremonies` row (C) flips between full and short, where every find plays at the rare tier with no pause. The setting is saved in `settings.json` as the comfort level `ceremony` (1 full, 0 short), written by the pause menu's store, so its volumes and this row never overwrite each other. Serves 1 and 3.
4. **Only rare and up stop the play.** A common first find or an uncommon synergy gets its card and a tick, but no flare and no stop. The first version flared every card, and `ui_word_budget_test` measured the difference. Boost Coil's first-find stop slowed the tick-counted fight, which left the ball inside a fabricator's reach. That fabricator prompt is 15 words on its own, so the fight read 33 words. The stop now follows rarity (`RewardCeremony.flares`). The budget test now presents a first-find rare flare and card two ticks before its count and reads 20 of 25. A fabricator prompt in a fight is still enough to break the budget on its own, which W13 does not fix. Serves 1 and 3.
5. **The card is salvage, the information is crisp.** The plate has a jittered chamfered outline, a rarity paint stripe, four rivets, a weld tack line and a tilted stamped tag, and the glyph, name and verb stay sharp. The stamp is NEW on a first common, uncommon or rare find, and otherwise the rarity (RARE+, EVOLVED). Rare-plus is amber with a mint heart in its socket. `scrap_frame.gd` is not on this base (W12 builds it), so `reward_view.gd` draws the plate inline in `_plate()`, which should adopt `scrap_frame` after the merge. The card rests left of the HUD's toast column, mirroring the discovery banner on the right, and drops under the band on a screen too narrow for it (1280 x 720). Serves 7.
6. **Builds are teased.** A part that completes a named synergy snaps a class-colour wire along the card's bottom rail to the partner's glyph, which lights up (for example Cross Split after Chain Arc, which completes Storm Lattice). A part that only feeds one draws a dim dashed wire to the partner still out there (Loose Trigger points at Spiral Wave, for Pinwheel), or to a question mark when the part is the synergy's named half. The codex banner names the synergy on the right as before. Serves 4.
7. **The payoff is felt, not read.** When a revealed or ceremonial part lands it is armed with the first fact that will show it at work. A triggered item waits for its own proc (`build.item_procs()`, an existing API, so W13 adds no bus signal). A passive part waits for the fact its knob changes (patterns and fire rate on `shot_fired`, dash parts on `dashed`, slam elements on `slammed`, magnets on `scrap_collected`, punch on `enemy_struck`). It fires once per part per run and never while the find is still waiting or a ceremony is up. Serves 4 and 6.
8. **The sounds are one family, in the live key.** All five cues are `sfx_bank` recipes in `KEYED_SOUNDS`, played through `audio.play`, and rung up the reward ladder by rarity (common 0, uncommon 2, rare 4, rare-plus 5, evolution 7). A skipped build cuts its drum roll (`audio.stop`) so the resolve lands clean. The band keeps playing, softer, through the pause, as it does for the pick. Serves 6.
9. **First finds are per viewer.** A find is first until you have ever found that part. The record is `reward-finds.json` beside the codex (the per-test storage dir in tests). Serves 3.
10. **Test mode never pauses.** Following the workshop's `armed` pattern, `pause_armed` is off in test mode, so the bots and every older test run as before and a ceremony plays as a reveal card. `presentation_ceremony_test` switches it on.

Known limits. No module on this base publishes the `cache`, `cage`, `warden` or `evolution` sources yet, so they are named in `CEREMONY_SOURCES` for L3's interactables and the ladder. W11's swoosh arcs scrap into the ball, but parts still fountain, so W13 adds no arc for a part. The stills are 1920 x 1080 captures from `tools/ceremony_capture.gd`. How long the pause feels, and whether the build is the right length, waits on Andrew's play.

## v3 W12 class cards and field notes

W12 answers playtest entry 8: "I don't know what I'm selecting... what's their offering", "the tab key overwhelms me with like a whole essay of text", and a UI that was "very sparse and tightly knit". The research lead was R1 sections 4 and 5: a card with an ability preview and a difficulty mark, and help drawn as pictures. Principles are entry 6's (1 momentum, 5 classes, 7 harmonizing) and 11 (readability). The new files are `src/ui/class_kit.gd`, `class_preview.gd`, `controls_diagram.gd`, `threat_sheet.gd` and `growth_sheet.gd`. `class_card.gd`, `field_notes.gd` and `help_page.gd` are rewritten or edited, and `tools/alpha_capture.gd` gains `--ui=1`. `screens.gd` is untouched.

1. **One source for what a pilot offers.** `class_kit.gd` reads the class row's four ability ids. Each id names its icon through `ability_state.gd`, each slot names its key, and the movement row's augments name the extra keys: vault on Space and pylon on E for Sable, glide on Space for Kest. It holds only words: two per ability, the DESIGN-V2 fantasy line cut to fit, and a difficulty from 1 to 3 (Rivet 1, Kest 2, Sable 3, by how many things the pilot juggles). Colour and shape stay on the class row. The card and the Tab diagram both read the kit, so a key looks and reads the same on the pick and in play. Serves 5 and 7.
2. **The card at rest is a picture, framed on the visual track's key art.** Storyboards 10 to 12 set the composition, used as direction only; no key-art PNG ships. Each card is a tall portrait. The sphere sits large in the upper part, caught mid-action with the pilot turned three-quarters toward you, over a lit sky that fades into a quiet dark lower region where the words sit. The rest pose is one held frame of the signature move, gently bobbing. Sable hangs high on a taut line from a pylon planted in a sandstone wall. Rivet sprints with the laser firing, dust and chevrons behind it, into a crowd. Kest rides its own amber blast upward over its sticky charges. Below the picture sit the role glyph (the row's shape) and name, the fantasy line, difficulty diamonds, and the keys as ability icons over keycaps: LMB, RMB, F, Ctrl, plus the Space extra. The whole screen reads in 35 words (38 counting the number caps the card draws). Serves 5 and 11.
3. **Hover or focus opens one card.** Hovering takes focus, so exactly one card is open. It lifts 6% from its base, the other two step back and darken (by colour, not alpha, so the world never shows through), and the role line and each key's two words fade in, stacked on two lines so every label in the row sits the same height. The fantasy line reserves two lines on every card, so the three key rows line up. The open screen reads 48 words with Sable open, 46 with Rivet and 48 with Kest, and every added word sits on the open card. That is over the calm budget of 40 on purpose. The extra words are labels under icons, one per key the player is about to press, and they leave when focus leaves. Serves 11.
4. **The live preview is the real shell with a scripted move.** Each card has a SubViewport with its own 3D world, holding the pilot's hero shell (`hero_assets.gd` glb, painted by its own `paint()`, pod facing its heading as `_steer_pod` does). When the card opens, a 3.6 s loop plays from the held frame and the camera trails the sphere about 6 m back, close enough that the pilot stays the subject while the wall, dummies or charges stay in frame. Sable rolls in, plants the wall pylon (mint ring discharge and dust), swings out on the line and launches like a cannonball, trailing rings. Rivet fires as it sprints through three dummies that fly, then blasts. Kest lobs three sticky charges, blinks out in smoke, draws the line, and rides the first detonation up. It then glides down on its vanes, the separate glide and blink reads the key-art critique asked for. The real ball and abilities were the preferred option but need the whole run context (terrain, spawner, run state), and `player_ball.gd` belongs to L3. So the motion is scripted and the model, colours and shapes are the game's. Serves 5.
5. **Keys: mouse and keyboard.** A hover opens a card. The arrows move focus, and with nothing focused the first arrow lands on a card. Enter or Space picks the focused card, and 1, 2 and 3 still pick at once. Serves 1.
6. **Tab is three pictures, then the codex.** The Keys page is the left half of a keyboard and a mouse, with every used key drawn in place and the rest blank. The pilot's own keys light in the class colour with their ability icon and words. Words come from `FieldNotes.CONTROLS` (rows with `caps` and `short`), and the pilot's overrides come from the kit. The What hurts page shows the four threat_grammar types, each as a looping miniature of its telegraph with its `thr_` icon, its THREAT_ROWS shape and two words, then an amber "yours, harmless" tile. The Growth page is a flow: scrap to heft to punch to the Warden, fed by gate parts, Moss and the tree fork. Each node wears its event row's colour and shape, and dots run along the arrows. The codex pages follow unchanged. Serves 7 and 11.
7. **Page row and turning.** The page row is Q, one icon per page (the open one wears its name and an underline), then E. Q and E turn pages. With Tab held the mouse is captured, so left click or wheel down goes to the next page and right click or wheel up to the previous one. When the notes are pinned from the pause menu, hovering a page icon opens it; W10's rule that a click closes pinned notes still holds. The panel now starts below the objective strip that presentation draws on top. Serves 11.
8. **Word counts, measured with `word_budget.gd`.** The Keys page reads 39 words for Sable, 33 for Rivet and 35 for Kest. What hurts reads 14 and Growth 17. The title's Help page, which now draws the same diagram with no pilot lit, reads 36. `ui_field_notes_test` fails any picture page over 40, and `ui_class_card_test` fails a calm class select over 40.
9. **What left the screen.** The Tab essay (TESTING, QUESTIONS, TRY_FIRST, SCREEN_KEYS and the colour legend) is no longer drawn. The constants and `legend_bbcode()` remain because README.md mirrors them and `int_play_text_test` and `int_screen_layout_test` read them. Deleting them is a cleanup call for the owner.
10. **Scrapped together, clean to read.** Andrew's mid-round direction for all UI (design-v3 ROADMAP, "HUD look: scrapped together, clean to read") is built as `src/ui/scrap_frame.gd`, a StyleBox that drops in wherever a StyleBoxFlat was. `plate()` is a cut sheet with uneven corners, a heavier bottom lip, a faint bevel, corner rivets and an optional strip of tape. `tag()` is a small stamped tag with a punched hole, `stamp()` draws words on one for controls that draw their own text, `wear()` shifts a part's value by up to 10% and never its hue, and `draw_tape()` tapes anything else. A serial fixes each frame's quirks, so a card keeps its cuts and tape when it lights up. The class card is a riveted plate taped at the top, its role line sits on a stamped tag, and its caps come from mismatched sets. Its key words stay plain, because five tags in a row read as clutter. Tab's panel is a riveted plate. Each diagram key is a cut plate (no rivets, since its cap owns the corner) with a worn cap, and the mouse is taped across its body. The mouse's words, the threat words and the growth words sit on tags. The threat tiles are riveted, and some are taped. Icons, glyphs, numbers, key letters and the threat miniatures are drawn on top, untouched. The tree pick's cards still use the flat `ClassCard._plate()`, so the HUD v3 wave can adopt the scrap frame there. `ui_scrap_frame_test` holds the colour rule: across 300 worn parts the hue and saturation move 0.0000 and the value at most 10%. It also checks that a tag holds its words. Serves 11.
11. **E is the use key.** L3 made E the universal interact key (lock a device up to 18 m ahead, hold 0.35 s, the lock holds after you pass), with Sable's wall mount as the fallback when nothing is in reach. `FieldNotes.CONTROLS` now gives E caps and the short "use / ride", so every pilot's diagram and the Help page show it. Sable's kit extra on E reads "wall mount". A 1U key has no room for the side-by-side split the Space bar uses, so a narrow key stacks the shared word over the pilot's own, in the class colour at 12 px, with the pylon icon in the corner. The coordinator's example "(mount at a wall)" is 111 px against a 70 px label and would put Sable's page at 41 words. The README's E row matches L3's text word for word, so the two branches merge cleanly.

## v3 AU2 sound bank

The AU2 bank (design-v3/AUDIO-V3.md section 8's AU2 row): bets, secrets, allies, opt-in fights, the escalation's ladder and the return's launch grammar, additive in `sfx_bank.gd`. Every pitched id carries `KEYED_SOUNDS` (`audio.gd`), so a Warden key lift transposes it the way every other motif cue already does; `bet_roll` (percussion), `door_tick` (engine-managed, its own explicit shift) and `lift_hiss` (noise) are the deliberate exceptions. `crater_hold` is a new `hold()` kind, the reactor's own hold, on the family's upper Ab pedal in its Geiger crackle; its length below is `_bake`'s fixed 2-beat sample, and the runtime hold calls `SfxBank.hold("crater", n)` directly at whatever length the punish state needs (5 s per the design). `music_engine.gd`'s `door_tick()` action now always resolves the `door_tick` id (AU1 had left a class-ready fallback for the id that did not exist yet; that branch is gone, nothing else in the file changed). `kill_chain_tier` and `drop_landing` are resolved by rung and by segments cut through a playback pitch ratio, the same mechanism the kill combo and `boss_weak_hit` already use, not by baking one buffer per tier. Listening board: `tools/au2_board.gd`, every cue mastered to -14 LUFS under a -1 dBFS ceiling, `artifacts/local/au2/`.

| Cue | Notes | Instrument | Length |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bet_roll` | A snare roll accelerating from the eighth at 172 toward the thirty-second, the bet's 0.35 s arm | snare | 0.55 s |
| `bet_clunk` | The question: an anvil accent under the fbass leaping the tritone, D to Ab | anvil, fbass | 1.10 s |
| `bet_crack` | The win: the fbass resolves Ab to A (home's dominant), a snare crack, the rice-cooker's two "done" beeps a fifth up (A5 D6) | snare, fbass, beep, mallet | 0.46 s |
| `bet_refusal` | The loss: the fbass resolves Ab to G and stops, a dry 40 ms click | fbass, noise click | 0.30 s |
| `sunken_bell` | The reward bell an octave down through a 900 Hz low-pass, a falling fourth A to D (the ladder's first interval reversed) | bell | 1.22 s |
| `ally_hum_sable`, `ally_hum_rivet`, `ally_hum_kest` | The class motif straight on the Mind's own sine, an octave up: the ally quotes you right where the Mind quotes you wrong | chime | 1.92 to 2.27 s |
| `ally_wake` | A Cherenkov flicker on bell, D6 A5 D6, 40 ms apart | bell | 0.24 s |
| `fight_arm_stamp` | One anvil stamp on the beat, the Fleet's brass lean (D to Eb) under it | anvil, brass | 1.12 s |
| `near_miss_whip_sable`, `near_miss_whip_rivet`, `near_miss_whip_kest` | A rising noise sweep plus the class motif's first interval, an octave up, on the class's own voice | mallet/clav/tom, noise | 0.31 s |
| `kill_chain_tier` | A two-note bell ladder stab, the chain tier's own accent over a lone `kill_pop` | bell, noise | 0.14 s |
| `drop_landing` | The reward note on the ladder, then the rebound's own spring snap | bell, sine sweep | 0.30 s |
| `door_tick` | A bright, class-independent ready-pulse tick for a held-door weapon hit | bell | 0.08 s |
| `return_launch_arpeggio` | The rice-cooker climb up A7 (`Harmony.LAUNCH_TUNE`), the ride family's own voice | mallet | 0.99 s |
| `lift_hiss` | The bone gauge's pressure hiss, rising through the cycle | filtered noise | 1.20 s |
| `crater_hold` | New `hold()` kind: the reactor's burning ring, a Geiger crackle on the upper Ab pedal | filtered noise, sine drone | 0.70 s baked (2 beats); the runtime hold is called at its own length |

`tests/audio_v3_bank_test.gd` checks every id above bakes non-silent, under the soft-clip ceiling, and within its role's length; that `crater_hold` loops seamlessly (both ends fade to near-silence); and that a keyed AU2 cue actually moves with a live `audio.gd` key lift, not just structurally listed. `presentation_sfx_test`, `presentation_gems_test`, `presentation_v3_test` and `audio_cue_resolution_test` are unchanged and green.

AU3's wiring: the three class-suffixed families (`ally_hum_*`, `near_miss_whip_*`) resolve by the run's motif the same way `mind_sable`/`mind_rivet`/`mind_kest` already do (`id.get_slice("_", n)` inside the bank; the event-side lookup is `"ally_hum_" + motif` and `"near_miss_whip_" + motif`). `kill_chain_tier` and `drop_landing` want a pitch ratio at `play()`, from the chain rung and from segments cut, not a re-bake. `bet_clunk`, `bet_crack` and `bet_refusal` already carry Andrew's option (a) ruling (AUDIO-V3.md section 11 question 5): no further pitch decision needed at the call site.

## v3 M0 delivered damage

Before any balance work, a baseline: how much HP is actually removed, from what, and where (design-v3/STRATEGY-SPINE.md §7 row M0, §8 row 1; design-v3/research/CODEX-REVIEW-2-systems.md's recommendation). `strike()` in `combat.gd` is the one place a hit becomes damage, so it is the one place a ledger can be written without double counting; `enemy.take_damage()` now returns the HP it actually removed, clamped to what was left, so overkill is clamped at the source instead of re-derived later.

**The ledger.** Per hit: source (`weapon`, `ram_or_contact`, `drop_or_slam`, `special`, `item_proc`, `persistent_actor`), action id (the strike kind: `arc`, `runner`, `ram`, `sticky`, `synergy`...), target class (`enemy`, `Warden`, `adds` for a Fleet reinforcement, `boss` for Brindle's hull, `boss_adds` for anything spawned while the boss arena is live, `cache` for a vault), and a range bucket (near ≤ 6 m, mid ≤ 16 m, far beyond, from the ball at the moment of the hit). Totals, overkill, hit count and zero-damage-hit count are cumulative for the run; retirement counts are kills by target class. Two per-stage counts are snapshotted on `beacon_unsealed` (the Warden's break) and reset every stage attempt. `drops_to_break` counts drops: one per `warden_drop_landed` (warden_drop.gd's own verdict, emitted once per landed drop), whatever number of strikes the landing made. It reads `null` for a stage broken outside drop mode, meaning unmeasured. `warden_slam_hits` counts damaging `stomp` and `slam` strikes on the Warden in any loop_mode, and it is a hit count, not a drop count.

**Source, honestly.** Where the kind string alone says the source (a ball contact, a synergy strike, a pin, a batted shell), classification is automatic. Where it does not (a discharge, an overdrive blast, an upgrade-tree proc, a sticky charge thrown versus dropped), combat tags the call site or the charge itself; every strike() call from outside this file (build.gd, element_field.gd) that names no source falls back to `item_proc`, since SEAMS.md says those are never the player's own weapon fire or gauge release. A contact warden_drop.gd's rule judges a drop carries resolve()'s `drop` key and lands under `drop_or_slam` whatever its kind; otherwise only the `slam` kind does.

**Scenery.** Never a special case: a piece that cannot be hit (boss_target.gd's world-layer wrecked hull) never reaches `strike()`, so it never reaches the ledger.

**Where it lands.** `run_log.gd` mirrors the ledger from `ctx.run`'s meta into each run's own `delivered_damage` record (present and at zero for a run with no hits, never missing), and `tools/balance_report.gd` sums it across runs into a "Delivered damage (M0 baseline)" section: HP by source, by action, by target class (with retirements), and by range, then the mean `drops_to_break` per broken stage for runs tagged drop mode and the mean `warden_slam_hits` per broken stage. A drop-mode run whose stage broke after the mode flipped carries a `null` drop count; the report lists it as unmeasured instead of averaging it.

**Measured.** Taken at 1e5f897, before the fix rounds, and not rerun since. One skipper seed (runner_gunner), stage 1 only: `godot --headless --fixed-fps 90 --path sketches/core-loop --script res://tools/bot_run.gd -- --out=artifacts/local/m0-bot --policy=skipper --seeds=1 --stages=1`, then `godot --headless --path sketches/core-loop --script res://tools/balance_report.gd -- --in=artifacts/local/m0-bot --label=m0-demo`. One run, not a trend; the table below is the report doing its job, not a balance reading.

| Source | HP | | Action | HP | | Target class | HP | Retirements | | Range | HP |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ram_or_contact | 269 | | ram | 243 | | Warden | 800 | 1 | | near | 365 |
| special | 72 | | runner | 557 | | enemy | 26 | 1 | | mid | 211 |
| weapon | 485 | | shell | 26 | | | | | | far | 250 |

359 hits, 826 HP delivered, 15 HP overkill clamped, 0 zero-damage runs. Every column sums to the same 826, on the same run: `tests/combat_delivered_damage_test.gd` proves that reconciliation directly (overkill clamp, zero-damage hits, scenery exclusion, retirements, drops_to_break) rather than trusting one bot run to show it.

**Fix round 1 (two Opus reviewers).** Four fixes: an upgrade-tree proc volley now threads its `source` through `_fire()` instead of every bolt hardcoding `weapon`, so `_proc_volley` correctly tags `item_proc`. A vault (the one static, non-combatant damageable in the game) now gets its own `cache` target class instead of inflating `enemy`; the scenery test kept its original plain-node case (a strike() guard, not evidence about vaults) and gained a real vault case. `_record_delivered` now reads a `drop` key off `resolve()`'s result, so a future warden_drop.gd tag wins over the kind-only `slam` guess (the key does not exist on this base yet, so behavior here is unchanged; a direct unit check on `_classify_source` covers the branch). The reconciliation test now also sums real HP lost off every struck target directly (a chaser, the Warden, a Fleet add, a vault and Brindle's hull, including a hit that passes a phase floor) and compares it with the ledger total, independent of the ledger's own buckets.

One finding not fixed: whether the v2 Fleet roster (mag_trapper, plated_brute, swarm_remnant, ...) belongs under `adds` or under `enemy`. fleet.gd's own vocabulary calls its spawns "reinforcement packs," and the existing test already encoded that as intended before this fix round; reclassifying it would contradict that framing on a 0.65-confidence finding, without an owner call on what "adds" is supposed to mean. What *did* change: something spawned while `ctx.flow.in_arena` is true (currently only Brindle's drone-dropped lobbers, since the field director's own spawns are gated off during the boss fight) now reports under a new `boss_adds` class, tagged at `spawn_enemy()` by provenance rather than by kind, since `boss.gd` is out of this unit's allowed files and could not be given a caller-set flag. Confirm the intended meaning of `adds` with the owner before tuning on either bucket.

**Fix round 2 (two Opus reviewers).** Two real bugs, one hardening, one rejection.

`strike()` used to trust `enemy.take_damage()`'s own return value for how much HP a hit removed. That misses a Brindle piece: its hp is a getter over the boss's one shared pool (`boss_target.gd`), and boss.gd's own `_on_enemy_struck` listener can empty that pool synchronously off the `enemy_struck` bus fact (the scooter catch's `_defeat()`), before `take_damage()` ever runs and outside its accounting; `boss.take_damage()` also returns 0 once the fight has left `FIGHT` (EJECT included), so the whole remaining pool vanished with no ledger entry and the boss was never retired. `strike()` now reads the target's hp before the `enemy_struck` emit and again after `take_damage()`, and takes whichever of the two removal figures is larger; for every ordinary target the two already agreed, so nothing double counts. A `boss_defeated` listener (already a bus fact; no new one needed) records the "boss" retirement `spawn_enemy()`'s own died-handler path can't reach, since Brindle's pieces are never spawned through it. The test now drives the fight to `jump_to_phase(4)`, lands the scooter on the ball and strikes it, reproducing the review's own repro (receipt cap-9c673077eed3fdfc) as `boss_defeat_ledger` and `boss_retirement`.

**Superseded by round 3 below.** `drops_to_break` used to count every Warden hit whose kind was `slam`, in every loop_mode, not only `drop`. It now gates on `LoopMode.is_drop(ctx.tuning)` (the spec's own "when loop_mode is drop") and counts both descending contact kinds that can land on the Warden from above, `stomp` and `slam`, not `slam` alone; outside drop mode it reports `null` (explicitly unmeasured, not a bare `drops_to_break` bucket, not a `0` a stage might read as "broken with zero drops"). Reclassifying that source vs. the count is a small distinction: whether a hit's own `by_source` bucket is `drop_or_slam` is unchanged (still kind-derived, per the existing `_classify_source` behavior); only what counts toward a *break's drop count* changed. `drops_to_break` is still each hit landed, not each landing (a multi-hit slam on one drop would over-count relative to "one landing"); that's unchanged from round 1 and still open pending warden_drop.gd.

The per-hit `duplicate(true)` publish into `ctx.run`'s meta, on every hit and every retirement, is gone; GDScript Dictionaries are references, so `_reset_ledger()` now publishes the ledger once and every later mutation (`_bump` and friends assign into the same object) is visible through that one reference. `run_log._write()` still reads it once, synchronously, right before `JSON.stringify` serialises the line, so nothing observes a half-written ledger.

The fourth finding (whether the v2 Fleet roster's ordinary field spawns belong under `adds`) is not fixed, on purpose: it is a repeat of the finding left open in fix round 1, at the same low confidence (0.6), and the evidence does not show what it needs to. `spawn_enemy()`'s own match statement means every kind that can reach `_target_class_of_kind`'s fallback is a `fleet.makes()` kind; `director.gd`'s own `KIND_NAMES` shows the field director spawns those same kinds directly (`mag_trapper` included) through the identical `spawn_enemy()` path used everywhere else, so "director-spawned" cannot be told apart from "Fleet-initiated" by kind, and neither `world.gd` nor `fleet.gd` is in this unit's allowed files to tag the call site. What the evidence does show is that `Roster.is_reinforcement()` already calls exactly that set of kinds a reinforcement, spawned by the director or not (fleet.gd's own header: "every reinforcement spawned in one physics frame is one pack"), and that this is the pre-existing, tested reading, not a guess this unit is defending out of inertia. `_target_class_of_kind` now asserts the fallback against `Roster.is_reinforcement()` explicitly (with a warning if a kind is ever neither), so the name and the roster cannot silently drift apart even though the buckets themselves are unchanged. Confirm the intended meaning of `adds` with the owner before tuning on it; this unit still cannot make that call.

**Fix round 3 (after merging proto/core-loop-v3 at b980ff5, which brought warden_drop.gd).** `drops_to_break` counted Warden hits, not drops: the round-2 test itself expected 3 for two slams and a stomp with no drop landed. It now counts one per `warden_drop_landed`, which warden_drop.gd emits once per landed drop, and stays `null` outside drop mode. The hit count survives as its own field, `warden_slam_hits` (damaging stomp and slam strikes on the Warden, any loop_mode), so neither is read as the other. That count is taken before `take_damage()`. A killing hit unseals the stage inside it (enemy.gd's `_die()` reaches flow.gd's `_unseal()` and `beacon_unsealed` synchronously), so a count taken after the ledger records the hit dropped the last strike. The old hit-based `drops_to_break` had the same off-by-one. In the lapper runs below it read 2 slam hits for 3 landed drops before this change and 3 after.

`tools/balance_report.gd` cast every drop-mode run's `drops_to_break` entry with `int()`, which raised "Nonexistent 'int' constructor" on a `null` (a run tagged drop whose stage broke after the mode flipped). It now lists those breaks as unmeasured. The eight `_forced_source` blocks now restore the previous tag instead of clearing it, so a nested block keeps the outer tag.

`tests/combat_drop_ledger_test.gd` flies Rivet (skid smash) off the line onto the open stage 1 core and fires the landing's smash on the contact tick. The Warden takes the drop and then two SLAM strikes, and the ledger counts one drop. Evidence limit: the smash is player_ball.gd's landing handler called directly, not one the augment state machine landed by itself, so it bypasses the landed-body guard: a slam the ball lands (`_on_ball_slammed`) names the body under it when that body is a Warden judging drops, and neither the shockwave nor the skid push strikes it again, so it takes the landing contact only. Every other landed body still takes the shockwave's strike (`tests/combat_slam_landing_test.gd`).

Measured, lapper bot, drop mode, stage 1, seeds 1 and 2: `godot --headless --fixed-fps 90 --path sketches/core-loop --script res://tools/bot_run.gd -- --out=artifacts/local/m0-drop-lapper-3 --policy=lapper --loop=drop --stages=1 --seeds=1,2`. Both runs read `drops_to_break` 3 and `warden_slam_hits` 3 for stage 1, and the bot log's `drops_landed` and `drop_speeds` show 3 landed drops for each. These are bot runs, not play.

**SimClock: a bot seed replays the same run (b662313).** Bot runs at `--fixed-fps 90` go faster than real time, so every gameplay gate timed on `Time.get_ticks_msec()` passed or failed by CPU load; a hit-stop that fired changed `Engine.time_scale` and the physics after it, and a seed stopped replaying (seed 1 ran 3765, 3766 and 3760 ticks at b4cd347). `src/core/sim_clock.gd` returns the wall clock unless `SimClock.ticks` is set, and then counts physics ticks; only `tools/bot_run.gd` sets it. The gates on it: the hit-stop budget in `game.gd` `hit_stop()`, the spacing, budget and multi-kill window in `juice.gd`, and the cc-ignored spacing in `player_ball.gd` that fires Rivet's tree procs. Measured: 12 of 12 lapper runs of seed 1 at 3709 ticks, under load. `tools/stress_capture.gd` and `tools/item_fuzz.gd` still read the wall clock. Presentation and audio timers stay on it, since they don't change the simulation.

## v3 E3 HUD redraws on change (perf-lab H4)

The rule F2 inherits: a HUD widget shapes text and calls `queue_redraw()` only when a value it shows has changed, or while something on it animates. `gauge.gd` and `objective_view.gd` compare the incoming reading with the last one and return early when equal (no `extent()`, no resize, no redraw). `ability_bar.gd` compares the slot list (a deep copy is kept, so in-place edits still register) and redraws on a change, or while a ready swell runs. `key_glyphs.gd` and `edge_markers.gd` build their screen placements in `_process`, compare them with the last set and redraw on a difference, drawing from the stored set; a glyph or marker that follows a moving ball or camera still redraws each frame it moves. Resize, theme and class changes arrive as changed values (size, tint, motif) or as engine redraws, so they need no separate hook.

Base drew the two gauges only when their size changed (or while warning), so the health bar was absent from a fresh HUD and a same-width scrap count stayed stale. Redrawing on a changed value fixes that; it is the one pixel difference against base. `tests/ui_redraw_gate_test.gd` fails against the base revision: an idle HUD drew the objective, ability bar, key glyph and edge marker layers every frame. H4's saving is unconfirmed. Headless `perf_bench` s3 showed none: process p50 moved +0.066 ms over a median of 4 interleaved runs at load 5 to 10, inside noise, against the predicted 0.3 to 0.6 ms. Headless skips most draw cost, so a native run on a quiet machine decides it; if that run also shows under 0.2 ms, H4 is refuted. The change stays either way, for the gauge fix and the per-frame layout it removes.

`hud.gd` follows the same rule for layout: `layout()` (which calls the objective `extent()` and so shapes text) reruns only when the screen size or a size it reads changes (`_piece_sizes()`: objective minimum size, the three gauge sizes, slot count), not every playing frame. Base compared the ability bar's size with the screen size, which never matched, so it relaid out every frame. The redraw gate test also counts `layout_runs` over an idle window.

## v3 ACE music on Spillway (`--music=ace`)

Andrew committed the music to full ACE-Step (the synth composes guides, ACE performs whole tracks) and asked to judge it while fighting, because nuance heard on a pack gets lost in play. This build puts the ACE takes on stage 1 behind a flag. The default stays `synth` until his verdict. Launch with `scrapkeep core-loop-v3 --loop=drop --music=ace`, and add `--breath=raw|hard|gentle` to pick the held breath (default `hard`). The pause menu's Breath row and the title's B cycle the style while music is ace.

1. **The package.** `tools/ace/package_music.py` cuts calm-F-085-s1001 and warden-F-085-s42 (pack 3's Y3; `--warden s7` swaps in the other take) into 8 clips of 3 Demucs stems each, Ogg Vorbis q6, into `assets/music_ace/spillway/` (gitignored). It also writes the committed `manifest.json`, which holds each clip's entry and exit, bpm, beat, bar, key D, loop and loop_offset, the sections, and the raw, hard and gentle breath as numbers. Rerunning the script reproduces the Oggs byte for byte.
2. **Systemic play follows the player.** `src/presentation/ace_sequencer.gd` is the production version of the POC sequencer (core-loop-v3-seq 43453ab) and keeps its measured rules: equal-length stem pulls split at every action, `start((entry+0.5)/44100)` with 2 frames dropped, native loops, a 3072-frame ring, and keys logged by transport sample and read at the audible sample. The music plays calm or travel on the shelf by pressure. The drop-line commit (or `warden_engaged`) brings the pivot on the next bar, then the build, which loops bars 9 to 16 and never returns to bar 1. The telegraph and the open come in on the Warden's next beat and keep the bar's beat. A contact starts the gap at the render head and drops on the next beat after it.
3. **One directed set piece.** When the Warden breaks, break, return and home play start to finish on the bar grid, whatever the game does during them. After a drop that is still sounding, the break waits for the drop's end.
4. **Every edge gets its whole join (fix round).** A change asked on the grid itself, or inside the 10 ms before a grid point, lands on the next grid point with its full crossfade, never as a one-frame cut. That covers a follow at a hold's end, at a commit and at the release, and a contact on a beat. A break asked while a beat-scheduled telegraph waits under the drop enters on the drop's next bar. `start_level` while the band sounds fades the old section out, so no one-shot is left without a follower. A one-shot fading under its follower is not counted as a miss, and gains snap within 1e-9 of their target.
5. **One band at a time, handed over without a hole (fix round).** The synth band plays until the ACE band's first fade-in. It then fades out over 441 frames and stays muted while ACE plays, drop gaps included. When ACE stops (a death, the run's end, a stage change), the synth band unmutes in the same frame and the ACE fade tail is summed over it, so the death boom and a restart's first beat are heard. A stage change hands over at once, with ACE fading under the synth over one beat, and so does a launch out of level or Warden mode (an unsealed stage's gate), so its launch riser sounds. A launch out of the break does not: the piece plays through the flight and the landing hands it over on a beat (Fix round 2, ruling B). SFX stay synth and keyed, and read the ACE key (D) at the audible sample.
6. **Fallback.** A missing manifest or stem logs one warning naming the file, and the synth plays on.

**Measured.** `tests/presentation_ace_test.gd`: the held breath's kick band in the open against the build, drums alone / whole mix, is raw -0.1 / +0.1 dB, hard -182 / -32.5 dB and gentle -12.1 / -9.7 dB. Contact to audible gap is 46.6 to 58.2 ms (the POC's max was 82). There were zero skips over 180 s at p512 and at p1024 with a hitch, and 0 misses. `tests/ui_breath_row_test.gd` drives the flags, the ACE key, the Warden facts through `audio.gd`, stage 2 back to the synth, and a death unmuting the synth in the same frame.

**Perf, fix round.** `tools/perf_bench.gd` from core-loop-v3-perf 0cd689c, copied in uncommitted and wrapped to print which band played. Headless, 3 runs each, medians. Logs are under `artifacts/local/ace-music/perf-fix/`. Another worktree's Godot tests were running at the same time (load average about 7 on 32 threads).

| Scene, music | process_ms p50 | frame_ms p50 | frame_ms p99 | ACE render per second of audio |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| s2, synth | 1.195 | 9.27 | 40.20 | |
| s2, ace | 0.739 | 8.43 | 39.16 | 10.4 ms (about 0.17 ms a 60 fps frame) |
| s3, synth | 1.948 | 18.48 | 46.91 | |
| s3, ace | 1.034 | 17.54 | 45.70 | 10.6 ms |

ACE costs less than the synth band it replaces, because the muted synth skips its mix.

**Ruled, 2026-09-28: the breath default stays `hard`.** The Warden opening must be unmistakable, and it doubles as an audio cue for a player riding the lift. Andrew's slight pack-3 favourite, Y3, is the raw breath; under raw the open measures within 0.1 dB of the build, so as a cue it barely reads. Raw and gentle stay one key away (B, or `--breath=`).

**Ruled, 2026-09-28: the launch no longer cuts the set piece (option b).** In the drop-mode lapper bounce, the killing drop and the break land together at 34.1 s, the break waits for the drop's 4 bars, and the bot launches at 39.85 s, before the break enters. The three options were (a) the break takes over at the killing drop's next bar, (b) the piece plays as the flight's soundtrack and hands over at the landing drop, (c) hand to the synth at the launch. Andrew chose (b): a directed passage runs from A to B, so the flight is its length. The launch holds the ACE band (`_ace_flight`), a stage change waits, and the landing schedules the band's stop on the synth drop's beat (`stop_at`) and un-mutes the synth so its 441-frame ramp ends on that beat. The piece fades over a beat under the drop. If the piece ends before the landing, systemic play resumes and the same handover applies. If the landing never comes (a retry, a death, the arena) the band ends as it always did.

**Fix round 2 (review and verifier findings, each with its test).**

- **No hole inside a begun join (major).** A request that cancels a section whose join has begun (the drop-line commit, the unseal) used to fade its voices in 96 frames, with the old section already fading out, and leave silence until the replacement's bar: 40,587 frames (0.92 s) for a telegraph under a drop, 61,125 for a commit inside a travel join. Begun voices now keep sounding until the replacement's own join fades them (`_cancel_pending(ramp, keep)`). `presentation_ace_test` `begun joins never leave silence` reads 0 silent frames and fails on the old code with those numbers.
- **`stop(ramp)` and `start_level` honour the ramp.** A begun join's voices fade over the ramp asked (peak 0.301 at 1500 to 2500 frames after `stop(4410)`; silent before), and `start_level` gives the new section its whole join (enters 441 frames on, not on the sample).
- **The synth un-mutes with a ramp.** Voices that kept sounding under the mute return over 441 frames (`_fade_in_band`, ramp checked to 0.499 at mid-ramp), and `_ace_end` calls `_release_grid()` itself.
- **The ring guard is measured.** A 0.1 s generator at 44.1 kHz reports 8191 frames available (Godot rounds the ring up), so the queue target deepens 3072, 4096, 5120, 6144 and stops under the ring's top; a 4410-frame ring would not deepen. The director logs the ring capacity once at start, `_deepen_for` takes the skip count, and skips the synth alone took no longer count as an ACE dry-run.
- **The 24 stems load off the main thread.** A worker starts loading at setup when `--music=ace`; `ace_ready()` waits only for what remains, and loads in place if the option or manifest changed. `tests/presentation_ace_director_test.gd`: 1296 main-thread frames from setup to stage 1's ACE start, the longest 9.6 ms. The same package built on the main thread takes 11 ms here with warm file cache, so this box would not have hitched either way; the change guards a cold disk.
- **Launch out of level or Warden mode (verifier).** Reproduced on the old director: ACE in level mode, launch fired, synth still muted, `_build_riser` null (the riser is refused while muted). It is reachable: a beacon that completes on an unsealed stage enters the gates without `beacon_unsealed`. A launch from any ACE mode but the piece now ends the band first, so the riser sounds (`a launch out of the level keeps its riser`).
- **Stale comment and manifest reason.** `_ace_stage`'s comment names the flight hold. The manifest's `build_loop.reason` named warden-F-085-s7; it now records the packaged take's own seam, measured on the s42 build clip with a numpy reimplementation of pack 3's seam step (librosa, which pack 3 used for its kick envelope, is not installed): the 16 to 9 wrap's join step is 26.3 dB under the loop's 99.9th-percentile step (pack 3's gate is under 0), and the kick sits 10.2 ms off the grid across the join, against 13.1 ms at an ordinary bar boundary in the same measure (gate 12 ms in pack 3's own envelope). The lag measure is a proxy: it compares bar 16 to bar 9, and different bars differ. Bar 9 stays.

**Evidence limit.** Headless tests and the bounce show the transport and the levels. Whether the music carries the fight, and whether the open reads as a cue from the lift, is Andrew's sitting.

## v3 E1 static telegraphs, fill union and kill-pop budget

Built from `design-v3/E1-DENSITY.md`; the measurements are the E1 entry in `perf-lab/ledger.md`. Combat telegraphs build their motion marks once, at full extent, and each frame only hands the shader new numbers. `Threat.drive(d)` computes those numbers from the same expressions `marks(d, true)` draws, and `contains()` is unchanged, so the drawn shape is still the damage shape (RR-01). Boss hazards and the landing flash keep the per-frame path.

Where this build departs from the design page, and why:

- **Drive per layer, not per role.** A mark's drive mode rides on each vertex (`CUSTOM1.x`) and the painter keeps one instance per layer, as today. One instance per role would have added 48 instances in s3 (four per wave, two per shell). Four instance uniforms are used (`centre`, `heading`, `clip`, `alpha_scale`). `governed` is a uniform on the shared material. `Painter.layer_governed()` sets it from the roles actually painted on the layer, through `Density.class_of_role()` (a keyline `*_under` takes its line's class). Only the stripe layers can be governed, since P6 is hostile ground. A layer that mixes governed and exempt roles stays exempt and logs an error.
- **The shader is an include.** `src/combat/threat_mark.gdshaderinc` is the one source. `Painter.mark_material()` wraps it with `#define STRIPES` and a `stencil_mode` line per variant, because a define set by the including file is the only way to vary one source. `shader_get_code` needs a material's shader RID, which scripts cannot reach, so the colour and stripe code was copied from Godot 4.7.2's `BaseMaterial3D::_update_shader` (`scene/resources/material.cpp` at 4.7.2-stable). The stills confirm it matches.
- **Stencil classes are whole values drawn in batches.** Godot exposes no stencil read or write masks, so bits 1, 2 and 4 cannot be combined in one byte. Each class writes its own value and tests not-equal, and a large negative `sorting_offset` per class (area, then fill, then run, then swept) runs each class as one batch before every line at the same priority. Without the batches, interleaved area and fill draws would redraw each other's ground. Every hostile fill now draws under every hostile line, which is the §1.1 order.
- **The static area stripes are on the shared shader too**, as stencil class `area`, so the union covers them. The other static layers keep their StandardMaterial3D.
- **Six shared materials, not three:** area, fill, run and swept stripes, plus plain lines at 40 and keylines at 39.
- **A LANE's run is its own stencil class (`run`, value 8), batched after the INSTANT fills.** The page puts run in the fill union, where the first-drawn fill wins. The mixed-overlap still showed that a live lane's brighter band disappeared wherever a pending INSTANT fill drew first. A running lane is live damage, so it now draws over the fill. Lanes still union with each other.
- **A wave that is not hostile stays on `marks()`.** Its swept ground shares the `motion` layer with its front, and `drive()` gives each layer one clip. Every live wave is HAZARD today, so this guards a latent bug.
- **The governor counts what is in view 10 times a second** (`Density.SAMPLE_HZ`), not every frame. Each frame costs one `update()` toward the last count and at most one server call. The count still walks the threat and enemy groups, so it is not O(1), but it runs one frame in six at 60 FPS. The governor's 0.25 s rise is longer than the 0.1 s gap between samples.
- **The kill-pop cluster scales its particles** by the same factor as its starburst (ceil, capped by the burst's own max). The page does not say.
- **P4 is not thinned.** `Density.CLASS_OF_KIND` names the reward, exit and read glyphs, so `glyph()` leaves them alone without editing their callers. Reward thinning past 0.8 belongs to F4.
- **One published governor.** `Density.live()` is the one `Governor`. Its band and shed state live on the instance. Vfx steps it once a frame and resets it in `clear()`, and bolts reads it. `SHED_ORDER` has only `bolt_tails`, the one step with an emitter.
- **`glyph()` returns its slot** so the density test can read what was drawn.
- **Stills render into a fixed 1920x1080 SubViewport** with MSAA 2x. The compositor tiles the window, so the root viewport came out 2455 px wide.
- **Order of work.** H1 was built for all four types in one step and measured once, with the baseline and candidate runs taken back to back, instead of benching after each type. The design's E0′ baseline is the paired baseline column.

Tests: `tests/threat_grammar_test.gd` adds `driven numbers` (drive against marks and `contains()` at 0.25, 0.5 and 0.95) and `no rebuild` (100 frames leave every driven mesh and vertex count unchanged; a telegraph forced back onto the per-frame path rebuilds, so the check can fail). `tests/presentation_density_test.gd` replaces F1a's: 60 kills in 200 ms at density 0 and 1.0 stay at 3 or fewer starbursts, pops and debris bursts in every 250 ms, against a raw-call control that puts 60 in one window. It also covers the juice path and shed hysteresis. It checks the P0 to P3 exemption: a wave built at density 0 and at 1.0 carries the same `clip` and `alpha_scale` instance uniforms on its line layers, and those layers are ungoverned. The wave's swept layer and an INSTANT ring's fill and area are governed. Its last test checks that the pop gate keeps at most 64 records and that `clear()` empties them. `threat_grammar_test` adds `drive layers`: an owned or neutral wave does not drive, a control shows the clip overwrite it would cause, and every driven role has a class in `Density.CLASS_OF_ROLE`.

Stills: `tools/still_capture.gd` and `tools/still_diff.gd` (E1-DENSITY.md §5.2). The budget is also measured inside each telegraph's box: the pixels where either still differs from an `empty` ground still, padded by 8 px. On the whole frame, a band cut from a fill (about a tenth of it) differs on 0.34% of pixels and would sit at the 0.3% line. In the box it is 2.57%, against a box budget of 0.5% share and 0.3/255 mean. Three controls fail it: that band, the hidden static keyline and the hidden fill.

Against the b980ff5 reference, 20 of the 24 type stills pass. The wave, INSTANT and projectile diffs are one-pixel edge seams, at most 0.33% of the box. The other four, `lane-lane-25/50` and `lane-wedge-25/50`, show a visible look change: the reference drew the translucent run stripes over the red rails and chevrons, and E1 draws the lines cleanly on top of them. The change comes from the class batching above. It is the §1.1 order (P1 over P6), but it is a change, it reaches 2.39% of the box, and it is listed with `--expect-change` rather than passed by the budget. At 95% the run covers the lane and the stills match again. The overlap stills differ by design: same-class fills union, cross-class translucent layers blend in batch order, and at density 1.0 the fills fade to 35%. `overlap-mixed-50` and `overlap-mixed-live` cover area, fill, run and swept together, plus a lane across a ring.

### E1 fix round 2: the proc spike, live lanes, classes

- **The proc-max spike was the music engine, not the telegraphs.** A per-frame series (`perf_bench --series=`) put the one 70 to 86 ms frame in `Audio._process`, inside `engine.render()` of about 190 samples. A headless run has no audio device, so `Audio._on_ready` skips the background band bake (`prepare_rest`) and each instrument is synthesised on the main thread the first time a bar asks. With E1's fast frames the 120-frame warm phase ends within a second, before those first bars, so the bakes land in the recorded window; the base's slow frames spent that time warming. Evidence: `--no-audio` removed it (max 2.2 ms); the base tree with `--warm-frames=5` shows the same spike (71 and 72 ms); the E1 tree with `--warm-seconds=15` has proc max 1.65 ms. It is not shader or pipeline compilation: a headless run compiles no shaders, and pipeline compilation would land in the render thread's frame time, not the proc probe. The bench now finishes the band bake before it warms (`--cold-band` reproduces the hitch). No game code changed for it. Follow-up for the audio owner: a real run pays the same first-bar synthesis if the worker has not finished when a bar first needs an instrument.
- **A live LANE run is its own stencil class (`live`, 16) at render priority 41.** It draws over a pending run, an INSTANT fill, a swept ring and another telegraph's front band from every camera depth, and no longer flips with depth. The mixed-overlap still measured the live band against the pending still at 5.34 mean difference (base 3.92, the previous head 2.53). The painter swaps the layer's shared material when `drive()` reports `live` changing, and swaps back if the telegraph goes pending again. Deviation: a live band also covers the lane's own rails and chevrons, as the base did; a pending lane keeps the accepted P1-over-P6 order.
- **A PROJECTILE's footprint is its own stencil class (`reach`, 32),** not a shared `area`, because its alpha is 0.14 against 0.10. One alpha per class is now tested (`fill classes`); unifying the alphas would have changed the projectile's look.
- **Accepted look change: LANE at 25% and 50%.** The red rails and chevrons draw cleanly over the run stripes where the base drew the stripes over them. It reads more clearly, serves readability first, and moves toward the thin-haze telegraph direction (ROADMAP, "Feedback and discovery"). Fix round 3 re-baked the reference stills for these four and the four overlap stills from head 9532a27 (hashes in `artifacts/local/e1/stills/ref/REBAKED.sha256`, originals in `ref-base-original/`) and dropped the `ACCEPTED` list, so they are compared again. The base-to-head change itself rests on the by-eye acceptance.
- **`lines_same` replaced.** The exemption test now measures what the shader multiplies alpha by (`alpha_scale * mix(1, threat_fill_scale, governed)`) for the wave's line layers and for the fill layers at density 0 and 1.0. Making `layer_governed` return true or false for every layer fails it.

- **Fix round 3 (9532a27).** Hot P1 lines (`base`, `motion`) draw at priority 42, above the live band at 41, so a WAVE front crossing a live lane reads at full strength (`overlap-front-live` still, a mutation back to 40 fails it). Deviation, open: the charcoal keylines stay at 39 under the fills, because lifting them failed every still's look budget; inside a live lane another telegraph's keyline is dimmed while its hot line is not. `Painter.warm_up` builds all 14 material keys at stage load so no shader is built mid-fight; the native hitch removal is unmeasured. Stencil class sort offsets are spaced 1000 apart. `holds.gd` binding lines sit at 41, tied with the live band, and are left for a later round.

## v3 durability prototype (scrap as armour)
