Andrew's 2026-09-28 ruling (ROADMAP, "Scrap is size, and every system earns its place") and Codex's PURPOSE-AUDIT-1 Option A, built so Andrew can feel it. Two flags, both default off; off is the base game and `tests/durability_test.gd` proves the damage path, heft, HUD and run log match it.

- `--scrap=armor`: mass is a temporary health layer. `run_state.gd` `damage()` resolves every hit in one order: passive mitigation (build hands `effects.damage_reduction` to `run.mitigation`, and `_soften` no longer heals that share back), the brace window's share for the hit's source (`run.brace_hook`, folded in before absorption; `_soften` skips armour mode), then scrap at 10 scrap per HP, then hull overflow. Of the scrap a hit removes, 75% spills as at most 6 valued recovery clusters laid 6 to 16 m ahead of and 4.5 m either side of the ball's line, and 25% is destroyed. A cluster is caught by the same magnet and arc pull, restores mass through `recover_mass` (no `scrap_collected`, wallet, pickup commitments or discovery) and expires after 20 s, which counts as `expired`. Hull regeneration never touches mass; a hit never touches the hull below the overflow. Heft is 1 + mass / 450 with no part term. Class holds until heft is 5% of the preceding class interval below its threshold. Scrap Armor's health ceiling only ratchets up, in `recompute()` as well as on pickup (reset at run start), so hits and wear cannot cut the hull's ceiling. Thefts (`lose_scrap`) keep their behaviour.
- `--wear=on` (armour only): `wear_rate` is 30 scrap a minute at cruise, scaled by speed below it and capped at cruise above it. `build.gd` `_step_armor` feeds it only in grounded stage, beacon and gate play, so not while paused, in the shop, launched or airborne. It emits no event, sound or effect; the armour bar just sinks.
- Fixed route: `--route-seed=<n>` (not `--seed`, which alpha_capture already passes; the route seed is inert unless set) reseeds `ctx.rng` and the global stream at run start, `--start-mass=<n>` opens the run with that mass and no wallet. Both live in `tuning.gd` beside the flags, which parse in `Tuning._init` (the fence excludes `loop_mode.gd` and `game.gd`), so bots and the game read them alike.
- Every hit publishes `player_hit` with the whole hit (fix round 2), so items with `on_damage_taken`, near-miss, the hurt sound and the screen-edge flash all see armour hits; the hull gauge reads `run.hp` and does not dip on an armour-only hit. An armour hit also shows as the amber pulse on the new HUD gauge (left of the hull gauge, scrap colour) and the spilling clusters. Audio and juice cannot yet tell an armour hit from a hull hit; a distinct armour-hit sound is a follow-up for their owner.
- One damage order for every hit: mitigation and brace, then deflect, then the scrap layer, then hull overflow with the ward floor. In armour mode the ward holds the hull at its floor once per stage, so it can save a lethal hit (flag-off keeps the old heal-after). No clusters drop in the boss arena, which runs no scrap step; that spill counts as `destroyed` and `arena_destroyed`.
- In armour mode `bot_driver.gd` and `balance_report.gd` count the whole hit as HP lost; read `metrics.hull_damage` for hull loss.
- Restart: the checkpoint now carries the held class and the wear remainder; `restore()` puts them back in armour mode, and the stage's spill clears with the stage's scrap. Metrics accumulate across retries.
- Metrics, in the run log's `durability` block only in armour mode: fresh, hit_loss, wear_loss, recovered, destroyed, expired, hull_damage, class_changes, class_drops, min_heft, heft_at_hits (heft after each scrap-removing hit, at most 200), reverse_seconds. The last three make the downward spiral (one hit cutting protection, punch and magnet together) readable per run. `reverse_seconds` is an approximation: physics seconds with a spill live and the ball moving above 1 m/s against the stage's entry heading (`terrain.entry_heading`), not against the path to the spill.

## First-discovery picture cards (`--discovery=cards`)

ROADMAP "Feedback and discovery": the first time a synergy or find lands, a card explains it with a small animated picture; every repeat is a quick pop. Built as an option on the existing discovery banner, default off (`banner`), so off is the old banner unchanged.

- `codex_banner.gd`: with `cards = true` and a `profile`, `show_discovery(title, category, detail, kicker, id, kind)` keys the discovery by `id` (default category and title). First time: a card, 360 x 124 (the banner is about 360 x 121; the toasts band caps a card beside the HUD toast column at 360 wide and 126 tall), a 92 px looping glyph, the name at 26 and its existing line in at most 3 lines, held 4 s. Repeat: a pop, glyph (36 px) and name only, 0.8 s. The glyph reads its shape and colour from the `kind` EventKinds row (default `synergy_discovered`: star, amber), so no new art; a 1.2 s loop pulses it with an echo ring, turns stars and crosses, and slides chevrons and arrows.
- Momentum: the card never touches `Engine.time_scale` or the pause. Any key, mouse button or pad button press, or a hard stick push, dismisses it at once and is not consumed. It sits in the toasts band and adds no words beyond the banner's (kicker, name, line: about 15 of the 40).
- `ui_profile.gd`: `discoveries_seen`, saved and loaded beside `callouts_seen`, with `discovered()` and `mark_discovered()`. A discovery is recorded when its card comes up to show, not when queued, so one queued behind another and then dropped by a flow change still gets its card later; the same id queued twice shows a card then a pop. `save()` now merges what is on disk (callouts, discoveries, the larger glyph use) so the HUD's profile and the banner's own never drop each other's records.
- Reach without tuning.gd or presentation.gd (both outside the fence): `LoopMode.discovery_cards(args)` reads `--discovery=cards` (anything else is `banner`); the banner asks it from the launch args in `_init`, turns `cards` on and opens its own profile in `user://`. It is not in `FLAGS`, which maps only to tuning rows, so nothing warns "unknown". Presentation's existing `show_discovery` call passes no id or kind, so a discovery is keyed by category and title and wears the default `synergy_discovered` glyph; passing the codex id and the `interaction_found` kind is a follow-up in `presentation.gd`. (Superseded at integration: `--discovery` is a `Tuning.FLAGS` row; see the one-flag-table entry.)
- Size: the card is not much bigger than the banner because the toasts band (126 px at 900p, 364 px wide beside the 800 px toast column) is the ceiling; a real picture card needs either a taller band or a landing spot below it, which is a CONTRACTS UI-regions decision for Andrew.
- Not built: the "waits for the next calm moment mid-fight" gate from ROADMAP (the card lands in place at once), the animated picture per discovery beyond its EventKinds glyph, and a card for interactions and reactions until presentation passes `kind`.

## Thin-haze telegraph, `--telegraph=haze` (2026-09-28)

ROADMAP "Feedback and discovery": the floor marking becomes support, the attacker's body and sound the message. This slice restyles only the floor, behind `--telegraph=haze` (default classic; `loop_mode.gd` FLAGS carries the key, `threat_grammar.gd` reads the launch flags through Tuning's own parse in `Painter.haze_on()`, so the last `--telegraph` wins; `Threat.follow_row()` makes the telegraph row follow at each stage entry and retry, and `Threat.set_haze()` still overrides it for tests and tools until the next stage entry). Under haze a hostile fill (`area`, `fill`, `swept`, `run`, and the live INSTANT slag) is HAZARD red with no stripe texture, its alpha multiplied by `HAZE_FILL` = 0.5 (the slag draws at `HAZE_SOLID` = 0.4, from 0.8). One factor for every fill keeps one alpha per stencil class and the LIVE/RUN ratio the drive hands the shader, so the classes, sort offsets, priorities, governor, exemption, clips, geometry, `contains()` and the no-rebuild property are the classic ones. Every P1 line and keyline is byte-identical, so the lines are exactly as crisp and now carry the read. Owned (amber) and neutral marks are untouched, and the landing Flash stays classic (a hit confirm, not a telegraph). The stripe-less shader variant is a new material key (`stripes` false), warmed by `warm_keys()` under haze.

Open, for play: whether 0.5 is thin enough or too faint at 25%; whether the pending `run` and live lane want a wider gap; the tuning.gd row (`telegraph`: classic, haze) was added at integration, and `--telegraph` is now a `Tuning.FLAGS` row. Andrew compares the looks in play.

**Follow-up, 2026-10-22 (Lighting L5b, PR #284): `HAZE_FILL` 1.15, `HAZE_SOLID` 0.61.** The 28 Sep entry above stands as written; these two constants have moved since, and `threat_grammar.gd` carries the live values.
- `HAZE_FILL` is 1.15. On the RTX 3060 the lowest hostile-fill gap (camp ledger, CIE76 over the 10 floor) read 8.65 at 1.0, 10.03 at 1.1 and 10.72 at 1.15 (#241 comment 6331769617). The coordinator ruled 1.15, the first 0.05 step that clears every place by at least 0.5 (#241 comment 6331787112, item 1).
- `HAZE_SOLID` is 0.61 (was 0.4). At 1.15 the pending INSTANT fill is `FILL_ALPHA` x `HAZE_FILL` = 0.4025 (0.4000 measured in the 8-bit vertex colour), so a live slag at 0.4 read as solid as the pending fill, against ATTACK-READS.md:27 ("Clear live/pending distinctions and safe holes survive haze."). Ruling 6331787112, item 2, asks for a live-over-pending alpha step of at least 0.2: that needs 0.6025, and 0.6 misses by 0.0025, so 0.61 (step 0.21 on the measured 0.4000). `threat_grammar_test` pins the step; `tools/grade_probe.gd` reports a `live~pending:<ground>` gap per place (not gated). (Superseded by the dark scorched core note below: the test now pins an L* step.)
- Evidence met: the held-pose pair on the 3060 sweep at 512b636f (#241 6345835130); the coordinator kept 0.4 / 0.8 (ruling 6345854759).

**Follow-up, 2026-10-22 (Lighting L5b, PR #284): the live slag is a dark scorched core, not a more solid red.** `HAZE_SOLID` (0.61) is gone; `LIVE_SCORCH` 0.4 and `LIVE_SCORCH_ALPHA` 0.8 replace it, and `fill_tint` returns `HAZARD.lerp(CHARCOAL, LIVE_SCORCH)` at that alpha for a hostile haze INSTANT while live. On the 3060 the 0.61 alpha step read -1.2 L* (stage 2) and +0.3 (arena) live minus pending (#241 6342833117): HAZARD ff5a3c is L* 60.7 and the pale floors are L* 64 to 67, so more of the same colour cannot add value contrast. A linear-light model over grey L* 67 predicted pending 64.2 and live 62.1; the 3060 measured 64.1 and 62.9. The scorch darkens the live disc instead (coordinator option A, #241 6343230462): model estimate over L* 67 is live about 48 at (0.4, 0.8), a 16 L* step, about 42 at (0.5, 0.9), about 51 at (0.3, 0.8). The HAZARD edge and its charcoal keyline stay on pending and live, and the hue family is HAZARD's (a lerp toward CHARCOAL). The boss slag annulus follows the same tint (`live_tone` is now the classic look only); quenched holes stay clear. Bars, judged on the 3060 and not here: live at least 10 L* under pending on a pale floor, and the live disc's edge and keyline at least 10 L* off the dark arena ground (tower base, about L* 29). `threat_grammar_test` models the step over L* 67.04 and fails the old 0.61 HAZARD tint; `grade_probe.gd` reports `live~pending:<ground>` as the L* step (not gated); `tools/haze_pose_capture.gd --scorch= --live-alpha=` sweeps candidates per place, and the bench picks the smallest that clears both bars. The E07 netcaster is exempt (air-only, so it keeps HAZARD at 0.61 and never the scorch, which reads as a ground hit), ruling #241 6347013047 section 4; 0.61 is the pre-scorch `HAZE_SOLID` from cf302195 (6331787112), not the stage's 0.4 (correction 6347961732, accepted 6347979378). `threat_grammar_test` checks it with a planted control and `enemy_counters_test` checks the real netcaster at its hold. Its live cue in the air band is a wave 19 card (6347979378).

## Armour sounds (durability prototype)

A hit in `--scrap=armor` no longer sounds like one thing. Serves the durability prototype's readability: you hear whether the hull or only the plating paid, without looking at the gauge. Strains no principle; it adds no gameplay and never touches the ball. Flag off, audio is unchanged.

- **Plate shed** (`armor_hit` row, `armor_shed_small`, `armor_shed`, `armor_shed_big` in `sfx_bank.gd`): inharmonic FM pings over grit, two, three or five plates, with a low thunk from three up. `audio.gd` picks the size by scrap removed (under 60 small, 150 and over big) and then lowers pitch (+2 to -3 semitones) and raises level (-2 to +2 dB) up to 250 scrap. Clatter, never a tune, so it is not mistaken for a pickup. Priority 1: no music or bass duck.
- **Hurt only when the hull took damage.** `armor_hit` fires before `player_hit`, so `_on_player_hit` reads the change in `run.metrics.hull_damage`: no change is silent (the shed already sounded), a change plays hurt sized by the hull damage, not the whole hit. A hit the mitigation or a glancing plate turned aside entirely (no scrap removed, no hull damage) plays the soft deflect tick (added later, see the deflect tick entry at the end of this file).
- **Reclaim chime** (`armor_reclaim`): a bright rising fourth on the bell (A to D), the tail of a rice cooker's melody, climbing the pentatonic by `reclaim_streak` (900 ms window, cap 7, a `JUICE` row) through a quick run and restarting after a pause. It follows the live key. New signal `ScrapField.recovered(value)`, emitted in `step()` where a cluster is taken; mass is still credited from `recovered_values`, and it posts no `scrap_collected`.
- Colour, shape and sound share one row each in `EventKinds.LOOT_ROWS` (`armor_hit`: the HUD armour flash's amber, burst; `armor_reclaim`: scrap's mint, square), because both facts are outside the bus ROWS table. Wiring is in `audio.gd` `_wire_armor`, connected once and only when the sources exist.
- Tests: `tests/audio_armor_test.gd` (shed not hurt, hull hit hurt, bare hull hurt, size, chime climbs and restarts, flag-off signature). Judged by ear: how the three sizes and the chime sound is Andrew's.

## One launch-flag table (2026-09-28)

The command line was parsed twice, once in `tuning.gd` (scrap, wear, route-seed, start-mass) and once in `loop_mode.gd` (loop, gun, music, breath, discovery, telegraph). Now `Tuning.FLAGS` is the one table (name to tuning key, `option` or `number`, and `start` or `launch`) and `Tuning.apply_args(args, when)` the one parse pass. Behaviour is unchanged: `start` rows apply as `Tuning.new()` runs (`apply_cmdline("start")`), `launch` rows when `game.gd` calls `tuning.apply_cmdline("launch")`, so a bare Tuning in a bot or tool still never sees the loop flags. An unknown option value warns and is refused; a non-number for a number flag and any flag not in the table are ignored. `LoopMode.apply_args(tuning, args)` stays as the launch pass for the tests that call it; `LoopMode.FLAGS` and `LoopMode.apply_cmdline` are gone.

Adding a flag is one row in tuning.gd `SPECS` and one row in `Tuning.FLAGS`. `threat_grammar.gd` `Painter.haze_on()` still reads `--telegraph=haze` from the launch args itself (the static floor marking has no tuning in reach); `tuning_flags_test` checks its `TELEGRAPH_FLAG` name is a row in the table. That test covers every flag with a valid and an invalid value and fails if a row is dropped or added untested.

`scripts/sketch-smoke.mjs` now lists every test that printed `SKIP` or `N skipped` (N above 0) in a `skipped` block of the summary, with the matching lines. It never fails the run, and the `PASS` line and final line are unchanged.

## Debt lane 1: holds draw layer, deflect tick, flag-off cue volume (2026-09-28)

Three open items from the merge ledger, no new features. Flag off, nothing changes.

- **Holds draw layer.** `holds.gd` binding line and bracket drew at 41, the same as `threat_grammar.gd` `LIVE_PRIORITY`, so they sorted arbitrarily against a live lane band. They now draw at `Holds.DRAW_PRIORITY` = 43, one above the hot P1 line layer (`LINE_PRIORITY`, 42), because a hold's line is a hostile P1 line and FEEL-READABILITY section 1.1 says no fill or band may cover one. 43 is used by no threat_grammar constant. `tests/holds_priority_test.gd` finds every `*PRIORITY` constant on the grammar by reflection and fails on a tie.
- **Deflect tick.** In `--scrap=armor` a hit turned aside entirely (mitigation or a deflect item: no scrap removed, no hull damage) was silent. `audio.gd` now notes when `armor_hit` sounded for the hit in flight, and `_on_player_hit` plays `armor_deflect` when neither the shed nor the hull did. The sound is one short bell partial (about 110 ms, trim -10 dB, priority 1: no duck) plus a breath of grit, in `sfx_bank.gd`, with an `armor_deflect` row in `EventKinds.LOOT_ROWS` (sand, square). It has no plates, thunk or second note, so it is not the shed, the reclaim chime or the hurt. Judged by ear: whether it is soft enough is Andrew's.
- **Flag-off cue test.** `audio.cue_log` "now" entries carry the volume in dB as a sixth element. `tests/audio_armor_test.gd` compares each cue of a scripted four-hit sequence against the base on name, pitch and volume, with the expected volume computed from the bank trim, the row priority and the hit size, not from `audio.gd`.

## World-one two-pick profile, `--world-one-builds=on` (unit G, 2026-09-28)

First slice of `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md` ("Build G first"). Three launch flags are registered as `SPECS` and `Tuning.FLAGS` rows: the master `--world-one-builds` and the independent `--hotwire-shell` and `--hotwire-tread`, all off/on, default off, `launch` phase. The hot-wired flags do nothing yet; their effects belong to later units. No node behaviour, mass verb or part is built.

- **Two depths.** `UpgradeTree.max_depth` (default `DEPTHS`, 4) caps `open_checkpoint`, `pending` and `offered`; `world.gd` `_open_pick` sets it to `WORLD_ONE_DEPTHS` = 2 from the flag, so a run gets the class-select pair and the pair after stage 1, and stages 2 and 3 open nothing. The tree still holds all 30 nodes; only the first six a pilot are ever offered, and `problems(class, true)` validates those six (default call unchanged).
- **Stable choices.** The once-per-stage `_picks_opened` guard already covered a skipped bay and a retry; the test asserts that skip, bay entry and retry leave one open depth-2 pair, the children of the first pick.
- **Calm delivery.** Under the flag a pick that falls due while the ball is airborne and outside the bay is held in `_picks_waiting` and opens on the first grounded (or in-bay) physics frame. A skipped bay at stage 1's clear lands mid-launch, so the second pick arrives on landing in stage 2, not before entering it. Flag off, the offer is immediate as before (measured on the base commit: offers at depths 1, 2, 3, 3 for the test script, and one offer in the air).
- **Outside the named files.** `tests/tuning_flags_test.gd` gained the three rows, since it fails on any `Tuning.FLAGS` row it does not list. `workshop.gd` and `tree_pick.gd` are unchanged: the pick screen reads `tree.offered` and `tree.pending`, which already honour the cap. `tree_crumb.gd` still draws four levels of dots.
- **Tests.** `tests/world_one_G_test.gd`. Open for play: whether the on-landing delivery feels calm, and whether the crumb should show two levels.

## Attack rows, prerequisite A (2026-09-28)

`EventKinds.ATTACK_ROWS` holds one row per attack ID in `design-v3/research/attack-reads/ATTACK-READS.md`: 18 enemy (E01-E18), 7 Warden (W01-W07), 5 Brindle (B01-B05) and 13 pilot (P01-P13), 43 in all. Nothing reads them yet: no audio, presentation, combat or telegraph change, and no launch flag. The contract is frozen here so the audio consumer (B), the cue bank (C) and the actor units read one row instead of each carrying their own colour, cue or label.

- **Fields.** `label`, `threat`, `glyph`, `color`, `pose`, `warn`, `release`, `floor`, plus `group`, `owner_kind`, `owner`, `allegiance` and `open`. Accessors: `has_attack`, `attack_row`, `attack_ids(group)`, `attack_label`, `attack_threat`, `attack_glyph`, `attack_color`, `attack_pose`, `attack_warn_cue`, `attack_release_cue`, `attack_floor`, `attack_open`. An unknown ID reads empty.
- **`threat` is a `THREAT_ROWS` key** (`threat_instant`, `threat_wave`, `threat_projectile`, `threat_lane`), not a `Threat.Type`, because `roster.gd` loads the threat grammar and `event_kinds.gd` must not load it back. `""` means no committed telegraph (player attacks, W06, W07, B04, B05). The test maps the key to `Threat.KINDS` and checks each enemy row against its roster telegraph; E04 (miniboss charge, a lane) is the one exemption, since the roster row names the leap.
- **Voices by reference.** `owner` names a roster ID (E rows), a Warden family from `Roster.WARDEN_FAMILIES` (W rows), `brindle`, or a class ID (pilot rows), so faction voice comes from `Roster.FACTIONS` and pilot identity from `ClassDef`. Pilot rows take colour and glyph from `ClassDef.SABLE_COLOR` and friends and the class shape; the test fails if a row drifts from its class. Hostile rows are `Palette.HAZARD`.
- **Cue ids are reserved names**, `atk_<id>_warn` and `atk_<id>_release`, not baked sounds: Audio owns synthesis. `none` marks a beat the document gives no cue (no wind-up player attacks, W07's absent warning, B04's absent release, B05's immediate launch). W03 reuses E17's cue ids, since the document says it uses E17's chord.
- **Floor mode** is the document's L, B or `none`. Mixed rows (W01, W02, W04 with a red core and bone lift; P09 with charges and a slam) take the stronger B; the bone second colour is not a field yet.
- **Test.** `tests/attack_rows_test.gd` parses the 43 IDs from the document, fails on a missing, invented or shared ID, a missing field, a non-palette colour, an owner that does not resolve, a placeholder not listed in `open`, and any change to `THREAT_ROWS`, `FIELDS`, the existing `ROWS` values or the existing accessors (the three hashes are of base e05c1e3; an intentional edit to an existing row must update `ROWS_HASH`).

Placeholders, frozen with their gaps visible (each is in the row's `open` list):
- **W06 `color`:** defensive brace with no damage and no floor; the document gives it no colour, so `Palette.SAND` stands in.
- **B05 `color`:** neutral boss read with no colour in the document; stands in with `Palette.BRASS`.
- **P11 `glyph`, `color`:** all-pilot contact row; `arrow` and `Palette.SAND` stand in, and the consumer should substitute the acting pilot's class shape and colour.
- **P13 `glyph`, `color`, `pose`, `warn`, `release`, `floor`:** the upgrade procs reuse each parent attack's pose, cue and floor, so no single value exists. `pose`, `warn`, `release` and `floor` hold the value `tbd`; `glyph` (`diamond`) and `color` (`Palette.SAND`) are stand-ins. The consumer resolves them through the parent attack.

Other gaps not marked `open`, because the document says something but not an ID: pose keys are named from the body description (`e01_brace_back`), cue ids are the reserved names above, and glyphs for enemy and Warden rows are chosen by geometry (lane `bar`, instant ring `ring`, projectile `cross`, wedge `chevron`, slam `burst`, net `star`). Keyframe status (K or N), lead times and the bone second colour of mixed floors are not in the rows; the document remains their source.

## Mass-K, Kest's mass verb (--world-one-builds=on, 2026-09-29)

Second slice of `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md` (Mass-K row): Kest turns mass into payload geometry. No new flag; it rides `--world-one-builds`.

- **One number.** `Combat.mass_radius_scale()` is `1 + 0.25q`, `q = clamp(run.mass / 990, 0, 1)`, and exactly 1.0 with the flag off. `_throw_sticky` reads it once when the charge is committed (the ring and the Sticky Rain scatter distance use the same scaled value), `_on_demo_slammed` reads it once per slam, and the aim ring in `_draw_throw` reads the same function. `_detonate` already tests the charge's own stored `radius`, so the hit test and the blast ring need no second copy.
- **Committed once.** A charge keeps the radius it was made with; mass gained afterwards does not grow it.
- **Untouched.** Blast damage, blink distance, glide physics and the other mini-charge sources (procs, glide drops, fuse charges) are unchanged. Constants `MASS_K_RADIUS` and `MASS_K_SATURATION` sit in `combat.gd` because `weapons.gd` was outside the slice.
- **Slam preview.** The demo slam has no aim preview, so only the throw ring shows the radius; the slam's blast ring is drawn by `effects.ring` from the committed radius.
- **Test.** `tests/world_one_mass_k_test.gd`: mass 0/450/990 commit 1.0x, 1.114x, 1.25x on throw and slam; ring equals committed; hit test reaches exactly radius x forgiveness + hull; damage, blink distance and glide velocity equal; late mass leaves committed charges small; flag off equals base. Blink and glide equality cannot fail by reverting combat.gd, since nothing on those paths reads mass; they guard against later coupling. Open for play: whether the wider blast changes where Andrew places it.

## Rivet's mass verb, Mass-R (2026-09-29)

Slice Mass-R of `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md`. No game code changes: the premise held. `combat.gd` `punch_of()` already returns `(base + force * k) * heft() * knob`, so a sprint ram and a ground smash carry mass once, through `run.heft()` = 1 + mass / 450 (+0.2 per part). This slice makes that contribution visible and pins it. Nothing multiplies mass into damage again, and `rivet.gd`, `combat.gd`, `augments.gd` and `player_ball.gd` are untouched; the Rivet row asks for no tree change in this slice (Second Wind's air-control cancel belongs to R6).

- **Fixture.** `tests/doubles/world_one_mass_route.gd` holds one route: the same 30 m/s ram (sprinting) and smash (slam kind) on a bare target at mass 0, 450 and 990, and a 30-frame side-input turn at 14 m/s. It reads results from the real `combat`; the formula is not restated there.
- **Measured** (Rivet, no parts, sprint knob 1.25 from `weapons.gd` SPRINT_PUNCH, read live): heft 1.00 / 2.00 / 3.20; ram 66.25 / 132.50 / 212.00; smash 36.00 / 72.00 / 115.20. Mass 990 over mass 0 is x3.20 on both, not x10.24. The turn heading is -0.6123 rad at all three masses.
- **Flag.** No new flag: the rows are identical with `--world-one-builds` on and off, because the slice adds no behaviour.
- **Test.** `tests/world_one_mass_r_test.gd` computes the expected punch from `Combat.STRIKE_DAMAGE` and `run.heft()`, and fails if damage rises by more than heft, drops out of order, or turning moves with mass. A scratch edit making `punch_of` multiply heft twice fails it three ways.
- **Open for play:** whether x3.2 at mass 990 reads as weight; mass 990 is BUILD-SYSTEMS' proposed saturation point, not a game value.

## Sable's mass launch, Mass-S under `--world-one-builds=on` (2026-09-29)

One slice of `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md` ("Mass becomes the pilot's action", the Sable row). Size is banked in the rope and the wall: q = clamp(mass / 990, 0, 1), and the slingshot release, the vault kick and the Coil Hook release each add up to 8 m/s x q. No new flag; the verb rides the master flag and both Sable depth-1 nodes (Cannonball, Beachhead) carry the new `mass_launch` knob.

- **Budget.** `AugmentKit.mass_gap` is the one shared grant: the first launch that takes a bonus starts a 1 s cooldown for all three verbs. A launch that gets nothing (cooling, or at the ceiling) leaves it untouched.
- **Ceiling.** `Augments.LAUNCH_CEILING` (45) bounds generated speed only. The bonus is `min(8q, max(0, 45 - speed))`, taken after Pylon Capacitor's slingshot mods (they change the launch speed before it), so the capacitor and the mass bonus resolve once and never past 45. A launch already at or above 45 keeps its speed and adds nothing; with the verb on the vault also stops trimming an incoming speed above its 45 cap. Long Lane and Spring Hull are item surges that already decline at a 45 cap (`item_effects.gd` `_surge`), so they add nothing on top of a full launch.
- **Where mass comes from.** `player_ball._read_mass` sets `kit.mass_q` each tick from `mass_of`, which `combat.gd` wires beside `heft_of` to the run's scrap mass alone, so held parts never count as mass and the verb works in every scrap mode. Unwired, mass reads 0. Reading it spends nothing.
- **Untouched.** Steering, friction, collider, gravity and air control never read q; the test compares an airborne run at mass 0 and 990. Flag off, or no depth-1 pick, every launch equals base.
- **Open for play.** Whether +8 m/s reads as a bigger launch without losing landing control, and whether the aim cone's speed should show the bonus (it does now).
- **Tests.** `tests/world_one_mass_s_test.gd`.

## Attack facts, enemies (prerequisite D, packet 1, 2026-09-29)

Each mapped enemy attack now publishes read-only facts so later audio and pose units subscribe to one source instead of re-deriving the attack. It adds no decision, clock or geometry: every value is read from the state machine and the committed telegraph that already exist. Written after the code was traced but before it was built; the names below are the ones built.

- **Signal.** `Enemy.attack_phase(facts: Dictionary)`, on the enemy node, once per phase change. **Accessor.** `Enemy.attack_facts() -> Dictionary`, the open attack with its current progress and geometry, `{}` between attacks. No event-bus signal: a consumer connects to the enemy it already holds. `game_events.gd` is untouched.
- **Payload.** `instance` (int, unique for the process, never reused), `row` (an `EventKinds.ATTACK_ROWS` id), `owner` (the enemy's `kind`), `phase` (`windup`, `strike`, `recover` or `cancelled`), `committed` (bool), `progress` (the telegraph's progress, 0 to 1), `geometry` (for the enemies that commit a telegraph, a copy of `Telegraph.descriptor()`, the dictionary `telegraph.gd` draws and whose fields `contains()` tests) and `at` (the enemy's position).
- **Phases.** `windup` when the attack's telegraph is committed; `strike` when it goes live; `recover` when the attack ends after striking; `cancelled` when a wind-up ends before striking (a knock at INTERRUPT), or the enemy dies or leaves the tree in any phase. `recover` is the last event: when each enemy's private rest ends is not published. A new commit closes the open instance first, so instances never overlap on one enemy; two enemies overlap freely, each with its own id.
- **Where each state machine emits.** Plain enemies (Chaser, Miniboss) go through `Enemy._commit_ring` and `_commit_lane`, which open the instance; `_facts_poll` (a `NOTIFICATION_PHYSICS_PROCESS` handler in `enemy.gd`) promotes it to `strike` on the first tick the telegraph's progress reaches 1, which is the tick the state machine flips its own state; `_clear_telegraph` closes it (`recover` after a strike, `cancelled` before one); `_die` and `_exit_tree` close it as `cancelled`. Counters (`counter.gd`) drive their own phases: `_warn` takes over the instance (or opens one for a telegraph set directly, the Swatter's wedge), the WARN to LIVE flip publishes `strike`, `_end` publishes `recover` or `cancelled`.
- **Lobber.** It commits nothing until release, so its wind-up is the public `charge` (0 to 1) that `lobber.gd` computes each tick from its clock, CYCLE and TELEGRAPH and draws the muzzle glow from; `enemy.gd` reads that value and nothing of the glow. Wind-up publishes `committed` false, empty `geometry` and the charge as `progress`: the aim is uncommitted. At release `lobber.gd` emits `released(ring)` right after `fired`, with the descriptor of the ring the shell's own Telegraph just committed (`projectile.gd` builds it, with tone, launch and bump; the lobber finds its newest shell in the `enemy_shot` group once combat has made it). `strike` publishes that descriptor with `committed` true, then `recover` in the same tick keeps it, since the shell's flight belongs to the projectile; the `fired` signal itself is unchanged. An empty ring (no shell was made) leaves the strike uncommitted. Range reset without firing publishes `cancelled`.
- **Row mapping.** Row is looked up from `ATTACK_ROWS` by roster owner, so there is no second table. Chaser E01; Lobber E02; Miniboss E03 (ring, slam) and E04 (lane, charge), told apart by the committed shape; Mag Trapper E05, Hooker E06, Netcaster E07, Bola Thrower E08, Plated Brute E09, Deflector E10, Winch Drone E11, Gyro Jammer E12, Swatter E13, Ramp Flipper E14, Catch Trap E15, Reactor Relic E16, Mind Hand E18, all through the counter loop.
- **Unmapped.** Swarm Remnant (E17): its formation lane is committed and driven by `swarm.gd` and the remnant has no attack state of its own; it publishes nothing and is listed in `FACTS_UNMAPPED` with that reason. Warden and Vault publish nothing: no enemy row belongs to them (W01 to W07 are the next packet). The Miniboss slam strikes and ends on one tick, so its `strike` and `recover` arrive together.
- **Tested.** `tests/attack_facts_enemy_test.gd` covers the Chaser (two overlapping lunges, geometry equal to the telegraph's descriptor and hit test at 25, 50 and 95 percent, knock and kill cancellation), the Lobber (wind-up progress equal to the public charge on every tick with the glow forced to 77 and the fact re-polled in the same tick, strike committed with geometry equal to the shell telegraph's descriptor and its hit test agreeing at 0, 50, 98, 102 and 150 percent of the radius, `enemy.gd` naming neither the muzzle nor the glow constants, kill cancellation) and the Mag Trapper (phases and cancel). The other counters and the Miniboss share the same code path but have no test of their own. Timing is checked against base 1c2f777 by a state-and-lane timeline whose hash was captured on base, and damage by a landed Chaser lunge that must emit `hit_player` with 9.0 (a scratch change to 90.0 fails it).
- **Open for the next packets.** Per-enemy files stay untouched apart from `lobber.gd`; a pose unit that needs the end of a rest, or the Lobber emitting its own wind-up, would edit `lobber.gd` again. The Warden, Brindle and pilot rows are unbuilt.

## Kest's world-one kit and the world-one card table (--world-one-builds=on, 2026-09-29)

Third slice of `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md` (### Kest). K1 to K6 already sat at heap indices 0 to 5 with the right parents, unit G already offered exactly them, and every knob already matched the table, so `kest.gd`, `combat.gd` and the tree are unchanged. What this adds is the card copy and the proof.

- **Card table.** `src/core/trees/world_one_cards.gd` holds `ROWS`, a node id to line map, with the bold line of every Kest, Sable and Rivet node. `effect_line(node, world_one, shell)` returns the row when the flag is on and a row exists, else the node's own `effect`; the three rows that name the Riveted Shell inversion (`s_face_runner`, `k_smoke_trail`, `r_second_wind`) read their `UNFITTED_ROWS` line instead while the shell is not fitted.
- **Flag-aware read only.** `TreeCard` takes `world_one` and `shell` as its fifth and sixth arguments and shows `effect_line`; `TreePick` passes both, read through the workshop above it (`game.tuning.world_one()` and `_shell()`, the `hotwire_shell` option), because `workshop.gd` was outside the slice. `Kest.NODES` effects are untouched, so flag off shows every card as before.
- **Test.** `tests/world_one_ktree_test.gd`: cards and offers with the flag on and off (all 18 cards across the three classes compared to base strings), each retained effect through the real combat, K3 springing at a 1.9 m hull gap and not at 2.1 m with the charge carrying the 2.0 knob as its reach, the four identities, and K5/K6 exclusivity.
- **K5 row.** `ROWS` carries `k_smoke_trail` ("Glide cloaked. Heavy fins steer harder."), so with the flag on and the shell fitted all six Kest cards carry Codex copy; unfitted, K5 reads "Glide cloaked.". `Kest.NODES` keeps its own effect ("Gliding keeps you cloaked") for flag off. Also open: `workshop.gd` could set the flag on the pick explicitly instead of the parent lookup.

## Sable's world-one kit nodes, and the world-one node-table seam

Fourth slice of `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md` (### Sable), after Kest, and the first that needs a node the flag-off tree does not have: S4 is Face Runner at heap index 3 under Cannonball, where flag-off Sable has Live Wire.

- **The seam.** `UpgradeTree` has a `world_one` flag beside `max_depth` and `nodes(class, world_one)` reads the pilot's `NODES_WORLD_ONE` when the flag is on and the pilot declares one (`WORLD_ONE_TABLES`, today Sable only), else `NODES`. `index_of`, `node`, `path_to`, `icon_of`, `problems` and the instance readers (`offered`, `pick`, `_resolve`) route through it. `world.gd` sets `tree.world_one` in the line that sets `max_depth`. `NODES_WORLD_ONE` is a `static var` (GDScript cannot fold `Array.slice` into a const): `NODES` with index 3 replaced. `NODES` and `TREES` are untouched, so flag off every reader sees exactly today's data.
- **World-one Face Runner.** Same id and icon as the base node, `vault_air` only, so a valid vault restores one air jump and the same face locks for the default `Augments.VAULT_FACE_COOLDOWN` (1.5 s). No engine change: `augments.gd` already read both. The base Face Runner (index 9, 1 s lock) stays in the table and is not offered at two depths.
- **Cards.** `world_one_cards.gd` gains S1 to S6 rows. The S4 row ("Vaults restore jumps. Heavy fins steer harder.") names the Riveted Shell inversion, so it reads "Vaults restore jumps." while the shell is not fitted.
- **Test.** `tests/world_one_stree_test.gd`: the seam and the world.gd line, offers and cards flag on and off, each retained effect (S1 vault discharge, S2 doubled reach against a wall 3.25 m out, S3 fling plus the mass bonus once and only under 45 m/s, S5 one more chain jump, S6 mounted CC immunity with damage still landing), Face Runner's air jump and 1.5 s lock against the flag-off 1 s, and the three identities.
- **Open.** The crumb passes the flag (`tree_crumb.set_path` takes `world_one`, `tree_pick.gd` passes it), and `world_one_stree_test` asserts the crumb's `offered` holds world-one indices (Face Runner at 3, not 9); reverting the `tree_pick.gd` pass now fails it. World-one Face Runner is not offered at two depths only because the world-one depth cap (2) keeps the base index-9 node out of reach; raising the cap for world one would need that node dropped or hidden. `combat.gd` reads `TREES` only to find proc facts, which the world-one Face Runner does not use.

## world_route_test stage 3 sky pillars flake

`tests/world_route_test.gd` failed "stage 3 sky pillars" (3 world pillars, expected 2) twice, only in the parallel smoke run.

- **Root cause: the test, not the game.** `EventView._build_offer` makes each offer's beam visible; only `EventView._process` then hides all but the first open beam. The test counted after 4 physics ticks. Under load the engine runs several physics ticks per rendered frame, so all 4 ticks can pass before one `_process` runs and both stage 3 beams plus the vault pillar are still visible. Stages 1 and 2 have one offer, so the unhidden count stays at 2 and never trips. A player never sees this: every drawn frame runs `_process` first.
- **Reproduction.** `godot --headless --fixed-fps 1 --path sketches/core-loop -s res://tests/world_route_test.gd` (60 physics ticks per frame) fails on base 0ab0047 with exit 1 and "shows 3 world pillar(s)" in 4 of 4 runs (`--fixed-fps 2` failed its one run too); it passes without the flag.
- **Fix.** The test counts frames in `_process` and counts pillars only once at least 4 physics ticks and 2 rendered frames have passed since the stage was entered. No sleep or retry; the moment is defined by the view having updated. Same command: 0 of 4 fail, and `--fixed-fps 2` and the plain run pass. No game file changed.

## Rivet's world-one kit nodes and Run and Gun's wear trail (--world-one-builds=on, 2026-09-29)

Fifth slice of `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md` (### Rivet), after Kest, Sable and Mass-R. R1, R2, R3 and R5 are existing effects, R4 keeps half sprint drain, R6 keeps its extra charge; every knob already matched the table, so `rivet.gd`, `upgrade_tree.gd` and `build.gd` are unchanged and Rivet has no world-one node table. What this adds is the card copy, one signal, the R4 trail and the proof.

- **Cards.** `world_one_cards.gd` gains R1 to R6. The R6 row ("Bank two sprints. Heavy fins open during sprint.") names the Riveted Shell cancellation, so it reads "Bank two sprints." while the shell is not fitted. The Kest test's "no row, own effect" loop for Rivet now skips nodes that have a row (one condition, the row table decides).
- **Wear signal.** `RunState.wear_taken(removed)` fires from `wear_step` with the whole units taken, after the mass changes; nothing else reads it but the trail. It exists only with `--scrap=armor --wear=on`.
- **Trail.** `src/combat/wear_trail.gd`, created and stepped by one hook in `combat.gd` (`wear_trail.setup` in `setup`, `wear_trail.step` in the tick). Active only with the flag on and `r_run_gun` picked. Each whole unit of wear leaves one patch at the ball's ground, only while `state.grounded` and a floor lies within 2.5 m below the ball. 14 damage, 1 m radius measured to the enemy's hull, 2 s life, six live (a new patch retires the oldest), one hit per enemy per 0.5 s across all patches. Damage goes through `enemy.take_damage`, never `strike()`, so `enemy_struck` and everything that listens to it (items, chain, elements) never see it; a patch is made only by wear, so a patch cannot make a patch; nothing touches water. Look: a thin amber ring pulsing at 0.25 s and a short arc to a struck enemy, through combat's Effects. Sound: `wear_sputter` (soft, -14 dB), one per patch, audio connects to the trail's `patched` signal the way it connects `armor_hit`.
- **Known gaps.** Trail damage skips the delivered-damage ledger (`_record_delivered`), so it is not counted in the M0 ledger. **A patch never kills:** a kill posts `enemy_killed`, which build turns into on-kill item triggers (with the stale last strike kind), shrapnel bloom and Rampage's refund, and `build.gd` is read-only, so each hit is capped to leave 1 hp (`LAST_HP`) and an enemy at 1 hp takes no trail hit. A non-proc kill path or a source tag honoured by build's ON_KILL handler would let patches finish enemies; that is a follow-up needing `build.gd`. Patches freeze while the flow is not in play (combat's tick returns early). Whether the ring reads on screen and the sputter suits it are unjudged; only the signal and geometry are asserted.
- **Test.** `tests/world_one_rtree_test.gd`: offers and cards flag on and off, each retained effect (R4 through the real sprint step, R5 through the real rebound test), the trail's damage, radius, life, cap, hit gap, ground, no-strike, wear off, flag off and no-R4 cases, and the three identities.

## Spark kills: Run and Gun's patches finish enemies without proccing items (--world-one-builds=on, 2026-09-29)

Andrew ruled 2026-09-29: the wear patches deal their full 14 and may kill. A spark kill drops scrap and counts as a kill wherever bookkeeping counts one, but procs no items and no tree effects. This replaces the `LAST_HP` clamp from the wear-trail entry above.

- **Seam.** `enemy.gd` gains `last_damage_source` (default ""). `wear_trail._touch` sets it to "spark" on the enemy immediately before `take_damage` and clears it right after, so a hit that does not kill leaves nothing behind and the death, which emits inside `take_damage`, reads it. Deviation from the brief's optional `take_damage(amount, source)` argument: `warden.gd` and `plated_brute.gd` override `take_damage(amount)` with one parameter and are outside the fence, so a second argument would break a patch landing on either. `combat.gd`'s `died` handler passes `enemy.last_damage_source` to `run.record_kill(kind, at, scrap, source := "")`, which sets `run.last_kill_from_spark` immediately before it emits `enemy_killed` and clears it after. No new signal, and every other `record_kill` caller (boss.gd:786) is unchanged.
- **Consumers.** Skip a spark kill (read `run.last_kill_from_spark`): `build.gd` ON_KILL trigger post (items), `build.gd` `_on_enemy_killed` shrapnel_bloom kill record, and `combat._on_proc_fact` for `enemy_killed` (tree procs, Rampage's `refund_move`; connected with unbind so it cannot read arguments). Still see a spark kill: `build._on_enemy_killed` scrap drop, vault part and core; `run.kills`; `combat._record_retirement` and `_boss_add_ids`; `boss.gd:443`; `flow.gd:485`; `world.gd:433`; `audio.gd:789` kill sound; `juice.gd:107` kill juice.
- **Still true.** The patch skips `strike()`, so `enemy_struck`, chain and elements never see it, and the M0 delivered-damage ledger does not count trail damage.
- **Test.** `world_one_rtree_test`: 14 to a 30 hp enemy; a 10 hp enemy dies to a patch, drops scrap, counts in `run.kills`, posts no ON_KILL, adds no shrapnel_bloom record and gives no Rampage refund, while a strike-sourced kill in the same fixture does all three; the flag is clear after the emit.

## Hot-wired H1, Riveted Shell: the armour/air tradeoff (--hotwire-shell=on, 2026-09-29)

Second slice after unit G's flag (`design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md`, "### H1. Riveted Shell"). With the flag on the ball behaves as if the part is fitted. Catalogue entry, preview, cues, Fit/Recycle and the S4, R6 and K5 inversions are separate units and are not built.

- **Premise checked.** `run_state.gd` had `SCRAP_PER_HP = 10` and spilled 75% of `removed` inside `_damage_armored` (mitigation, then scrap, then hull); `ball_rules.drive_scale` returns `tuning.f("air_control")` airborne and 1.0 (or the brake boost) on the ground; `sheet.apply` clears every tuning mod and refills them on each `Build.recompute`.
- **Upside.** `RunState.scrap_per_hp` (default `SCRAP_PER_HP`) is the only cost `_damage_armored` reads; `removed = absorbed * scrap_per_hp`, so spill, `hit_loss` and `destroyed` follow the mass actually removed: a 12 HP post-mitigation hit on 200 mass takes 60 (spills 45, destroys 15), not 120, no hull loss. Build writes it at each recompute (`_apply_shell`); nothing else does. Size and mass-powered attacks read mass and are untouched; fitting adds no mass or hull.
- **Downside.** `_apply_shell` runs right after `sheet.apply` and multiplies the `air_control` tuning mod by 0.75, so it stacks with other parts (Slipstream x1.15 gives x0.8625) and survives every recompute. Ground steering, launch, jump, glide, speed and the collider read other keys and are unchanged (33 other tuning values compared).
- **The seam.** `Build.shell_air_factor()` (0.75, or 1.0 when not fitted) is the one place the air penalty is resolved, and `shell_scrap_per_hp()` the one for cost. An inversion (S4 for 2 s after a vault, K5 while gliding, R6 while sprinting) changes the return of the first; because build recomputes only on part changes, a timed inversion also needs a recompute or a direct mod refresh when its window opens. `ball_rules.gd` needed no change.
- **Armour mode off.** The upside does nothing: with `--scrap=armor` off `damage` skips the scrap layer, so cost is never read. The air penalty still applies, so the part is then a pure downside. That follows "behaves as if fitted"; the production offer (unit V) should not offer it outside armour mode.
- **Test.** `tests/hotwire_shell_test.gd`: the order in isolation, overflow to the hull, the rules' x0.75 airborne and unchanged grounded drive, and on the real Game the flag off equals base, fitting gives nothing, the mod survives repeated recomputes and a part's own air mod, and the armour-off case. Each check fails when `build.gd` or `run_state.gd` is reverted. Evidence limit: maths and a fixture; how the air penalty feels is unjudged.

## Debt pass 2: trail ledger, Face Runner guard, and the Brindle spark kill that cannot happen (2026-09-29)

Closes two of three open items and reclassifies the third. Old entries are unchanged.

- **Closed: trail damage in the M0 ledger.** `combat.gd` gives the wear trail a `deal` callable (`_trail_deal`). The patch still calls `take_damage` directly, never `strike()`, so `enemy_struck`, chain and elements see nothing, but the HP actually removed is now written through `_record_delivered` as action `spark`, source `persistent_actor`, with the target class read before the hit (a kill retires a boss add's tag). A 14 patch on a 10 hp enemy records 10 and 4 overkill. `_record_delivered` gained an optional `known_class` argument. Without `deal` set (a bare trail) the patch calls `take_damage` itself as before. This supersedes the "skips the ledger" gap in the wear-trail entry and the "ledger does not count trail damage" line in the spark-kills entry.
- **Closed: world-one Face Runner duplicate id.** The tree is unchanged. `world_one_stree_test` now fails if any id is offerable at two depths within `UpgradeTree.WORLD_ONE_DEPTHS`, fails if the cap reaches the depth of the base index-9 Face Runner while the id still sits at index 3 and 9, and proves the guard trips at cap 3. Raising the cap to 3 on a scratch copy fails two checks.
- **Not closed: spark kills on Brindle. A patch cannot kill a Brindle piece.** Pieces are in group `enemy`, so a patch does reach them and calls `boss_target.take_damage` -> `boss.take_damage`, but that only runs in state FIGHT and clamps hp at the phase floor (`maxf(_floor_hp(), hp - amount)`), and the floor at phase 3 is above 0; at the floor it calls `_begin_eject`, not a kill. The one `record_kill("brindle")` is `_defeat`, reached only from `_on_enemy_struck`, which fires from `strike()`; a patch never strikes. The scooter's `take_damage` returns 0 outside FIGHT. So no spark source is needed on the boss path today. Revisit if Brindle's HP model gains a killing floor or a patch is ever routed through `strike()`. The old rtree/spark "Open" line is retired as unreachable rather than fixed.
- **Test.** `world_one_rtree_test` (ledger delta of 10 with 4 overkill, one patch adds one hit of 14; fails with the `deal` line removed) and `world_one_stree_test` (three guard checks).

## Debt pass 3: the camper stops reading the wall clock, and the fuzz slice was never wall-budgeted (2026-09-29)

- **Fixed: `loop_camper_test` full span.** It ended at `FULL_WALL_MS` of wall time once 30 s of sim had passed. Under load that was 30 s of sim with 4 windows, and the verdict (`windows >= 10`) failed with the same 1 core miss an unloaded 80 s run passes with. Reproduced deterministically by shrinking the budget to 1000 ms in a scratch copy on base: `30 s camping ... windows 4 ... FAIL`. Both spans now end on sim time alone (`CAMP_SECONDS`, `MIN_WINDOWS`) and `lasted` needs the whole span, so the verdict cannot depend on machine speed. Both spans then shrank to 120 s hold and 60 s full with window floors of 15 and 8 (this seed opens about 0.16 windows per sim second; measured 19 and 9), so the fixed span fits the smoke's 120 s per-test limit with headroom: 83 s wall for 240 + 80 s at load 11 became 48 s for 180 s of sim at load 15. The bot runner still measures 240 s of both. A wall-clock cap was the earlier fix for the timeout; it is what made the verdict load-dependent, so the timeout margin now comes from a shorter fixed span instead. Mutant: a camper that removes 1 Warden HP every 10 s fails both checks (776 / 800 and 788 / 800).
- **Premise partly false: `item_fuzz`.** No wall-clock budget decides how many seeds run. The seed list and `--seconds` are fixed, each seed ends on physics ticks (the tool sets `SimClock.ticks`, and `--fixed-fps 90` makes a frame a tick), and the only wall-clock read (`Time.get_ticks_usec()` at the top of `_physics_process`) feeds `tick_p99_ms` and the `frame_time` anomaly, which the smoke prints but does not fail. A run under 48 busy loops on 32 cores passed all four checks (7 soft frame_time anomalies, 200 s wall). So the ledger's `8 seed records` failure is not reproduced and not explained by a wall budget; the likely load effect is the child process outrunning the smoke's timeout or the child dying. The smoke now prints exit code, script-error presence and the per-seed span term in the "fuzz slice ran" line so the next failure names its term. `item_fuzz.gd` is unchanged; the M0 note that it "reads the wall clock" is accurate but it is not a verdict input.

## S4 Face Runner inversion: the shell's air factor flips after a vault (--hotwire-shell=on --world-one-builds=on, 2026-09-29)

First of the three hot-wired inversions (S4, then R6, then K5; `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md`). Only the inversion; the vault restore and 1.5 s face lock are the world-one Face Runner's and are unchanged.

- **Rule.** With Riveted Shell fitted and Sable's world-one Face Runner taken, a valid vault sets `Build._shell_window` to 2.0 s; while it is above zero `Build.shell_air_factor()` returns 1.25 (`SHELL_AIR_FACTOR_VAULT`) instead of 0.75. A second vault inside the window resets it to 2.0 s (per vault, not stacking). Flag or node missing: nothing changes. Ground handling, jump count, vault detection and the face lock are untouched.
- **Trigger.** The `vault_kicked` event, the one `augments.gd` posts for a valid vault, which `build.gd` already listens to for ON_VAULT. "S4 taken" is `tree.world_one` and `s_face_runner` in `tree.picks`: the base Face Runner (index 9) has the same id, and `world_one` tells them apart. No `sable.gd` marker or new knob was needed.
- **Refresh: both.** Build recomputes only on part changes, so the window opens with a direct `_refresh_air_mod()` (part-derived `sheet.mod("air_control")` times the current factor) and closes with the same call from `Build._physics_process`. `_apply_shell` already multiplies by `shell_air_factor()`, which now reads the live window, so a recompute inside the window keeps the flip and stacks with a part's own mod (Slipstream x1.15 gives x1.4375, then x0.8625 after). Chosen over reading the factor live in `ball_rules.drive_scale` because the mod is the seam H1 established and `ball_rules.gd` stays unchanged.
- **Clock.** The window counts down by the physics `delta` inside Build's `_physics_process`, after its `in_play` guard, so a paused tree or the bay does not spend it. `SimClock.msec()` is the wall clock in play and was not used. A stage reset does not clear the window (at most 2 s of carry).
- **Test.** `tests/hotwire_s4_test.gd`: x0.75 before, x1.25 after a vault, still x1.25 at 1.8 s, x0.75 at 2.2 s; a second vault restarts the window; a recompute inside the window keeps it and stacks with a part; close with a part fitted; no S4 (Cannonball only), the base Face Runner (world-one off; the test picks Cannonball, Live Wire, Face Runner to reach heap index 9 and asserts it is in the picks) and no shell all leave the factor as it was. Scratch mutants each fail it: no refresh on open (3 checks), no refresh on close (3), recompute ignoring the window (1), no restart (1), no S4 gate (3), window never ticking (6), factor ignoring the window (4), dropping the `tree.world_one` test from `_face_runner_s4()` (the base Face Runner case; an earlier version of the test picked only Cannonball there and let this mutant survive), and the whole change reverted (script error, hangs to the timeout).
- **Evidence limit.** The vault is the posted `vault_kicked` event on the real Game, not a kick off a wall; vault detection is `combat_vault_test`'s and `world_one_stree_test`'s. How x1.25 for 2 s feels is unjudged.
- **Follow-ups.** The S4 card row in `world_one_cards.gd` ("Vaults restore jumps. Heavy fins steer harder.") was held back until H1 landed; it can be added now, outside this slice's fence. A cue for the window opening (ring or sound) is not built.

## Smoke runner: wall-time tests run alone after the parallel batch (2026-09-29)

- **Change.** A test file that declares `# smoke: solo` in its first 5 lines is run by `scripts/sketch-smoke.mjs` one at a time after the parallel batch, while the machine-wide lock is still held. Unmarked files run in the batch as before. The summary now reads `N solo (Ts)`. Caps are unchanged. A new timing test opts in where it is written; the runner has no list. `scripts/sketch-smoke.test.mjs` covers the parsing (marker at the top, an unmarked file, a marker past line 5 or inside a string).
- **Marked (verdict reads wall time):** `presentation_music_test` (`ms < 200` per second of music), `presentation_themes_test` (`RATIO_CAP` on paired CPU-time runs, wall time where /proc is missing), `presentation_ace_director_test` (no main-thread frame over `FRAME_LIMIT_MS`, skips without the ACE package), `presentation_juice_test` (real freeze under 100 ms), `spine_restart_test` (retry and replay under `SECOND_USEC`), `stage_gorge_perf_test` (stage 2 median frame time against stage 1's).
- **Left parallel:** `presentation_chain_test` and `presentation_hitstop_test` schedule steps on `get_ticks_msec` but assert counts and ordering, not a duration. `presentation_audio`, `bass`, `rows`, `retry`, `escalation` and `juice`'s waits only delay. `presentation_ceremony_test` uses a 45 s deadline as a timeout. `hero_warden_assets_test` prints its timing. `presentation_ace_test` prints its total.
- **Evidence.** Under 30 busy loops (load 35 to 46 on 32 threads, `SMOKE_JOBS=7`), the old runner failed `presentation_music_test` twice in two full smokes (219 and 239 ms against the 200 ms cap); the new runner passed all six marked tests in both of its two smokes. Both runners also timed out `item_fuzz_smoke_test` at the 120 s limit under that load; that is a timeout, not a marked test, and is not touched here. Cost: an idle-ish full smoke went from 383 s to 456 s (load 11 and 9), of which the solo phase is about 85 s, mostly `presentation_themes_test` at 45 s. Two of the three planned rounds per runner ran; the third was skipped because the busy loops were slowing other lanes.

## R6 Second Wind inversion: the shell's air penalty cancels while sprinting (--hotwire-shell=on --world-one-builds=on, 2026-09-29)

Second of the three hot-wired inversions (S4 merged, then R6, then K5; `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md`). Only the cancellation; the extra sprint charge is the existing `r_second_wind` node row (`charges: {movement: 1}`, `rivet.gd`), which needed no marker.

- **Rule.** With Riveted Shell fitted and Rivet's world-one Second Wind taken, `Build.shell_air_factor()` is 1.0 (`SHELL_AIR_FACTOR_SPRINT`) while `kit.sprinting` is true, instead of 0.75; outside a sprint the penalty returns. "R6 taken" is `tree.world_one` and `r_second_wind` in `tree.picks`. The `world_one` gate is parity with S4's `_face_runner_s4()`, not a need: Rivet has no world-one node table, so the id is unambiguous at every flag state. It follows the brief's "world-one Second Wind" and is the coordinator's to confirm.
- **Trigger source: the `kit.sprinting` edge, polled.** No sprint-end signal exists (`game_events.gd` declares only `sprint_started`), and adding one would touch `augments.gd` and `game_events.gd`, outside the fence. `augments.gd` ends a sprint at three sites (SPRINT flag off, drained or released, and the slot/cruise follow-through); polling the one live state covers all three. `shell_air_factor()` also reads `kit.sprinting` live, so a recompute at any moment is correct.
- **Refresh points.** `_refresh_air_mod()` runs when Build's `_physics_process` sees `kit.sprinting` change (start and end alike), in addition to S4's window open and close; `_apply_shell()` multiplies by the live factor on every recompute, so a part's own mod stacks (Slipstream x1.15 gives x1.15 while sprinting, x0.8625 after).
- **Clock.** Physics ticks, after Build's `in_play` guard, like S4's window. The poll can lag the player by up to one physics tick (the test bounds it at 2, never same-frame). A sprint that ends while not in play (a pause, the bay) refreshes late, on the first in-play tick; not fixed with a wall-clock read (`SimClock.msec()` is rejected above).
- **S4 and R6 precedence: an implementation choice, not a design ruling.** BUILD-SYSTEMS.md:84 lists S4 (flip for 2 s after a vault), K5 (flip while gliding) and R6 (cancel during sprint) as separate conditions and does not rank them; the design text is silent. Trees are per class, so S4 (Sable) and R6 (Rivet) cannot co-occur through `UpgradeTree.pick` (the test asserts Rivet cannot take `s_face_runner`). For a forced overlap `shell_air_factor()` takes the highest active factor (`maxf`), so a vault window over a sprint gives 1.25, in either order, and the rule generalizes to K5 without a ranking.
- **Test.** `tests/hotwire_r6_test.gd`: x0.75 before a sprint, x1.0 while sprinting airborne (sprint driven through `intent.movement_pressed/held`), a recompute during the sprint with a part, x0.75 again after release, after a drain and after the SPRINT flag goes off (each within 2 ticks), no R6, no shell, world-one off, and the forced S4+R6 overlap in both orders. Scratch mutants each fail it: sprint ignored (6 checks), no refresh on start (5), no refresh on end (3), no R6 pick gate (1), no `world_one` gate (1), sprint overriding the vault window (2); reverting `build.gd` alone leaves the test calling missing methods and it hangs to the timeout, as S4's did.
- **Evidence limit.** A fixture on the real Game with the ball lifted into the air; the sprint start is the game's own player tick, but nothing here says the cancelled penalty feels different in play.
- **Follow-ups.** The R6 card line in `world_one_cards.gd` stays held back (`world_one_rtree_test` asserts R6 has no row); adding it is outside this fence. K5 is next.

## Unit V, first half: Riveted Shell's entry, preview and gated offer (2026-09-29)

BUILD-SYSTEMS.md defines V as two serial units (choice, preview and transaction, then cues). This is the entry, preview and offer gate only. Riveted Shell still comes from `--hotwire-shell`; nothing in this slice fits it.

- **Built.** `Parts.HOTWIRED` holds the row, read by `Parts.hotwired("riveted_shell")`: name, tag `mass`, the label template, and an `upside` and a `downside`, each an icon id (`cat_defense`, `cat_mobility`) and at most three words. It is a separate table, not in `CATALOGUE`, so it never enters `rows()`, `ids()`, `offer_pool()` or `roll()` (BUILD-SYSTEMS.md: hot-wired parts are authored offers, never loot). `Parts.hotwire_offer(armor_mode, held)` returns the hot-wired ids an offer may show: none with armour mode off (the part is a pure downside then), none once a hot-wired part is held. `src/ui/hotwire_choice.gd` is the preview: `resolve(id)` fills the templates and the control draws two rows, upside then downside, one icon box, font and row height for both, tinted brass and rust at the same alpha. It reads "2x armour" and "Air steering -25%"; the label reads "Double armour. Air steering -25%.".
- **Derived, not typed.** The multiplier is `RunState.SCRAP_PER_HP / Build.SHELL_SCRAP_PER_HP` (10/5), the steering percent `round((1 - Build.SHELL_AIR_FACTOR) * 100)` (25). The templates use `{armor}`, `{armor_x}` and `{steer}`, so a tuning change moves the words. The multiplier and percent are resolved in the UI file because `build.gd` preloads `parts.gd`; a preload back would be a cycle.
- **Gate source.** The function takes a bool. The caller reads `tuning.armor()` or `RunState.armor_mode` (synced by `Build._sync_scrap_flags`); it does not test `wear_on` or `scrap_per_hp`.
- **Test.** `tests/hotwire_shell_offer_test.gd`: entry fields, derived words, 200 seeded offers (0 with armour off, all with it on, 0 when held), a digest of `ids()`, `rows()`, seven pool rarities and 50 seeded rolls (seeds 1000-1049) equal to base 39b0f3f, and the preview's measured icon (40x40), font (22) and row height (48) for both rows plus at most 8 words across them (5). Scratch mutants each fail a named check: gate removed (armour off), entry added to `rows()` (loot and parity), downside row dropped (two rows) and a smaller downside font (equal font).
- **Not built.** Fit and Recycle, the cog payout, wiring the offer into a live gate, shop or fabricator (the first offer after Warden I and the one-copy rule are the caller's), look and sound cues, and inversion-aware preview values (S4, K5 and R6 flip the air factor; the preview shows the plain tradeoff). `build.gd` is unchanged.
- **Evidence limit.** Layout measured headless; how the rows read on screen is unjudged.

## K5 Smoke Trail inversion: the shell's air factor is x1.25 while gliding (--hotwire-shell=on --world-one-builds=on, 2026-09-29)

Last of the three hot-wired inversions (S4, R6, K5; `design-v3/research/build-systems/BUILD-SYSTEMS.md` K5 row and inversion rule). Only the flip; keeping the cloak while gliding already existed (`combat.gd` `glide_cloak`, the `k_smoke_trail` knob) and is confirmed, not changed.

- **Rule.** With Riveted Shell fitted and Kest's world-one Smoke Trail taken, `Build.shell_air_factor()` is 1.25 (the existing `SHELL_AIR_FACTOR_VAULT`, no new const) while `kit.gliding` is true, instead of 0.75; when the glide ends it returns. "K5 taken" is `tree.world_one` and `k_smoke_trail` in `tree.picks`. As with R6 the `world_one` gate is parity, not a need: `k_smoke_trail` has one id at every flag state, and the base tree's node would otherwise trip it. The coordinator confirms.
- **Trigger source: the `kit.gliding` edge, polled.** `augments._glide` writes `kit.gliding` each tick and no end signal exists. `Build._physics_process` polls it after the `in_play` guard beside the sprint edge and calls `_refresh_air_mod()` on any change, only when the shell is fitted. `shell_air_factor()` also reads it live, so a recompute mid-glide keeps 1.25 and stacks with a part's own mod (Slipstream x1.15 gives x1.4375, then x0.8625).
- **Precedence.** K5 is one more `maxf` branch, the same code choice as S4 and R6 (not a design ruling). With every factor at most 1.25 it cannot lower another; a mutant that assigns 1.25 instead of `maxf` is equivalent today (nothing exceeds 1.25) and the test cannot tell it apart.
- **Glide itself unchanged.** `air_control` scales only airborne horizontal acceleration; `augments._glide` reads `gravity`, `glide_gravity` and `glide_lift`. `src/movement/` is untouched. The test drives the same input with K5+shell, no shell, and the K6 path from the same height: per-tick v.y over 90 ticks, the tick gliding starts (45) and the final fall speed (-2.6881) are equal to 0.0001.
- **Test.** `tests/hotwire_k5_test.gd`. The glide is real Kest input: a jump press spends the air jump (fixture: `state.air_actions` set to 1 beforehand, as the lifted ball never touched ground), the held jump makes `augments._glide` set `kit.gliding` after 46 ticks; `kit.gliding` is never assigned. Checks: x0.75 before, x1.25 gliding, x0.75 within 2 ticks after release, recompute mid-glide, cloak kept, K6 path, no shell, world-one off, K5+S4 in both orders (forced `s_face_runner` pick), K5+R6 in both orders, glide trace equal to base. The R6 overlap needs a sprint Kest does not have: the fixture swaps the BLINK augment for SPRINT, gives the movement slot a duration and a charge; the sprint still starts from the real movement press. Scratch mutants (each on `build.gd`, reverted): no K5 branch (6 checks fail), no refresh on start (4), no refresh on end (1), no pick gate (1), no `world_one` gate (1), K5 assigns 1.0 over S4 and R6 (7), K5 assigns 0.5 (7). K5 assigning 1.25 instead of `maxf` survives as an equivalent mutant.
- **Evidence limit.** A fixture on the real Game with the ball lifted 30 m; how x1.25 steering in a glide feels is unjudged.
- **Follow-ups.** The K5 card row in `world_one_cards.gd` stays held back (the invcue unit). Cue: the glide edge refreshes through `_refresh_air_mod()`, so the invcue entry's `shell_air_changed` cue covers the flip with no K5 code (`hotwire_cue_test` K5 checks, added at wave integration).

## Inversion cards and one air-factor cue (--hotwire-shell=on --world-one-builds=on, 2026-09-29)

S4 and R6 gain their card rows, and every change of Riveted Shell's resolved air factor now shows and sounds once. Nothing about the factor, its numbers or any mechanic changed.

- **Cards.** `world_one_cards.gd` ROWS gains `s_face_runner` ("Vaults restore jumps. Heavy fins steer harder.", BUILD-SYSTEMS.md S4) and `r_second_wind` ("Bank two sprints. Heavy fins open during sprint.", R6). `world_one_stree_test` and `world_one_rtree_test` had pinned them as held back; only those pins now assert the ruling text and `has_row`. `k_smoke_trail` (K5) keeps no row and its held-back comment; `world_one_ktree_test` is untouched and passes.
- **One emit point.** `Build` declares `shell_air_changed(factor, rose)`. `_note_air_factor()` runs at the end of `_refresh_air_mod()` and `_apply_shell()`, the two places the factor is applied, so S4, R6 and the K5 glide edge (which refreshes through `_refresh_air_mod()`) are covered with no per-inversion code. It emits only when the resolved factor differs from the last one read while the shell was fitted; `rose` is `factor > SHELL_AIR_FACTOR`. `shell_air_factor()` is byte-identical.
- **Silent cases (pinned).** The first read of a fit, fitting or unfitting the shell (option toggle plus recompute: 0 cues, the 0.75 to 1.0 step is the tradeoff, not an inversion), a recompute inside a window or sprint, and a second vault restarting the S4 window (the factor is already 1.25). Unfitting resets the memory, so an inversion that opens before the first fitted read of a run is also silent (a vault before any recompute since the shell was fitted); the game recomputes on every part change and stage entry, so this only bites a fixture.
- **On the ground.** The factor changes whenever Rivet sprints with R6 taken, airborne or not, so a grounded sprint cues too (one rise on start, one return on end; `hotwire_cue_test` pins it). Nothing gates on air state because the resolved factor is the fact.
- **Visual.** `hero_assets.gd` `air_cue()`: a flat ring (TorusMesh, unshaded, radius 0.62, on the player's HeroPod, so it exists on Sable, Kest and Rivet, not only on Kest's vanes), `AIR_CUE_SECONDS` 0.45, fading out. Rise swells out (scale 0.9 to 1.9); return draws in (1.9 to 0.9). juice.gd's shell pulse (heft) is untouched, so the motion carries no second fact. No CanvasLayer, ColorRect or `vfx.gd` flash.
- **Sound.** `audio.gd` `_on_shell_air` plays `world_fact` with the row's sound `shell_spring` at a fifth up (`SHELL_RISE_SEMITONES` 7) on the rise and a fourth down (`SHELL_RETURN_SEMITONES` -5) on the return: one recipe, two directions an octave apart. Keyed to the live key (`KEYED_SOUNDS`).
- **One row, one sound.** `event_kinds.gd` WORLD_ROWS `shell_air_shift` (group movement, `Palette.AMBER`, shape ring, sound `shell_spring`); the ring's colour and shape and the cue's sound id are read from it. The row sits in WORLD_ROWS because it names no GameEvents signal (spine_event_kinds_test's orphan rule). New because no existing row carries this fact: `ride_glide_drop` and the other WORLD_ROWS rows are sandstone ride cues with whooshes, `world_fact` rows name terrain and lifts, `part_gained`/`reward_chosen` mean loot, `jumped` (air jump) and `vault_kicked` mean the player's own action. New sound `shell_spring` (SfxBank, trim -9 dB, about 0.27 s) because the nearest, the spring snap inside `drop_landing`, is fused to a reward note; `dash_whoosh`, `boost_whoosh` and `grapple_release` read as motion, not a released spring (BUILD-SYSTEMS.md:83). `ui_tick`, `scrap_tick` and `found_chime` mean UI, scrap and discovery.
- **Test.** `tests/hotwire_cue_test.gd`, counting the signal, the ring nodes and the sound log per scenario: S4 (1 rise on open, 1 return on close, none on recompute or a second vault), R6 airborne and grounded, K5 glide start and end (real Kest glide input; added at wave integration), shell off (vault and sprint: 0), shell toggle (0), shell on without the inversion (0), rise and return differ (pitch 1.498 vs 0.749, ring 0.9 vs 1.9), colour/shape/sound from the row, no overlay node.
- **Scratch mutants (each fails its checks, reverted).** no emit; emit on every refresh; same cue for rise and return; no shell gate (fails the toggle check); no sound; no ring; ring under a CanvasLayer; same ring motion both ways; same pitch both ways.
- **Evidence limit.** Counts, parameters and node types on the real Game, headless. Whether the ring reads at chase distance and how the spring feels are unjudged; Andrew's play decides both.
- **Follow-ups.** K5's card row. If the ring is too faint on a heavy shell, tune `AIR_CUE_RADIUS`; the pitch split lives in two constants in `audio.gd`.

Smoke Trail's world-one card now shows its ruling text, "Glide cloaked. Heavy fins steer harder." (BUILD-SYSTEMS.md K5). No mechanic, number or cue changed.

Closes the haze perf debt (lane-ledger "haze": inconclusive at load 19 to 25). Ten native `perf_bench` runs on s3 at 1920x1080 (Godot 4.7.2, RTX 4080, X11), strictly alternating classic, haze x5 each, warm 300 frames and 20 s, load1 3.16 to 5.02 before each, none discarded. Mode proved by the command line plus a probe of `Painter.haze_on()` and the material key; `mark_materials` is 14 in both modes, so it is not a mode receipt.
