## Harpoon and winch: the feel prototype (--harpoon=off|shot|lock, --harpoon-pull=self|heft, --harpoon-keys=shift|keep, 2026-09-30)

One flag-gated prototype of Andrew's direction: RMB fires a line for every pilot, a winch shortens it, and the looser end travels. Every flag defaults to off, so the Part 1 lab build plays as before. It serves the Warden demand (the harpoon is a drop finisher, never a bypass) and strains "momentum is sacred": Ctrl on a body line is a commit the player chose, and a haul never slows the ball.

- **What it is.** The line lives on `AugmentKit` beside the grapple's fields (`on_body`, `anchor_body`, `anchor_offset`, `line_state` IDLE/LATCHED/SMASH, `ball_share`, `smash_left`, `smash_floor`, `tow`, `rewind_left`), and `Augments._grapple` stays the one rope. `harpoon.gd` holds the static rules: `ball_share()`, `target()` (shot or lock over `Augments.grapple_target`, which now takes a mask and returns the body as `AIM_BODY`) and `burst_ready()`.
- **Flags.** `--harpoon=off|shot|lock` (shot: the centre ray takes the enemy under it; lock: an enemy inside `harpoon_lock_deg`, sticky out to 1.5x, seen from the ball by one world ray, else the shot). `--harpoon-pull=self|heft` (self: you always fly in; heft: the ball's share is weight / (weight + heft), 1 for weight 100 and up, and the body is towed the rest). `--harpoon-keys=shift|keep` (shift: Shift is the class burst when its charge is ready and the plain boost otherwise; keep: the burst is on C). F1 rows: `harpoon_smash_speed` 24, `harpoon_lock_deg` 10, `harpoon_rewind` 1.0.
- **Rules.** RMB fires, holding it winches a line on a body (a world line keeps Sable's swing and Q winch; Rivet and Kest also winch a world line on RMB). Ctrl while latched to a body commits the SMASH: the haul homes at `max(speed, smash_speed x share)` and never slows, for at most 1.2 s. Contact releases the line first, the ordinary stomp, ram or rebound resolves, then `slammed` and `harpoon_smashed` fire once each and the hook rewinds for `harpoon_rewind` s (a kill or a stomp re-seats it). Ctrl is neither brake nor slam while a body line stands and until that press is let go. Every other exit keeps the velocity: RMB release, timeout, jump, boost or dash, the body dying, a stretch past 1.5x the rope range, any outright velocity set (a ride, a launch, a deflect), a landed hostile hold, and (latched only) passing inside the minimum rope and the radii, so an enemy line has no pendulum. Walkable ground (normal.y above 0.7) cannot be hooked; other surfaces can, for every pilot. Under heft `enemy.tow(v)` writes the planar knock without `knock()`, so pins do not re-key every tick.
- **Cues.** `harpoon_bit` and `harpoon_smashed` each have one `EventKinds` row (no HUD words) and are heard through `CHARACTER_KINDS`, on `grapple_clink` and `slam_boom`, never the Hooker's `atk_e06` cues. The rows hold Sable's colour and `vfx.gd` draws all three grapple glyphs in the live class colour; `grapple_fired` no longer hard-codes AMBER (Kest's). The reticle draws four corner brackets on an `AIM_BODY` target, the rope brightens during a haul, and a class-colour head sits in the bitten body. The ability bar's movement key reads Shift or C for Rivet and Kest under the harpoon.
- **Warden.** `warden.gd` and `warden_drop.gd` are untouched. From the bowl floor a line smash scores `floor`: 0 damage and the clank. Off a lip, a line smash onto the top cap at 20 m/s or more descending, from beyond 50 m within 6 s, scores `drop`. Each smash's landing shockwave also strikes every body in reach, the landed one included, except a Warden judging drops: its second strike would score `floor` inside the rearm and clank, so the shockwave spares it and every other landed body still takes its strike (bug hunt C35, `harpoon_smash_landing_test`).
- **Bots.** `bot_driver.gd` gets a harpoon hand for the ram lap: it bites the fight target when the reticle holds it, holds RMB and Ctrl through `_drive`'s `source.hold`, and commits. It is the bowl-floor camper's hand: grounded, within 30 m of the mast and more than `DROP_MEMORY` after the ball was last beyond the drop line.
- **Exposure found (X1).** With only the floor and grounded gates the same hand dropped the Warden in 4 of 4 `loop_camper_test` runs (Warden 213 to 621 of 800 HP; evidence in `artifacts/local/harpoon/`). The mechanism is the drop rule working as written: a bite while still inside `DROP_MEMORY` of the approach makes the smash an arrival off the map that is not over the core, the rule answers `miss` and throws the ball to the lift, and the lift and lip give a real drop lap. So a camper with a harpoon laps sooner and cheaper than one without. The gated hand is the `floor` case the plan names; a rim harpooner bot and a cap or cost on a haul that starts inside the drop memory are open work for the design.
- **Tests.** `harpoon_line_test` (rules through real contacts), `harpoon_warden_test` (camper 0 damage over four windows with four smashes landed; the drop-finisher positive case) and a harpoon arm in `loop_camper_test` (the bot lands smashes on the Warden and deals 0). Each fails under a scratch mutant of the rule it guards (15 for the line test, 3 for the Warden test, 2 for the camper arm, 1 for the flag rows; all reverted).
- **Evidence limit.** Rules, real physics from placed starts, a fixed camera and a scripted or bot pilot. Native frame cost is measured by the verifier on workspace 10. How any of it feels is unjudged.
- **Open play questions (Part 3).** Is shot a skill you would grow into, or a chore next to lock? At heft 1 fodder meets you halfway under heft: too much for the base? Does the yank into pins at heft 3 read as growth you earned? Did you miss the plain boost as Rivet or Kest under shift, or does Shift-as-escape belong in your hand? Is a shelf haul at 24 m/s onto the Warden a fair drop or a cheat? Does Sable still feel like the rope pilot? Does the lock grab the wrong enemy in a crowd? Is RMB-hold winching a body line intuitive, given the reel felt unintuitive in entry 3?

## Look pass 4a: lighting and material correction

Built from the independent pass-3 judgment (`look-pass-3/judge/JUDGE.md`). Appearance files only; no meshes, proportions, camera, collision, UI or input changed. Sun azimuth -55 and exposure 1.1 are kept as JUDGE.md directs.

- **Shared rig** (`graphics_preset.gd`): fix round 4a-2. Sun 1.75 to 2.35 with color `ffd49a` for warm crest planes; ambient 0.45 to 0.28 with color `9db5cf`; the SkyFill now sits opposite the sun's yaw (`sun yaw + 180`; it was at 298, the same side as the sun at azimuth -55, so it filled nothing) at energy 0.5 and color `a3c4e6`, so the shaded right wall and dam read as cool recesses against sunlit warm planes. Fog density 0.0004 to 0.0006, color `a3bdd0`, sky affect 0.25, sun scatter 0.25. Fill 0.30 with fog 0.0009 turned the frame blue-grey and was backed off.
- **Sky** (`palette.gd`): ridge layers `a5b3b4`/`8c9ea6` to `93aecb`/`7d9dc0`, horizon `e6c898`, zenith `4a78a8`, for blue distance under a warm horizon.
- **Spill mist** (`spillway_scenery.gd`): the alpha stays 0.12 (`spillway_scenery_test` caps it), tint `bcd4da`. Card count goes from 16 to 116: 14 base cards and 15 ledge cards per fall, the ledge ones at y 38 to 58 so they sit above the rim line from the launch camera. The cards are two-triangle quads with a radial gradient alpha (the 25000-triangle budget rules out spheres; the test now reads 23244). Only the four falls carry it. In the launch frame it shows as a pale, slightly blocky dashed band at the dam foot: restrained but still weak.
- **Warden** (`hero_assets.gd`): shadowless 9 m OmniLight `a8c2d4` at 0.9 on the Warden's base, offset to the camera side. Ancient enamel is untouched.
- **Moss** (`hero_assets.gd`): fix round 4a-2. The amber lamp goes from 1.4 to 7.0, range 5 to 8, attenuation 1.3, plus a second shadowless amber light (`ffbb70`, 4.5, range 5) under the roof at y 5.2, and the baked pool alpha 0.26 to 0.42. No cool entry fill. The rug, slats and shutter now carry amber; the interior is still teal-dominant.
- **Pilot glass** (`hero_assets.gd`): roughness 0.08 to 0.32, specular 0.7 to 0.08 plus an edge term of 0.5, alpha 0.05 plus 0.22 at the edge, and the glass no longer casts a shadow. The forehead hotspot is gone in the frame.
- **Pilot speckling (corrected).** An earlier version of this entry blamed the glass shell's shadow; that was wrong, the stipple survived turning the glass caster off. Measured by toggling one thing per capture on the same frame: SSAO off, pilot meshes not casting, and the pilot texture removed all left the stipple; sun shadows off removed it; `light_angular_distance` 0 cut it to a faint pattern; shadow blur 0 made hard acne. Cause: the sun's `light_angular_distance` 1.5 turns on PCSS, and its noisy sampling dithers the penumbra of the pod hoop's real shadow across the fox's head, cheek and chest (the hard-filter capture shows that hoop shadow as a stepped band). Fix in `graphics_preset.gd`: `light_angular_distance` 0.0 and `RenderingServer.directional_soft_shadow_filter_set_quality(SHADOW_QUALITY_SOFT_HIGH)`. The face is smooth in the final frame. Every cast shadow in the game now uses the fixed-width soft filter, so contact shadows are a little less variable in softness.

Not judged here: Codex scores these frames independently. Geometry (Warden upper mass, Moss alcove, cliff profile) is the next pass.

## Look pass 4a-3: mist cleanup

Codex's blind judgment kept 4a and asked for one bounded fix: the spill mist at the dam foot read as a dashed grid of pale blocks. Only `spillway_scenery.gd` and this entry changed.

- **Cause.** The shared `_batch` material sets `uv1_triplanar` at 1/6 scale, so the mist's radial falloff texture was projected in world space and tiled every 6 m, printing hard-edged tiles across the overlapping cards. The 5-wide grid placement then lined them up. A softer texture alone would not have fixed it.
- **Fix.** WaterMist turns triplanar off and takes the quad's own UVs (copied for that batch only), so each card carries one radial falloff. The falloff is five eased stops (alpha 1, 0.7, 0.3, 0.06, 0) instead of two. Placement is a seeded cluster per fall: 11 base patches (10 to 22 by 6 to 13 m, y 30 to 42, z 211 to 222, x within 15 m of the fall) and 8 ledge patches (8 to 16 by 5 to 11 m, y 38 to 58, z 222 to 225), each rolled in the card plane, up to 0.7 and 1.0 rad. No row, column or grid.
- **Kept.** Alpha 0.12, mist only at the four falls, one batch, 23,164 triangles, 6 batches (the test's budget). The mist is fainter and softer than before; the fix did not raise opacity.
- **Not touched.** The beige square puffs around the Warden's mast in the launch frame come from another effect and are unchanged.

## Look pass 4 geometry: Moss sandstone shelter

Built from the approved geometry storyboard (`look-pass-4/geometry/GEOMETRY.md`, Moss section). Visual only: no collider, target, telegraph or gameplay constant changed, and the new mesh has no collision.

- **New recipe** `tools/blender/moss_shelter.py` writes `assets/models/moss_shelter.glb`: two side cheeks (four chamfered strata each, broken at different heights per side, 0.47 to 0.64 m thick, |z| 3.3 to 4.0, fronts varying from X -15.6 to -14.6) and a rear skin, all inside the cabin collider's X -19.5..-14.5, Z -4..4, Y 0..6; a neck over the same footprint to Y 7.55; and one asymmetrical cap (10 perimeter points, 6 height rings with a monotone bulge so the flank is one mass, front lip 1.1 to 1.7 m thick, underside scooped 0.40 m, crown of two offset planes meeting at a fold) at X -19.5..-11.5, Z -6.3..6.3, Y 7.35..9.2, over the awning at 7.1. One mesh, one slot; 578 triangles against the 1,600 ceiling.
- **Attachment** (`hero_assets.gd`, `_dress_shelter`, called from `_dress_bay`): the glb is a child of `Barge` beside `HeroWorkshop`, so it shares every dock and park transform and never follows the shutter. A single shared `StandardMaterial3D` matches the scenery `_batch` contract (roughness 1.0, `sandstone-strata`, 6 m triplanar, vertex colour, tint (1.12, 1.32, 1.55), which cancels the orange cast of the strata texture); vertex colour carries warmer crown planes and a muted underside. No `wet-spill-stone`, no new light.
- **Workshop recipe**: the hull box no longer overhangs the stone (4.36 x 6.05 x 6.8, rear at X -18.86) and the riveted roof overhang box, which would have buried the neck, is removed. Front dressing, shutter, awning, shelves, rug and sign are unchanged.
- **Test** `tests/moss_shelter_test.gd`, on a running game: it fails if any stone vertex below Y 7.35 leaves the live cabin collider's volume, if any vertex above it leaves the cap envelope, if any stone sits ahead of the cabin front below the cap (the LANDING, PAD and MOSS side), on a second mesh, surface or material, on a collision node, or when the shelter does not follow a moved barge. Lowering the cap ceiling to 9.0 fails it.
- **Budget** (native 1920x1080, Barge subtree at the moss view, default preset, same script at base aeeacfe and here): barge vertices 32,496 to 33,786, triangles 46,142 to 46,708, renderer draw calls 142 to 145 (the counter includes shadow passes; the stone is one colour-pass draw and casts shadows).
- Not judged here: Codex scores the look blind. The Warden and cliff units are separate.

## Look pass 4 geometry round 2: Moss shelter as one broken sandstone mass

Direction: `look-pass-4/geometry/round2/ROUND2.md`, Moss section. Visual only; no collider, target or gameplay constant changed.

- **Recipe** `tools/blender/moss_shelter.py` rewritten in place. Cheeks and rear are single closed shells per side (0.46 to 0.64 m thick, |z| <= 4.0, X -19.5 to about -14.6) whose front edge steps in long oblique planes: visible side (Godot +Z) X -14.65 at Y0, -15.15 at Y2.3, -14.75 at Y4.8, -15.25 at Y6; the other side breaks at Y1.4 and Y4.2. No stacked slabs and no bevelled strata. The neck continues the same planes to Y7.62 (the brief said 7.55; the cap underside reaches Y7.61 over the cheek strip, so 7.55 would leave a gap) and is buried in the cap.
- **Cap**: 12 perimeter points inside X -19.5..-11.5, Z -6.3..6.3, underside 7.35 at the lip with the 0.40 m scoop (7.75 at the hub). The front points keep their belly (inset 0 to 0.10 m until the last ring); only the corners roll in. Crown: three planar regions (49/31/21% of top area) that share their ridge chords, so it is one closed rock. Rear plane crests at 9.187, the middle plane runs about 9.0 to 8.9, the forward shoulder about 8.85 to 8.76, and the lip is 1.41 to 1.50 m at the rim vertices.
- **Material**: `_dress_shelter` now uses `sandstone.gdshader` with `cliff_only = true`, the dry cliff rock calculation, roughness 0.94, no texture or tint, vertex colour white. To anchor the pattern in Barge-local metres the shader gained one uniform, `local_pattern` (default false, cliffs unchanged), which takes the position and normal from the mesh instead of the world. The shelter mesh sits at the Barge origin, so mesh-local is Barge-local, and the test checks that.
- **Test** `tests/moss_shelter_test.gd` measures the lip with vertical rays through the cap triangles (upper minus lower surface at the same X/Z, 0.15 m inside the front edge, columns where the edge is ahead of X -13): at least 70% must be 1.30 to 1.60 m and none under 1.10 m except the last 0.30 m at each end. `-- --mutate=thin-lip|outside|entry` bends the vertices in-test; each fails the run (exit 1). Current: 124 columns, 87% in range, thinnest 0.606 m (chipped corner).
- **Budget**: 619 vertices, 266 triangles, one surface, one opaque draw. Renderer draw calls at the moss view: 145 with shadows, 143 with the shelter's cast shadow off, so the shelter adds 2 shadow draws.
- **Not judged**: the look; Codex judges it blind. Frames and sheets are under `artifacts/local/geo-moss/r2/`.

## Asset G05: water edges

The four fall sheets and the tailrace were five boxes (60 T). `water_edges.glb` replaces them with two meshes in world metres, `SpillSheets` and `Tailrace`, built by `tools/blender/world_one/water_edges.py`. Wired by one commit in `spillway_scenery.gd`; the batches keep their names, `spill_water.gdshader` and shadow-off setting.

- **Measured after Godot import.** Before: 120 V, 60 T, 2 draws. After: 106 V, 112 T, 2 draws (ceiling 180 V / 120 T / 2 D). Smooth shading is required: flat faces split to 302 V on import.
- **Shape.** Each sheet is two columns by five rows with unequal row heights; the left and right edges bite inward by different amounts per ring, the lip and foot the most, and the ridge between them shifts in depth. The tailrace is a 16-quad strip whose play-side lip wanders 0.6 to 4.6 m inward and whose ends round off.
- **Bounds.** Every vertex stays inside the box it replaces, so the silhouette never grows; `tests/water_edges_test.gd` fails otherwise, and on the V, T and draw ceilings.
- **Not done.** No wet-stone band at the shoreline: it would need a third surface or a rock-shader change outside this unit. Flood and event surfaces are untouched. Codex judges the look.

## Look pass 4b: eroded cliff skyline

One slice of the lane board, built from GEOMETRY.md section 3. Only `spillway_scenery.gd` and its test changed; terrain, dam and water are untouched, and Codex judges the look blind.

- **Families.** The nine-instance decorative rim rocks per side (18 in all, with their cottonwood crowns on the west rim) are replaced by 18 authored crowns: three 120 m families a side of a 48 m crown (+18 m), a 36 m saddle (+6 m) and a 24 m headland (+12 m), with 6 m gullies. Chosen where the spec was open: 6 m joints between families (pitch 126 m, so three families cover z -186..186), variant order per family and side picked so no neighbours match in height or width, depths 16, 12 and 14 m, and roots 5 m below the lowest sampled terrain under each footprint. West crowns stand at x -167 (plateau, vertices at most x -158), east at x 194 (at least x 184), so nothing enters the play rectangle plus its 8 m apron. Crest `h0` is the highest `Spillway.sample` under the footprint.
- **Profile.** `crown_rings` gives each variant 10 to 12 perimeter points, six rings with unequal scale (alternate rings contract 15 to 17 percent), per-ring sideways offsets of 1 to 3 m, and a top ring that runs a long slope, a blunt crest and a short fracture face. Measured: largest lip 2.67 m, depth 15.5 m, tops exactly the add over the crest. Vertices are flat-shaded.
- **Draws.** The crowns are baked with the unchanged shoulder shelves into the existing `SandstoneShoulders` batch (`sandstone.gdshader`, `cliff_only`), so the batch count stays 6 and the extra opaque draws are zero. The shader reads world position, not vertex colour, so baking loses nothing.
- **Kept, not replaced.** The shingled shoulder shelves at |x| about 246 stay: cottonwoods are rooted on them and the spec's silhouette test concerns the rim. Follow-up if the owner wants them recut.
- **Measured** (headless, one revision each). Crown set 6,670 vertices and 2,376 triangles against 12,000 and 8,000. `SandstoneShoulders` went from 5,460 vertices and 10,400 triangles to 11,374 and 11,336, because flat shading unwelds vertices; the scenery total is 23,776 triangles against the test's 25,000.
- **Native perf is inconclusive.** Two interleaved s3 runs per side at machine load 13 to 26 of 32: frame p99 base 25.3 and 42.9 ms, head 16.4 and 44.0 ms; GPU p99 base 2.0 and 1.9, head 2.1 and 5.2; draws p99 base 1,244 and 1,185, head 1,284 and 1,007. Run-to-run spread exceeds any base-to-head gap, so this shows no regression or gain.

## World-one asset I01: dam facade detail

One slice of the asset sheet's I01. `tools/blender/world_one/dam.py` builds `assets/models/world_one/dam.glb`; `spillway_scenery.gd` swaps it in for the 252 BlockFaces boxes.

- **What it is.** Seven rows of staggered, raised cast panels (broad courses, 8 to 50 m wide) and five large repaired service frames (hatch, patch, brace) on the dam's front face at Godot z 236. Slots are `paint`, `bare_metal` and `rubber`; the game tints them cream/bone courses, worn steel `7a7a70` (copper is Rivet's, so the dam keeps it out) and charcoal `454943`. `_merge_opaque` keeps the first scenery material, so the slots' sheet roughness (.45 bare metal, .95 rubber) is not applied; all three read rough 1.0 like the stone. No emission, alpha or light was added, so the lamps stay the existing LanternLenses.
- **Numbers.** 2,340 T (old blocks 3,024), 4,600 V after Godot import, 3 primitives. Every vertex is within 1.2 m of the face (the old blocks stood off 1.225 m) and inside x +-131.7, y 26.4 to 114.9, so the 270x100x22 m wall, the three towers and the four falls are unchanged. Scenery triangles go 23,164 to 22,480.
- **Draws.** The nodes are baked to world space and merged by `_merge_opaque` with the rest of the opaque scenery, so the asset adds no colour draw. Native perf_bench s3 draws p99 went 1,247 to 1,249 (max 1,252 to 1,293) at load average about 26 on both runs, inside the run-to-run spread; treat frame p99 (25.3 to 17.3 ms) as load noise, not a gain.
- **Where you see it.** Only in the 20 m bays between buttress piers, mostly behind the falls' bays: the piers stand 12 m proud of the face and hide the rest, as the old blocks were. The frames sit in the fall-free bays at x -70, -14, 14, 70 and the open wall end at 124.
- **Limits.** `tests/dam_asset_test.gd` fails on more than 3,000 T, 9,000 V or 3 surfaces, an alpha surface, an unknown slot, a surviving BlockFaces batch, stand-off beyond 1.225 m or leaving the wall's extent.
- **Not judged here.** Codex scores the look. Follow-up: the petrol plates read dark under the strata texture; a lighter plate colour is a recipe-only change.

## Asset C07: Sable pod wear pass

One slice of the world-one asset sheet. `tools/blender/world_one/shell_sable.py` ports `tools/blender/shell_sable.py` and exports `assets/models/world_one/shell_sable.glb`; `hero_assets.gd` SHELLS points at it (the old GLB stays for `hero_assets_test.gd` and `perf_bench.gd`). Not judged here; Codex scores the look blind.

- **Added.** A rubbed-through metal chip and a mismatched repair patch on each of the five plates, two bare-metal arcs on the saddle edge, three weld beads, an eight-tooth ratchet wheel and pawl on the outer spool flange, flange bolts, two braces from saddle to spool bracket, a rope run to the fairlead, a collar and guy wire on the mast.
- **Kept.** Ears, flank spool, stubby mast, node names (`Core`, `Pod`, `Dome`, `Pilot`, `Gyro`, `RopeExit`), slots, paint colours (petrol, oxide, bone), the glass hull radius 0.98, the pilot rig and its five clips. No collider, light or alpha surface added.
- **Numbers.** Shell 10,800 to 11,304 T and 11,028 to 11,990 V; with `pilot_sable_anim.glb` the pod is 15,392 to 15,896 T and 13,978 to 14,940 V, 15 draws, against the 16,000 / 15,000 / 15 no-growth ceiling. The vertex margin is 60, so a later pass has to trade, not add. The Pod kit mesh bounds are identical to base (`world_one_shell_sable_test.gd` pins them and the ceilings).
- **Correction 1: petrol band marks.** The judge read the pale marks on the ring finish as regular fittings. They are the 16 bone arcs `shell_base.spherical()` lays at tau/16 on the petrol band (r 0.86 to 0.92); `shell_base.py` stays untouched because other shells call it. `shell_sable.py` now runs `band_patches()` after `sb.spherical()`: it deletes those 16 arcs (320 T) and lays three broad bone patches at the same radii (0.916 to 0.925) and colour, unequal in arc and height (0.95, 0.6 and 0.3 rad; island areas 0.148, 0.077 and 0.045 m2). The 16 bolts, ears, spool with ratchet and mast are unchanged and the Pod bounds are identical. Shell 11,990 to 11,570 V, pod 14,940 to 14,520 V; 11,076 shell T, 15,668 pod T, 15 draws. `world_one_shell_sable_test.gd` counts pale islands in the band (two to four, each at least 0.02 m2, largest at least 1.5x smallest) and the 16 bolts; it fails on the base glb and on one, five, tiny, equal-size and no-bolt scratch builds. Lit pixels change most at the pilot camera, then asset-use6. Not judged here; Codex scores the look blind.
- **Seams.** The perf-bench s3 scene runs Rivet, so its draw and frame numbers do not include the Sable pod. In the full black silhouette the glass hull hides the kit, so the pod-only mask (glass omitted) is the one that shows the ears, spool and mast; the verifier's rerun, with only the Pod mesh changed, gives the same bbox top (y498) at chase in every reaction pose and pixel counts within +0.5% of base. Fix round: reaction poses now have pod-only masks (`blkpilot-chase-*`), the slump frame is the clip's lowest-head sample (frac 0.65, not the resting end), the gyro mechanism is captured at 0, 120 and 240 degrees lit and glass-omitted, scrap pickups are hidden so before and after share a scene, and the 39 degree frame is paired with `look-pass-4a/final/pilot.png`. The writer's earlier 6 px bbox shift and 8% chase difference came from captures that did not freeze the chase camera or the gyro ring, so they are not evidence; the before/after captures are redone with the coordinator's shared silhouette mode before Codex judges.

## Asset C10: Chaser wedge

The Chaser was a capsule with a torus visor and a box fin. `enemy_chaser.glb`, built by `tools/blender/world_one/enemy_chaser.py`, replaces that dressing: a plough wedge at the front, a raked shoulder shield, a charcoal fin leaning back, two braced haunches on flat feet, welded patch plates and one brass visor slit. Wired by one `wiring:` commit in `chaser.gd`; the collider, lunge lane (11 x 2.8), timing and damage are unchanged.

- **Measured after Godot import.** Before: 7,564 T, 3 draws (default CapsuleMesh plus torus and box). After: 228 T, 3 draws, one mesh with three surfaces (`fleet_paint`, `rubber`, `requisition_tag`), no alpha. Ceiling 1,200 T / 3 D. Radius 0.981 m and half-height 1.5 m at rest, inside the 2 x 3 m core.
- **Posture reads existing state.** `_pose()` yaws the wedge toward the ball (the committed lane during WINDUP and LUNGE), sits it back on its haunches with nose up through WINDUP (the same crouch scale as before, plus 0.14 rad), and drives it nose-down at 0.18 rad in LUNGE. It reads `state` and `_clock` only; no new attack clock. The posture pitch and the old crouch scale are visual and can leave the 2 x 3 m core; only the rest pose is gated.
- **Materials.** The glb is read once into a static cache (`Chaser._parts()`): the mesh copy carries the charcoal and visor materials, and every chaser shares it and adds only its own flashing material as a surface override on a fresh `MeshInstance3D`, so hit flash and tint still work and a spawn no longer loads or copies a scene. The first cut copied the mesh per spawn; perf_bench harness p99 read 6.5 to 7.1 ms against 1.9 to 2.5 ms at base (max 11.6 to 14.0), and the cache brings it to 2.11 ms (max 2.93). The visor is a slit standing 0.12 m proud of the shield ridge (front face y 0.74) so its amber shows.
- **Native perf_bench s3** (1920x1080, X11, RTX 4080, one run each, load 17 at base and 35 at head, so read as no regression seen): frame p99 18.0 to 17.3 ms, GPU p99 2.15 to 2.13 ms, draws p99 1,306 to 1,195, harness p99 2.51 to 2.11 ms. Receipts under `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_chaser/perf-base` and `perf-head2`.
- **Test** `tests/enemy_chaser_asset_test.gd` fails above the ceiling, outside the core, on alpha, and if the chaser's capsule, radius, half-height or any lunge/timing/damage constant differs from base.
- **Not judged.** Codex judges the look blind. Facing uses the glb's forward (Godot -Z); the base capsule had none.

## Shared asset capture tool

One slice of the asset sheet's evidence contract. Three of four asset units failed their gate on evidence because each wrote its own capture script: invented cameras, a chase camera and gyro not frozen between before and after, and silhouettes made by colour threshold. `tools/asset_capture.gd` replaces those; `tools/capture_cameras.gd` holds the one camera table, and `look_capture.gd` now reads its FOVs, overview view and aim from it (its five frames' cameras are unchanged).

- **Cameras.** By name: the five look views (world-fixed, as in look-pass-4a `frames.json`) and `asset-front30`, `asset-side30`, `asset-rear30` (30 m from the ground anchor, 3 m above, FOV 65, aimed at the asset centre) and `asset-use6` (6 m). The anchor comes in by node path plus an optional offset; the aim point is the subject's visible-mesh AABB centre unless `--centre` fixes it. Front is the anchor's -z (yaw only), side +x, rear +z. Assumption: `asset-use6` is 3 m above the anchor like the 30 m views; the sheet gives only the distance.
- **Frozen state.** Every capture pauses the tree after 120 settle physics frames, applies each `--freeze=<path>=rx,ry,rz[:px,py,pz]` (or `<path>@<clip>:<s>` for an AnimationPlayer) and writes every camera eye, target, basis, FOV, exposure and frozen-node transform to `frames.json`. Ball physics does not repeat exactly between runs (the pod origin moved 0.01 m), so a pod run also freezes the pod's parents with a position.
- **Modes.** `lit`; `subject-black` (subject meshes take one opaque, unshaded, textureless, cull-off black material, every other visual node is hidden, `--hide` omits a subtree such as glass); `assembly-black` (every visible mesh black). Both black modes swap in a flat grey environment with fog, sky, glow, ambient, SSAO and tonemapping off, and turn MSAA, FXAA and TAA off, so a frame holds exactly two values (0 and 127).
- **Output.** 1920x1080 plus a 480x270 thumbnail per frame under `<out>/<mode>/`. `--pair` reads two runs, exits 4 and writes nothing when a mode's cameras, exposure, freeze specs or common frozen-node transforms differ (a node on one side only is a note), and otherwise writes one base-left, head-right sheet per mode.
- **Limits.** Dam and other wall-sized subjects fill the 30 m and 6 m frames, so their black frames are one value; the outline reads at `overview` only. The warden, launch and moss look views stage what `look_capture.gd` stages, not a live fight. Tests: `tests/asset_capture_test.gd`.
- **Order.** Each camera stages first (the moss view shows the bay barge), then the mode applies and reverts, so nodes a stage step shows are blacked or hidden too; an earlier build applied the mode once before staging and left the bay lit at moss.

## Asset C11: Lobber mortar cart

The Lobber was a box, a 64-segment sphere, a default cylinder and an emissive waist band. `enemy_lobber.glb`, built by `tools/blender/world_one/enemy_lobber.py`, replaces that dressing: a braced tower on four charcoal wheels, a platform with a raised lip, a loading bulb held by four claws and the base 2.6 m tube leaning out of the bulb, with breech, mid and muzzle bands. Wired by one `wiring:` commit in `lobber.gd`; the collider (2.4 x 4 x 2.4), hitbox, aim (lead 0.5, range 75), telegraph, flight, cycle, health and scrap are unchanged.

- **Measured after Godot import.** Before: 5,016 T, 4 draws (live Lobber, measured by the new test's live check at base). After: 840 T, 3 draws, no alpha. Two meshes: `LobberBody` (`fleet_paint`, `rubber`) and `LobberBulb` (one surface). Ceiling 1,200 T / 3 D. Cart x and z within 1.2 m, wheels on y -2.0, tube top at y 4.995, bulb radius 0.9 centred at y 2.6, all as the base forms.
- **State.** The bulb is the muzzle: `_show_charge` still scales and lights it from the existing clock, as before. The mortar does not track aim or recoil; the base had no aim state (the lobber never turns), and adding either needs a clock or a `_physics_process` change outside a mesh-only wiring.
- **Materials.** Same cache pattern as the Chaser (`Lobber._parts()`, glb read once); the body surface takes the enemy's flashing VERDIGRIS material, the bulb its own hazard muzzle material, everything else charcoal. Body colour was VERDIGRIS because `_base_color` also drives flash and UI reads; the correction below (Fleet oxide body paint) decouples them.
- **Dropped.** The lit waist band (emission 2.2) is gone: a fourth draw would break the ceiling. The bulb carries the far-read glow.
- **Native perf_bench s3** (1920x1080, one run each, load 17 to 18): draws p99 1,219 to 1,194 (max 1,226 to 1,202), GPU p99 2.05 to 2.21 ms, frame p99 20.1 to 23.2 ms (noise at that load), render CPU p99 1.83 to 1.73 ms. Base is head with `lobber.gd` restored to 4d69a55. Receipts under `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_lobber/perf-base` and `perf-head`.
- **Test** `tests/enemy_lobber_asset_test.gd` fails above the ceiling (live and glb), outside the collider footprint or y range, if the bulb moves or grows, and if the collider, radius, half-height, weight, muzzle point or any aim, timing, health or scrap constant differs from base. A mutant that adds one BoxMesh to the dressing fails it at 852 T / 4 D (`test-mutant-extra-mesh.log`).
- **Blocked.** No lit or black captures: `tools/asset_capture.gd` stages only the pod and Warden and has no way to place an enemy, and a Lobber is not in its scene (probed: only the Warden). Needs a `--spawn=<kind>` style hook in the tool, outside this unit's fence. Codex has not judged the look.

## Asset C12: Mag trapper cart and pit discs

The mag trapper was the shared Fleet capsule crew (capsule, sphere head, two cone ears, box tag) with a four-cylinder stack hovering behind it. `enemy_mag_trapper.glb`, built by `tools/blender/world_one/enemy_mag_trapper.py`, replaces all of it: a barrel-torso crew with a charcoal snout, ears, strap and the one brass chest tag, a low cart (bed, four boards, hitch, axle, two 12-sided wheels) and three broad 12-sided pit discs stacked on the bed with hazard cores and charcoal spacers. Wired by one `wiring:` commit in `mag_trapper.gd`; the collider (capsule 0.8 x 2.2), pit (4.5 / 1.4 / lead 0.7), hold, rating, timing (warn 1.1, live 3.0, rest 3.5) and damage constants are unchanged.

- **Measured after Godot import, whole live node.** Before: 15,244 T, 13 draws (13 meshes: default capsule, sphere head, ears, tag, four discs and four cores). After: 760 T, 3 draws, two meshes and three surfaces (`fleet_paint` for the crew body, which takes the enemy's flashing material as a surface override; `paint`, one vertex-colour surface, for the cart, ears, tag and the stack). No alpha, no collider added. Ceiling 1,200 T / 3 D.
- **Tilt reads existing state.** `_pose()` tilts `MagStack` about its cart-bed pivot during the WARN phase from `_warn_progress()` (`_t / warn_seconds`): smoothstep to -0.42 rad (back, away from the crew) plus a 0.012 rad shudder that scales with it. The pit's LIVE phase hides the stack and zeroes it, as the base hid it; ROAM eases it back with a frame-delta lerp, not a clock.
- **Deviation from base geometry.** The stack moved from 1.2 m behind the crew and 1.3 m up (overlapping the crew capsule, hovering) to 1.95 m behind, sitting on the cart bed at ground + 0.87 m, so the discs read as on the cart. Visual only; the pit is committed at the aimed point, not from the stack.
- **Budget trade (superseded by C12 round 2 below).** Round 1 baked the cores and discs into one vertex-colour surface at 3 draws, which lost the disc hit flash and the cores' 0.6 emission.
- **Round 2: threat material response restored (4 draws).** Codex's JUDGE ruling lets C12 alone use 4 colour-pass draws at the unchanged 3,600 V / 1,200 T ceiling. `MagDiscs` now has two surfaces: `paint` (discs and spacers, vertex colour) and `class_accent` (the hazard cores; the recipe uses that slot name only to get a separate surface, the runtime replaces it). Wiring in `mag_trapper.gd`: the cores take one shared `Palette.solid(HAZARD, 0.6)` (`CORE_EMISSION`), the discs take a per-trapper vertex-colour material (a shared one would flash every trapper), and `_update_flash` calls the base's, then mirrors the enemy's own `_flash` (set by the existing `flash()` hit event) onto that material as the same amber pulse, restoring at rest. Whole live node: 760 T, 4 draws (body `fleet_paint`, body `paint`, disc `paint`, cores), no alpha, no extra geometry or light. The decorative split given up is none: the cart and tag stay one surface. Native scene cost and frame time are unmeasured; at n visible trappers the exception costs at most n more draws. Test: the ceiling is 4 D, the cores' live emission reads 0.6, a hit changes the disc material's colour and emission and it restores, and a second trapper's discs stay unlit; each new check failed on a scratch revert.
- **Test** `tests/enemy_mag_trapper_asset_test.gd` fails above 1,200 T / 4 D on the live node, on alpha, if the collider, pit, hold or timing constants differ from base, or if the stack does not tilt on WARN (upright at 0, -0.205 rad at half, -0.430 at full, hidden and upright in LIVE). A mutant that adds one mesh under `MagTrapperBody` reads 4,984 T / 4 D and fails it (the pit-disc SphereMesh default is heavy).
- **Native perf_bench s3** (1920x1080, X11, RTX 4080, one run each, load about 17 to 19 at both, so read as no regression seen, not a gain): draws p99 1,210 to 1,204, GPU p99 2.22 to 2.10 ms, frame p99 20.9 to 27.1 ms (load noise; the two runs tracked different enemy counts). The bench scene does not appear to feature a mag trapper. Receipts under `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_mag_trapper/`.
- **Captured (round 2).** The shared tool's `--spawn`, `--freeze` and `--centre` pair base (look-pass-4a) and head in `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_mag_trapper/capture-r2/`; Codex has not judged the look.

## Asset C08: Rivet pod wear pass

One slice of the world-one asset sheet, built the way C07 was. `tools/blender/world_one/shell_rivet.py` ports `tools/blender/shell_rivet.py` (the port reproduces the shipped GLB byte for byte, sha256 e916dc67) and exports `assets/models/world_one/shell_rivet.glb`; one `wiring:` commit points `hero_assets.gd` SHELLS at it (the old GLB stays for `hero_assets_test.gd` and `perf_bench.gd`). Not judged here; Codex scores the look blind.

- **Added.** Rubbed-through metal chips on each fin layer's trailing edge and a mismatched repair plate on each fin, a second rivet row and a bone patch on each fin mount, a standoff heat shield and a brace to the saddle on each stack, a gauge (bezel, bone face, needle) on the rear petrol box, rack teeth along both edges of the optical rail, a focus knob on the carriage, two lens struts, two bolts on the lens face ring, two rubbed arcs, three weld beads and two riveted repair patches on the saddle.
- **Kept.** Fins, stacks, optical rail, pilot, gyro and glass hull keep every base vertex (the test checks each region for lost base vertices; wear adds new geometry on the fins, stacks and rail on top, shell 5,956 to 6,684 T), and a per-cell extent check fails if any Pod vertex lands in space the base leaves empty, node names (`Core`, `Pod`, `Dome`, `Pilot`, `Gyro`), slots, paint colours, the pilot rig file (sha256 pinned) and its five clips. The Pod mesh bounds equal base. No collider, light or alpha surface added; no new slot, so no new draw.
- **Numbers (after Godot import, shell plus `pilot_rivet_anim.glb`).** Shell 10,084 to 10,812 T and 10,196 to 11,554 V; pod 14,332 to 15,060 T and 12,996 to 14,354 V, 13 draws, against 16,000 / 15,000 / 15. Margins are 940 T, 646 V and 2 draws. The first cut (1,360 T, 2,000 V) left 172 V; wear was trimmed to keep V margin, since flat-shaded boxes cost 24 V each after import.
- **Premise.** The brief names a visor. The shipped shell has no Visor mesh; `visor()` in the recipe is unused, and the goggles live on the pilot, which is not edited. The forward optical rail, raked fins and twin stacks are in the Pod mesh as named.
- **Limits.** `tests/world_one_shell_rivet_test.gd` fails above the ceilings (V, T, draws), on an alpha surface change, a missing node, an unknown slot, off-palette paint, a Pod bound past base, a lost base vertex in the fins, stacks, rail, pilot or gyro regions, a changed rig file or a missing clip; each failure was shown on a scratch mutation.
- **Seam.** `tools/asset_capture.gd` calls `flow.choose_class(0)`, which is Sable, and has no class flag, so it cannot capture the Rivet pod. Follow-up: a `--class=<index>` arg (one line) before the before/after sheets can be built with `--pair`.


## Asset C09: Kest pod wear pass

One slice of the world-one asset sheet, built the way C07 and C08 were. `tools/blender/world_one/shell_kest.py` ports `tools/blender/shell_kest.py` (the port reproduces the shipped GLB byte for byte, sha256 25d2fcc0) and exports `assets/models/world_one/shell_kest.glb`; one `wiring:` commit points the `demolitions_caster` line of `hero_assets.gd` SHELLS at it (the old GLB stays for `hero_assets_test.gd` and `perf_bench.gd`). Not judged here; Codex scores the look blind.

- **Added.** On each satchel a bare buckle plate on the upper strap, a mismatched repair patch between the straps and a rubbed-through chip at the lower corner; on the saddle a rubbed bare arc, two weld beads and a petrol repair plate; on the mortar two collars, a pressure gauge (bezel and bone face), a pivot bolt on each cheek, a cocking lever with a rubber knob on the left cheek, two chips in the mouth ring and a second brace to the saddle; under the vane a hinge barrel on two posts from the spine; on each blade a rubbed-through chip and a petrol repair plate.
- **Kept.** Crest (the Pilot mesh is untouched), the three horizontal satchels, the mortar and its mouth ring, the open vane and its hinged blades, node names (`Core`, `Pod`, `Dome`, `Pilot`, `Gyro`, `VaneL`, `VaneR`), slots, paint colours (petrol, oxide, bone), the glass hull radius 0.98, the pilot rig file (sha256 pinned) and its five clips. Every base vertex of Pod, both blades, Pilot and Gyro is still present; no mesh gains a surface, so no new draw; no collider, light or alpha surface added.
- **Numbers (after Godot import, shell plus `pilot_kest_anim.glb`).** Shell 10,292 to 10,788 T and 10,654 to 11,596 V; pod 14,784 to 15,280 T and 13,752 to 14,694 V, 21 draws, against 16,000 / 15,000 / 21. Margins are 720 T, 306 V and 0 draws. The pod is already at the draw ceiling, so a later pass cannot add a slot to any mesh, and it has 306 V to spend: flat-shaded boxes cost 24 V each after import, an 8-sided cylinder 48.
- **Silhouette.** Pod and blade mesh bounds equal base to the millimetre, and a per-cell extent check (0.12 m cells, as C08) finds zero Pod or blade vertices in space the base leaves empty; the Pod is checked against the union of Pod and blade cells because the hinge barrel sits in the gap the blades stand over. At the asset cameras the subject-black outline box is identical at base and head: 30 m front, side and rear 54 by 54 px (933,513 to 986,566; side 934 to 985), 6 m 246 by 222 px (837,422 to 1082,643), and the 39 degree pilot view 572 by 578 px. Pixel counts move by at most 35 px at 6 m and 5 at the pilot view, so the change is interior detail, and at 30 m only 47 to 98 lit pixels differ. That is the brief's no-growth rule showing up as a faint read at range, not a defect.
- **Limits.** `tests/world_one_shell_kest_test.gd` fails above the ceilings (V, T, draws), on an alpha surface change, a missing node, an unknown slot, off-palette paint, a Pod or blade bound past base, a mesh gaining a surface, a lost base vertex in a satchel, the mortar or its mouth, the saddle, the vane spine, either blade, Pilot or Gyro, a Pod or blade vertex in cells the base leaves empty, a changed rig file or a missing clip; ceiling, lost satchel, lost mouth, blade growth and rig change were each shown on a scratch mutation (receipts in mutations.txt); the writer's own cell-growth mutant landed outside the extents and passed, so that check rests on the verifier's extent mutant, which failed with 'kest extent Pod 3'.
- **Seams.** `tools/asset_capture.gd` has no real rear chase camera: `asset-rear30` is 30 m, and `asset-use6` looks from the front, so the seated (`drop_land` at 2.2 s), maximum cheer (`big_cheer` 1.4 s) and slump (`slump` 0.975 s) poses were captured at those two views, with `--class=2`. Follow-up: a named chase row in `capture_cameras.gd` (outside this slice's fence). `--pair` reports the frozen AnimationPlayer as present on one side only for pose runs although both frames.json record it at the same clip time; a note, not an error. Both base and head logs carry the same two engine lines `BUG, indexing did not unpair geometries from light`.

## Eroded cliff skyline, round 2 (CLIFF-R2)

One slice of the lane board, built from Codex's `CLIFF-R2.md`. Not judged here; Codex scores the look blind.

- **Crowns.** `spillway_scenery.gd` `crown_mesh` is now one closed solid per crown: 12 perimeter stations (six on the arena face, six on the back, both ordered by world Z, so the east yaw no longer reverses the profile), five rings (buried base, h0, then .35, .70 and 1.0 of the local rise) at 100, 95, 85 and 75 percent depth about the same centre, zipped caps with no radial fan, and 18 crowns at the spec's C/S/H profiles (table maxima 18, 6, 12 m). East centres are 192, 194 and 193; F2 is C/S/H on both sides. h0 and root come from terrain sampled at 2 m over the final footprint, boundary included, with the base 5 m under the lowest sample.
- **Folds.** Each fold is a diagonal groove of two points pushed 0.72 m into the flank per band (six triangles instead of two). C has two folds, each in band .35 to .70; S and H run one groove through three and two bands (`RIM_FOLD_BANDS`), so the mesh rise is 3.06 m and 5.42 m (spec 3 and 5; the test asserts at least 2.9 and 4.8). F1 mirrors the slope. Deviations: a fold is a shallow dish across the face quad, not a 1 to 2 m slot (narrowing it needs vertices the 12-triangle budget does not allow beyond S at 12); C's two folds leave 5.8 m of broad face between them (spec 8 m, which contradicts the .28 and .70 centres with 14 m folds). Extra triangles per crown: S 12, H 8, C 8.
- **Shoulders.** The 64 shelves are 16 hulls, 10 stations by three bands with fan caps and two recessed folds each (88 T). Levels nest inside the level under them, stay inside the union of the old shelf bounds, and the top passes 0.15 m or more over the root height with at least 3.4 m of plan support around every trunk. Cottonwood transforms come from `cottonwood_transforms()` and equal round 1 exactly. The 48 small rocks stay.
- **Counts.** Crown V/T 6,670/2,376 to 5,958/2,256; shoulders 4,120 V and 1,408 T; scenery T 23,144 to 19,312 (base at bcd0a7e, head at one revision, same test). Terrain and collider hashes are equal at base and head.
- **Capture tool.** `asset_capture.gd` gained mode `subject-context-depth` (subject black, every other mesh unshaded background grey and still writing depth), `--aabb` plus `--isolate=<mesh>` (keeps the triangles of one mesh whose centroid is in a world box, so one family of the merged cliff batch can be shown; `--isolate-rest` keeps the other triangles as a sibling mesh, drawn as grey depth context in `subject-context-depth`), and the cameras `ortho-front`, `ortho-side` and `ortho-top` (250 m, span = largest extent times 1.10, near 0.05, far 1800), which are accepted by `--cameras` but never in the default set. Existing flags and modes are unchanged. Each addition has a check in `tests/asset_capture_test.gd` that fails on a scratch revert.
- **Limits.** Unit masks (`unit-*` in the evidence folder) are isolated with `--isolate-rest`, so the shoulder hulls and neighbouring crowns occlude as grey context. At launch the west and east saddle and headland each pass 18 px wide with 12 px exposed (west S 90 columns, max 20 px; west H 106 columns; east S 178; east H 112), the crown is frame-clipped by design. At overview, west F2 is fully in frame (C 76, S 30, H 36 columns) and east F1 is fully in frame in the depth-occluded unit masks (H 150, C 115, S 70 columns); east F2 is seen end-on (its saddle shows 8 columns of 12 px or more) and east F0's headland is off frame. Whether east F1's unequal tops and gullies read is the blind judge's call; crowns were not raised. Over the dam at launch, columns 400 to 1500 hold 0 subject pixels at base and head, and all 10,005 new pixels sit at x 0 to 400 or 1500 to 1761; the dam's extent (about 540 to 1440) comes from the lit frame, as no dam-only mask exists. The subject-black frame holds exactly two values, 0 and 127 (the flat background is linear 0.5).

## Moss shelter round 3: expose the cheek fracture (GEO-MOSS3)

One slice of the lane board, from the blind judge's named correction on Moss round 2 (JUDGE.md row Moss / I02). Not judged here; Codex scores the look blind.

- **Cheeks.** `tools/blender/moss_shelter.py`: the outer front edge of each cheek now runs in three broad, unequal oblique fractures instead of a near-vertical line. Visible side (Godot +Z) X -14.6 at Y0, -16.0 at Y1.5, -14.85 at Y4.3, -16.5 at Y6.0, -15.8 at the neck top; other side X -15.9, -14.6, -16.2, -15.0, -15.4 at Y0, 1.2, 3.9, 6.0, 7.62. The X swing is 1.9 and 1.6 m (round 2: 0.6 and 0.7). The end face is oblique in plan as well: the inner edge sits 0.5 to 0.6 m forward or back of the outer one, never past X -14.52.
- **Cap.** The corners and sides of the cap hold their wall and break in one planar chamfer over the last third (rings 3 to 5 collinear in inset, top inset 0.55 to 0.80 m scaled per point 0.4 to 1.6) instead of the rolled bevel. Front points keep their inset, so the belly and lip are unchanged: 92% of 124 lip columns in 1.30..1.60 m (round 2: 87%), thinnest 0.880 (round 2: 0.606).
- **Kept.** Envelope, entry clearance, neck and cap bounds (crown top 9.173), scoop, barge attachment, shared sandstone material, one mesh and surface. Budget after import 619 V and 266 T to 608 V and 266 T against 2,400 and 1,600; one opaque stone draw.
- **Test.** `tests/moss_shelter_test.gd` measures each cheek's outer face in elevation (max X per Y row for stone at |Z| above 3.9, ahead of the rear skin, up to Y 6.01) and requires at least three fracture planes 20 degrees or more off vertical, longest over shortest plane 1.3 or more, and an X swing of 1.2 m or more. It fails on the round-2 mesh (base with this test: side +1 has 1 plane and swing 0.60, side -1 has 0 planes and swing 0.70) and passes here (3 planes, ratios 1.48 and 1.77, swings 1.90 and 1.60). `-- --mutate=straight-cheek` flattens the front edge in the test and fails the run.
- **Limits.** The shared capture tool stages the moss bay only for the `moss` camera; the round-2 approach and dock views are not named cameras in `capture_cameras.gd` (outside the fence), so evidence is the `moss` view in lit, subject-black and subject-context-depth. The cheek front is a thin edge at that view. Follow-up: named approach and dock rows that stage the bay.

## Asset C11 correction: Lobber body paints Fleet oxide

Codex's blind judge accepted C11 with one named correction: a large verdigris body competes with the petrol player grouping, so the resting body takes Fleet oxide. `lobber.gd` gains `BODY_PAINT := Palette.RUST` (the Chaser's colour); the body material is built from it, and `_base_color` stays `VERDIGRIS`.

- **Coupling.** The only readers of `_base_color` are `enemy.gd` `_update_flash` (the hit flash lerps from it toward cream with amber emission) and `_apply_rest_look` (restores it when the flash ends). No UI, codex, HUD or event code reads the Lobber's `_base_color` (grep of `src/`). The flash is unchanged; `Lobber._apply_rest_look` calls the base version, then puts the albedo back to `BODY_PAINT`, so recovery ends on oxide and the plating tint emission still applies.
- **Unchanged.** Charcoal tool metal, the hazard bulb (own material), the brass tag; 840 T / 3 D, no new surface or draw.
- **Test.** `enemy_lobber_asset_test.gd` asserts resting oxide, `_base_color` verdigris, hit flash from verdigris, and recovery back to oxide. Reverting `lobber.gd` fails the three oxide checks; setting `_base_color` to RUST fails the three verdigris checks.
- **Seam left as is.** The flash starts from verdigris, so the first hit frame pops from oxide to verdigris-toward-cream. That is what "keep the existing hit-flash colour" says; lerping from oxide instead is a follow-up for Codex to judge. Captures in `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_lobber/capture-r2/`; Codex has not judged the look.

## Asset C13: Bola thrower crew and overhead loop

The Bola thrower was the counter's capsule crew (head, two ears, tag) under a default 64 x 32 torus. `enemy_bola_thrower.glb`, built by `tools/blender/world_one/enemy_bola_thrower.py`, replaces that dressing: a Fleet oxide crew with a raised throwing arm and charcoal glove, and a large tilted hoop (ring outer radius 0.85 m) carrying a weighted end at each side (reach 1.10 m). Wired by one `wiring:` commit in `bola_thrower.gd`; collider (capsule r 0.8, h 2.2), hitbox, throw timing, damage and every projectile constant are unchanged.

- **Measured after Godot import.** Before: 13,196 T, 6 draws (live thrower at the base bola_thrower.gd). After: 640 T, 3 draws, no alpha. `BolaThrowerBody` has two surfaces (`fleet_paint`, `paint`), `BolaLoop` one (`class_accent`). Ceiling 1,200 T / 3 D. Crew radius 0.771 m inside the 0.8 m footprint, floor y -1.1, top 2.17 m; the loop seats 2.2 m over the origin as the base torus did.
- **Materials.** Same cache pattern as C11/C12 (`BolaThrower._parts()`, glb read once). The body takes the enemy's own `_material` (Fleet oxide RUST, flashing through the existing `flash()` event), the crew details and glove take one shared vertex-colour material, and the hoop takes the base's hazard material (HAZARD, emission 0.6), shared by every thrower because the base never flashed it. The tilt (0.42 rad) makes the ring read as a hoop from the 30 m side and rear cameras, not a thin edge-on line.
- **Pose reads existing state.** The loop's spin steps 0.25 rad per physics frame as before and ramps to 0.35 through `Phase.WARN` off the existing `_t / warn_seconds` clock; no new clock. During the throw the loop is the flying bola (`top_level`, unchanged), so the hoop stays visible in flight.
- **Test** `tests/enemy_bola_thrower_asset_test.gd` fails above 1,200 T or 3 D (glb and live), on alpha, outside the 0.8 m footprint or the capsule's floor, if the loop moves, exceeds 1.12 m or shrinks below the base ring, if the collider, radius, half-height, weight, warn, live, rest, range, keep-near, scrap or any flight, ring, height, wrap, drag, pull, sting or health constant differs from base, if the resting body is not RUST, if a hit does not flash through the event (or leaks to a second thrower) and if the WARN spin does not ramp. Scratch reverts that each fail it: base `bola_thrower.gd` (13,196 T / 6 D), FLIGHT 1.4, an extra BoxMesh (652 T / 4 D), an alpha surface, a verdigris body and a flat spin (logs under `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_bola_thrower/mut-*.log`).
- **Captures.** Base (integration pin c782620) and head, lit, subject-black and assembly-black at `asset-front30`, `asset-side30`, `asset-rear30` and `asset-use6`, paired with zero errors; a subject-black frame holds exactly the values 0 and 127. Godot logs one engine line, "BUG, indexing did not unpair geometries from light", at base and head alike. Not judged: Codex judges the look blind.
- **Not done.** No throw-state captures (the freeze pins the resting pose); the flying loop does not tumble because `_pose` skips it while `top_level`; the attack-facts test named in the brief is not in this checkout.
- **Correction (C13b, after Codex's blind judge).** The loop read as a bar because the glb node carried the 0.42 tilt and the runtime reads only the mesh, so the hoop stood flat (ring axis vertical) and showed a rim from 30 m; a diameter cord and hub ball then bisected it. Now the mesh has no tilt and no cord or hub (592 T / 3 D, no new draw); `bola_thrower.gd` seats the loop with `SEAT_AXIS` (0.6, 0.53, 0.6), so front, side and rear cameras each see the opening at about 0.6 of face-on, and the whirl (0.25 rad per step, 0.35 in WARN, same clock) is about the ring's own axis, in flight too (`_flight_basis` keeps the seat the loop left with). Endpoints, hit geometry and constants are untouched. Opening measured as background pixels enclosed by the subject in the subject-black frames (`scratchpad/c13b/hole.py`): base front30 11, side30 0, rear30 11; head 356, 391, 378 (minimum 250). Throw states are not staged: no existing method enters the wind-up without arguments, and a capture-only method was kept out of gameplay code, so the wind-up read comes from the test (40 WARN steps) and the in-flight limit below. Test adds: mesh ring axis is local Y, ring axis |x| and |z| >= 0.45 at rest, through 40 WARN steps and in flight, and nothing crosses the opening; scratch reverts (flat `SEAT_AXIS`, the old glb with the cord) fail them (`c13b-mut-*.log`). Limit: the opening depends on the thrower's yaw against the camera; in flight at 25 frames the loop is open at side30 (81 px) and slim at rear30 (166 px), and out of the front30 frame. Codex has not judged the look.
- **Throw-state captures (C13b evidence, no code change).** Matched lit and subject-black front30, side30 and rear30 at base (int-pin2 d70ef7a) and head for the wind-up at 25, 60 and 95 percent of `warn_seconds`, the release at flight 0.30, 0.70 and 0.95, and the recovery just after landing, staged only by `--call` `Node.set` and `set_indexed` chains; each state's exact `phase`, `_t`, spin and loop position are in `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_bola_thrower/MANIFEST.md`. Codex has not judged them.

## Asset C14: Hooker anchor post and harpoon

The Hooker was a capsule crew, a charcoal cylinder post, a brass gun box and a hazard cylinder. `enemy_hooker.glb`, built by `tools/blender/world_one/enemy_hooker.py`, replaces that dressing: the animal crew in Fleet oxide (barrel torso, head, ears, the one brass tag), a slim tapering post at x 0.9 with a foot plate, stays and a tether spool, an anchor crown at its head (stock, two flukes, shackle ring) and a long level harpoon with a hazard barbed head lying in a cradle at the anchor height. Against the Lobber's wide braced tower under a fat leaning tube, the Hooker is a thin pole with a crossbar crown and a thin spear pointing at the ball. Wired by one `wiring:` commit in `hooker.gd`; the capsule collider, hitbox, lane, tether, rating, timing, health and scrap are unchanged, and `anchor()` still reads 3.1 m above the origin.

- **Measured after Godot import.** Before: 10,648 T, 8 draws (live Hooker, measured by the new test run against base `hooker.gd`). After: 754 T, 3 draws, no alpha; two meshes: `HookerBody` (`fleet_paint`, `paint` vertex colour) and `HookerHarpoon` (one `paint` surface). Ceiling 1,200 T / 3 D. Bounds x -0.77..1.58, y -1.10..3.93, z -0.81..2.35 at rest.
- **State.** The harpoon reads the existing warn and live phases: over the warning it cocks back 0.45 m along its own axis, easing with `_warn_progress()`; while the line is live it lunges 0.30 m forward; in roam it settles. It uses `Counter._pose()` and adds no clock. The post, crown and cradle are static, so `anchor()` and the tether endpoint never move; the test pins the harpoon cradle at (0.9, 3.1, 0), the body at the origin and the anchor offset across all three phases.
- **Materials.** Same cache pattern as the Mag trapper: `Hooker._parts()` reads the glb once; the body's `fleet_paint` surface takes the enemy's own flashing material (already Fleet oxide RUST, so the resting body needs no paint change and the hit flash is the existing event), all other surfaces a shared vertex-colour material.
- **Dropped.** The gun's brass box (emission 0.3) and the hazard cylinder's emission 0.8: the harpoon head is a plain vertex-colour hazard red, since a fourth surface would break the 3 D ceiling. The base brass gun was a second brass accent; the crew tag is the only one now.
- **Test** `tests/enemy_hooker_asset_test.gd` fails above the ceiling (asset and live), on alpha, outside the footprint (x -1.0..1.6, z -1.2..2.7, y -1.1..4.0, checked at rest, cocked and lunged), if the collider, radius, half-height, weight, timing, rating, lane, tether or health constants differ from base, if the anchor or the harpoon cradle moves, and if the body is not the flashing Fleet oxide material. Each check was made to fail with a scratch mutant of `hooker.gd` (base revert, POST_HEIGHT, LANE_WIDTH, THRUST, harpoon offset, extra mesh, alpha, warn time, wrong body material); logs in `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_hooker/mut-*.log`.
- **Captures** through `scratchpad/enemy_caps.sh` under `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_hooker/capture/` (base pin c782620 against head, paired with zero errors). The subject-black frames hold exactly two pixel values; bounding boxes beside the Lobber's are in `silhouette-bbox.txt`. At `asset-use6` the harpoon points away from the camera and foreshortens. Codex has not judged the look.

After: 812 T, 3 draws (`NetcasterBody` 800 T over two surfaces, `fleet_paint` and `paint` vertex colour, plus the net box 12 T), no alpha outside the net plane. Ceiling 1,200 T / 3 D. ## G01 cliff round 3: oblique crown and shoulder solids

Round 3 replaces the aligned-station crowns and the scaled-outline shoulder hulls with oblique solids: per-variant plan tables (crowns 12 stations, hulls 10) with a per-face batter, and corner lines solved so every face stays planar (`corner_slopes` in `spillway_scenery.gd`). Folds are dropped; the crest profile, rim plan, envelopes, roots, trees, terrain and colliders are unchanged. The tables are exactly the proposal that passed `tools/cliff_gate_report.py` (the values are inlined in `spillway_scenery.gd` lines 37-54, rounded to 4 decimals; the gate was re-run on the rounded values).

- **Gate change (Codex RULINGS item 1).** The neighbour dominant-face share gap is 5 points (was 10); the 12 degree azimuth rule and zero dam overlap stay. All 14 same-side pairs pass (share gaps 5.8 to 11.3, azimuth gaps 29.1 to 49.2).
- **Launch dam overlap cleared.** 3 px came from variant-0 hull 14, on the top band (ring 2 to 3) of its arena face near screen (28, 311). The variant-0 plan moved station 1 by (-0.20, +0.36) m and the face 0, 1, 2 batters rose from 0.152/0.276/0.122 to 0.18/0.302/0.188 (a steeper lean pulls the top edge off the dam). Not a hull rescale. Overlap and lost dam pixels are 0 at launch and overview.
- **Gate result (combined, on the built proposal).** No form failure across 18 crowns and 16 hulls, six distinct directions per family, nesting and roots pass, budget 2,088 T / 6,264 V crowns and 1,280 T / 3,840 V hulls (ceilings 2,376 / 1,536 T), visibility_pass true.
- **Port check.** After Godot import, `cliff_gate_export.gd` on the built source gives 3,368 unit triangles (2,088 + 1,280), and their centroids match the analysis solids to 0.01 m, so the gate result describes the built geometry.
- **Test.** `spillway_scenery_test.gd` cuts plan sections from the mesh (crowns at h0 and half rise, hulls at 35 and 70 percent): longest merged run at most 25 percent, axis-aligned runs at most 35 percent, occupancy at most 90 percent, three arena-facing runs. It fails on the round-2 source (379 crown and 32 hull rule failures) and passes on this one. Face-size ratio, normals, retreat and visibility stay in the gate, which measures connected planar patches; the test does not re-derive them. Follow-up: mesh tests for face-size ratio, normals and retreat, so a later mesh edit cannot drift on them without a Godot failure.
- **Removed.** The empty-flank-ray count in the open-edge check (an oblique plan legitimately has no hit at some z and height; closure is still checked by the edge count) and the fold tests.

## Asset C15: Plated brute and one plate group

The Plated brute was the counter's capsule crew (head, ears, tag) plus three boxes: a 2 x 1.9 x 0.5 m front slab and two pads. `enemy_plated_brute.glb`, built by `tools/blender/world_one/enemy_plated_brute.py`, replaces that dressing. Andrew's coordinator chose option A: the armour is ONE plate group (front slab with brow, ribs and bolts, shoulder cladding at the base pad positions, a rear slab and two kick blocks), visible exactly while `plates > 0`, and the whole group goes when `on_struck` pops the plate. The brute still has one plate; plate count, plate hit points, slam and timing are unchanged. Wired by one `wiring:` commit in `plated_brute.gd`.

- **Measured after Godot import, visible meshes only.** Before (base d70ef7a): 9,136 T, 8 draws intact and 9,124 T, 7 draws stripped, because the base pop hid only the slab and left both pads. After: 924 T, 3 draws intact (`BruteBody` two surfaces, `BrutePlates` one) and 580 T, 2 draws stripped; the group is 344 T, the body 580 T. No alpha. Ceiling 1,200 T / 3 D.
- **Body.** Fleet oxide on the enemy's own `_material` (RUST, flashes through `flash()`), vertex-colour details (snout, ears, fists, feet, strap) on one shared material, and the one brass tag on the chest, hidden by the slab until it pops. Body radius 0.79 m inside the 0.8 m footprint, floor y -1.1, top 1.55 m. The plate group reaches 1.79 m in plan (base pads reached 1.65 m) and 1.14 m up, with the front face at z 1.35 m as the base slab's.

The amber crack glyph stays a child of that node (named `PlateCrack`, 3 strokes, drawn only in amber, not counted in the budget) and hides with it. `_crew` now points at the body node, so the WARN scale pulse scales the body as it scaled the capsule and its children.
- **Mass loss measured** (subject-black, black-pixel area, intact to stripped): `asset-front30` 4,178 to 2,508 px (base 3,968 to 3,400), `asset-side30` 4,258 to 2,209 (base 3,704 to 2,759), `asset-rear30` 4,445 to 2,503 (base 4,176 to 3,400), `asset-use6` 112,682 to 52,849 (base 95,788 to 86,436). Bounding boxes narrow at head (front30 x 917-1002 to 938-981); at base they hold for front30, rear30 and use6, and side30 narrows from x 920-982 to 936-982. Figures in `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_plated_brute/capture-measure.txt`.
- **Test** `tests/enemy_plated_brute_asset_test.gd` fails above 1,200 T or 3 D (glb and live), on alpha, a body outside the 0.8 m footprint or floor, a plate group past 1.8 m, 1.3 m high or over 400 T, if the collider, radius, half-height, weight, warn, live, rest, range, keep-near, walk speed, scrap, plate count, plating, health or any slam constant differs from base, if the body is not RUST, if a hit does not flash through the event, and if any part of the plate group still renders after `on_struck` with a full result or the stripped brute does not lose mass. Scratch reverts that each fail it: base `plated_brute.gd` (9,172 T / 11 D), the plate not hidden, SLAM_DAMAGE 13, an alpha plate, a verdigris body, `plates := 3` and a plate group scaled 1.5x (logs `mut-*.log`).
- **Captures.** Base (integration pin int-pin2, d70ef7a) and head, lit, subject-black and assembly-black at the four asset cameras, intact and stripped through `--call=/root/Game/Spawn_plated_brute:on_struck:2:["x","y",{"full":true}]` at both revisions, paired with zero errors; subject-black frames hold exactly 0 and 127. Godot logs one engine line, "BUG, indexing did not unpair geometries from light", per run. `frames.json` does not record plate count; the test confirms `plates` 0 through the same event and arguments. Not judged: Codex judges the look blind.

## Asset C17: Deflector dish

The Deflector was the counter's capsule crew (head, ears, tag) behind a base disc (CylinderMesh, 64 sides) and a brass torus rim (64 x 32), which sank its lower 0.65 m into the floor. `enemy_deflector.glb`, built by `tools/blender/world_one/enemy_deflector.py`, replaces that dressing: a Fleet oxide animal crew (barrel, chest, head, charcoal snout and ears, strap, a pack on the back carrying the one brass tag) braced behind ONE concave dish, 16 sectors and four rings, with a worn-bone hexagonal bead at the 1.95 m outer radius (the base torus's) as the rebound rim, a charcoal recess at the centre, oxide plate bands out to the rim, and a charcoal back frame (boss, two ribs, two arms and grips to the crew). The dish stays where the base disc was (0, 0.2, 1.4) and its lower half is squashed to 0.64 so the foot stops at the floor. Against the Hooker's one slim post, the Bola thrower's overhead hoop and the Netcaster's gate of masts, it is one broad round plate that hides the crew from the ball's side.

- **State read.** The facing is the enemy's existing yaw: `counter.gd _facing()` sets `rotation.y = atan2(to_ball.x, to_ball.z)` at commit, and the dish sits on local +Z, so it turns to the incoming ball with the enemy and the rim shows toward it. No new pose and no clock; the mesh is static. `deflector.gd` only builds the mesh.
