After: 844 T, 2 draws (`DeflectorBody`: `fleet_paint` and `paint` vertex colour), no alpha, no runtime mesh outside the glb. Ceiling 1,200 T / 3 D. Bounds x -1.95..1.95, y -1.10..2.15, z -0.88..1.68.
- **Materials.** Same cache pattern as C14 to C16: `Deflector._parts()` reads the glb once; the crew's `fleet_paint` surface takes the enemy's own flashing material (already Fleet oxide RUST), the rest one shared vertex-colour material. The dish does not flash; a hit flashes the crew through the existing event.
- **Test** `tests/enemy_deflector_asset_test.gd` fails above 1,200 T or 3 D (glb and live), on alpha, outside the footprint (x +-2.0, z -0.95..1.75, y -1.1..2.3), if the dish rim does not reach 1.95 m or lacks its bead, if the dish is not ball-side after `_facing` at three bearings, if the collider, radius, half-height, weight, timing, scrap or any restitution, face, closing, vault or health constant differs from base, and if the body is not the flashing Fleet oxide material on the crew. Scratch mutants of `deflector.gd` that each fail it: base revert (13,964 T / 7 D), HP, RESTITUTION, warn time, a wrong body material, an alpha surface, two extra meshes (4 D) and a 1.4x body (logs `mut-*.log` under `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_deflector/`).
- **Captures** through `scratchpad/enemy_caps.sh` (resting) under `capture/` and a facing variant under `capture-facing/`, base 3d2a0cb against head, paired, no pair errors; Godot logs only the known "BUG, indexing did not unpair geometries from light" line, twice per run at base and head. The capture cameras take their yaw from the anchor, so a rotated enemy anchored to itself looks identical; the facing set anchors to `/root/Game` at the spawn point, freezes the enemy at yaw 180 (dish toward `asset-front30`) and sets `rotation:y` through `--call` with Node.set identically at both revisions. Subject-black frames hold exactly the values 0 and 127; bounding boxes are in `silhouette-bbox.txt`. Not judged: Codex judges the look blind.
- **Evidence retake (Codex JUDGE section 4, no geometry change).** On native 1920x1080 subject-context-depth masks at identical camera and frozen state (base int-pin2 d70ef7a, head this branch), `count(base_dam_visible AND NOT head_dam_visible)` is 0 at launch and 0 at overview, and head G01 over base dam overlap is 0 at both, for the DamDetail plus LanternLenses wall set and for the gate's wider set that adds DamAndLedgeDressing; unlabelled clay pairs come from the new `asset_capture.gd --clay=<node path>` (lit mode, one flat neutral material on that subtree); masks are at exposure 1.0 as CLIFF-R3 writes, and the clay pairs were retaken with the new `--exposure=1.0` (lit mode otherwise reads the scene's 1.1), so every frame records 1.0 (`cap/*/clay-e1`, `pair/clay-e1`). Receipts: `scratchpad/cliff3d/dam_measure.json`.

## Asset C18: Winch drone

The Winch drone was the counter's capsule crew (head, ears, tag) with three cylinders: a 1.6 m charcoal rotor disc, a mast and a brass drum. `enemy_winch_drone.glb`, built by `tools/blender/world_one/enemy_winch_drone.py`, replaces that dressing: a small Fleet oxide animal crew (barrel, chest, head, charcoal snout and ears, waist strap, a battery pack behind it and the one brass tag) on a thin charcoal mast under ONE flat 16-sided rotor (1.6 m radius, 2.0 m up, oxide and charcoal blades in quarter turns so the spin shows), with a 16-sided cable drum (0.45 m radius, 0.9 m long, darker oxide with charcoal end flanges) hung at y -1.4 on one central hanger. Its vertical mass is stacked at the two ends around a slim waist: wide at the top, weight at the bottom, air between them, where the Hooker is one post, the Bola thrower an overhead hoop, the Netcaster a gate and the Deflector one broad plate. Wired by one `wiring:` commit in `winch_drone.gd` that changes only `_build_tool` and adds `_parts()`.

- **Cable exit.** The cable pays out of the drum's front-bottom quarter along (0, -0.707, 0.707), on the drum's mid plane. The glb has nothing on that line: one central hanger and a saddle over the drum (no side straps, so the drum's ends are open too), and a pale grey-slate cable wrap band across the drum's middle where the line leaves. The cable is drawn by the runtime hold, not the glb.
- **Measured after Godot import.** Before (base 3d2a0cb): 11,404 T, 8 draws (live drone at base `winch_drone.gd`, measured by the new test). After: 552 T, 3 draws (`WinchBody` two surfaces, `fleet_paint` and `paint` vertex colour, plus `WinchRotor` one), no alpha. Rotor 64 T and drum 64 T, 128 T for the two 16-sided shapes (ceiling 150). Ceiling 1,200 T / 3 D. Bounds x -1.60..1.60, y -1.90..2.04, z -1.60..1.60.
- **State read.** The rotor is its own mesh with its origin at its centre (Godot y 2.0), and `_pose()` still adds 0.6 rad to `_rotor.rotation.y` each physics tick, so the base's spin is the drone's existing clock. Nothing else moves; the drum is static. The drone has no visible working pose: `phase`, `winched` and the lane telegraph do not change the body, so the working capture shows the lane telegraph leaving the drone's own state machine instead.
- **Materials.** Same cache pattern as C14 to C17: `WinchDrone._parts()` reads the glb once; the crew's `fleet_paint` surface takes the enemy's own flashing material (already Fleet oxide RUST), everything else one shared vertex-colour material. A hit flashes the crew through the existing event; the rotor and drum do not flash.
- **Test** `tests/enemy_winch_drone_asset_test.gd` fails above 1,200 T or 3 D (asset and live), on alpha, outside the footprint (x and z +-1.7, y -1.95..2.3), if the collider, radius, half-height, weight, timing, scrap or any hover, climb, lane, pull, winch, pack or health constant differs from base (the runtime values, keep_far 22 clamping to 16.2 included, and the source literals of `_configure` and `_live`, so a change under the clamp still fails), if the rotor radius, height or pivot moves or `_pose` stops turning it 0.6 per tick, if the drum is not the lowest 0.9 m mass, if any triangle sits within a 0.12 m corridor round the exit line out to 3 m (five rays), and if the glb does not name exactly one surface fleet_paint or the flashing material sits on any other surface, and if the body is not Fleet oxide (RUST) on it. Scratch mutants of `winch_drone.gd` that each fail it: base revert (11,404 T / 8 D), HP 31, WINCH 2.0, keep_far 24 (source literal only), a box on the exit (4 D), an alpha rotor and a 1.5x rotor and the flashing material moved to the wrong surface (logs `mut-*.log` under `artifacts/local/asset-units/enemy_winch_drone/`).
- **Captures** through `scratchpad/enemy_caps.sh`, base 3d2a0cb against head, paired, no pair errors; Godot logs only the known "BUG, indexing did not unpair geometries from light" line. The drone hovers 5.5 m up, so it is frozen at the ground point plus 5.5 m and the cameras anchor to the ground point through `/root/Game` and `--anchor-offset`, as it is seen in play. The working set is staged by the drone's own state machine: it is spawned 15 m ahead of the ball's settled path (ground 33.25 at -62,-128, frozen at hover 38.75), and `--call` sets `_rest` to 0 with 40 physics frames to wait, identically at both revisions; the drone commits its lane, the frames are in the warn phase (state `warn`, lane telegraph up, drone stationary) and the lit use6 frame shows the drum and cable exit with the telegraph beside them. The hold itself and the cable are not staged (the ball is airborne and off the lane); the packet's follow-up is a Vector3-capable `--call` to stage a live hold. The resting set keeps the drone far from the ball so it idles. Subject-black frames hold exactly the values 0 and 127; bounding boxes are in `silhouette-bbox.txt`. Not judged: Codex judges the look blind.
- **Plate cue on the rim, not the body (2026-09-29, Andrew's ruling).** "Your punch beats this plate now" no longer floods the body and plate with class-colour emission (0.9 and 1.8 energy on every plated enemy, the pale-mint wash). `enemy.gd` `_plate_material(rim_mask)` gives the one plate material an emission texture: nearest-filtered, mipless, emission operator multiply (the default add spreads the colour over the whole face; caught in the first capture). Amber turns emission on at 1.8 and only white mask texels light; slate turns it off; plate albedo is `PLATE_CLOSED` in both states and `_apply_rest_look` never glows the body. No surface is added: brute 3 draws before and after. Where the mask comes from: the Plated brute recipe paints the brow lip, ribs, shoulder and rear caps and all bolts vertex-colour black; `plated_brute.gd` `_mask_uvs` moves each vertex's UV to the lit or dark texel of a 2x1 mask (glb regenerated, 924 T / 3 D unchanged). Warden jaw plates (BoxMesh) take `rim_frame_mask()`, a 1/4-width white border on every cell of the box's 3x2 UV atlas, so a rim on each face. Not carried: the Mind hand plate bone (cylinder, its UVs span the whole texture) and the Vault bands take the dark default and show no face light, the Mind hand's crack glyph still shows amber; `boss_target.gd` recolours boss-supplied materials' albedo amber or slate (boss.gd owns the meshes) and was left as is. Measured, use6, linear luminance: brute plate face 0.698 amber (flood) to 0.048 (head), slate 0.016 both; rim 0.707 to 0.690 (still lit); body 0.538 to 0.063 (slate 0.062). front30 brute bbox mean 0.654 to 0.283, slate 0.071. Test `plate_cue_test.gd` (fails on scratch reverts to the flood, dark mask made white, slate glow, ignored state and an all-lit brute UV). Receipts: `artifacts/local/asset-units/plate-cue/`. Look in play at a plated brute and a Warden jaw when a punch crosses the plating: rim and bolts flare, faces stay charcoal. Round 3 (Plated brute, Andrew 2026-09-29): the recipe had painted whole boxes as rim (brow lip, shoulder caps, rear cap), so they lit as solid bars. Those boxes are plate again; the cue rides thin trim on the same BrutePlates surface: 0.05-0.07 m strips on the brow's front edge, the caps' outer edges, the slab's two outer edges, 0.08 m ribs and the bolts (six strips added, 996 T, 3 D; the plate-group test cap moved 400 to 450 T). The crack glyph moved to GLYPH_AT (0, -0.05, 1.42) at size 1.5 on the slab's front face, because the capture cameras see a brute from -Z, its rear, and the glyph only reads from the ball's side (+Z); round-3 captures put the eye on +Z with --anchor-offset 0,0,12 (use6) and 0,0,60 (front30). `plate_cue_test` now measures lit triangle area over the plate group's total area (9.1% now, ceiling 15%; the old recipe's RIM boxes give 28.5% and fail) and checks the glyph's projected box inside both frames. Pixel share of the lit trim over the visible plate group: about 20% at use6 and 37% at front30 (small sample, the glyph counts). Receipts: `artifacts/local/asset-units/plate-cue/` (crops4.png, measure4.txt, logs3/).
- **Plate cue fix round (2026-09-29).** The miniboss and the vault call `_plate_material()` with no mask and have no crack glyph, so the dark default gave them no state read at all. `_plate_material()` now defaults to `rim_frame_mask()` (their plates are BoxMesh, no new draw); only the mind hand (round bone, own glyph) stays dark, named by script in `_plates_are_round()`. `plate_cue_test` now drives `combat._tint()` (shred set or cleared) instead of the always-true tint_state check, and asserts the miniboss and vault rim (1.80) against face (0.00); a scratch revert to the dark default fails both.

## Harpoon canon (wave R1, unit C2, 2026-10-02)

The harpoon is one mode for every pilot, and it says every state it passes through. This replaces the `--harpoon`, `--harpoon-pull` and `--harpoon-keys` rows above: they are gone, with every branch that read them. The self-pull law and lock aim are the only behaviour. `harpoon_smash_speed`, `harpoon_lock_deg` and `harpoon_rewind` stay.

- **Keys.** Right mouse is the line: a press bites, holding keeps it, releasing lets go and flings. Ctrl hauls: on a latched body it commits the smash (unchanged, same floor and homing), on the world it winches while held. A line held on a body reels the ball in at the winch numbers, as it did under the lab's harpoon; Ctrl then commits the smash. Shift is one verb per pilot: Sable boosts, Rivet sprints, Kest blinks, with no plain-boost fallback. The keyboard fills `shift_*` on `Intent`; `player_ball._read_shift` turns it into `boost_pressed` (Sable) or `movement_*` (the class burst). A scripted source still fills `boost_*`, `movement_*`, `harpoon_*` and `brake_held` itself. Q (`reel_held`, removed from `Intent`), the wheel and C have no effect on the line; `cl_reel` and `cl_dash` stay registered, and `player.reel_step` is an empty stub until the unit that removes the registrations also removes game.gd's wheel call. While any line stands Ctrl is neither the brake nor the slam, until it is released. With no line standing Ctrl is the brake on the ground and the slam in the air, and refuses nothing.
- **States.** `player_ball._publish_line` emits `line_state_changed(state, target, at, cause)` on each change, from the kit: `searching` (nothing a press would bite), `locked` (a press would bite; target is the body, null on the world), `dry_fire`, `latched_world`, `latched_body`, `smash_committed`, `smash_lands`, `rewinding`, `let_go`, `broke`. A transient state holds for its beat (dry fire 0.25 s, let go 0.15 s, broke 0.3 s, smash lands 0.1 s) before the steady one returns. `harpoon_bit` and `harpoon_smashed` fire as before.
- **How a break's cause travels.** `AugmentKit.drop_line(end)` records `kit.line_end` for a line that stood: `let_go` (the player's own release or escape), `landed` (smash contact, published as `smash_lands`), `reset` (silent), else the cause. The player reads it the tick after, publishes `let_go` or `broke(cause)` and clears it. Causes: `body_died`, `out_of_reach` (stretched past BREAK_STRETCH ranges), `passed` (inside the minimum rope and radii), `timeout` (a haul ran out SMASH_MAX), `hold` (a landed crowd-control hold), `launched` (a ride, launch or rebound set the velocity). `sight` has no rule behind it and is not published.
- **Refusals.** `verb_failed("harpoon", reason, at)`: `no_aim`, `rewinding`. `verb_failed("haul", reason, at)`: `committed` (Ctrl on a haul already committed). `verb_failed("boost", reason, at)`: `uncharged` (Rivet's sprint or Kest's blink has no charge), `busy` (planted or mounted), `cooldown` (Sable's boost is cooling). A refused right mouse press with no line also publishes `dry_fire`.
- **Ready.** `player.verb_ready(verb)` is true on a tick where a press would succeed, for `harpoon` (not frozen, grapple fitted, no line standing, no gauge charging, not rewinding, an aim), `haul` (a line stands, and a body line is not yet committed) and `boost` (Sable: not cooling and not planted; Rivet and Kest: the burst can take a press).
- **Rope Anchor** (`winch_mount`): winching a world line up to a steep wall with Ctrl, within `MIN_ROPE + 2.5` of the anchor, plants the pylon there. Pylon Relay's `winch_bank` rides the same Ctrl winch.
- **Momentum.** A bite, a let-go and a forced break never lower the planar speed, and a smash commit never lowers the total speed. Free-flight drag still costs about 0.012 m/s per two ticks at 20 m/s; the test allows 0.03. A committed haul redirects the ball onto the body, so a steeply rising or falling haul trades planar speed for vertical, as it did before. The reel on a held body line is the rope's own pull: a ball moving away from the body is turned back toward it, as it was in the lab.
- **Drawing is unchanged.** The rope, hook head and aim marker in `player_ball.gd` read the same fields; the next unit replaces them.
- **Tests.** `tests/harpoon_canon_test.gd` is new. Deleted with their options: `harpoon_toggle_off_test` (off), `harpoon_burst_route_test` (shift routing and the plain-boost fallback), `harpoon_stale_tow_test` (heft tow), `burst_key_rule_agreement_test` and `dead_screen_keep_key_test` (C as the burst key). The death screen's C guard is gone with it.

## WY1: synergy facts on the slice

The slice keeps five synergies; `cause_armed` fires once per run when one goes live, and `cause_fired` fires wherever its effect resolves. The first firing is the discovery, and the discovery burst stays at the ball. A retry or arena retry clears the build's fired record, so a synergy still live across the rewind is found again at its next firing (the codex deduplicates within an attempt).

- **Owed: Storm Lattice and Thermal Shock emit from approximate sites.** Storm Lattice's `cause_fired` comes from every `shot_fired` while it is live, and Thermal Shock's carries a null body and the ball position. Both are cut from the slice. They move to combat's chain resolve and `element_field._shatter` when a writer has combat and the element field in scope.

## HLv: the latch you can see (wave R1, 2026-10-02)

`src/presentation/line_view.gd` draws the harpoon line for every state, and reads every colour and motion from the state's `LINE_ROWS` row. `player_ball.gd` no longer draws a rope, hook head or aim marker.

- **Cable.** A camera-facing ribbon from the ball to the anchor: 0.09 m and dimmed while slack (sagging by 0.3 of the slack, at most 3 m), 0.14 m and lightened when taut (a body latch, a winch, a committed smash, or the rope at length). Its width is the radius or 5 px at 1080p, whichever is wider, so it is 5 px at 45 m with the camera's own projection (a 0.14 m cable is about 5.3 px at 45 m at the chase camera's resting 65 degrees and about 3 px at its 88 degree cap, so the floor does the work at range and the radii show near). A smash commit flashes it white over 0.12 s. A let-go retracts it in 0.15 s; a break draws two rust halves for 0.3 s.
- **Lock and latch.** Lock: four live corner brackets close from 1.8x to 1x over 0.1 s and pulse at 2 Hz (motion `pulse`). Body latch: a barb at the anchor and a collar whose radius is 0.9 m plus 0.07 m per metre of gap, clamped to 3.2 m, steady (motion `steady`); both are freed on the first step after the line ends. World latch: a steady ring on the post, facing the ball, never on the floor. The smash landing pops a ring for 0.1 s.
- **Reticle.** Searching is a sand cross, out of range a hollow slate ring (a read-only ray finds a surface beyond the line's reach), locked a filled live ring on the world or a snap line to a body, whose brackets the line view draws. While a line stands the reticle is a dot. The inner ring at radius 19 is the harpoon's readiness (`harpoon_fill`, one minus rewind left over rewind time); the special's `gauge` ring at 24 is unchanged. A dry fire shakes it 3 px for 0.25 s and draws a slate line that darts 8 m and goes slack, gone at 0.25 s.
- **Bite.** `juice.gd` sends one 40 ms stop on `harpoon_bit` through `request_hit_stop`, so it shares the stop budget. A world latch emits no `harpoon_bit` and sends none.
- **Owed.** `GRAMMAR_SIGNALS` listening: `line_state_changed` (line view and reticle). `verb_failed` is not read here. Stills for a play look are in `artifacts/local/harpoon/`; they come from a synthetic scene and certify no feel.

## HLs: the line you can hear, and the notes of a synergy

Every harpoon state, refusal and synergy has its own cue, read from the grammar tables (`EventKinds.LINE_ROWS`, `Bindings.VERBS`, `CAUSE_ROWS`). `audio.gd` listens to `line_state_changed`, `verb_failed`, `cause_armed` and `cause_fired`.

- **States.** The bite is its own id, `line_bite`, two layers (a knock under its ring) at -7 dB trim; `grapple_clink` keeps its old single ring for the JUICE stick cue and the ride row; the world clunk (`anchor_clunk`, -12) carries a rope creak. The dry tick (`dry_tick`, -25) sits 14 dB under the bite, measured as the RMS of the baked samples plus the trim. The lock tick is gated to 0.25 s. The crack (`smash_crack`) is a D, a fifth under the bite's A ring, keyed like the bite, played with the row's boom (`slam_boom`, which `slammed` already started on the same landing, so it is not requested twice). Whistle, dock click, zip and twang have their own recipes; the twang is lower and 0.6 s against the zip's 0.16 s.
- **One event, one sound.** `grapple_fired`, `grapple_released`, `harpoon_bit` and `harpoon_smashed` no longer play from audio.gd: their sounds are the line's states. A refused right press publishes `verb_failed("harpoon")` and `dry_fire`, which share one dry tick through a 0.1 s gate.
- **The hum.** `line_hum` loops (a whole number of cycles) from a latch and stops on any other state, or on the tick the kit is no longer hooked, so a silent reset ends it too. On a body its pitch follows the gap from 1.0 at 45 m to 2.0 at 3 m (`Audio.hum_ratio`); a world line holds 1.0.
- **Refusals.** One quiet cue per verb (`verb_fail_*`, -18, under 0.3 s), at most one per 0.25 s per verb.
- **Motifs.** Notes are `cause_note_<midi>` bank ids baked from the row's note names (`Harmony.note_midi`). Arming plays notes one and two 0.14 s apart; the first firing of a run plays the third; later firings play it at most every 4 s per synergy. `run_started` resets "first".
- **Sketches.** `tools/sound_sketches.gd` writes WAVs to `artifacts/local/harpoon/sounds/` at their game levels: bite and crack A to C, clunk, dry tick, zip, twang, a 3 s hum with the octave climb, and the five motifs. Only A of each is wired. The owner picks by ear; nothing here certifies feel.
- **Test** `tests/line_sound_test.gd`.

## WY2: the callout that shows why a synergy fired

When a synergy's effect resolves, the player sees its two causes and its effect where it struck, in one form for every row (LEGIBILITY section 5).

- **Form.** `src/ui/why_callout.gd`, a HUD piece. On `cause_fired(id, target, at)` the two cause glyphs snap together, an arrow draws to the effect glyph in the row's `CAUSE_ROWS` shape, and the whole part runs 0.9 s beside the struck body, clamped between the toasts and prompts bands and off a 70 px square round the reticle. Every colour is `EventKinds.cause_color(id)`; the file holds no colour literal. The cause glyphs are the row's two tags (`Synergies.ROWS[id].tags`) through `EventKinds.TAG_GLYPHS` (`tag_glyph`, `cause_glyphs`), the one vocabulary the callout and the link at the ball read: arc bolt, kinetic disc, mass core, anchor bar, speed chevron, spiral wave, split diamond, chain square, frost diamond (frost is off the slice and owed a glyph of its own). No tag glyph equals an effect shape.
- **Pulse.** `vfx.gd` draws a pulse in the row's shape on the struck body (at `at` when the body is null) on every `cause_fired`, including the firings the callout skips. The discovery burst and ring at the ball are gone; the persistent ring per synergy stays.
- **Name.** The synergy's name rides the callout on its first three firings per profile (`why:<id>` in `UiProfile.uses`), counted only when a callout is drawn. `drawn_words()` on the callout reports it.
- **Limits.** One callout per row per 4 s; one on screen while a fight is on and two when calm. "Fight" is the card's "calm" turned round: `WhyCallout.is_calm`, 3 s with no live telegraph and no damage, and never while the tree is paused. Over a limit only the pulse draws.
- **Armed.** `cause_armed(id)` links the two cause glyphs and a bar at the ball for 0.6 s, with no name and no banner.
- **The card waits.** `codex_banner.gd` holds a first-discovery card (cards mode) at the head of its queue until `calm()` (`WhyCallout.is_calm`, shared with the callout); a retry stage entry dismisses a held card unseen, and a paused tree holds it. The banner finds the bus through its presentation ancestor (presentation.gd is another unit's), so damage is counted without a wire there. A card raised when calm shows at once; `dismiss(true)` still drops a held one.
- **Test.** `tests/why_callout_test.gd` steps every clock by hand. A scratch change of `NAME_USES` from 3 to 4 fails its name check.

## Nine verbs from one table (wave R1, unit C1, 2026-10-02)

Every key in play comes from `Bindings.VERBS` (roll, look, jump, fire, harpoon, haul, boost, special, interact). Each verb row adds `actions` (the InputMap actions it registers, with default keys, a mouse button, or `motion` for the look), `glyph` (the key glyph id), `label` and `icon`. `game.gd` registers every action from `Bindings.actions()` and holds no key table. `AbilityState.KEYS`, `ClassKit` (slot and extra keys, the movement slot reads RMB for Sable and Shift for Rivet and Kest) and `FieldNotes.CONTROLS` (nine verb rows first, then menu keys) read the same table, so the field notes, the class card, the controls diagram and the ability bar cannot name a key the table does not bind. `tests/bindings_table_test.gd` checks it.

- **What went.** The Q reel (`cl_reel`), the mouse-wheel reel and the C dash (`cl_dash`) have no registration and no handler; the wheel handler's call to `player.reel_step` is removed and the stub stays in `player_ball.gd` for the coordinator to delete. The field notes lost the Q and wheel rows; the class kit lost the Sable "winch on Q" extra. Menu and screen keys (Tab, P, Esc, R, the dead-screen keys, page-turning Q and E inside the notes) are not verbs and stay.
- **Dev keys.** F1, F3 and F4 act only when the game was launched with `--dev` (`play.mjs` forwards it). `Game.dev` is also settable by a test.
- **Action names.** `cl_movement` is now `cl_harpoon`, `cl_brake` is `cl_haul`, `cl_mount` is `cl_interact`. Nothing outside `game.gd` and `intent.gd` read them.
- **State table.** `Bindings.STATES` lists the ball states highest first (line on body, line on world, charging, mounted, air, ground), `ball_state()` picks the highest that holds and `reading(verb, state)` says what a verb's key does there; the test walks all 24 combinations and finds one verb per key in each.
- **Prompt.** `key_glyphs.gd` draws a sand 28 px keycap and the verb's icon in the live colour at the thing the verb acts on (aim point for the harpoon, anchor for the haul, ball for the boost), only on ticks `player.verb_ready(verb)` is true, two at most with the line's state first. Retire after three successes on the profile (five for the smash, filed as `smash`; a winch is filed as `haul`; a bite as `harpoon`; Sable's boost, Rivet's sprint and Kest's blink as `boost`). It returns for 3 s after `verb_failed`, after 90 s unused in a run and once at the first availability of each run. HOOK rides the harpoon prompt until the first bite on the profile and SMASH the body-haul prompt until the first smash, both from `LINE_ROWS`. The haul prompt waits for the line to stand 1.5 s without a Ctrl press (a smash, a winch or a refused haul restarts the wait). The brake has no prompt.
- **Owed: the pylon and slingshot glyphs are gone.** The old E-to-mount and Space-to-slingshot glyphs had no `verb_ready` answer, so they left with the old table. Sable's mount and slingshot teach through the field notes only until `verb_ready` answers for `interact` and `jump`.
- **Word budget.** `ui_word_budget_test` (quarantined) counts 34 HUD words in the stage-1 fight, up from 32 at the base: the prompts add HOOK and a Shift key.

## One Warden loop (wave R1b, unit B0b, 2026-10-02)

The owner played all three loops and ruled for `v2`, the loop the default game runs. The laps and drop loops, with their rules, drawing, sounds, bot policies, launch flags and tests, are deleted; nothing in the game asks which loop is running.

- **What went.** `loop_mode.gd`, `warden_drop.gd`, `lap_course.gd`, `lap_course_view.gd` and `drop_juice.gd`; the `loop_mode` and `gun_role` tuning rows and the `--loop=` and `--gun=` flags; the title screen's L and G keys; the lap and drop signals and their event rows; the Spillway's drop lines, return lift and parked lip swap; the beacon's collars; the Warden's sleep, strip and near-miss reads; the director's lap step; the objective badge's segments; the lapper bot policies and their notes; the combat ledger's `drops_to_break`; the hit-stop budget that only the loop modes used.
- **What stayed.** The music, breath, discovery and telegraph rows keep their options, defaults and flags; their key names are constants on `Tuning` (`MUSIC_KEY`, `BREATH_KEY`, `DISCOVERY_KEY`, `TELEGRAPH_KEY`). The harpoon hand's two reads moved into `bot_driver.gd` as `LINE_REACH` (50.0) and `DROP_MEMORY` (6.0).
- **Old records.** `balance_report.gd` still joins and reads a run log that carries `loop_mode`, `gun_role` and drop fields, and ignores them; `tests/balance_report_old_record_test.gd` holds one.
- **Demand:** the Warden fight, which the next unit rebuilds on a posture bar.

## The posture fight (wave R1b, unit B0a, 2026-10-02)

On the slice table the Warden is fought on one rule: hit it until its guard bar fills and it cracks open, then smash the open core to shatter one of four health chunks. Every other table keeps the plating rule; the stage row's `warden_fight` picks which.

- **The numbers** live in `boss_posture.gd`: gain is `base x clamp(punch / plating, 0.2, 1.5)`, base 30 for a ram, stomp or slam, 45 for a harpoon smash, 1.5 for any other kind and 3 while the Warden charges its pulse. Drain starts after 3 s without a gain, 12 a second. The stagger lasts 4 s. A shatter needs a smash, a ram at speed tier 2 or faster, or a chunk already cracked by an earlier stagger.
- **One gain per frame from contacts.** A smash landing strikes twice or more in one physics frame; contact gains in a frame take the largest, so the landing counts once at 45.
- **Chose where the packet was silent.** The hit carries `hp` and `max_hp` so the rule can price a shatter without scene access. With plating 0 the multiplier reads as the 1.5 cap. The rule object also emits `posture_changed` from the drain, not only from hits.
- **Guns cannot hurt an open core.** A gun, blast or synergy strike on an open core deals 0 and cracks nothing, so holding fire cannot win.
- **After review (coordinator).** A crack pays off only in a later stagger: a second lesser contact in the same opening does nothing more, so bouncing on an open core cannot shatter it. A glancing contact on the open core still cracks and a glancing smash still shatters, so a build at the floor is slow, not walled. Contacts in the frame a chunk shatters gain nothing, so the landing's own shockwave does not start the next bar. Health is armed only when a chunk actually fell. A hit the rule did not judge a core hit reports no part. The posture Warden's plates take no tint.
- **Measured, not tuned.** Bots on today's Reach break the slice Warden in about 30 s of fighting whether they grew first or not, so the numbers do not yet make growing matter. The bands unit before the sitting owns that, after the map retrofit.
- **Demand:** the Warden fight on the slice, which the plate, cracks and sounds units will draw.

## Packs from one table, holds that never slow the ball (wave R2, unit EN, 2026-10-02)

On the slice table enemies arrive as four authored packs and the two holds that remain can bend the ball's path but never lower its speed. The three-stage game's director, roster, events and other holding kinds are untouched.

- **Packs.** `slice_defs.gd` carries `PACKS` (run_down, grounded, rebound), `ELITE` (plated brute and winch drone) and `ELITE_ARRIVAL` 3, answered by `packs()`, `elite()`, `elite_arrival()` and `has_vault()`; `stage_defs.gd` answers empty, empty, 0 and true. `world.gd` hands them to the director, which with authored packs spawns nothing else: every arrival is one whole pack, drawn uniformly and never the same twice in a row, with the third arrival the elite, once. Pacing is the director's own, and a pack waits until the cap has room for all of it. A director with no authored packs draws exactly as before (arrival counts and positions pinned for two seeds in `slice_packs_case`).
- **No events, no vault.** The slice's `event_mix` is `{}`; the event director returns before building anything on an empty mix, so it schedules nothing, including a map's unique events. `world.gd` queues and spawns the vault only when the table has one.
- **The bound.** `Holds.bound(velocity, accel)` removes the part of a hold's acceleration against the planar velocity, caps the part across it at `speed * PI / 3` (the heading turns at most 60 degrees a second) and passes everything below 1 m/s. `pull` and `catch` apply it to the kinds in `Holds.BOUNDED` (`mag_pit`, `winch_pull`), so the two holds cannot skip it on any table; the other holding kinds take it when they return to a table.
- **Mag trapper.** The drag term and `DRAG` are gone; the pit pulls toward its centre only, through the bound.
- **Winch drone.** Takes over the hooker's job: planar speed at `SNAP_SPEED` 19 snaps the cable through `Holds.snap` with reason speed (the drone's row gains `speed` in its own copy; `roster.gd` is not edited), and `strain()` reads speed over `SNAP_SPEED`. The drone draws no cable of its own (the holds node draws every binding), so no glow was added.
- **Measured.** A ball at 20 m/s through a live pit left at 22.12 m/s against 21.34 on the same line with no pit; at the base it left at 6.32. Packs in the first 90 s of a slice game: 4 to 6 over the seeds run.
- **Owed.** The pack rate (4 to 6 packs, 9 to 13 live enemies in 90 s) is the director's old pacing, not tuned for packs.

## Enemy state on the body (wave R2, unit C5, 2026-10-02)

Three facts about an enemy now read on its body from `STATE_ROWS`, and none of them draws amber or the class colour.

- **Before the hit.** Combat still passes `"amber"` (your hit breaks the plate), `"slate"` (it glances) or `""`. `"amber"` draws the `will_break` row: the plate rim and the crack in the live colour, the rim pulsing at 2 Hz about 14 percent either side of 1.8. `"slate"` draws the `glancing` colour on the same rim at steady energy 0.5 and hides the crack. `""` is the rest look. Same material, same mask, same three draws as before. `Enemy.rim_color` stays the pilot's identity colour and no plate cue reads it.
- **A glance.** The `strike_deflected` glyph takes the `glancing` row's shape (burst) and colour (slate), where it was drawn before. The sound is unchanged.
- **A broken wind-up.** `status_marks.gd` listens to each spawned enemy's `attack_phase`. On the `windup` phase it records that enemy's `knocks` (raised only in `knock()`), keyed by instance; on `cancelled`, while the enemy is alive and not queued for deletion, it draws the `interrupted` row (a live star) 2.4 m over the body for 0.5 s through the vfx glyph pool, only if `knocks` rose since the wind-up began, at most once a second per enemy. Both break branches already publish cancelled: the chaser through `_end_attack` and `_clear_telegraph`, the counter through `_end`. `attack_facts_enemy_test` pinned that; `status_marks_test` pins the star, including a chaser broken through the game's ram contact path and one knocked directly.
- **Plated kinds that receive a tint.** Plated brute (24), miniboss, vault (60) and mind hand (20). The Warden has plating 30 but takes `""` on the posture table.
- **Chose where the packet was silent.** A cancelled phase that no hit caused draws no star. That covers a lobber whose ball leaves its range mid-charge (it publishes cancelled with the lobber alive and `knocks` unchanged), a counter whose telegraph vanished, and a parent torn down around a child. The star claims that your hit counted. The per-enemy records (last star time, knocks at wind-up) drop the enemy on `tree_exiting`. The rim pulse runs from the enemy's physics tick while `tint_state` is amber; `set_tint` never changes whether a node processes, so the Warden's own `_process` (look and kit) keeps running whatever combat tints it.
- **Demand:** the Warden's own look, which `hero_assets.gd` reads from the plate when `tint_state` is amber and otherwise paints ancient blue, to take the same rows.

## The boss plate and a Warden that shows its fight (wave R2, unit B3, 2026-10-02)

A Warden fought on the posture rule shows the fight three ways from one source: a plate, its own body and a four-note phrase. A Warden on the plating rule runs the code it ran before.

- **The plate** (`src/ui/boss_plate.gd`, 420 x 44 px, `bind` and `unbind`) is a name from a table keyed by family (Reactor, Hive or Mind Warden), four chunk pips, the guard bar with ticks at 25, 50 and 75, and one glyph at the bar's end from `STATE_ROWS`: `open` while staggered, `glancing` for 0.6 s after a glance on this Warden, else `posture_step`. A cracked current chunk wears a crack mark in the `chunk_shatter` colour. While staggered the bar is full in the live colour and drains over `stagger_left()`. No number is drawn; `drawn_words()` returns the name's words. The HUD mounts it at merge.
- **The body.** The three seam levels on the jaws are the guard: level n lights at 25 n and seals as the guard drains, in the neutral colour, and the model's copied strokes follow the look's. While staggered each jaw leans 14 degrees further out from its bottom edge (two opposite jaws stand 28 degrees further apart) and the core takes the live colour, steady; both return after. The health ring and its outline are not drawn and the diamond stays, in the neutral colour at rest. Combat's hit flash is kept off the body's emission, so no amber is on a posture Warden and the live colour is on it only while open.
- **The phrase** (`SfxBank.BREAK_PHRASES`, variant 0): D4 F4 A4 D5. A hit plays the note of the guard's quarter as the hit's pitch, read before the hit lands. The stagger sounds the four notes together (mallet notes) with the `open` row's sound and cuts the charge cue it cancelled, once. A shattered chunk plays the `chunk_shatter` row's sound over a new baked low hit, `chunk_low`, once. A draining guard is silent and a glance keeps its clank.
- **The owner picks the break by ear** from `break-a`, `break-b` and `break-c` in `tools/sound_sketches.gd`: A is D F A D, the minor arpeggio, even at 0.16 s; B stacks fourths (D G C F), slower at 0.2 s; C steps D F G A with the gaps closing (0.26, 0.17, 0.11 s) and a longer wait before the chord.
- **Tests:** `boss_plate_test`, `warden_posture_look_test` and `warden_posture_sound_test`. The colour check scans `boss_plate.gd` and the code added to the look and the model for a `Color(` literal.

## One objective board, and a HUD cut to the fight (wave R2, unit O1, 2026-10-02)

The plate says one thing about the goal, from one table: a glyph and five words or fewer. `src/core/objectives.gd` holds the rows (`find_warden`, `break_guard`, `smash_core`, `grow_return`, `break_warden`, the three lesson rows, `charge_beacon`, `roll_gate`, `hold_line`, `break_brindle`, and since w22 `break_yard_crew` and `break_clamp_crews` for the Spillway's yard lock and Warden clamp, whose plate ring fills with the clamp's pips) and `Objectives.choose(reading)`, a pure function over a small dictionary, so a unit case walks every row without a scene. `OBJECTIVE_SHORT` and `LESSON_SHORT` left `event_kinds.gd`. The edge marker takes its glyph from the same row (`edge_markers.objective_id`, set by the HUD each frame). `flow.objective_text()` and the long `objective` strings are unchanged.

- **Struggling.** `Objectives.Struggle` counts simulated seconds in which the posture gained within the last 3 s; 3 s with no gain drops the tally, so only sustained effort adds up; 10 s of it with posture never at 50 reads "Grow, then return". Reaching 50 or a stagger clears it for the engagement, and `warden_engaged`, a new stage, a retry and a new run start it over. It is fed by `posture_changed` and the HUD's physics tick, never a hit count or the wall clock.
- **What left play.** The heft dial, the punch and plating bars and notch, the core pips, the run clock, the HUD's scrap gauge, the four first-run callouts (the HUD no longer builds `callouts.gd`), and the `+ {part}`, `Took {part}` and `Heft {class}` toasts (`Hud.RETIRED_TOASTS`; the rows keep their wording). Stage pips, the threat tier, health, armour, the speed glyph, the ability bar, the plate and the prompts stay.
- **Scrap.** The workshop's counter (`shop_counter.gd`, the wallet row) already shows scrap, so the HUD's scrap gauge is deleted rather than shown only while the workshop is open.
- **Part name at the ball.** `part_name.gd`, owned by the HUD: on every `part_gained` the first two words of the part's name ride above the ball in the amber role colour for 1.2 s of physics time; a second pickup replaces it.
- **Top-centre slot.** `Hud.mount_plate(plate: Control)` puts any Control top-centre and moves the objective plate `PLATE_GAP` under it; `Hud.unmount_plate()` restores the layout. The plate is listed in `piece_rects()` and its `drawn_words()` count in the word budget.
- **Keys not drawn twice.** `AbilityBar.hide_keys(glyphs: Array[String])` hides the keycap of a slot whose key a prompt on screen already shows; the HUD passes `KeyGlyphs.shown_glyphs()`. LET GO is the RMB keycap, a fling arrow and the words at the anchor, below the Ctrl winch prompt, on the first three world latches of a profile (counted as `letgo` uses), inside the two-prompt limit.
- **Word budget.** The fight case is rebuilt on the slice table (posture Warden engaged, a 420 by 44 stand-in plate reporting two words, two prompts, one named callout, a repeat part pickup) and counts 24 against 25, so `ui_word_budget_test` is out of quarantine. It was 34 before. A first find's reveal card (the banner "FOUND · BUILD", the part name and its line, 10 words in the 24 above) is presentation's and still shows on a repeat; a first find adds the reveal view's 6 on top.
- **Demand:** the Warden's plate, which mounts in the slot.

## PR: the pilot reacts to the harpoon, 2026-10-02

The three `pilot-v1` pilots now brace when `harpoon_bit` fires and heave when `harpoon_smashed` fires. `brace` lasts 0.6 s: the torso snaps forward and compresses, the head dips, both hands stay at the controls and Sable's ears or Kest's crest lay back. `heave` lasts 0.8 s: the torso recoils into the seat, then punches forward with the right arm raised and the left arm counterbracing. Rivet clamps both claws, Sable whips the tail and Kest flares the fans through the existing hand bones. Each clip starts and ends at exact seated rest.

The reaction rows use the packet's priorities and cooldowns unchanged: brace 35 and 1.5 s, heave 50 and 1.0 s. `player_hit` remains priority 60 and interrupts either with `wince`. The existing bus wiring handles both events. No rig, shell or gameplay changes accompany the clips.

At base `37bc501f674a8229fbdef70256fec530cd0a9184`, decoded before/after GLB dumps prove all key times, values, targets and interpolation of `big_cheer`, `wince`, `slump`, `phew` and `drop_land` equal the base for all three pilots. The 25 joints, rest transforms, vertex attributes, oriented triangle sets and materials also match; Blender reordered some triangles. The new clips have zero endpoint component error. Evidence, cached-reference wrapper, exported poses and editable Blender scenes are under `artifacts/local/tasks/alpha-x1-PR/`.

The real Game test passes duration, rest, signal playback, interruption and unchanged velocity checks. Removing only the `harpoon_bit` row makes six checks fail, two per pilot; restoring it passes. Lint reports 0 findings. The requested smoke suite imports successfully with writable XDG config/cache directories and passes every test except `world_one_shell_rivet_test` and `world_one_shell_kest_test`, which pin the old complete GLB hashes. Those tests are outside this unit's scope and need coordinator hash updates before PR-6 can pass. No rendered capture was run; the coordinator still owes chase-camera readability and human acceptance.

## The Cable Reach rebuilt as three lines (wave R2, unit Z3, 2026-10-02)

The slice table's stage is now `map_reach.gd`, which extends `map_gorge.gd` and leaves it unedited. Each pilot has an authored line of chained rides from the spawn to the Warden; the Warden's bowl has four swing pillars, one kicker and a berm; the reward gates stand at three fixed slots where the workshop bay docks.

- **Lines.** Sable: deck, marble run (extended one point so it pours into tunnel w1, no end cup), tunnel dive, merge under the bridges, kicker. Rivet: cable (moved to run from (96,-104) to (84,-34)), carve right, cracked wall at cruise, tunnel e1, merge, kicker. Kest: the same cable, straight into the skylight halfpipe, the new berm onto the bowl's bank. The waypoints are in `tools/traversal_lines.json` as the layout sheet wrote them, except two second waypoints moved 8 m downhill, the sheet's own flow-share adjustment (Sable (-40,-152) to (-36,-145), Rivet (48,-132) to (44,-126)).
- **Not built here.** Barges, lifts, cannon, trebuchet, geyser, sky dish, valves, cave-in, impact, flood basin. Thermals: one on Kest's shelf, one at the berm exit.
- **Engine behaviour.** `_init` of a subclass does not run the parent's: `map_reach.gd` calls `super()` first. `_marble_points` is overridden so the parent's `_init` builds the seven-point channel.
- **Gate slots.** `StageMap.gate_slots` (each `{at, facing}`) makes `reward_gates.gd` stand its gates there; with two offers the outer slots are used, with one the middle, with more offers than slots the old placement runs. 16 of 16 parked-ball breaks dock the bay with the slots; with the slot path off, spots fail (stand-at-the-ball placement, bay not docked on some spots), which is the owner's "bay did not appear" bug. `StageMap.activity_sites` is data for a later unit: ambush altar (-16,44), cracked cache (70,-4), hauler bounty (0,-88).
- **The wall.** A cracked-wall spec with `break_at_cruise` breaks for any ball at 0.85 x cruise or faster (`Activities.RAM_MIN_PER_CRUISE`, ruling ad); the Reach sets it, the gorge does not.
- **Measured.** Heights match the sheet within 0.5 m at nine points; every post and wall stands on its ground; Rivet 0.884 and Kest 0.914 flow share meet the bars, with the wall breaking and the cable firing on every seed.
- **Sable does not meet the bar** (median 0.735 against 0.8974, 0 to 3 dead stops a seed, 6 to 11 m back toward the spawn). From per-tick logs: the ball hits the marble's second bend at about 37 m/s (the bot's Shift boost on the long straight; the layout assumed 25 to 30), leaves the channel on the 55 degree bend and rolls the west shelf, where it touches the satellite tower at (-70.8,-20.8), a 5.0 m centre distance for a 4 m tower and a 1 m ball, or a shelf face at (-112,-47.6); then it lands on tunnel w1's 30 degree dive at about (-51,-1) and loses more than half its planar speed. No remaining adjustment in the sheet covers these. Candidates for the next round: a taller or more banked outer wall on the two bends, a longer easing into the dive, and a post-sweep of the tower with the ball riding the real line.
- **Cache pocket at the spawn.** The interactables table places `plinth_cache` (a cracked cache) at (-5,-156) for stage 1 whatever the map, so its three wall bodies stand on the Reach's spawn apron and under the first leg of each line. The ball falls in from above so no run was stopped by it, but the line sweep names them. Not in this unit's files.
- **Chose where the packet was silent.** The line sweep passes the ball-radius sphere at 1 m steps and counts only map props and contraptions; interactable devices are printed as a note. `uphill_m` is the largest drop in z from the furthest z reached.

## Z3 round two: Sable's west strand (wave R2, 2026-10-02)

Round one's Sable line missed her bar (median 0.7345 against 0.8974, 12 dead stops, 6.4 to 11.2 m back, 0.74 of ticks near her line). Two independent builds of the sheet gave the same numbers, so the cause was the sheet. The strand is rebuilt in `map_reach.gd` only; `map_gorge.gd` has no diff, and Rivet's and Kest's strands are untouched.

- **What the strand is.** Deck glide, a banked sweeper on the west shelf, an open dive ramp through tunnel w1's old notch, the merge, the kicker. It leaves the deck to the west, it is about 370 m of travel, and it chains a banked ride and a dive.
- **The sweeper.** One left turn of ball radius 68 about (-32.3,-78) with a `Features.banked_arc` (feet at 58, crest at 70, bank 10, width 12) on its outside; no channel and no lip. The gorge's marble channel is switched off while `super.sample` runs (`marble_path` is held empty for the call), and `marble_path` is the sweeper's polyline so the ride fact still fires.
- **The dive.** The ramp lies on w1's axis from 45 m before the mouth to 60 m past it, 12 m flat floor plus an 8 m blend, its floor from a table (`_build_dive`): the slope eases in over 40 m to about 23 degrees and out over 35 m onto the trough floor, so its vertical radius is 63 m at the top bend and 55 m at the bottom one: under the 80 m a 28 m/s ball needs to stay on it, so she hops it for up to a second (longest flight 1.11 s) and lands on the ramp. The tunnel's carve is overwritten inside the corridor. `_tunnel_roofs` is overridden to skip w1, and `_landmark` to skip the satellite at (-72,-16), by calling the gorge's own helpers.
- **The measured loop.** Eight tries on 8 seeds, one change each (flow share is the median, back is the largest over seeds, on-line is the median at the time):

| Try | Change | Flow | Dead stops | Back (m) | On-line | Kept |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | Round-one build | 0.7345 | 12 | 11.2 | 0.736 | |
| 1 | Marble channel replaced by one banked sweeper (centre (-46.7,-90.8)) | 0.8708 | 0 | 13.8 | 0.858 | yes |
| 2 | Sweeper moved to start at the deck's edge (centre (-32.3,-78)); a post and a wall moved off it | 0.7674 | 12 | 9.5 | 0.908 | yes: the stops are now the w1 roof, which she reaches on its axis |
| 3 | Dive opened: ramp over w1's axis, w1 roof and the (-72,-16) satellite dropped | 0.8277 | 0 | 4.5 | 0.888 | yes |
| 4 | Opening waypoints aimed down the deck so the glide holds speed, sweeper entered at 225 degrees | 0.9387 | 0 | 23.8 (two seeds loop at the kicker) | 0.801 | yes |
| 5 | Sweeper re-centred (-46.7,-90.8) and entered at 207 degrees, ramp to 60 m | 0.7605 | 16 | 3.0 | 0.702 | no: she left the berm at (-139,41,-89) |
| 6 | On try 4's sweeper, ramp starts 45 m before the mouth and ends 60 m past it | 0.9469 | 0 | 4.7 | 0.982 | yes |
| 7 | Deck-leg waypoints 2 m south | 0.9473 | 0 | 3.3 | 0.910 | yes |
| 8 | Waypoints on the berm's foot (radius 61), the wall moved to stand on its ground | 0.9453 | 0 | 3.0 | 0.935 | yes |

- **What the traces said.** The bot holds Shift and reaches 28 to 33 m/s at the mouth, 47 to 56 m/s down the dive. The spawn's glide decides where the first 1.4 s goes: steering the glide down the deck (south-west) keeps it at 21 m/s instead of 9 to 11, and the only ticks under tier 2 are then 0.6 s of the glide (57 of about 1000), which took the flow share from 0.83 to 0.94 with the sweeper moved. A ball leaving a 45 m/s dive turns about 49 degrees to the kicker in about 40 m, so the ramp ends 50 m earlier than the tunnel's axis exit would put it (try 6).
- **Chose where the packet was silent.** Wall `(-45,-100)` was built on the lip and floated 21.8 m; it stands at (-64,-105). The step check walks Sable's line from waypoint 2 to the first waypoint at or past z 44 and leaves out nothing; the waypoints from the sweeper on lie at the berm's foot, where the ball climbs onto the bank by physics, so the check sees no kerb. The two heights on her strand are re-stated.
- **Tests.** `reach_map_test`: the heights at (-62.1,-25.4) and (-58,-10) are 26.8 and 20.0 (round one: 30.7 and 28.2), the four-point marble step check is now the line step check, the marble side sweep covers the dive.
- **Not changed or not checked.** The bot is not a person: 0.9453 says a ball can hold speed down the strand, not that it feels good. The kicker fires on 3 of 8 Sable seeds. The seeds give near-identical runs, so the median is one run's number.

## SS: one launch name, and a run log that records the sitting, 2026-10-02

`scrapkeep core-loop` was refused with ten prefix matches and `scrapkeep <worktree>` opened `ball-lab`. A name that is exactly a sketch's directory now resolves first, in the worktree the command was typed in, else the first worktree; prefixes are tried only when no sketch has that exact directory name. A worktree's bare name opens its `core-loop` sketch before the old fallbacks. The run log gains new keys only: the record's `table`, and on each stage row `packs` (the faction of each arriving pack, in order), `causes` (synergy id to times fired) and, for a posture Warden only, `posture` with `staggers`, `chunks_lost`, `glances` and `fight_ticks` (first posture gain to the break, or to the line's tick while open). Chunks lost and glances are read from the Warden when the line is written, because a glance moves no bar; `glances` reads the Warden's posture state, since no bus signal carries it. `node scripts/run-summary.mjs` prints one line per run and shows a dash for what an older record lacks.

## A find never puts a card up during a fight (wave R3, unit RV, 2026-10-02)

When the ball picks up a part or a synergy lands in a fight, the only thing drawn is the part's name at the ball. A first find's card waits until the player is calm. A repeat find never shows a card.

- **One gate.** `ScreenBusy` in `src/ui/screen_busy.gd` (wave 36, PF-19) replaced `WhyCallout.find_may_speak`: a find's card or crate shows only when the screen has been quiet for `FIND_HOLD` (3 s: no fight, no pick or briefing or crate up, no damage, no live telegraph) and, for a card, it is the oldest ready one, alone on screen. The fight is the stage's pressure through `MixRows.combat_after` (the music's own hysteresis), a named fight that has entered a phase until its enemy dies, or an engaged Warden standing. The ceremony and the banner keep no damage clock of their own; both ask `settled(FIND_HOLD)` and `may_show(key, FIND_HOLD)`.
- **Held, in order.** A first find that fails the gate is held and opens one at a time, the first time the gate is true; held cards open in the order found within each queue (the reveal cards, the ceremonies, the banner), not across them. At most three are held (`MAX_HELD`); a fourth shows its name only and stays unseen. A second find of a part whose card is held does not queue a second card.
- **Seen means shown.** The ceremony's `reward-finds.json` and the banner's `discoveries_seen` are written when the card opens. `find_of` no longer marks a part seen, so callers that need a repeat (`word_budget_fight.gd`, `ceremony_capture.gd`) call `_mark_seen` themselves. A retry, a death, a screen change and a new run drop what is held; the part stays unseen.
- **Repeat finds.** No card and no pop, in a fight or out of one, at any tier. The tier drops to its quiet form (a ceremony or common reveal to the common tick, a rare one to the ring and bell) so the sound stays. The banner's pop is gone, with `POP_TIME` and `GLYPH_POP`.
- **The ceremony tier** waits for the gate and for its own `calm()` (the pause rule: nothing near, on the ground) as long as that takes. The `calm_wait_s` demotion into a card in the flow is gone, and so is its number in the `reward_ceremony` row. With the pause not armed (test mode) a ceremony still plays as a reveal card, now behind the gate. `calm()` is the ceremony's pause-safety rule layered on the gate: it can delay a ceremony and never opens one early. A boss's own reward (source `boss`) asks only `calm()` and the calm hold, because the boss's death ends the fight and the victory roll is shorter than the damage clock. A reveal card that is up when a Warden fight turns live closes at once; the part stays seen. The banner's gate covers every head, in the banner option too.
- **No hit-stop from a pickup while the gate is false.** The ring and the sound stay at find time.
- **Where the packet was silent, the choice that puts fewer words on screen in a fight:** a repeat part that completes a synergy shows no card; a repeat synergy shows nothing in either discovery mode (cards mode drops it on the banner's seen record, banner mode in `presentation._on_synergy`); a first find past the cap or a held duplicate takes the quiet form of its tier; a Warden-break gate or vault find that is a repeat is a sound and a name only.
- **A fight going live closes what is up.** The ceremony closes its reveal card and the banner closes its card or banner (`codex_banner._process`); the discovery stays seen and anything queued waits for the gate.
- **Accessor.** `warden_fight_live` reads `flow.engaged()`, a public method added to `flow.gd` for it.

## ER: an enemy shows what your hit did, 2026-10-02

`EventKinds.IMPACT[hit].react` chooses the body's reaction. `EventKinds.REACTIONS`, beside that table, supplies numeric angles in degrees and times in seconds. Flinch tilts 10 degrees and recovers in 0.12 s. Stagger tilts 28 degrees, holds for 0.07 s and recovers through one opposite overshoot, finishing in 0.36 s. Launch tips 65 degrees and adds a tumble about the axis across the knock. Its length follows the initial planar knock divided by the existing knock decay, plus recovery, capped at 0.9 s. Its tumble amplitude follows the remaining planar knock. All reaction angles, including tumble and overshoot, scale by `1 / sqrt(max(weight, 1))`: weight 3 draws about 58% of weight 1's angle. The knock itself is unchanged.

The base enemy owns `ReactionPivot`. Its mesh helpers and each kind's visual root parenting put meshes below this pivot, with one weak-point exception. The mind hand's lens remains directly on the body because `lens_point()` reads that mesh's global position as a hit point. Its base and arm joints react below the pivot; the lens and its child meshes preserve the existing hit geometry. A numeric test checks zero lens-position difference during a ram reaction. This preserves the no-hit-box-change rule without editing the kind's logic. Collision shapes, telegraphs and other modules' attachments retain their parents. Each kind's `_pose()` still works on its existing nodes. The pivot adds rotation only; it neither moves the body nor changes velocity, collision or an attack's clock. New hits start from the current reaction rotation. Completed reactions write exact identity.

A body latch eases a fixed 10-degree lean toward the real ball over 0.15 s. This reads through the sight wrapper to the real player, so a cloak decoy cannot redirect the lean. `smash_committed` eases to the opposite lean as a brace; subsequent line states ease to rest. Lean and hit rotation multiply together on the same pivot. The existing line signal has four arguments, including `cause`; ER handles that signature and adds no signal or colour.

The structure exclusion uses the existing `kind` values `warden`, `vault` and `miniboss`, plus the `brindle_` prefix. Warden needs no edit. Brindle's pieces use `boss_target.gd`, do not inherit the enemy base and have no reaction method or pivot.

Weapon `_hit()` hands over its actual impact row, including source overrides such as overdrive and spill. Ball contacts call `strike()` directly, so the base also listens to the existing `enemy_struck` fact. A handed marker prevents the same `_hit()` from restarting twice. Both bus connections disconnect when the enemy exits. `reactions_enabled` is a visual-only test seam.

`enemy_reactions_test` runs a live slice Game at its existing 90 physics ticks per second. The acceptance windows are 18 frames for gun and lean, 90 for ram and 27 for line release. Placed starts keep unintended ball contacts from restarting the prescribed hits. Its seed-11 comparison records position, velocity and local/global collider transforms for the six slice kinds and the mind hand over 90 frames, with the same scripted hits and line states in enabled and disabled runs. It also compares the mind hand's weak-point positions between those runs, with an exact-zero bound. Counter handedness, normally chosen by instance-id parity, is pinned equally in both runs. `react_rows_case` checks every impact reaction, including the existing stomp row, and each reaction's numeric schema. The two tests with direct brute mesh paths, `status_marks_test` and `plate_cue_test`, now include `ReactionPivot`; their assertions are unchanged. No model or pose body changed. Numeric evidence does not establish chase-camera readability; the coordinator still renders and Andrew plays.

## Unit AC: shell corrections rebuilt on the current line

Job `alpha-x2-AC`, base `1c289a94bc1cbaf6e71db61d8ace39f714a381a4`. The world-one recipes generate these shells. The legacy recipes and `shell_base.py` are unchanged. Ported C07 from `eddd9b0` with its `6c97d54` note, and C09 from `e51d3c7` plus `b39047b`, preserving this branch's later changes.

The older C07 correction also replaced thirteen small plate marks, five chips, five repairs and three weld beads, with three unequal patches at 300, 100 and 220 degrees. Ported those plate patches and their numeric checks. The generated band has three connected pale islands with triangle areas 0.045, 0.077 and 0.148 m2, and retains all sixteen bolts. The three plate patches have bounding areas 0.0101, 0.0121 and 0.0245 m2. Scratch exports with sixteen arcs, tiny patches or equal patches fail the corresponding band checks. The three shell tests now check one skeleton with the 25 ordered bone names, exact mesh and material names, bounded rig triangle counts, seven required clips and each clip's duration within 0.001 seconds. Only Kest and Rivet originally had rig hashes; Sable gains the same structural coverage.
