## The Warden carries its four health chunks (unit WM, 2026-10-02)

A posture Warden now loses its upper armour as it loses health. The recipe cuts the existing shoulder crowns across their width into four meshes: `ArmourSegment0` is the left outer half, `ArmourSegment1` the right outer half, `ArmourSegment2` the left inner half, and `ArmourSegment3` the right inner half. Each stays under its original `JawL` or `JawR` hinge. The pieces disappear in that order. Each cut removes crown area from the facing silhouette, rather than hiding one piece behind another along the viewing direction. The guard strokes still fit the unchanged cast jaw surfaces.

Under both halves of each shoulder is an `InnerL` or `InnerR` structure. Its materials take charcoal and dark oxide from `Palette`, with no emission. The original exterior is painted before it is cut, so the cut faces inherit its vertex colours; the new internal caps are charcoal. With the armour assembled, an exported-triangle audit against the assigned base found no unmatched exterior geometry and no changed vertex colours. The exterior area differs by less than one millionth of a square metre. This is numeric evidence, not a rendered comparison.

`CoreAssembly` sits in the facing gap at base-local `(1.2, 3.9, 0)`, ahead of the beacon mast. It contains the one-metre `HeartSurface` and the charcoal-and-metal `CoreCage`. The heart uses the same private `ancient_light` material that the kit's old `WeakPoint` used. On posture Wardens the old weak-point mesh is hidden, and the caged heart replaces it as the visible thing to smash. The kit node stays at its original gameplay position above the jaws. No weak-point coordinate, collider or contact rule changes: while open, a contact anywhere on the Warden remains a core contact. The assembly is hidden while closed and shown while staggered, with the existing open-state tint driving its emissive surface.

`hero_assets.gd` reads `fight()`, `chunks_left()`, `chunk_cracked()` and `staggered()`. A posture change updates the model immediately, including the last shatter before the Warden leaves the tree. The first `4 - chunks_left()` segments are hidden. Only the next segment carries `ChunkCrack` when the chunk is cracked. Its three fitted ribbons use the `chunk_shatter` row while staggered and the neutral role while closed. The new colour-setting functions are included in `boss_plate_test.gd`'s source scan. The plating branch retains all four armour segments, the old visible kit and its existing cues; it hides the new inner structure and caged heart.

A shatter creates a detached `MeshInstance3D` copy, with private non-emissive materials held by the presentation hook. The copy has no collider, body or children. Its centre travels outward along its radial normal at 14 m/s and falls by `18 * seconds²` metres, while it turns about its centre. It stops drawing after 0.18 s and is freed at 0.55 s. Time comes from physics-frame count, the same clock the sketch tests use, so a hit stop cannot extend its visibility or lifetime beyond the acceptance limits. Its physics callback binds the fragment node and disconnects on cleanup. The final copy survives the Warden's deletion long enough to complete this motion. A stage entry, retry or presentation teardown clears outstanding copies.

The exported base has 4,956 triangles, up from 4,492. Its bounds are unchanged: minimum `(-3.232532, 0.075000, -3.753399)`, maximum `(3.497463, 7.810000, 3.753399)`, with dimensions `(6.729995, 7.735000, 7.506798)` metres. Two complete recipe runs produce identical bytes. The family-kit outputs also equal their existing files, so no kit file changes. Use `--base-only` to rebuild this base without writing the kits.

`hero_warden_assets_test.gd` retains every existing check and adds named-piece, assembled-bound, caged-heart and triangle-projection checks. The sampled facing-crown silhouette loses area at each shatter: 1,170, 1,026, 882, 441, then zero covered samples in the band above the inner structure. `warden_chunks_look_test.gd` uses real harpoon contacts on a live slice game for all four shatters, including death. It checks chunk visibility, crack colours and placement, core visibility and tint, fragment motion and lifetime, callback cleanup, retries, stage changes, new runs, and plating Wardens through death on all three default stages. Deliberate implementation and GLB faults prove that the new assertions fail; the checks themselves are not edited for those red runs.

The coordinator still renders the assembled and damaged states at the 8 m and 12 m chase distances. That check must include crack readability, the open heart and the existing harpoon brackets, whose gameplay anchor remains above the jaws. No rendered capture, GPU measurement or human readability verdict is claimed by this unit.

## Chaser brace-back wind-up, Unit WC, 2 October 2026

The Chaser loads its body backward over planted claws. Its nose lifts and its raked fin flares. `e01_brace_back` is the pose named by attack row E01. The pose reads the existing `WINDUP` clock through `smoothstep(0, 1, _clock / WINDUP)`. It has no separate timer. At rest, every part retains its imported transform. The full brace moves the body 0.55 m back and 0.22 m down, lifts its nose 18 degrees, and turns the hinged fin another 32 degrees. Total body travel is 0.592368 m. The claws stay planted. No scale squash remains.

The transition into `LUNGE` resets the existing clock to zero. That zero sample retains the full brace, then the lunge clock drives a forward, nose-down snap over 0.08 s. The completed snap places the body 0.25 m forward and 0.08 m down, with a 12-degree nose-down pitch. At 90 physics ticks per second, the test observes the completed snap eight ticks after release, or 0.088889 s. The pose restores imported transforms on recovery or cancellation. Death, deletion and retry remove the posed nodes; a replacement instance starts at rest.

`ChaserBody` remains the visual root under `ReactionPivot`. Its imported body mesh retains the same name. The recipe now exports `ChaserFin` with a hinge at Blender coordinates `(0, -0.40, 0.95)` and `ChaserClaws` as separate nodes. `_parts()` preserves their names and rest transforms, and gives only the `fleet_paint` surface the existing flash-material override. Surface names remain `fleet_paint`, `requisition_tag` and `rubber`. The fin and claws each use `rubber`. The recipe regeneration preserves all 228 triangles and the original rest geometry. Independent parts increase surface draws from three to five; the asset test's draw ceiling changes from 3 to 5 for that reason. Its triangle ceiling, opacity, rest-size and collider checks retain their original bounds. It now also pins node and surface names and a ten-percent triangle bound against the original 228 triangles.

The test seam `windup_pose_enabled` disables only these part transforms. Facing still runs. Neither the pose nor the asset touches the collider, enemy position, velocity, attack clock, committed telegraph, hit geometry or colour. All posed nodes remain below `ReactionPivot`, and `_pose()` never writes that pivot. The live slice test compares 240 physics frames at seed 11 with the seam enabled and disabled. It records position, velocity, local and global collider transforms, telegraph geometry and progress, attack state, clock and phase facts. It also compares reaction transforms during a ram and the harpoon lean. The real ball is frozen at the same endpoint in both traces because the harpoon lean reads that endpoint.

`enemy_chaser_windup_test.gd` prints body travel, nose pitch and fin flare at progress 0, 0.5 and 1, checks 61 monotonic samples, checks the release and snap, and exercises recovery, stagger, a ram during wind-up, the smash signal with a combat knock, death, `queue_free()` and a real stage retry. Deleted-part instance IDs must disappear. Recovery and stagger must restore every surviving part. Numeric checks establish motion and timing only. The coordinator still renders the chase-camera views and judges the 30 m body read.

The release check allows the packet's one-physics-frame tolerance. Repeated 1/90 s additions leave the last wind-up clock just below 0.6 in double precision; the eased transform and telegraph progress round to their full values on that tick. The test therefore observes the first full pose and strike fact on one tick, and `LUNGE` on the next. It also checks that the first `LUNGE` sample still has the full brace. An initial equality-only assertion was revised to the specified one-frame bound; no attack timing changed.

Before editing, the test search found `enemy_chaser_asset_test.gd` pinning `ChaserBody` and the asset path. That test retains the root-name check and adds the articulated node and surface checks. `enemy_reactions_test.gd` reads the Chaser's shared `reaction_pivot` and collider handles; it remains unchanged. `status_marks_test.gd` inspects the Chaser's materials through a mesh wildcard and remains unchanged. Its explicit `ReactionPivot/BrutePlates` paths belong to the Brute. No other pre-existing test pins a named Chaser model node.

Verification at base `85432aa9950321b2b9f30125fa06c580cfc1bf32`, branch `codex/job-8398965b416524c340da-46a1d093b9`, job `alpha-x2b-WC`:

- Godot 4.7.2 ran the exact packet's fourteen named tests and import. All fifteen runs exited 0, with no error lines, skips or missing verdicts. The run took 77 s. Receipt: `artifacts/local/receipts/wc-2df1e5543a0d-2026-10-02T15-21-30-977Z.json`.
- `node scripts/sketch-lint.mjs --sketch core-loop` exited 0 with 0 findings. `git diff --check` exited 0.
- The final numeric capture passes all 23 wind-up checks. Progress 0, 0.5 and 1 produce `[travel m, nose degrees, fin flare degrees]` of `[0, 0, 0]`, `[0.296184, 9, 16]` and `[0.592368, 18, 32]`. First full pose and strike fact are frame 58; first `LUNGE` is frame 59 and retains the full brace. Position, velocity, collider, telegraph and attack differences are exactly 0 over 240 physics frames. Reaction transforms also match exactly.
- Nine temporary pose mutations each exit 1 without script errors. They disable the seam, detach the parts, ignore the clock, skip the full release pose, slow the snap, retain the loaded pose after interruption, move the physics body, overwrite the reaction pivot, or squash the visual root. Each mutation fails the corresponding check. The clock mutation prints the same `[0.296184, 9, 16]` sample three times and fails WC-1. The release mutation initially exposed a missing release-frame assertion; the strengthened check now rejects it.
- A temporary recipe mutation renames all three parts and adds 24 triangles. Blender regenerates a 252-triangle glb; the asset test exits 1 on node/surface pins, the ten-percent triangle bound and live handles. The restored recipe regenerates 228 triangles and the final asset test passes. Every glb generation used Blender headless; none used a binary edit.
- The initial run hit the sandbox's blocked home-settings write. Verification then used XDG data, configuration and cache folders under `artifacts/local/wc/`. A new test's inferred boolean type and its initial six-frame snap deadline were corrected before the final run. The actual project tick rate is 90 Hz. The final release assertion uses the packet's one-frame tolerance. Existing bounds change only for the five required surface draws.

Raw numeric outputs and mutation command records are under `artifacts/local/wc/`. The code comparison confirms `_physics_process()`, `_begin_windup()`, `_check_hit()`, `_end_attack()` and `state_name()` are byte-identical to the base, excluding the replaced comment preceding `_pose()`. The full suite and `slice_packs_test` were not run. There is no GPU evidence. Chase-camera stills and Andrew's readability judgment remain with the coordinator. This work is uncommitted; the coordinator owns the commit, and this worktree's Git metadata is outside the writable roots.

## The Lobber raises its tube before firing (unit WL, 2026-10-02)

The E02 body pose, `e02_tube_raise`, uses the Lobber's existing charge and cycle clock. Smooth easing turns the barrel 26 degrees about its breech and settles the cart four degrees about its rear wheel axle. The barrel turns 30 degrees in the enemy's space. Progress 0, 0.5 and 1 produce 0, 15 and 30 degrees; 101 samples confirm monotonic travel. The full pose is present when `fired` emits. Floating-point rounding can make the transform numerically equal to full pose one physics frame before release, within the packet's tolerance. The loading bulb's original scale and emission formulas remain unchanged.

After release, the same cycle clock drives a 0.24 m recoil along the raised barrel and one damped cart rock. Both return to their exact rest transforms at 0.28 s. The pose restores rest when the Lobber is airborne, outside shot range, inactive, dead or braced for a harpoon smash. Death and tree exit restore the moved nodes before deletion. The Lobber already pauses its attack clock while airborne rather than cancelling it on a hard hit; this unit preserves that behavior and clears only the visual pose during the pause. No attack timing, aim, telegraph, damage, collider, knock, colour or reaction-pivot transform changes.

The recipe separates the existing tube and bands into `LobberBarrel`, with its origin at the breech. `LobberBody` and `LobberBulb` keep their names. Headless Blender regenerates the GLB; its 840 triangles equal the assigned base, and its resting envelope is unchanged. Splitting the tube requires two more surfaces, so the recipe and asset test draw ceilings increase from three to five. The triangle ceiling and every existing envelope and gameplay bound stay fixed. The asset test pins node names and the `fleet_paint`, `rubber` and `requisition_tag` surface names, without a file-hash pin. A restored recipe export is byte-identical to the first WL export. No other model changes.

`enemy_lobber_windup_test.gd` runs on the live slice game. The `windup_pose_enabled` seam disables only this pose. Seed 11 produces identical positions, velocities, collider transforms, telegraph descriptors, shell rings, shots, charge and cycle clock over 360 physics frames with the pose enabled and disabled. The largest difference is zero. The unhit reaction pivot stays at identity; ram reactions match the disabled control at every sampled tick. The test also reads the pivot immediately around pose calls, because the base reaction update can hide an illegal write by restoring the pivot later in the tick. Stagger, harpoon smash, death, queued deletion and stage retry all restore the posed nodes.

Before editing, searched all tests for Lobber node names, paths, asset references and generic mesh/collider assumptions. `enemy_lobber_asset_test.gd` pins the dressing and bulb; it gains barrel/surface-name and triangle-delta checks. `asset_capture_test.gd` parses `/root/Game/Spawn_lobber` calls; its path stays unchanged. `enemy_reactions_test.gd` checks the six slice kinds' meshes below the reaction pivot and colliders on the body; it stays unchanged. No other test pins the changed model's node names or file hash.

Deliberate implementation faults make the clock, range of travel, release alignment, recovery, stagger, harpoon smash, deletion, retry, death, physics equality, pivot ownership, disabled control and bulb checks fail. Recipe faults make the node/surface pins and triangle-delta check fail. The checks themselves remain intact during each red run. Logs and mutation records are under `artifacts/local/alpha-x2b-WL/`. Evidence level 4 covers executable numeric checks. The coordinator still renders the chase-camera views; no GPU, 30 m visual-readability verdict or human acceptance is claimed here.

Godot 4.7.2 headless passes the exact packet VERIFY list: import and all 13 selected tests, zero skips and no error-prefixed lines. The final direct wind-up run passes 35 checks. Lint reports zero findings. The final smoke receipt is `artifacts/local/receipts/wl-2df1e5543a0d-2026-10-02T15-26-09-759Z.json`. The initial import could not save editor settings under the home directory; setting all three XDG roots under local evidence resolved it. No full suite or `slice_packs_test` ran. The generated new-test UID is disposable and removed from the handoff because the packet does not permit it. The six delivered files remain uncommitted for the coordinator; worktree Git metadata is outside the permitted write roots.

## Mag trapper body wind-up, Unit WG

E05's `e05_stack_hoist` now lifts one disc from the stack. `MagTopDisc` rises 1.4 m above its rest position and accelerates through 1.25 turns during WARN. The crew-and-cart mesh leans back by 0.22 radians. The two remaining discs stay upright on `MagStack`. Only the top disc hides while the pit is LIVE; the stack is whole again at rest. These nodes remain children of `reaction_pivot`, whose transform the pose never writes. The existing paint, hazard core emission and hit flash materials are unchanged.

The pose reads `_warn_progress()` and adds no clock. Lift and body lean use smoothstep; spin uses progress squared. Counter changes to LIVE and resets `_t` before calling `_pose()`, so LIVE at `_t == 0` supplies the release endpoint. The next live tick restores the body and hidden disc transforms. A one-millionth margin below full lift keeps a floating-point WARN tick just short of release distinct from the endpoint. At zero progress and on return to ROAM, both moved nodes have their exact rest transforms. `pose_enabled` is the test seam that turns all this presentation off.

Blender 5.2.2 regenerated `enemy_mag_trapper.glb` from its recipe. The split retains all 760 triangles, the old `MagTrapperBody`, `MagStack` and `MagDiscs` names, and the material roles `fleet_paint`, `paint` and `class_accent`. Independent top-disc paint and core surfaces raise the assembly from four to six draws. The asset test's draw ceiling changes accordingly; its triangle ceiling, opacity, collider, attack constants, timing and material checks stay fixed. The obsolete whole-stack tilt and whole-stack hiding checks are replaced by independent hoist and top-disc-only visibility checks. The new checks pin node and surface names, never a file hash. No other model changed.

The headless slice test samples lift at progress 0, 0.5 and 1 as 0, 0.7 and 1.4 m, and checks 101 samples for monotonic lift, lean and spin. The first observed full pose is one physics frame after the strike event, within the specified bound. Two seed-11 runs compare 430 physics frames with presentation enabled and disabled. Their positions, velocities, local and global collider transforms, telegraph state, attack phases and relative strike frames are exactly equal, with largest difference zero. The test freezes the target in both runs to avoid unrelated RigidBody contact-order differences between sequential game instances; the trapper continues its real physics and attack loop.

A completed cycle returns to rest on its first ROAM frame. Real combat ram and the harpoon smash landing callback restore the pose within 0.3 s. Death, `queue_free()` and retry remove every old posed node within that bound, and replacement bodies start at rest. Freed nodes have no surviving transform to inspect. A ram still drives the existing reaction, and the pivot stays at identity immediately after every pose call in a whole unhit cycle. Intentional faults to the clock, hoist distance, endpoint timing, reset, visibility, physical position and pivot all fail the wind-up test. Recipe faults to triangle count, material names and the top-disc node fail the asset test; every fault is restored before final verification.

The scoped lint command reports zero findings. The packet's 13 named tests and project import pass, with no skips or error lines. Evidence is under `artifacts/local/wg/` and the `wg` smoke receipt under `artifacts/local/receipts/`. No full suite, rendered capture or standalone `slice_packs_test` ran. The coordinator still checks the pose from the chase camera at 30 m and owns independent review, commit and integration.

## Winch drone body wind-up, Unit WD

The E11 pose, `e11_cable_guide`, pitches the complete Winch dressing down the committed lane by 32 degrees. It moves the `WinchBody` assembly below `reaction_pivot`. The pivot remains owned by the hit reaction and harpoon lean. The body uses smoothstep of `_warn_progress()`: 0, 16 and 32 degrees at warning progress 0, 0.5 and 1. The drum and rotor turn from still to fast as the square of that same progress. Their sampled angles are 0, 3.534292 and 14.137167 radians for the drum, and 0, 5.105088 and 20.420353 radians for the rotor. The final tenth completes the aim; there is no early full-pose hold.

The existing counter loop changes to LIVE on release. The pose then holds the full aim and continues both spins from their warning endpoints using the existing live clock. On rest or stagger, the existing counter clock levels the body and coasts the drum and rotor forwards to whole turns in 0.25 seconds, then restores exact rest transforms. No wind-up timer was added. A death, direct removal or stage retry frees the old posed nodes; a replacement starts at rest. `pose_enabled` is the numeric test seam and changes only these dressing transforms.

The exported drum was part of the body mesh. The Blender recipe now exports it as `WinchDrum`, on its own X axle, while retaining `WinchBody`, `WinchRotor`, `fleet_paint` and `paint`. The regenerated assembly remains 552 triangles, a zero-percent change. Splitting the drum increases the draw ceiling from 3 to 4 in the recipe and asset test. The triangle ceiling and all geometry, collision, timing and colour checks remain. The former fixed 0.6-radian rotor increment check now checks rest, warning-clock rotation and recovery. The existing drum is dark oxide rather than the brief's brass; its colours were preserved.

The new test runs a live slice game, samples the pose, compares two seed-11 traces over 360 physics frames, observes the real E11 strike, and exercises ram interruption, a staged harpoon-smash brace with an interrupting knock, death, direct removal and a real stage retry. It also calls the pose directly after the normal reaction update so the owner cannot mask an illegal pivot write. The cable remains held for 163 frames in the cycle under test. Fault injections break each added check, including clock independence, insufficient travel, early release pose, stuck recovery, physical motion, pivot writes, dirty replacement transforms and asset names or triangle count. Tests and logs live under `artifacts/local/tasks/alpha-x2b-WD/`. Numeric travel is the range evidence here; rendered chase-camera readability and Andrew's play remain with the coordinator.

## Unit WF: Deflector body wind-up, E10 `e10_dish_wall`

The Deflector carries its dish raised and angled to one side. During its existing 0.9 s
warning, the dish swings into the upright wall and the crew crouches behind it. The
released wall keeps the original dish geometry and facing. It holds through `LIVE`
and returns to the carried transform when the attack rests. This is a visual pose.
The reflect, 0.45 s vault window, attack clock, lane, collider, movement and colours
are unchanged.

`deflector.gd` reads `_warn_progress()` during `WARN`, uses 1 during `LIVE`, and uses
0 otherwise. `smoothstep(0, 1, progress)` gives eased travel without an extra clock.
At 90% of the warning it has completed 97.2% of its travel. Its first full pose is on
the existing transition to `LIVE`. The dish centre travels 0.828 m and its orientation
turns 52.530 degrees, measured in enemy space. The crew compresses vertically to
85% of its resting height with a 0.165 m downward offset that keeps its base planted.
Both transforms are below `reaction_pivot`; the pose never assigns that pivot.
`windup_pose_enabled` is the test seam. Setting it false restores the carried dish
and the resting crew. The existing exit hook also uses that seam to restore both
meshes before disposal, including death and stage retry.

The Blender recipe separates the original mesh into `DeflectorBody`, with
`fleet_paint` and `paint` surfaces, and `DishWall`, with one `paint` surface. The
latter includes the dish, rim, frame, arms and grips. Its origin is the face centre.
No triangles or colours were added. Blender 5.2.2 LTS regenerated
`assets/models/world_one/enemy_deflector.glb` with **844 triangles**, equal to the
base asset, and **3 draws**, within the existing 1200-triangle / 3-draw ceiling.
Only this model changed. The asset test pins node and surface names, not a file hash.
It checks the original dish footprint and facing at full pose. The old assertion
that the crew stays at identity now asserts the specified braced transform. No
footprint, health, collider, colour, timing or mechanic bounds were widened. The
recipe's surface limit changed from 2 to 3 to permit the split; the asset test's
existing draw ceiling remains 3.

`enemy_deflector_windup_test.gd` boots the slice game and checks clock samples
0 / 0.5 / 1, monotonic dish translation and rotation plus crew crouch and scale at
101 samples, the full live cycle, release timing, ram reactions, interrupted
warnings, death, `queue_free()` and the real stage retry. The observed samples are
0.000000 / 0.499999 / 1.000000. Release and first full pose share physics frame 85
in the standalone check. The unhit reaction pivot stays exactly at identity.
The ram reaction reaches 89.699 degrees while the child pose resets. Disposal and
retry restore the child transforms on exit, within two observation frames.

The physics control compares 480 physics frames at seed 11 in two fresh Godot
processes, with the pose enabled and disabled. It compares global position,
velocity, local and global collider transforms, telegraph descriptor and progress,
attack phase, warning duration and committed attack facts. Attack instance ids are
excluded because they identify separate attacks. The largest measured numeric
difference is **0.000000000** and the full snapshots match exactly. Both traces
release on sample frame 81. A preliminary comparison that replaced games within
one process found a 0.000025546 difference in velocity on the first walking frame
after rest. Fresh processes give the runs the same Jolt body creation history;
the zero-difference requirement was retained.

For red/green evidence, the production pose temporarily ignored the clock and
always used progress 1. The unchanged sample check printed
`1.000000 / 1.000000 / 1.000000 -> FAIL`; release, resets and the disabled seam also
failed. Restoring the clock printed
`0.000000 / 0.499999 / 1.000000 -> PASS` and all 18 wind-up checks passed. Logs are
under `artifacts/local/tasks/alpha-x2b-WF/` in this worktree. The named verification
receipt is `wf`; the coordinator owns admission and any rendered range check.
These are headless numeric checks, not chase-camera or human readability acceptance.

## The Plated brute raises its slam pads (unit WB, 2026-10-02)

E09's `e09_pads_raise` pose reads the counter's existing 1.1-second wind-up clock. Both arm/fist assemblies rise 2.5 m while the torso rears 30 degrees around the feet. The progress curve is quadratic: pad travel is 0, 0.625 and 2.5 m at progress 0, 0.5 and 1. The last tenth continues the rise. A float32 endpoint guard prevents a nearly complete warning from rounding to the full pose before the counter releases.

The release frame begins LIVE at elapsed time zero and shows the full pose. Over the next 0.08 seconds the pads descend to the collider-floor height and the torso straightens. The pads remain planted through the rest of LIVE, then return to their original transforms on the first ROAM frame. The pose adds no timer, tween, event or attack rule. `windup_pose_enabled` switches only the pose off for the seeded control.

The base recipe had no separate ground-level pads. Its shoulder cladding is part of the plate group, and its low fists and arms are part of the body. This unit uses those existing arms and fists as the slam pads. Four rigid glTF joint nodes become Godot skeleton bones: `BruteFeet`, `BruteRear`, `SlamPadLeft` and `SlamPadRight`. Skinning separates their transforms while preserving the two body material surfaces and the plate surface. The recipe adds no geometry or colour. `BruteBody`, `BrutePlates`, `PlateCrack`, `fleet_paint`, `paint` and `bare_metal` retain their names. The plate mesh and its cue remain still. The GLB was regenerated with Blender 5.2.2 headless and remains 996 triangles and three surfaces, a zero-percent triangle change.

The pose never writes `ReactionPivot`. The existing reaction and harpoon lean still own that parent. On a live slice game, actual skinned pad vertices move 2.5 m and their full-pose bottom is 1.87 m above the enemy origin. The first full pose and the first slam both occur on physics frame 99. A real combat ram tips the parent while the pad pose remains raised; an unhit 140-frame cycle keeps the parent at identity. Seed 11 with the pose enabled and disabled gives identical position, velocity, local/global collider transforms, committed telegraph geometry/progress and attack frames across 160 physics frames. The largest measured difference is zero.

This base sets `interruptible = false`, so a ram does not stagger-cancel the warning. The test preserves that rule and exercises the existing `_end()` cancellation path directly, which resets the pose within two physics frames. Mid-warning removal, death and a real stage retry remove the posed rig within three physics frames; a replacement brute starts at rest. There is no surviving removed node whose transform can be measured. Numeric cleanup and timing evidence do not establish visual readability at 30 m. The coordinator must render the wind-up and planted poses from the chase camera, including whether the raised arm/fist assemblies read as slam pads.

`enemy_plated_brute_windup_test.gd` prints the sampled pose, checks actual skin deformation, release timing, planting, cancellation, cleanup, reaction composition and seeded physics equality. Deliberate implementation faults exercise clock, travel, release, rest, physics and pivot failures before restoring the implementation. The asset test adds joint, surface and rigid-skin binding checks and pins the unchanged triangle count without a file hash. Existing bounds and checks are retained. The other tests that name brute visual nodes are `plate_cue_test.gd` and `status_marks_test.gd`; both remain unchanged. `enemy_reactions_test.gd` also covers this kind's pivot behavior and remains unchanged. Disposable command logs and negative controls are under `artifacts/local/wb/`.


The final requested VERIFY smoke command passes import and all nine named tests in 45 seconds, with zero skips and no missing verdict lines. Receipt: `artifacts/local/receipts/wb-2df1e5543a0d-2026-10-02T15-17-28-058Z.json`. The wind-up fixture passes 27 checks, including the harpoon smash brace and an enabled/disabled pose control. Lint reports zero findings. Godot's first import printed home-directory settings-write errors; setting all three XDG roots under `artifacts/local/wb/xdg/` resolves them. The deliberate asset faults rename a pad joint and `fleet_paint`; both named-structure checks fail, then the recipe is restored and Blender regenerates the final GLB. No check is removed and no existing bound is widened. Evidence level 4 applies to the numeric, structural and live-code checks.

## Cut stone and dry scrap clutter kit (unit CK, 2026-10-02)

`tools/blender/world_one/clutter_kit.py` exports ten independent mesh nodes to `assets/models/world_one/clutter_kit.glb`. Each node has an identity transform and its lowest vertex at Godot y = 0. This unit adds the kit only; a later unit places it. Cut planes, irregular outlines, solid blades, a broken open pipe rim and folded plate edges replace the sphere, flat box and flat star shapes. All geometry is procedural bmesh with seed 20261002 and no external assets or textures.

The stone uses `common.py` sandstone mixed with charcoal. There is no separate strata or straw palette name in that helper. Slabs use a darker sandstone mix, and dry blades use sandstone mixed with bone and darkened. Upward faces receive lighter loop colours than downward faces. Scrap uses oxide `fleet_paint` with dull, dark `bare_metal` on the cut sides and pipe interior. The shared polished-bone bare-metal preview is darkened in this recipe only. The kit uses no mint, amber, hazard colour or emission, and its surfaces stay below sand brightness.

The kit totals 256 triangles. All per-mesh size, triangle, ground and height limits pass. The test compares sorted, normalized rock dimensions, so rotating a copy cannot count as a distinct silhouette. Plates start as 5.5 cm extrusions. Matching cap triangles preserve the sheet across the fold. The test pairs opposite triangles by equal translation of all three corners and checks the normal gap across each complete facet. Their minimum gaps are 0.038100 m and 0.050566 m. This replaced an earlier centroid-ray test whose coverage missed locally thin corners.

CK-8 changed only the recipe so `plate_a` had zero thickness, exported a scratch GLB and ran the unchanged asset test. The red process exited 1 and printed `FAIL CK-4 plate_a physical thickness 0.000000 m >= 0.030000 m -> FAIL`. The recipe was restored, and the green process exited 0 with `PASS CK-4 plate_a physical thickness 0.038100 m >= 0.030000 m -> PASS`. Twenty-one additional scratch mutations reject wrong/missing/extra nodes, node transforms, excess triangles, oversize meshes, low height, ground offset, a cloned rock, unknown slots, emission, missing/constant/reversed colours, excess brightness, short tufts, a missing underside, hazard/mint/amber scrap colours and a locally thinned plate corner. Constant colours exposed a floating-point false pass; the brightness comparison now requires a difference above 0.000001. No specified bound was widened.

Godot 4.7.2 imports the `paint` vertex-colour arrays but leaves `vertex_color_use_as_albedo` false. An extra enabled-material assertion beyond CK-6 was removed after confirming this import behaviour. Colour-presence and directional-brightness checks remain. The later placement unit must enable that material flag or use the existing presentation palette mapping, which enables paint vertex colours. This unit adds no loader or material override at runtime.

The final recipe was run twice. Both GLBs match byte for byte with SHA-256 `1c80b6308b27a08ecaad568c5eca643339e27d97e4958a494697e5d0ac748a78`. Neighboring world-one `.import` files are not tracked, so the generated clutter import descriptor stays ignored. The Godot test UID is included.

Verification used Blender 5.2.2 LTS and Godot 4.7.2 headless. `node scripts/sketch-lint.mjs --sketch core-loop` reports 0 findings against the existing baseline. The exact requested smoke command with receipt `ck` passes the import and all three tests: `clutter_kit_asset_test`, `asset_capture_test`, `spillway_scenery_test`. It has zero skips and no error-prefixed lines. Godot initially failed to save editor settings under home; the retry with all three XDG roots under `artifacts/local/alpha-x2b-CK/xdg/` succeeded. The final receipt is `artifacts/local/receipts/ck-2df1e5543a0d-2026-10-02T15-20-43-281Z.json`.

A test search found no existing clutter-kit references or hash pins. The two existing tests in VERIFY remain unchanged and pass. `dam_asset_test.gd` supplied the direct-GLB inspection pattern and was left unchanged. Independent review found the scrap hue check gap, now fixed with mutation evidence. Evidence, executable-check logs and a command ledger are under `artifacts/local/alpha-x2b-CK/`. Evidence level 4 establishes numeric geometry and real import/test behaviour. ## Renderer target experiment, unit LK, 2026-10-02

This round adds `--preset=target` and `tools/target_capture.gd` for comparison with the keyframe. It changes renderer settings only. The original `high` and `low` rows and their original `_apply_world` setter body remain byte-identical to base `37bc501f674a8229fbdef70256fec530cd0a9184`. Target overrides run after that body; leaving target restores its extra properties and the project defaults for its global server settings. Target reuses the existing sun and SkyFill. It adds no light node or sky material.

The first settings aim for a warm low sun, lit shadowed rock, mild air scattering and contact definition. They are starting estimates. No still or frame time was taken in the writer sandbox, and headless checks do not establish colour hierarchy, readability or fidelity.

| Feature and row key | Target values | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `sun`, `sun_rotation`, `sun_properties` | Energy 2.15, rotation -28/-55/0 degrees, colour #ffe4bf, angular size 0.5 degrees; existing fill 0.30 | Long warm shadows with PCSS softness. The lower saturation key should leave hazard orange-red distinct. Check PCSS noise on the pilot in the returned still. |
| `shadow_mode`, `shadow_distance`, `shadow_atlas` | Four splits at 0.10/0.25/0.55, 160 m reach, 4096 atlas with 16-bit depth, normal bias 0.40 | Give the next 40 m more shadow resolution and keep contacts attached. |
| `sdfgi`, `sdfgi_rays`, `environment.sdfgi_*` | On, four cascades, 1 m minimum cells, 32 rays, occlusion and sky read on, bounce feedback 0.35, energy 1.0 | Broad rock and ground bounce. Engine probe reports mesh default `GI_MODE_STATIC=1`; no geometry participation flag is changed here. |
| `ssil`, `ssil_quality`, `environment.ssil_*` | On, medium quality at half resolution, two blur passes, radius 3 m, intensity 0.65, sharpness 0.8, normal rejection 1.0 | Nearby visible bounce and recess fill. Screen-space coverage must be checked in all three views. |
| `ssao`, `ssao_quality`, `environment.ssao_*` | On, medium half-resolution quality; radius 0.70, intensity 1.35, power 1.20, detail 0.50, direct-light affect 0.08 | Ground contact and small gaps without painting large shadows black. |
| `ambient`, `environment.ambient_light_*` | Energy 0.30, colour #b4c3d1, sky contribution 0.35 | A restrained cool shadow floor while GI supplies local variation. |
| `volumetric_fog`, `fog_volume_size`, `fog_volume_depth`, `environment.volumetric_fog_*` | On, volume size 96, depth 64, density 0.0015, length 160 m, albedo #dfded8, anisotropy 0.45, GI inject 0.5, ambient inject 0.30, sky affect 0.10, temporal blend 0.90 | Sun shafts where existing geometry occludes the light; neutral air colour to preserve mint and hazard reads. Sun volumetric contribution is 1.2. |
| `fog`, `environment.fog_*` | Density 0.00015, colour #c0c9cf, energy 0.60, aerial perspective 0.20, sky affect 0.10 | Distant separation with little near-ground veiling. Uniform extinction from the two density terms alone would retain about 94% transmission over 40 m. Aerial perspective and scattering change the actual result; measure readability from the stills. |
| `environment.tonemap_*`, `environment.adjustment_saturation` | AgX, exposure 1.05, contrast 1.15, white 16.29, saturation 1.05 | Filmic highlights without the established preset's stronger saturation. |
| `glow`, `environment.glow_*` | On, intensity 0.22, HDR threshold 1.8 | A small halo on bright lamps and mint accents. |
| `sky_properties` | Incremental processing, 256 radiance size; authored panorama retained | Keep sky reflections and aerial depth consistent with the existing sky. This does not add cloud geometry or change the panorama. |
| `msaa`, `lod_threshold`, `scenery_distance` | 4x MSAA, threshold 1.0, 780 m scenery reach | Match high's geometry reach and antialiasing while comparing light. |

`RenderingServer` applies the directional atlas size, SDFGI ray count, SSIL quality and volumetric volume size at runtime. No `project.godot` setting changes. Each expensive effect has its own row key. If the play median exceeds 8 ms at 1920x1080, first disable `ssil`, then reduce `sdfgi_rays` from 3 to 1, meaning 32 to 8 rays, then reduce fog volume size from 96 to 64. Next use two shadow splits or angular size 0 if PCSS is costly or noisy. Disable SDFGI after those reductions, then volumetric fog. Keep SSAO and the near-field shadow reach as long as possible. These are expected tradeoffs, not measured costs.

The new `Cams.TARGET` table owns `play`, `site` and `wide`. Play samples the Spillway ground at x=-80, z=-93 and puts the camera exactly 8 m behind and 3 m above the ball at 70 degrees. Two chasers stage 28 m and 42 m farther down the fall line. This follows the west chute beside the west rock family; visibility and wall shadow composition remain unverified without a GPU. Site uses the Warden's real site at (-46,16.6,-12) and a three-quarter offset. Wide uses a fixed overview. Existing camera tables stay unchanged.

The capture tool forces the default table, seeds 280928 and advances 120 physics frames. It measures 240 live draw intervals after 180 warmup frames with vsync disabled and `Engine.max_fps=0`. It writes the median, nearest-rank p95, all samples, preset, adapter, engine, rendering method, source hashes and each PNG hash to `frames.json`. Times are whole-frame wall intervals between `frame_post_draw` signals, not isolated GPU timings. Simulation runs during the measure, so presets can advance different distances through time. The tool rebuilds with the same seed and physics count before pausing for the stills. It waits 180 draws at each frozen camera for GI and fog history to settle. Capture or metadata write failure exits 3; headless display, missing output or unknown preset exits 2.

The coordinator runs two visible captures on the RTX 4080, using separate output directories:

```sh
"$GODOT_BIN" --path sketches/core-loop --script res://tools/target_capture.gd -- --out="$PWD/artifacts/local/lk-high" --preset=high
"$GODOT_BIN" --path sketches/core-loop --script res://tools/target_capture.gd -- --out="$PWD/artifacts/local/lk-target" --preset=target
```

Writer checks ran on Godot 4.7.2 stable, build `ed1daf0bf`. The requested smoke suite passed its import and all six tests with no skips; three existing tests do not print a final verdict line, but exit/error scanning still fails loudly. Lint reports zero findings. The headless capture exits 2 without script error. The inherited staging path builds the target game and advances 120 physics frames with no `SCRIPT ERROR` or `ERROR` line.

Baseline comparison (`artifacts/local/lk/baseline-check.log`, disposable local scratch) applies the base and candidate code to fresh Environment, sun, viewport and sky instances, including the existing fill. It compares 76 properties against the base value table (`artifacts/local/lk/base-values.json`), for fresh high/low and target-to-high/low. All four comparisons pass. A server spy records the six server-setting groups because RenderingServer has no quality-setting getters; actual engine calls also run in the preset tests and staging probe. The preparation script (`artifacts/local/lk/prepare_baseline.py`) extracts the accepted base and asserts unchanged row text and original setter body. The table includes target's additional properties at their original defaults.

The complete ClassDB dump (`artifacts/local/lk/classdb.log`, disposable local scratch) lists properties, methods and constants of all five requested classes. The API check (`artifacts/local/lk/check_api.py`) verifies every touched property and renderer method against that dump. Relevant lines printed by the engine follow verbatim:
