Renderer settings leave these gaps for later rounds, ranked by expected visual weight:

1. Terrain and architecture mass, including the keyframe's enclosed ledges, eroded overhangs and giant wheel. Light cannot turn the current dam and broad open ground into that composition.
2. Surface detail and material response, including chipped paint, rock fractures, roughness variation and dusty edges. Existing materials determine the remaining flatness under better light.
3. Prop and vehicle silhouettes, density and placement, including the plated pod bowl and readable machinery in the middle distance.
4. Local dust trails, particles and spatially varied haze. Global volumetric fog can produce occluded sun shafts but cannot author localized drifting dust or impact plumes.
5. A richer sky panorama and cloud treatment if aerial perspective alone leaves the sky shallow.

The next round needs the six PNGs and both `frames.json` files. Judge the next 40 m of ground, hazard/mint separation, pilot shadow noise and the play median before changing settings. The writer leaves the scoped changes uncommitted for the coordinator.

## Renderer and surface correction, unit LK round 2, 2026-10-02

The coordinator's RTX 4080 captures at `artifacts/local/alpha/look/r1/` showed a worse play view under round-1 target: the ball lost its separate ground shadow, the dune lost its shaped shadow and haze washed out the dam. High measured 8.556 ms median and target 10.1895 ms, a 1.6335 ms difference. Both wall-time samples include simulation and were taken under host load 21–26 on 32 cores. They do not qualify either absolute frame budget. The writer verified all six PNG hashes against the two capture manifests and retained their metadata in `artifacts/local/lk-r2/r1-source.json` (disposable local scratch).

Part A restores high's sun rotation (-36,-55,0), energy 2.35, fill 0.5, colour #ffd49a, two shadow splits, 180 m shadow distance, normal bias 0.65, angular size 0 and split 0.18. Exposure 1.1, AgX contrast 1.25, saturation 1.2 and all depth-fog properties also match high. SDFGI, SSIL, volumetric fog and aerial perspective are off. Global shafts are dropped because the first capture did not show a useful shaft worth its veil. SSAO remains a small contact improvement at radius 0.55, intensity 1.25 and detail 0.40. No additional light or fog node is created.

The headless shadow probe (`artifacts/local/lk-r2/shadow-probe.log`, disposable local scratch) isolates the changed sun elevation geometrically. At the play anchor, the ray from the ground toward the -36 degree sun is clear. At -28 degrees it intersects the western terrain rim at (-141.60,70.40,-49.86). That change puts the ground below the ball in the rim's shadow, so the ball cannot cast a separate direct-light shadow there. The row comment and preset test now pin the proven sun, distance, bias and split. The probe establishes light-path occlusion; a returned GPU still must confirm the ball's visible height cue.

The site's hard dark edge is a renderer coverage artefact rather than the boundary of a real cast shadow at that location. Rays through site pixels x=1450–1900, y=550/750/950 meet the same western terrain occluder on both sides under the -28 degree sun; those ground points should all be shadowed. They lie well inside the 160 m fog length. A directional shadow cascade/frustum edge is the leading explanation, but the first images cannot isolate it from SDFGI coverage without a GPU ablation. Restoring high's directional shadow configuration and disabling SDFGI and volumetric fog removes the round-1 coverage changes. Exact subsystem attribution and disappearance in the image remain GPU checks.

Part B adds procedural detail only under target. The original Palette file is preserved as a byte-identical prefix; all new construction is appended. The terrain edit adds one material-registration line after the original ground material selection. The preset binds scenery materials after the existing opaque merge, so shaders cannot prevent that merge or split its batch. It stores the original overrides and active materials, uses instance surface overrides on the imported dam, and restores the exact resources on high/low. It does not mutate original materials, mesh slots, vertices, colliders, transforms or shadow-caster flags.

| Surface | Intended look in the returned stills | Readability guard |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Ground, `target_ground.gdshader` | Broad packed-sand variation, fine grain and shallow ripple relief; slope blends add small strata cavities on steep terrain faces. | Existing vertex colour carries the hue. Value modulation stays within 0.84–1.08; fine grain fades with screen footprint. No displacement or moving noise. |
| Rock, `target_rock.gdshader` | Warped horizontal beds, dark thin cavities, a lighter worn lip and fine relief. | Desaturated stone #bca382, shallow relief, mild AO and filtered seams. No mint, amber or hazard pigment is introduced. |
| Dam, `target_dam.gdshader` | World-space 3 x 2 m plate seams, vertical dirt variation and small worn/chipped seam edges. | Source vertex colours and material tint carry hue. The shared dressing batch applies seams only inside the authored dam envelope, z>=215, y>=60, abs(x)<146. Water, actors and telegraphs retain their materials. |

The shaders share `target_surface.gdshaderinc`, use world metres and derivative normal relief, and add no texture file. `material_ground`, `material_rock` and `material_dam` are independent row switches that restore each surface's original material when disabled. `material_detail` and `material_relief` independently control visual detail and normal relief in the target resources.

For the revised 3 ms incremental play budget, first turn off `material_relief` to cut derivative normal work. Then set `material_dam=false`, which removes the additional plate shader and its weathering calculations. Next set `material_rock=false`; keep the ground grain as long as it remains stable at speed. If necessary, restore high's SSAO radius/intensity/detail. GI, volumetric fog and PCSS are already off. These cuts need back-to-back GPU and wall-time captures; no incremental cost was measured here.

The label leak was `WorldPrompt`, a screen-space Label outside the HUD layer, not Label3D. Capture now hides all CanvasItems, CanvasLayers and Label3D nodes, disables layer processing that can reveal prompts, and repeats the hide immediately before each still. The tool still measures the unfrozen scene and rebuilds a seeded frozen scene for the three stills.

The engine's ClassDB dump printed these timing methods:

```text
METHOD RenderingServer.viewport_set_measure_render_time(viewport, enable)
METHOD RenderingServer.viewport_get_measured_render_time_cpu(viewport)
METHOD RenderingServer.viewport_get_measured_render_time_gpu(viewport)
METHOD RenderingServer.get_frame_setup_time_cpu()
```

Capture enables measurement on the root viewport before warmup and retains 240 wall intervals beside 240 samples for each engine measure. `timing.gpu_render`, `timing.cpu_render` and `timing.cpu_frame_setup` each contain `median_ms`, `p95_ms`, `samples_ms`, `sample_count` and `available`. Any zero sample marks that summary unavailable rather than certifying a zero-cost renderer. GPU timestamps can arrive behind the corresponding wall sample. The CPU viewport timing excludes scripts and simulation; setup is recorded separately, so their medians must not be added as if they were one median. CPU scheduling and GPU clocks still respond to host conditions. See the [engine timing documentation](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/latest/classes/class_renderingserver.html#class-renderingserver-method-viewport-get-measured-render-time-cpu).

The base renderer comparison (`artifacts/local/lk-r2/baseline-check.log`, disposable local scratch) passes for fresh high/low and target-to-high/low against commit 79f62ab: 76 properties and six recorded server-setting groups. The preparation check (`artifacts/local/lk-r2/prepare_baseline.py`) pins unchanged high/low rows, the original renderer setter body, all original Palette constructors and the one-line terrain fence. `target_material_test.gd` stages a live Warden and chaser, then checks 544 mesh instances and 568 active material slots. It also pins every unbound actor, prop and effect material under target. It verifies resource identity and every stored material value on high/low, all target roles, unchanged mesh/caster state, shader uniform/include parsing, label hiding, median/p95 calculation and unavailable timestamps. The target game also stages 120 physics frames headlessly without an error line.

The round-1 smoke list plus `target_material_test` passes; lint reports zero findings. Headless capture still exits 2 without script error. The checks establish engine resource parsing and binding, not Forward+ shader execution or appearance. No rendered image was generated in the writer sandbox. Ball shadow visibility, absence of the site edge, dam colour at 150 m, fine-detail stability, actor/telegraph separation and the <=3 ms GPU increment remain coordinator checks. Changes are uncommitted for coordinator admission and capture.

## Colour, readable detail and dressing, unit LK round 3, 2026-10-02

Base: `e1f734d`. The coordinator's RTX 4080 captures put GPU medians at 0.875 ms
for high and 0.883 ms for target. Round 2 restored the ball's ground shadow and
removed the site edge. This round leaves the accepted sun, shadow, fog and grade
unchanged. All comparisons of rendered colour, detail and cost still require the
coordinator's next capture. No GPU capture runs in the writer sandbox.

### Part A: retain the surface colours

Ground gets `Palette.SAND` through a `source_color` base uniform, with
`Palette.SANDSTONE` for its slope blend. Rock gets `Palette.SANDSTONE` through the
same uniform path. This replaces round 2's raw terrain vertex tint and separate
pale stone colour. The dam keeps the original material's albedo colour, source map
and baked linear vertex colours. Round 2 explicitly removed that map inside the
dam envelope, losing the map's rust hue; this round samples it there too.

The established ground and rock shaders do not expose one base-colour uniform,
and the dam's merged material contains multiple authored vertex tints and a colour
texture rather than one Palette constant. The target factory therefore uses the
terrain's shared palette constants and copies the dam's actual source inputs.
Changing the high constructors to invent a common constant would violate scope.
The colour test independently compares ground/rock uniforms to those constants
and every dam uniform/map to its original material. Procedural detail multiplies
each source albedo by a scalar clamped to 0.75–1.2, with detail strength clamped to
0–1. It adds no hue. For the dam this bound is relative to the original textured
pixel, not its white material tint alone.

The shader source scan rejects numeric vec3/vec4 literals outside uniform defaults,
including signed and exponential literals. The red run deliberately set the ground
base to white and exited 1. Restoring the palette base passed the same test.

### Part B: patterns in world metres

| Surface | Layers and scale |
| --- | --- |
| Sand | Ripples every 0.6 m, bent over 12 m; drift lines every 4 m, bent over 18 m; continuous packed-sand field with principal wavelengths 16 m and 19.75 m and phase-warp wavelengths 21.92 m and 26.23 m; non-repeating fine grain on a 0.026 m noise lattice. |
| Ground slopes and rock | Strata every 1.2 m vertically, warped over 24 m in X and 18 m in Z; darker recesses and worn lips share that period. Rock erosion has principal wavelengths 8 m and 9.88 m, with phase-warp wavelengths 10.96 m and 13.11 m. Rock grain uses a non-repeating 0.083 m lattice. |
| Dam | Panels 3 × 3 m, seams roughly 0.05–0.17 m wide after antialiasing; streak field has principal wavelengths 0.8 m horizontally and 9.88 m vertically, phase warps over 10.96 m vertically and 1.31 m horizontally; seam-edge chips use a non-repeating 0.111 m lattice. The existing source map repeats every 6 m. |

The old broad field used a 4.17 m value-noise lattice. It already interpolated its
samples, so missing interpolation was not the cause. That cell-shaped field, raw
2 m terrain vertex tint and derivative relief from fine grain were the patch-producing
paths present in round 2. The new macro field is continuous sine-based variation,
uses palette uniforms instead of raw vertex tint and excludes fine-grain derivatives
from relief. Pinning the exact contribution of each old path would require rendered
ablation; removal of the pictured patches remains a GPU check. Ground and rock
have no time input or displacement. Fine grain fades with pixel footprint.

### Part C: deterministic dressing and air

`set_dressing`, `air_motes` and `air_streaks` are independent target keys. High and
low create none of their nodes. Presentation creates/frees one target-only root on
preset changes. Stage entry rebuilds it from the map id's hash and a private RNG;
no gameplay RNG, terrain, collision, player, enemy or camera state is written.

Pebbles, charcoal scrap flakes and muted sand tufts use three MultiMesh batches.
Their placement avoids the scrap lines, beacon bowl, spawn, vault and device sites.
The height test transforms every vertex from the exact submitted instance buffer
and compares it to the sampled terrain. It measured a maximum 0.170 m protrusion
across 1,173 small props in the Spillway. Broken rocks use a fourth batch on
unclimbable slopes outside the play rectangle plus an apron. Their actual mesh
extents stay inside 0.5–2 m. There are 53 in the tested Spillway. No batch casts
shadows or has a collider.

Air uses 160 tiny camera-facing dust cards and one batch of low ground streaks.
Motes are seeded 2–29 m ahead; fragment opacity fades to zero by 30 m radial camera
distance. Streaks move along the common XZ wind `(0.93, 0.367)` and use the same
30 m cutoff. Peak alphas are 0.025 and 0.018 respectively, with feathered edges,
no emission and no depth writes. A simulation-driven effect clock freezes with the
capture. These cards cannot establish volumetric shafts or long-distance haze.

Each prop and air shader clears padded screen rectangles around the player,
enemies, scrap pickups and committed telegraphs. More than 64 protected objects
hides the entire dressing root. The active camera drives the masks and dust box;
the capture tool explicitly refreshes them after a frozen camera change. This avoids
masking the wrong view when the tool uses a camera separate from the chase camera.
The tool hashes the new presentation sources and shaders too.

The headless dummy server does not support MultiMesh transform readback. Production
submits one packed instance buffer and retains that exact buffer on the batch for
the test to decode. This proves placement heights and seed reproducibility from
submitted instances; GPU execution and pixel coverage are separate evidence.

### Cost estimates and cut order

These are planning estimates for incremental GPU time at 1080p, not measurements:
material changes 0.05–0.20 ms; four prop batches 0.15–0.50 ms; dust 0.02–0.10 ms;
streaks 0.02–0.10 ms. The expected total increase is under 1 ms, with the actual
acceptance cap at 3 ms above high. Live protection rectangles also add unmeasured
CPU work; GPU headroom does not establish a wall-time improvement.

First disable `air_streaks`, then `air_motes`, then reduce prop density or disable
`set_dressing`. If materials are costly, disable `material_relief` before restoring
the dam/rock materials. Keep the accepted sun/shadow/fog values and readable sand
ripples. The coordinator checks hue/value against high, ripple/strata/seam scale,
patch removal, masking around feet and pickups, mote/streak opacity, shimmer at
speed and the target-versus-high GPU delta. Native shader backend compilation and
all of those image/playing judgments remain outside headless acceptance.

## Target look, round 4 (rendered and viewed)

Round 4's writer could render. The causes found by looking, not by test:

- Every prop batch was invisible. A `visibility_range_end` is measured to the batch's bounds centre, and a map-wide batch's centre sat over 100 m from the camera. Props now rely on frustum culling; only the camera-anchored motes keep a range.
- Sand was pale and blue-grey in shadow because `target_ground` ignored the floor map and the baked vertex tint. It now uses both (the floor map comes from the original ground material) so the mean colour sits within CIE76 1.3 of `high`.
- Ripples and drift are weighted by flatness (zero at 15 degrees and above); steep faces take strata that follow height (bright lip, dark undercut, cracks).
- Debris gathers in seeded drifts, is wide and low (protrusion stays under 0.2 m), and broken rock only stands at a cliff foot.
- Scrap protection rects fill the slots actors and threats leave, nearest the camera first; actor and threat overflow still hides the dressing. A harmless telegraph protects its edge, not its whole disc.
- `tools/still_compare.gd` prints per-rectangle mean colour and CIE76 distance between two stills.

## The spawn launch on the Cable Reach (unit ZL)

Momentum is sacred, so the Reach's 16 m drop from the spawn now lands on a flank that falls 3.5 m faster over its first 23 m (map_reach.gd, `LAUNCH_*`) instead of a near-level deck. The first try used 5 m and over-fed Sable (42 m/s through the dive, stalled before the kicker); 3.5 m gives the east lines their speed at the landing. Sable's entry changed: her slowest time is 11.49 s against 11.1 s before, and her median on-line share is 0.893 against 1.000. The shape is a function of z only, so a ball that lands off the fall line meets no scarp. Measured numbers are in design-v3/TRAVERSAL.md, "The spawn launch (unit ZL)".

## Unit SA: three standing activities on the Cable Reach (wave R3, 2026-10-02)

The Reach's `activity_sites` now stand as three optional activities, lit from the stage's first frame. `world/activities.gd` holds one table (`RULES`: how each starts, what finishes it, what it pays) and the node that runs them; `world/activity_view.gd` draws the amber column (70 m, readable from 150 m), the row's glyph, the ground ring and, within 30 m, the row's one word. Colour, shape and word are read through `EventKinds.ACTIVITY_ROWS`, and a new bus signal `activity_done(id, at)` (row in `ROWS`, one listener in `audio.gd`, sound `found_chime`) fires once when a site finishes.

| Id | Starts | Finished when | Pays |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ambush_altar` | The ball is inside the 8 m ring | The chaser, chaser and lobber it spawned are all dead | A part at the altar |
| `cracked_cache` | Nothing | A ram into the face at `cruise` speed or better, or a slam of at least `cruise` strength within 7 m of the face | A part on the pedestal |
| `hauler_bounty` | The ball within 30 m, or the brute is hit | The plated brute is dead | A part where it fell |

- **The pay** is a pickup, `ActivityView.gem`, that takes the ball that rolls onto it (a ball standing there when it appears does not take it) and then calls `run.add_part`. The part rolls from the uncommon and rare pool with a seeded stream (`hash([seed, run_id, stage, id])`), so `ctx.rng` is never drawn from.
- **Sources** are `activity_ambush`, `activity_cache` and `activity_bounty`. None is in `EventKinds.CEREMONY_SOURCES`, so no ceremony pauses the game; the find still takes its rarity's tier (an uncommon is `common`, a rare flares with juice's hit-stop budget).
- **The bounty** is a `plated_brute` with `carry_bounty` (an amber crate on its back, apart from its plates). It stands until the ball is within 30 m or it is struck, follows the ball while the ball is within 40 m of the site, and walks home at 9 m/s once the ball is further. It spawns on the stage's first physics tick (combat clears the stage's enemies in its own stage entry) and the director's cap does not count it (`World._step_director` subtracts `activities.standing()`).
- **Once per attempt.** `stage_entered` rebuilds all three, so a retry and a new run restore them; nothing else does. Calm mode builds only the cache, the one site it can pay; the ambush and the bounty are not built.
- **The slam threshold.** `slammed`'s second argument is the ball's own strength for the landing, `PlayerBall.slam_strength(speed, flatness)`: the landing speed over 30 m/s, held between 0.4 and 1.6, times how flat the ground was. It is not a speed. A ram breaks the wall at `RAM_MIN_PER_CRUISE` (0.85) times `cruise` (ruling ad, 26 Oct: a held stick never quite reaches cruise), and a slam breaks it at `SLAM_MIN_PER_CRUISE` (1.0) times the strength of a flat landing at the tuned cruise. The slam bar stays at full cruise because `slam_strength` floors at 0.4, so 0.85 would let any flat landing break the wall. A harpoon smash's landing goes through the same function, so it breaks the wall too. The first version compared the strength with a speed, so no slam in play could break the wall; the test now sends its slams through the ball's landing. `harpoon_smashed` is not connected: it fires only on an enemy body, so it cannot reach a wall. `World.director_alive()` is the count the director is given, so a test can check the subtraction.
- **The cache's site moved 2 m west, to (68, -4).** At (70, -4) the pocket's back wall stood on Kest's authored line (`reach_map_test` NOTE: `(70.0, 29.43, 4.5)` under kest, the back wall's centre); at (66, -4) a side wall stood on Rivet's. At (68, -4) the NOTE lists no device. A scan of 15 sites within 4 m found (68, -4), (68, 0), (70, -6) and (70, -8) clear. The pocket's width stays 6 m, since narrowing it could not clear the back wall's centre.
- **What stayed as it was.** The Spillway's three devices, `EventDirector` and every timed event, the director's pack table and the three-stage game. The cache wall is the activity's own collider (not the `cracked_wall` contraption), so Rivet's sprint wall into tunnel e1 is unchanged.

## BN: the Warden posture sweep (wave R3, 2026-10-02)

Growing did not matter in the Warden fight: bots measured a starting ball breaking it in 28 to 34 s and a grown one in 28 to 32 s. Unit BN made the rule's numbers come from the stage row and built a sweep that finds them.

- **What changed.** `BossPosture.new(numbers)` reads a `posture` dictionary from the stage row (`combat.warden_spec` carries it, `warden.gd` hands it over) and falls back to its constants per key. New: `curve`, the exponent on the clamped ratio (default 1.0), and `ram_shatters` (default true). With no `posture` key every result equals the old rule's; `boss_posture_case` keeps every check and adds a 20-hit table for that, plus hand-computed checks for curve 2 and 3, `ram_shatters` false and the row's other numbers.
- **The sweep.** `tools/posture_sweep.gd` swaps `ctx.stages` for a table that returns the slice row with one plating and one `posture` dictionary, plays the brawler, skipper and camper over seeds 1 to 5 in six child processes, and writes `tools/posture_sweep.tsv` (one row per cell) and `tools/posture_sweep_runs.tsv` (the 360 runs). `tools/posture_sweep.md` has the command, the columns and the reading rules. The driver gained `stage_limit` and `fight_limit` and notes the heft at the first turn to the Warden; its policies are unchanged.
- **The result.** 57 minutes for 360 runs. No cell met every band: the brawler's heft at its first strike is 1.04 to 1.10, so plating and curve move both bots together (ratio 0.92 to 1.66 across the grid). The packet said to stop and report the closest cells, so the slice row is unchanged and `posture_bands_test` is not committed (it would fail until a pick exists; the draft is in `artifacts/local/bn/`).
- **Repeatability.** Two runs of one cell differed by up to 23 percent on a brawler seed. Not the sweep (plain `bot_run.gd` does it too), not the camera jitter (a copy without it still did). Audio bake worker tasks and Jolt body ordering are the open suspects.
- **Next.** Rerun the sweep after the map's activities land. If the brawler's heft at the Warden is then clearly above the skipper's, the same grid may separate the bots; if it stays near 1.1, the lever is growth, not posture numbers.

## Unit SD: repeatable headless bot samples, 2026-10-02

Checked base `85432aa9950321b2b9f30125fa06c580cfc1bf32`, Godot 4.7.2, brawler seed 3 on the slice. Gameplay directions depended on process-local object IDs: `src/build/scrap_field.gd` chose pickup swirl from ID parity, and `src/combat/counter.gd` chose Fleet circling direction the same way. Audio bakes allocate resources on worker threads while the game starts. Their allocation timing can change those IDs without changing the seeded bot or run stream. An in-process seed list also retains physics and presentation state between games.

The first observed difference in the detailed concurrent reproduction was tick 2424: the same attracted pickup had side -1 in one process and +1 in the other. At tick 2437 the wallet was 21 versus 22 and mass was 12 versus 13. With pickup side pinned alone, the Mag trapper's circling side still differed at tick 2207, followed by different death-drop positions. Pinning both directions made a full concurrent pair agree. An in-process list retained an early velocity difference at tick 278, so list seeds now run in separate child processes with separate data folders. The experiment establishes process-state sensitivity; it does not identify a particular Jolt internal cache or prove general engine determinism.

The runner supplies alternating signs by seed and spawn order, waits for the audio bake before play, and isolates list seeds. The two source hooks preserve their original ID-based defaults when bot metadata is absent, so ordinary player runs keep their existing behavior. No game rule, tuning value or existing test expectation changed. `--trace-ticks=1` writes full-precision pre-driver snapshots, and `--ticks=N` bounds diagnostic runs. `--allocation-noise=N` is a regression fixture that changes object allocation without changing the policy, seed or table.

Fight seconds here follow `tools/balance_report.gd`: break minus the later of engagement and bot commitment, using spine ticks when available. Baseline concurrent runs measured 20.755556 versus 26.744444 seconds, a 28.9 percent spread relative to the shorter run. Final concurrent runs, and seed 3 after seeds 1 and 2 versus alone, all measured 20.755556 seconds and matched all 20,097 trace rows. Earlier session figures of 26.66 seconds used commitment alone; that is not the balance report's fight metric.

| Experiment | Data folders | Execution | Seeds compared | Fight seconds | Result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Baseline | Fresh per process | Sequential | 3 / 3 | 26.744444 / 26.355556 | Different |
| Baseline, full trace | Fresh per process | Sequential | 3 / 3 | 24.633333 / 24.633333 | Fight matched; enemy state differed |
| Baseline, pickup trace | Fresh per process | Sequential | 3 / 3 | 26.355556 / 26.355556 | Fight matched; pickup state differed |
| Audio bake wait only | Fresh per process | Sequential | 3 / 3 | 26.355556 / 26.355556 | Full trace matched |
| Baseline | Fresh per process | Concurrent | 3 / 3 | 20.755556 / 26.744444 | Different |
| Baseline, pickup trace | Fresh per process | Concurrent | 3 / 3 | 26.355556 / 24.633333 | Different |
| Audio bake wait only | Fresh per process; list shared internally | Sequential | Third of 1,2,3 / alone | 22.577778 / 26.355556 | Different |
| Stable pickup side only | Fresh per process | Concurrent | 3 / 3 | 26.744444 / 24.633333 | Different |
| Both stable directions | Fresh per process | Concurrent | 3 / 3 | 20.755556 / 20.755556 | Full trace matched |
| Both stable directions, cross-comparison | Fresh per process; list shared internally | List versus earlier standalone | Third of 1,2,3 / alone | 22.622222 / 20.755556 | Different |
| Final runner | Fresh for every seed | Sequential | Third of 1,2,3 / alone | 20.755556 / 20.755556 | Full trace matched |
| Final runner | Fresh per process | Concurrent | 3 / 3 | 20.755556 / 20.755556 | Full trace matched |

`tests/bot_repeat_test.gd` runs two separate 6,000-tick children with fresh data folders and different allocation noise, then compares every trace row. It is marked `smoke: solo` and allows 40 seconds per child. With both source hooks disabled and the check unchanged, it exits 1 at differing tick 213. Restoring the hooks gives exit 0 and seven passing checks. The named smoke run passed all seven tests plus import, with zero skips; the repeat test took 43.4 seconds. Lint passed with zero findings. The six existing tests were not edited.

Evidence is under `artifacts/local/sd/`, including the complete report, exact child commands, traces, red/green output and comparisons. Smoke receipt: `artifacts/local/receipts/sd-2df1e5543a0d-2026-10-02T16-18-31-661Z.json`. Re-sweep tuning bands after adopting this sampler. Repeatability evidence is limited to this build, machine, policy, seed and table; gyro phase and device luck still contain identity-dependent inputs and need separate coverage if later policies exercise them.

## GR: growth pays on the slice (wave R4, 2026-10-02)

Bots measured the ball's heft at 1.04 to 1.10 after 110 s of fighting: a kill paid 3 scrap, class II costs 225 and wear takes 30 a minute. Unit GR makes fighting and the standing activities pay enough to show.

- **Two numbers, each in its owner's table.** The slice stage row carries `kill_scrap: 4.0`, a multiplier read in `build._on_enemy_killed` beside the sheet's own (`scrap_value * sheet.kill_scrap * row.kill_scrap`, rounded; a row without the key pays 1.0, so the three-stage table is unchanged). Each activity rule's `pay` carries `scrap: 180`. A chaser now drops 12 and a lobber 16 on the slice; the Warden's line scales the same way.
- **The pay moment.** When the ball takes an activity's gem, `ctx.run.add_part` and `ctx.run.add_scrap(180, at)` run in one call site. A roll with no part pays the scrap at `_finish`. `add_scrap` raises mass and the wallet together and publishes the class change, so the shell swells through the juice that already exists.
- **The arc.** Four arrivals and the two activity fights pay about 264 scrap at 4 times, three activities 540. Less about 100 of wear a clean player holds about 700 (class III starts at 540). A player who goes straight to the Warden arrives with almost none. The run log's stage row now records `mass_at_engage`, `heft_at_engage` and `heft_class_at_engage` when the Warden wakes; a retry's engagement replaces them.
- **Side effect.** Scrap is also the wallet: three gems add 540 to what the workshop can spend on the slice. No price changed.
- **The ambush.** Its three arrivals now start 30 degrees past the ring point opposite the ball's entry, so none stands within 4 m of the ball's line through the ring (before, one could drop onto it).
- **Not measured.** Whether a bot or a player really reaches class III before the Warden is untested here; the Warden unit and the next posture sweep will say.

## PC: wind-up pose corrections, checked by eye (wave R4, 2026-10-02)

Four wind-up poses were written without a render; stills showed a fault in each. Every fix is visual only, so each wind-up test's seeded control (pose on against pose off) still shows zero difference in position, velocity, telegraph and release.

- **Plated brute.** The pads rise 2.5 m and the arms were skinned rigidly to them, so at full wind-up the arm and fist floated free of the shoulders. One mesh, `BruteArmLinks` (two 0.115 m sleeves inside the 0.14 m arm, the body's own flashing material), now runs from the shoulder joint (y 0.6) to the pad's arm top on the same clock. The raise stays 2.5 m. The glb is unchanged. `windup_pose_enabled` off hides them.
- **Chaser.** A brace of 18 degrees and 0.22 m vanished head-on. The wind-up is now a rear-up: nose 42 degrees, body up 0.3 m and back 0.7 m (`REAR_PITCH`, `REAR_SHIFT`), and the underside shows from the front at 10 m. No `STATE_ROWS` row means "a hit now breaks this attack": `will_break` marks a plate that breaks now and `interrupted` marks a break that already happened. The chaser shows no mark, and none was invented.
- **Mag trapper.** `MagTopLink`, a charcoal mast 0.16 m in radius, runs from the stack's top disc (y 0.5 in stack space) to the lifted disc and goes with it when thrown. Each link is a node of its own, so the live assembly is one draw over the asset's: the two asset tests keep the asset's ceiling (3 and 6) and gain `LIVE_CEILING_D` 4 and 7. Folding the links into the skinned glb would keep the old ceilings; that is a Blender change left open.
- **Winch drone.** The rotor idles at `IDLE_SPIN` 4 rad/s (0.64 turns a second, 2.5 degrees a tick at 90 ticks/s). The wind-up's rate is 40.8 * progress rad/s, so it passes the idle rate by progress 0.1 and is 2.5 times it at progress 0.25. The spin-up starts from the rotor's idle angle, so release never jumps it. After an attack the rotor coasts to a whole turn and idles again. The turn is a function of the physics frame count. Open: the rotor is nearly symmetric, so at 10 m the idle turn shows as a small shift of the spoke pattern; one marked blade would read better.
- **Tests.** The seam is `windup_pose_enabled` on all six. The clamps that kept a warn tick one float32 ulp short of full pose are gone from the game code. The tests compare with a tolerance and allow the release tick to be the first full-pose tick or the one before it. Two tests (winch drone, deflector) pin literal values at progress 0, 0.5 and 1 instead of recomputing them with the code's expression.

## Target look, round 5: the clutter kit placed (unit LK5, wave R4)

`set_dressing.gd` now dresses the ground under the `target` preset with the ten models of `clutter_kit.glb` in place of round 4's flat spheres, boxes and stars. One `MultiMeshInstance3D` per kit mesh (`Clutter_rock_a` and so on). `Palette.target_clutter_material` gives the kit's paint slot vertex colour as albedo (the importer leaves it unused) on a copy; the kit's own resources are untouched. No collision, no new asset. The kit is small beside a 1 m ball, so each piece is scaled up: anchors 3.5 to 10 times, small pieces 1.5 to 4 times (`ANCHOR_SCALE`, `SMALL_SCALE`).

- **Clusters, not scatter.** A cluster is one big anchor (stone, slab, offcut or pipe stub) with 6 to 12 small pieces at its feet. Centres: the foot of a cliff (flat ground with a steep face 3 to 7 m off), the base of a post, wall, tower or satellite, and the clear edge of each ride line (`EDGE_SHARE` 0.6 of the 9 m steps). Centres keep 9 m apart. About 81 percent of the Reach's play ground has no piece within 6 m. 992 pieces on the Reach, 1001 on the Spillway.
- **Keepouts come from the map's data** (`SetDressing.keepouts`, one row per place with a clear distance, `blocked` and `distance_to_row` read it): spawn 6 m, the Warden bowl floor plus 3 m, the vault 11 m, scrap lines 3 m, kicker ramp (its length and half width plus 3 m), reward gate slots 6 m, activity sites 11 m, devices 7 m; on the Reach also the three pilots' authored lines at 4 m, the sweeper and berm (the whole radial band of face and bank, across the span `Features.banked_arc` gets, plus 3 m), the dive ramp (`DIVE_HALF + DIVE_SIDE` plus 3 m), the cable, the skylight halfpipe, the four tunnel floors (`TUNNEL_HALF` plus 3 m) and the thermals. A piece's footprint is added when it is placed. The pilots' lines are read from `src/world/reach_routes.json`, because `tools/*` is left out of the export; `set_dressing_test` checks that file equals the waypoints in `tools/traversal_lines.json`, and a missing or non-Dictionary file places no pilot keep-outs and warns once.
- **The test measures from surfaces, not from the rows.** `set_dressing_test` rebuilds each riding surface from the map's constants (tunnel floor, dive ramp, kicker footprint, sweeper and berm bands, routes, gate slots, activity sites, the bowl) and requires every piece's origin 3 m clear of it, on the Reach and, for its gate slots, sites, bowl and scrap lines, on the Spillway. It fails if a surface is not measured. Setting the tunnel row's radius to 0 fails it (w2 at -3.6 m); the first version of the check, which measured centrelines, missed the sweeper and berm, and the berm row covered only the inner 14 m of a 22 m band.
- **Clutter keeps the dressing rules.** `src/presentation/clutter.gdshader` (albedo times vertex colour, a contact shade at the foot, the shared screen-space protection) replaces the kit's standard material through `Palette.target_clutter_material`. Clutter casts no shadow. The shadow and protection checks cover its batches. Protection had a defect that clutter exposed: a far object whose box straddles the camera's plane returned a full-screen rectangle and blanked all dressing in the Reach still; it now returns none beyond 4 reaches, and a test covers it.
- **Tufts.** Their vertex colours are rewritten at build time to sandstone, lifted, darker at the root (the kit's dark straw read as spikes). In the Reach still they read as pale straw tufts.
- **Cost.** GPU render median on the Reach play still: `high` 0.875 ms (the earlier scratch-tool capture; `high` builds no dressing), `target` 0.889 ms (+0.014), 941 pieces placed on the Reach, 1001 on the Spillway.
- **What the kit lacks.** The stones and slabs are dark against the sand; a lighter, warmer paint and a taller stub that reads as a pipe from 20 m belong to the kit's recipe.
- **Capture.** `tools/target_capture.gd` takes `--table=slice` (the Cable Reach on `Cams.TARGET_REACH`, outside the default set), `--ball=x,z` and `--face=x,z`, and writes the table, the ball and the placed clutter count into `frames.json`.

## Unit WG: the Warden's health as a ring on the ground, 2026-10-02

Base `f42b3cd4d07135b7b18c49dfab53b187ca2a251f`, Godot 4.7.2. Two earlier rounds put the chunk count on the Warden's jaws and failed a blind count. This unit moves the count to the floor: `src/presentation/warden_ring.gd`, one ring per posture Warden, a child of the Warden so it is freed with it, wired in `presentation.gd`. Four arcs with 20 degree gaps centred on the camera's front, back, left and right axes, so from any side the nearest seam faces the camera. The ring's inner edge, its dark backing, sits 0.6 m beyond the Warden's own core telegraph radius (`CORE_RADIUS`, 6 m, read from `warden.gd`), so the backing runs from 6.6 m to 8.1 m and the lit band from 6.85 m to 7.85 m (centre radius 7.35 m) and lies outside the pulse's core telegraph, not inside it. It is lifted 0.12 m and follows the ground height. Unshaded, alpha-blended, depth-tested and under the telegraph render priority, so nothing standing on it is drawn over.

Every arc sits on a dark backing, `boss_plate.gd`'s `crack_backing()`, 0.25 m wider than the band on every side with square ends, so a seam reads as a dark cut between two lit pieces. A lit arc is the plate's filled-pip colour on that backing; a lost arc is the plate's empty-pip outline on the same backing, so lit and lost differ by fill, not by backing. Lit arcs take the plate's open colour while the Warden is staggered. The next arc to go carries the plate's crack mark: a dark zigzag across the band with a thin line of `crack_color()` in it, no white, drawn at 8, 50 and 92 percent of the arc because the middle copy alone is hidden by the body from the side and rear cameras. Colours come from `boss_plate.gd` through static readers (`pip_color`, `bar_color_for`, `crack_color_for`, `crack_backing`), and the plate's own draw uses them. Arcs are lost from the highest index down, so the lit ones stay a run.

Design finding, not a defect: the Warden's body hides the far half of a ground ring from the chase camera. At 12 m the two far arcs show as slivers beside the body, so a count reads from two near arcs plus two slivers. The outer ring at 7.1 m shows more of them than the first round's. The open colour (the plate's open role, the arena's `Palette.ARC` mint) matches the pale teal band at the edge of the stills; a likely source is the beacon ring tinted by `beacon_charge_changed` (`src/core/beacon.gd` line 48, colour `event_kinds.gd` line 36), not isolated. It is not changed here.

`tests/warden_ring_test.gd` drives a real fight on the slice table through `combat.strike()` and a real harpoon smash and compares the ring with the Warden's facts and the plate's readers, with the expected lit count and crack arc hardcoded per state, and checks the geometry from the meshes: four 20 degree gaps on the camera axes, a backing wider than the band under every arc, the crack's colours from the plate's readers with no white, and an inner edge that clears `CORE_RADIUS` by 0.6 m. `tools/warden_states_capture.gd` renders the five states from three sides plus one still during a pulse charge (`charging_front.png`, not in the blind set), writes only under `--out`, and exits 1 when its own checks fail or no Warden spawns. The Warden's own badge is left as play shows it. Stills and logs are under `artifacts/local/alpha/stills/wg/`.

## Unit BT: the Warden is tuned against ball size, 2026-10-02

Base `f42b3cd4d07135b7b18c49dfab53b187ca2a251f`, Godot 4.7.2. Andrew said he could just go to the boss and win. The first sweep could not fix that: its "grown" bot reached the Warden at heft 1.04 to 1.10, since a bot does not know the map's activities. This sweep takes growth as given and starts the ball with the scrap a grown player would hold (small 0, mid 225, grown 540; a camper at 0), then picks Warden numbers under which size decides the fight.

- **Method** (fixed before the run, in `tools/posture_sweep.md`): 16 cells (plating 70, 90, 110, 130; `curve` 1, 2; `ram_shatters` true, false), five seeds per arm, 320 runs, each in its own process so it repeats exactly. Bands on medians: grown 25 to 45 s; small at least 2.0 times grown, at most 150 s, 5 of 5 broke it; mid strictly between; camper 0 of 5. Pick: largest small-to-grown ratio with a small median at most 120 s, confirmed by a second run.
- **Result.** Four cells pass: 110 / 2 / true, 110 / 2 / false, 130 / 2 / true, 130 / 2 / false. The 130 cells have small medians over 120 s. The pick is plating 110, `curve` 2, `ram_shatters` false (grown 25.5 s, mid 44.9 s, small 91.8 s, camper never, ratio 3.59). The second run matched the first run for run (20 of 20). The slice row carries it; `tests/posture_bands_test.gd` holds it on seeds 1 to 3 (small 91.8 s, grown 25.2 s, within 10 percent).
- **Why curve 2.** Curve 1 gave a ratio under 2.0 at every plating where grown still fights 25 s or more. Squaring the punch-to-plating ratio makes a small ball's under-plating rams gain little and a grown ball's gain full; below plating 110 the grown ball (heft 2.20) breaks it in under 25 s whatever the curve.
- **The margin is thin at the low end.** The grown median is 25.5 s against a floor of 25, and 25.2 s on seeds 1 to 3. Any change that speeds a grown fight by a second (a stronger ram, a faster approach) fails the band and the test; that is the test doing its work, and the sweep is the way to re-pick.
- **Measured here, not assumed.** Mass at the first strike is above the start, not under it: the skipper picks up scrap on the roll faster than wear takes it (small 3, mid 226, grown 542; heft 1.01, 1.50, 2.20).
- **Tool changes.** The arms are one table in the tool. Each run is its own process (the sweep's earlier in-process list did not repeat). Each sweep invocation has its own data folder, keyed by the parent process id and cleared per job. The fight clock starts at the later of the bot's turn and the first contact on the Warden (`struck_s`, its own column). The sweep's command writes under `artifacts/local/`.
- **Tests that read the row.** Seven posture tests now take the plating (and, where a ram's gain is aimed, the curve) from the slice row instead of 45; three needed a change for rams that no longer shatter or a count the synergy strikes now move (the mount, sound and run-log sitting tests), and the stage table test now compares the slice Warden to the slice row.

## Unit SP: a twelve-part slice, and a size step that opens a tree pick, 2026-10-02

Base `b3d07031b35a46462a4f2710af6a4cedaed2f2c9`, Godot 4.7.2.

- **One list.** `SliceDefs.PARTS` holds twelve ids and `part_in_play(id)` reads it; `StageDefs` returns true. `Parts.offer_pool` drops anything the table does not play, but only when `Synergies.table.parts_listed()` is true (the slice), so every default-table path is what it was (the hotwire loot digest passes unedited). On the slice the generated share is 0, the shop's empty-pool fallback (`_roll_one`) draws from the shelf's rarities instead of a generated id, and the rare-plus top slot, which has no listed part, reads the same fallback. The list: the five shown parts of the kept synergies; `hot_rivets` (kinetic), `loose_trigger` (speed) and `rivet_plating` (mass) as the carriers; the two class mods; `sunforged_core` and `gatling_heart` for the rare tier. Arc comes from `arc_coil` itself, and from the pylon mod for that pilot.
- **Not covered.** `Parts.milestone_for` and the vault's rare-plus bonus are not filtered: the slice has no event mix, so no milestone is awarded there. Nothing else gives a part outside the list.
- **The class step.** `RunState.class_rewarded` is the highest class a pick was granted for (1 at a new run, saved in the stage-start snapshot, restored with it). `World._on_class_rise` owes one pick per class above it, and `_deliver_class_picks` opens them with `tree.open_checkpoint()` once the ball is grounded (or in the bay) and `WhyCallout.warden_fight_live` is false. A retry clears what was owed and restores the memory, so the climb after a retry earns its picks again. The tree's own cap holds: four depths, or two under `--world-one-builds`, which defaults on, so on the shipped flags a slice run gets the start pick plus one class pick and the end-of-stage workshop pick adds none past the cap. Whether that is the intended count is an owner call.
- **Tests.** `tests/slice_parts_test.gd` and `tests/class_step_pick_test.gd`. Each was run red by breaking its rule (the pool filter and generated-share guard off; the rewarded-class memory off) and green with it restored.

## Unit BC: the beacon ring stops wearing the live colour, 2026-10-02

Base `b3d07031b35a46462a4f2710af6a4cedaed2f2c9`, Godot 4.7.2. The beacon ring, mast bulb and edge marker were tinted by `beacon_charge_changed`, which is `Palette.ARC`, the same mint as `Palette.live()`, so a pale tall ring at the arena edge read as "your move works here". They now read one fact, the beacon's state, from two new `STATE_ROWS` rows: `beacon_sealed` (slate, "not yet") while a Warden clamps the beacon, `beacon_open` (amber, "the world is offering") once it opens or is unsealed. `beacon.gd` holds `sealed` (true after `place`, set from `advance`'s argument each tick, false in `open`), `state_id()` and `tint_color()`, and refreshes the bulb, its glow and the ring on a change; the ring keeps its alpha. `edge_markers.gd` colours the beacon's marker from the same row. The beacon's marker only appears when the stage is unsealed, so a sealed marker colour is checked through the shared row, not drawn. There is no table switch; the three-stage game follows the same rule.

Not changed: the `beacon_charge_changed` row itself, because `warden_look.gd` (`_rest_core_color`) and the HUD objective row (`ROW_COLOR["charge_beacon"]` in `hud.gd`) still read it and are outside this unit. Both still draw mint for the beacon; the HUD row only shows after the unseal, where amber is right. Follow-up for the owner of those files. Sound rows are untouched. `tests/beacon_colour_test.gd` pins sealed slate and open amber for ring, bulb, glow and edge marker, each at least 20 CIE76 from live and hazard.

## Warm stone and baked straw clutter (unit KC, 2026-10-02)

The clutter recipe now shades sandstone in linear light. Stones mix sandstone with 6–12% sand, and slabs with 8% sand. Their upward planes are lighter than their sides and undersides. The shared helper has no separate strata tone, so the directional shading supplies the darker strata planes without a new palette role. Scrap plates retain their original `fleet_paint` and `bare_metal` slots and geometry.

Tufts bake sandstone lifted 1.32 times in linear light, with roots at 85% of the tip colour. This follows the former runtime treatment, whose 1.4 times lift exceeded KC's 95%-of-sand upper limit at the tips. The baked tip luminance is about 91.6% of sand. `_dry_grass` and its call are removed from `set_dressing.gd`; placement copies the imported vertex colours without changing them.

`stub_a` is a 0.55 m six-sided pipe with a broken open rim, a dark inner wall and a foot flange. The flange preserves the existing 0.21 m horizontal footprint. The stub's longest-side bound changes from 0.20–0.35 m to 0.45–0.60 m, and its triangle cap changes from 60 to 72. It uses 72 triangles, up from 48; the kit totals 280, up from 256. Other geometry and size limits are unchanged.

| Mesh | Mean upward linear luminance | Ratio to Palette.SAND |
| --- | --- | --- |
| rock_a | 0.337543 | 0.599004 |
| rock_b | 0.319569 | 0.567107 |
| rock_c | 0.332591 | 0.590216 |
| slab_a | 0.380219 | 0.674736 |
| slab_b | 0.360919 | 0.640486 |
| tuft_a | 0.460396 | 0.817018 |
| tuft_b | 0.460552 | 0.817295 |

`clutter_kit_asset_test.gd` checks the area-weighted upward mean against 0.45–0.95 of sand and prints each mesh's value. Every stone, slab and tuft must provide measured paint faces, so changing its material slot cannot bypass that check. The test converts linear vertex colours to D65 CIE Lab and checks every vertex against the live palette's amber, mint and hazard colours. The minimum CIE76 distance is 26.774609, above 20. The former paint-channel cap `r < 0.6` is removed because it rejected the required lifted straw; the existing sand-luminance, opacity and warm-channel-order checks remain, and the explicit CIE76 check now excludes gameplay colours. No existing check is removed otherwise.

Mutation evidence under `artifacts/local/kc/` includes a black baked `rock_a`, which exits 1 and prints `FAIL KC-1 rock_a up-facing mean luminance 0.000000 / sand 0.563508 = 0.000000 within 0.45..0.95 -> FAIL`. The restored asset exits 0 and prints `PASS KC-1 rock_a up-facing mean luminance 0.337543 / sand 0.563508 = 0.599004 within 0.45..0.95 -> PASS`. Bright stone, amber, mint, hazard, missing paint, short pipe and missing inner-wall mutations also fail the intended checks. Flattened tuft colours fail the darker-root checks. Restoring the original runtime recolour fails both the source and baked-colour preservation checks. A missing tuft fails the two-tuft count check through an isolated driver that inherits the unchanged test function. The full missing-tuft mutation run was interrupted after the game path assumed a complete kit; the shipped asset is restored and passes the full named suite. The checks stay unchanged during each mutation.

Two recipe exports match byte for byte: SHA-256 `0c413a7ed2bda1e63e89317c98559504c702635273ff618d10017419bb43715f`. Blender 5.2.2 LTS rendered `artifacts/local/kc/contact-sheet.png` in Cycles on the CPU with ten meshes on Palette.SAND patches. I viewed it: warm tan stone, lighter upper facets, darker sides, tan dry straw, oxide scrap plates, and a pipe with a visible dark opening and foot flange. This does not establish chase-camera readability at 20 m; the coordinator's in-game stills remain the visual follow-up.

Godot 4.7.2 initially could not save editor settings under home. The allowed retry with all XDG roots under `artifacts/local/kc/xdg/` passes import, `clutter_kit_asset_test`, `set_dressing_test` and the unchanged `target_material_test`, with zero skips and no error-prefixed lines. The final named receipt is `artifacts/local/receipts/kc-29391b12f821-2026-10-02T19-17-04-012Z.json`; later direct restored-asset runs retain full KC check output. Sketch lint reports 0 findings. Source changes are confined to the six authorized files. Evidence level 4 covers numeric asset checks, real imports, executable placement/material checks and CPU rendering; no GPU capture or human play acceptance is claimed.

## Unit DR: the winch drone's rotor reads as turning, 2026-10-02

Checked at base `b3d07031b35a46462a4f2710af6a4cedaed2f2c9` on `codex/job-3a6e3d1828e1578cd7a1-b5916db190`. The 16-sided disc is now three solid oxide blades centred at 0, 115 and 245 degrees. It uses neither mint nor amber, adds no material slot, and keeps `WinchRotor` and its pivot at y 2.0 with a 1.6 m radius.

| Asset and live assembly draws | 4 | 4 | 4 |
| Rotor triangles | 64 | 76 | Rotor plus 64 drum triangles below 150 |

With the original stale base, the largest coast-restart step is 1.027895 radians against a normal wind-up's 0.451265 radians, and the check fails.
