## Unit CP: one command for the checkpoint, 2026-10-02

Base `b3d07031b35a46462a4f2710af6a4cedaed2f2c9`. `npm run sketch:checkpoint -- --before` prints one table of the ALPHA-PLAN admission bands and exits 1 on a miss; `-- --after <runs.ndjson>` reads Andrew's sitting from the game's run log. Output goes to `artifacts/local/checkpoint/<timestamp>/summary.json`. `--quick` runs 3 seeds per policy and skips perf.

- **Bots.** completionist (the explorer), skipper and camper on the slice table, 16 seeds each, one process per seed, six at a time. Bands: camper 0 wins; skipper at least 1 win and a longer median Warden fight than the explorer's wins, read from the last stage's `posture.fight_ticks` (first posture gain to the break; `ticks_to_warden_break` is the stage clock and counts exploring too); explorer at least 60 percent; explorer median heft (`heft_at_engage`) and parts (`part_ids_at_exit`) above the skipper's, each read from the last stage row that carries it.
- **Perf.** `tools/perf_bench.gd --scene=s3 --table=slice` native at 1920x1080 through `claude-open` (vsync off). CPU is `process_ms` p99 (every node's `_process`), at most 10 ms; GPU is the viewport's measured render time p99, at most 7 ms. `perf_bench.gd` gained `--table=` and records it.
- **Sweep and smoke.** The slice row's plating, curve and `ram_shatters` pick the line of `tools/posture_sweep.tsv`; its `pass` column is the band. The newest `gate` receipt under `artifacts/local/receipts/` whose `head` is HEAD must be `ok`.
- **Sitting questions to run-log fields.** Two or more activities: `stages[].events_joined` summed per run (prompts are not tied to an event, so unprompted reads as entered). Harpoon smashes into packs, kept up: `smashes` and `bites` are counted but the target and time of each are not, so this prints "not measurable". Time in the zone: `stages[].ticks_to_warden_engaged` in seconds, passing at 60 s or more (`BANDS.zoneSeconds`, a knob, since ALPHA-PLAN gives no number). Second run: two distinct slice `run_id`s; whether he was asked is not recorded.
- **Review fixes (coordinator).** The fight band reads `posture.fight_ticks`; the smoke band needs a clean gate receipt; the activities question counts distinct events, so a retry's re-entry counts once; `--after` checks its file before it makes an output folder. Four ALPHA-PLAN lines print as "by hand" rows and never count toward the verdict: red on the rejected builds, the lair-chaser (Hunt is parked), the Fable integrated read, and per-arm questions (one fight, no arms). The summary line says "bots and fixed checks pass" instead of ADMIT.

## Unit EB: the explorer works the standing activities before the Warden, 2026-10-02

Base `261994d807c0698d37a3e0f6436dac393dac35e1`. The `completionist` policy (the checkpoint's explorer) now takes the slice's three standing activities first, in `tools/bot_driver.gd`. Before this the policy predated them: it rolled scrap lines, reached the Warden at 146 to 206 s at heft 1.30 and stalled at `STAGE_LIMIT` in 8 of 16 first-run seeds.

- **Order.** High ground first, ties nearest first (ground height in 8 m bands; `LEVEL_BAND`). On the Cable Reach that is the cache on the east shelf, then the bounty at the trough head, then the altar down the trough. A ball cannot climb back out of the trough: the first attempt took the bounty first and stuck below the shelf's cliff at x 31 for 36 s.
- **Cache.** From far off it squares along its own latitude to the run-up point 24 m out on the cracked face's open side, then rams at the wall along the face's axis with the wall-avoiding turn off (`_ignore_walls`, read only by `_drive`). A ram that does not break it in 3 s runs up again.
- **Altar and bounty.** It rolls into the altar's ring and fights the nearest living pack member with the existing `_fight`; it fights the bounty's brute with `_fight`. Each pay is then rolled onto, stepping off first if the ball already stands on it, because a pickup takes only a ball that arrives.
- **Budget.** Each activity may hold the bot `ACTIVITY_SECONDS` = 40 game seconds from the first frame it is the target, pay included; past that it is abandoned for good that stage.
- **Other exploring.** Scrap-line waypoints, events and the vault run only after the activities and stop at stage second `ENGAGE_BY` = 140, when the bot goes to the Warden, so the fight keeps 100 s of the 240 s stage limit. This applies only on a map with activity sites. `STAGE_LIMIT`, the bands and the checkpoint script are untouched; skipper, camper, random and traversal take no new code path (their first-three-seed `bots.ndjson` lines are identical to base's).
- **Notes.** The completionist's stage note gains `activities_entered` and `activities_finished`.

Result, 16 seeds on the slice: 16 wins; 3 of 3 activities finished in every seed; median heft at engage 2.5 (1.77 to 3.13); engage at 145 to 152 s; fight 15.8 to 50.2 s. `tests/bot_explorer_activities_test.gd` checks the first target is a site, not the Warden; it fails with the targeting off (first goal `waypoint 26`) and passes with it. The bot is a harness: this measures the path the game was tuned for, not that a person finds the cache's ram or the shelf's route, and it says nothing about feel. The explorer now also gets there at the engage deadline rather than when it is done, so its engage time is fixed by `ENGAGE_BY`, not by play.

One harness change outside the route: `src/world/activities.gd` `_roll` hashes `run.run_id`, whose first half is the clock's second, so two runs of one seed paid different parts and `bot_repeat_test` failed once the explorer took the pay. The explorer pins that half to a constant in `start()` (and the run log's copy of it), keeping the seeded half; other policies keep the id the run made. The source fix, a pay roll that does not read the clock, is `src/` and is not part of this unit.

## Unit A5: a Linux export that finds its music, 2026-10-02

Base `261994d807c0698d37a3e0f6436dac393dac35e1`. `npm run sketch:export` imports `sketches/core-loop`, exports preset `Linux x64` (embedded pack, `export_presets.cfg` is now tracked; it excludes `tests`, `tools`, `design-v2`, `design-v3`, `perf-lab` and `assets/music_ace`) to `artifacts/local/core-loop-export/<run>/core-loop.x86_64`, copies `assets/music_ace/` to `music_ace/` beside it, and boots it headless with `--quit-after 300 --music=ace --ace-check`. It fails on a `SCRIPT ERROR` or `ERROR` line, on a missing `ACE_CHECK` line, or before exporting when the manifest names a stem file that is absent.

- **Why a sidecar.** In an exported game `res://` is inside the pack, so the `globalize_path` disk path the sequencer loads Oggs from does not exist, and the packaging tool's `.gdignore` keeps the Oggs out of the pack anyway.
- **Resolution order.** `AceSequencer.resolve_manifest(manifest_path, sidecar_dir)`: the source tree path first, then `music_ace/<rest of the path>` beside `OS.get_executable_path()` (or `sidecar_dir`), else `""`, which leaves `missing` set and `ready()` false, the existing not-ready path.
- **`--ace-check`.** The sequencer prints one `ACE_CHECK ready=... clips=... streams=... missing=...` line at the end of its load when the flag is on the command line. Nothing else reads it.
- **Limits.** Headless boot only: no audio device, no window, no human listening. The build is Linux x86_64 and is not distributed.

## Unit A1: world and boss tables, 2026-10-02

Base `261994d`. `src/core/worlds.gd` holds `ROWS` (three worlds: Spillway Works, The Dig, The Highdraft, each with a boss id) and `BOSSES` (name, script path, piece-kind prefix, music id, victory line, epitaph, `built`). World one's name is the Bible's region name; the Waking and the Mind rows are unbuilt (`"built": false`, script paths that do not exist yet). Each stage row gains `world`; `stage` stays the global run index. `stage_defs.gd` and `slice_defs.gd` gain `world_of`, `local`, `first_of`, `last_of`, `worlds`, `is_world_last` and `is_run_last` as one-line calls into `worlds.gd` helpers that take the table's `DEFS`; `is_last` is unchanged. `tests/doubles/two_world_defs.gd` is a six-stage, two-world table for driving a two-world run early. `tests/world_table_test.gd` checks every helper on all three tables and the `BOSSES` rows. Red: `local` without its `+ 1` fails the double's stage 4 to 6 checks; restored, it passes. Nothing is wired into play; `posture_sweep.gd`'s `Table` is unchanged because no caller there needs the helpers.

## Unit BC: the chaser, lobber and deflector show on their body when your verb works, 2026-10-02

Base `a56959e`. LEGIBILITY section 6 promised three body cues; they are built, each from a STATE_ROWS row, visual or sound only (no position, timing, damage or AI changes; each kind's wind-up test passes unedited).

- **Chaser** (`chaser.gd`). The body paint's emission wears the `will_break` row for the whole wind-up, pulsing at the row's motion, and the `interrupted` row for 0.9 s after a knock at `INTERRUPT` breaks it, with the `launch` reaction as the flip. A cue wins over the hit flash's glow, and `_apply_rest_look` hands the emission back so nothing glows at rest. The interrupted star over the body is `status_marks.gd`'s existing mark.
- **Lobber** (`projectile.gd`). The shell wears a mint billboard ring (the `will_break` colour) while `planar distance from the ball to the landing point / the ball's planar speed < the shell's remaining flight`, and plays `vault_rise` (SfxBank's rising sweep) once, the first time it lights. The sound plays from an `AudioStreamPlayer3D` on the shell because no event row carries it; a row in `WORLD_ROWS` would be the right home. The cue ignores the speed tier `combat.gd` `_bat_by_ball` also requires (CRUISE), so a ball too slow to bat can still light the ring; the brief's formula is what is built.
- **Deflector** (`deflector.gd`, `key_glyphs.gd`). While `VAULT_WINDOW` runs after a rebound, a torus on the dish rim wears `will_break` (in the tree only during the window, so the asset tests' live measure is unchanged), `vault_anchor()` returns the dish, and `key_glyphs.gd` puts the jump prompt there, shows it even when the slingshot would not and ignores its retirement for that window. Outside the window the prompt is back at the ball.
- **Test.** `tests/enemy_body_cues_test.gd` (22 checks). `BC_DISABLE_CUE=chaser|lobber|deflector` turns one kind's cue off, which is how the red run is shown.
- **Reads at 10 m.** Chaser strong; deflector a thin arc; lobber a halo that is weak against bright sky (`artifacts/local/alpha/r8/BC/STILLS.md`).

## Unit W1: a run carries across worlds, 2026-10-02

Base `b7882b5`. `flow.gd` routes by world. `_finish_stage` enters the boss when the cleared stage is the last of its world (`is_world_last`); after the VICTORY beat `_advance_from` enters the next stage, or ends the run won when the cleared stage was `is_run_last`. One-world tables behave as before (`is_world_last` equals `is_last`; a table with `BOSS == ""` still ends on the summary).

- **`world_entered(world)`.** Emitted at run start and when the stage entered is in a new world, before that stage's `stage_entered`; never on a retry. Its event row is quiet (priority 0, no HUD wording, `ui_tick`) and the signal sits in `EventKinds.GRAMMAR_SIGNALS` (no presentation listener yet, which `presentation_sfx_test` otherwise requires); the unit that adds the world banner wires a listener and removes it from that list.
- **Which boss.** `_world_boss(world)` reads `Worlds.boss_of(world)`. A row with `built` false skips to the next world with one warning (`boss_unbuilt_<world>`); on a multi-world table a built row with no module in the scene is skipped with a warning too. The scene still holds one `ctx.boss` module, so a second built boss needs its own module wiring when it exists. `flow.boss_row_override[world]` is a test seam for a row.
- **Replay.** `flow.final_stage()` is the last stage of the last world; `can_replay_final_stage` and `game.gd`'s T key use it instead of `COUNT`. A world-one arena death screen on a multi-world table therefore offers no replay (stage 6 has no start yet).
- **Test.** `tests/spine_worlds_test.gd` subclasses `Game` to choose `two_world_defs.gd`. Built: `world 1, stage 1-3, arena, world 2, stage 4-6, arena`, ends won. Unbuilt world two: one arena, one warning. Red: `_finish_stage` on `is_last` fails 4 of 14 checks; restored, it passes.
- **Not done here.** `screens.gd` and `hud.gd` still read `COUNT` ("Replay stage 3", stage counters); `boss.gd` does not yet tear down its arena for a second entry.

## Unit W4: a stage row names its Warden family, boss pieces come from the boss table, 2026-10-02

Base `d939492`. Each stage row (`stage_defs.gd`, `slice_defs.gd`, `two_world_defs.gd`) carries `warden_family`, the Roster strings as literals (core files do not import combat). Default table: reactor, swarm, mind. Slice: reactor. Two-world double: reactor, swarm, mind, reactor, swarm, mind.

- **`warden_spec(stage)`** returns `family` from the row, which `warden.gd` already prefers. A row without it falls back to `Roster.warden_family(stage)`; a stage past the fallback's range (families or `WARDEN_FALLBACK`, when the row lacks a Warden number) logs one `push_warning` per stage.
- **`Roster.is_boss_piece(kind)`** is true when `kind` begins with a `Worlds.BOSSES` row's `kind` plus `_`. `combat.gd` `_target_class_of_kind` and `enemy.gd` `_ordinary_reaction` call it. `enemy.gd` preloads roster inline because its subclasses each declare a `Roster` constant.
- **Test.** `tests/combat_worlds_test.gd` (36 checks). With `warden_spec` ignoring the row, the stage-4 and stage-5 checks fail; restored, it passes.
- **Not done here.** `director.gd`'s `FAMILY_ANCIENT` array still indexes by stage without a clamp; `boss_target.gd` still builds `brindle_` kinds.

## Unit P1: achievements earn themselves from what happens in play, 2026-10-02

Base `d939492`. `src/core/progress.gd` is a Node like `run_log.gd`: `setup(events, run, profile, now := Callable())`. It connects once to each signal an `Achievements.ROWS` row names, plus `world_entered`, `run_started`, `player_died` and `run_ended`, with a handler of the signal's arity, and packs the arguments by the names in `get_signal_list()`.

- **Match and count.** Every `where` key must equal the fact's value. `sum` adds the named argument instead of 1. Counts are lifetime, in profile stats as `ach.<id>` through `add_stat`. A row already earned is skipped.
- **Need, min, max.** Read `run.summary()` at the moment of the fact (`parts` by size) or an evaluator key: `class` is `run.class_id()`, `won` is `run_ended`'s argument (false before it and after), `world` is the last `world_entered` (0 before one). A `need` with no bound passes when the value is non-empty, true or non-zero.
- **Earn.** `profile.earn(id, now)` once, reward applied (`pilot`, `cosmetic`, `world`), `save()`, then `achievement_earned(id)` on the bus. A failed save keeps the earn in memory, `push_warning`s `profile.last_error`, counts in `Progress.warnings` and still emits. The first-win secret paint rows (`secrets.gd` `PAINT_ROWS`, 2026-10-17) earn silently: no `achievement_earned`, so the first win's own toast is the one announcement. `player_died` and `run_ended` also save when the profile can.
- **Event row.** `achievement_earned` has a quiet amber star row (priority 0, `ui_tick`) and sits in `GRAMMAR_SIGNALS` until the toast unit gives it a listener and a tune. This changes `EventKinds.ROWS`, so `attack_rows_test`'s `ROWS_HASH` was re-pinned (3685590879 to 2505535723).
- **Test seam.** `Progress.rows` (default `Achievements.ROWS`) can be set before `setup`; the shipped table has no `sum`, `class` or `world` row, so `progress_bus_test` supplies its own for those.
- **Not wired.** `game.gd` and the context do not hold a profile or a Progress yet. `enemy_killed` carries `kind == "brindle"` when Brindle dies: `boss.gd` calls `ctx.run.record_kill("brindle", at, 0)`.
- **Test.** `tests/progress_bus_test.gd` (30 checks) on a real `Game` bus, profiles under `storage_dir`. Red: with the once-only guards removed the second `beacon_unsealed` announces `warden_broken` twice and P1-1 fails; restored, it passes.

## Unit W6: the HUD and screens know which world you are in, 2026-10-02

Base `d939492`. On a table of more than one world, `world_entered` raises a launch card titled with `Worlds.row(world).name`; the first stage's card replaces it after `Hud.WORLD_CARD_BEAT` (1.2 s). One-world tables show no world card, so their stage cards are as before.

- **Status pips.** `hud.gd` passes the stage's number within its world (`local`), the world's stage count (`last_of - first_of + 1`) and a boss tag that follows `Worlds.boss_of(world).built`. Pips only; no HUD words added. `_on_launched` previews the next stage's card only while that stage is in the same world.
- **Dead screen.** (Superseded: the dead screen has no Retry button now.) The replay label reads `flow.final_stage()` through `local`, so it reads "Replay stage 3" in each world of the double and on the default table.
- **Victory and summary.** `BOSSES` gains `title` ("BRINDLE ROUTED", "THE WAKING STILLED", "THE MIND IS QUIET"). The victory screen shows the beaten world's `title` and `victory`; the won summary adds that boss's `epitaph` under the class line (no epitaph when no boss was beaten). The old hardcoded "The Requisition is scrap." is gone.
- **`GRAMMAR_SIGNALS`.** `world_entered` stays in it: `presentation_sfx_test` counts only presentation listeners, and the HUD is not one.
- **Test.** `tests/ui_worlds_test.gd` (19 checks) on the double, default and slice tables. `UI_WORLDS_DISABLE_CARD=1` disconnects the world card, which is how the red run is shown (3 W6-1 checks fail).
- **Not done.** `field_notes.gd` and the summary's stage chip stay world-one text and the global count.

## Unit P2: the profile lives in the game, a locked pilot cannot start a run, 2026-10-02

Base `8244add`. `Game.setup` builds `Profile.new(storage_dir/profile.json)`, loads it (a failed or refused load logs `profile.last_error` once and the game runs on) and stores it as `ctx.profile`. The game owns one `Progress` node on its own bus; it joins the bus on the game's first physics frame, so a test that builds its own `Progress` in its first frame hears each fact once (`progress_bus_test` runs unedited).

- **Starters.** `ClassDef.starter` (default false); Sable, Rivet and Kest are all starters until an achievement row rewards a pilot (Rivet's lands with world one's content).
- **Gate.** `Game.pilot_unlocked(def)` is `def.starter or ctx.profile.has_pilot(def.id)`. The class-select branch of `_screen_key` consumes the key for a locked pilot and starts nothing; card clicks share it through `screens._press`. `flow.choose_class` stays ungated, so bots and tests are unaffected. The locked-card look is a later unit.
- **Purity.** `profile_purity_test` scans movement, combat, build, world and boss for `ctx.profile`, `Profile`, `Progress`, `Achievements`, `Cosmetics` and preloads of their files (whole identifiers), and runs `Achievements.validate()`.
- **Test.** `tests/profile_unlock_gate_test.gd` (18 checks). Red: with `pilot_unlocked` returning true the locked key starts a run and P2-2 fails; restored, it passes.

## Unit S2: key bindings are saved with the other settings, 2026-10-02

Base `8244add`. `settings.json` gains an optional top-level `bindings` map, `SafeStore.STRING_MAP`: action name (a `Bindings.actions()` key) to a comma-separated token list. A token is `key:<name>` (`OS.get_keycode_string` of the physical keycode) or `mouse:<button index>`; `pad:` is reserved for the gamepad unit and is not decoded. `Bindings.encode(events)` and `Bindings.decode(text)` are the codec; a token that does not parse is skipped with one warning per token text. Mouse motion (`cl_look`) cannot be bound.

- **Store.** `SettingsStore` keeps `bindings` and offers `binding`, `binding_events`, `set_binding` and `clear_binding`; each setter saves. An entry for an action this build does not hold is kept and written back, and `binding_events` returns `[]` for it.
- **Unchanged files.** `save()` writes `bindings` only when the map is not empty, so a file with no overrides is byte for byte what it was. No version field; a file without `bindings` means no overrides. A wrong-typed `bindings` quarantines the file like the other keys (a fifth case in `persistence_truncation_test`).
- **Test.** `tests/unit/settings_bindings_case.gd`. Red: `save()` dropping `bindings` fails the round-trip case; restored, it passes.
- **Not done.** `game.gd` does not apply saved bindings at boot and the pause menu has no rebind page.

## Unit T0: a fourth pilot row plays without crashing, 2026-10-02

Base `8244add`. A pilot row the per-pilot tables do not know can be selected, play stage 1 to the Warden's break and the reward, and open the workshop with no script error. Sable, Rivet and Kest are unchanged; no pilot content was added.

- **Fixes.** `UpgradeTree.nodes` returns an empty tree for an unknown pilot id and warns once (`UpgradeTree.unknown_warned`); `offered` returns `[]` on an empty tree and `pending` is false for one, so the workshop never opens a pick of two empty cards (that was a native crash in `tree_pick.gd:91`). `Harmony.motif_or_first(id)` returns the motif row, else the first pilot's, and warns once per id (`Harmony.unknown_warned`); `sfx_bank.gd` (`_ready_pulse`, `_break_free`, `_near_miss_whip`) and `music_engine.gd` (counter-line voice and class counter) read through it.
- **Test.** `tests/pilot_fallback_test.gd` (the probe is Rivet's row as `probe_fourth`, motif `probe`, appended to `flow.classes` only). The test fits the ball with `player.fit_class(probe)` itself, because `player_ball.gd` resolves a run's class through `ClassDef.by_id`, which a probe outside `ClassDef.all()` cannot reach; a real pilot is in `all()`. Red: with the `offered`/`pending` empty-tree guards removed the run dies in `tree_pick.gd:91`; with `_ready_pulse` reading `Harmony.motif` directly it throws a `SCRIPT ERROR` at `sfx_bank.gd:1122`.
- **Table of three-pilot assumptions.** Fixed now: `sfx_bank.gd` `_ready_pulse`, `_break_free`, `_near_miss_whip`; `upgrade_tree.gd` `nodes`, `offered`, `pending`. Fine as is (safe fallback): `ability_state.gd` ABILITIES `.get`; `weapons.gd` `base`, `color`, `strike_kind`; `combat.gd` `_patterns` (safe while a pilot reuses a weapon id) and `_specials` guard; `class_kit.gd` WORDS, FANTASY, DIFFICULTY; `ability_slots.gd` (icon ids come from the ability rows); `director.gd` `CLASS_AXIS`; `items.gd`, `shop.gd` `CLASS_LINES`, `parts.gd`, `cosmetics.gd` (class-restricted rows never match); `screens.gd`, `flow.gd`, `game.gd` (sized by `classes.size()`). Left for the real pilot units: `class_def.gd` rows and `SABLE/RIVET/KEST_COLOR`; `hero_assets.gd` PILOTS, SHELLS and `class_id()` (falls back to Sable's shell); `class_preview.gd` props and pose `match`; `audio.gd` and `sfx_bank.gd` per-pilot cue name lists and gain table; `combat.gd` hard-coded SABLE/RIVET/KEST ring colours; `upgrade_tree.gd` TREES and WORLD_ONE_TABLES; `harmony.gd` MOTIFS and CLASS_MOTIFS; `event_kinds.gd` P rows; `AbilityState.ABILITIES` rows for new ids; `weapons.gd` BASE and `color` for a new weapon id and `combat.gd:716, 1290` pattern lookups for one. Also left for them, found by the review: `augments.gd` DEFAULT_MOVEMENT and SPECS (`augment_kit.gd:106`); `harmony.gd` `mind_quote`; `sfx_bank.gd` release cues; `combat.gd` proc wiring from TREES (`:264-273`); `rows.gd:56, 65` per-motif cue names; and `tools/bot_driver.gd:257`, where `seed % ClassDef.all().size()` makes bots pick a fourth pilot as soon as one is added. The music engine needs no fallback: an unknown motif already gets no class counter-line, so `pilot_fallback_test` pins that instead.

## Equipped paint on the shell (alpha R10, P4)

`HeroAssets.profile` (set from `ctx.get("profile")`, or directly) names the equipped paint for the player's class (`equipped(class_def.id, "paint")`). `paint_tint(pilot, id)` accepts a row only if its slot is `paint`, its pilot is "" or this pilot, it has a `tint`, and the profile owns it or the row is a starter; anything else warns once per id and gives no tint. The tint multiplies the paint slot's existing 1.2 plate lift, so `class_accent`, the pilot slot and every other slot keep their colours, and `class_color()` never reads the profile. The material cache key already carries the tint, so enemy, gate and Warden paint stay untinted. `_fit` compares the equipped id as well as the class, so a new paint repaints a live shell at its next fit. `ClassPreview.paint_id` applies the same rule (no ownership check: the preview holds no profile); its rim light and floor ring stay the class colour. Only Pod has paint surfaces today (Core is glass), so the tint shows on the gyro ring and cockpit trim. Trail and topper slots are not wired.

## Unit S1: saved bindings apply at boot, a gamepad plays the game (alpha R11)

Base `ef709ba`. `Game.apply_saved_bindings()` runs in `_ready` once `storage_dir` is settled: it reads `SettingsStore` and, for each action with a saved override, replaces that action's keyboard and mouse events with the decoded ones. Pad events stay, unless the file names a `pad:<index>` token (`decode` now reads it; `encode` still writes only `key:` and `mouse:`, and rebinding a pad button has no screen). A text that decodes to nothing keeps the defaults; `decode` warns once per token.

| Action | Pad default |
| --- | --- |
| `cl_forward/back/left/right` | left stick axes |
| `cl_jump` | A |
| `cl_fire` | right trigger (axis, positive) |
| `cl_harpoon` | left trigger (axis, positive) |
| `cl_haul` | X |
| `cl_boost` | B |
| `cl_special` | LB |
| `cl_interact` | Y |
| `cl_look_left/right/up/down` | right stick axes (pad only, no key) |
| `cl_retry` | RB (key R) |
| `cl_pause` | Start (keys P, Escape) |

- **Table.** `pad` and `axis` sit on the action record, not the verb. `cl_retry` and `cl_pause` belong to no verb, so they live in `Bindings.EXTRA_ACTIONS`, which `Bindings.actions()` merges; `VERBS` still holds nine verbs. The four look actions are in the `look` verb.
- **Look.** `Game.step_stick_look(delta)` reads `Input.get_vector` on the look actions every frame, captured mouse or not; `ChaseCamera.look_stick` cubes each axis and turns 2.6 rad/s of yaw and 1.6 rad/s of pitch at full deflection. Up looks up. Mouse look and its sensitivity are unchanged.
- **Retry.** The hold reads `Input.is_action_pressed("cl_retry")`, so R and RB both fill it; still 0.5 s, one restart per press (a new run as the same pilot, world 1).
- **Pause.** `pause_menu._toggles` reads `cl_pause` when the action exists, else the old fixed keys and Start. Start never quits; Escape's quit path in `game._input` is untouched.
- **Screens.** `Game.pad_screen_key(event)` maps a pad A press to Space (title, summary) or R (death), and B on death to N; `_input` feeds it to `_screen_key`, which is unchanged.
- **Last device.** `Game.last_device` is `"kbm"` or `"pad"`, set by `note_device` from every input event (stick past 0.2 counts as pad).
- **Test.** `tests/input_pad_test.gd` (49 checks). Red: with the `apply_saved_bindings()` call removed from `_ready`, three S1-2 checks fail; restored, all pass. `bindings_table_test` changed in two rules: "every verb has an action with a key or a button" now also accepts a pad button or an axis, and the no-shared-key check also covers pad buttons across verbs.

## P3: locked pilots, the achievements page, paint on the card, the earn toast

- A locked pilot is its own card state (`ClassCard.set_locked`), never `disabled`, which still means the screen is not live. The card keeps taking clicks; the gate in `game._screen_key` and `pilot_unlocked` refuses them, and the card shakes 0.25 s. Slate plate, a preview greyed to 40 percent by a small shader, a padlock where the role glyph sits. The hint (`Unlock: <achievement text>`, or `Locked` when no row rewards the pilot) is a card detail, so it reads only on the open card and the 40-word calm budget holds. No real pilot is locked today; tests swap in a locked copy of a row.
- The title's Achievements action (key A, back on A or Escape) lists `Achievements.ROWS` in table order from `AchievementsPage`. Hidden rows read "???" and "Keep playing." until earned. Progress comes from `Progress.count_of`; the date from `Progress.earned_at`, which reads the profile's saved document because `profile.gd` has no reader. Follow-up: a `Profile.earned_at` would replace it.
- A card shows its paint (swatch and name) over the preview's foot when open. A click on the swatch or P cycles `none` plus the paints this profile owns (starter row or `has_cosmetic`); each pick calls `profile.equip` then `profile.save()`. The preview shows only an owned equipped paint.
- An earned achievement toasts at the top right for 3 s on its own layer above the menu screens. It waits while a Warden fight is live (`flow.engaged()` and `flow.sealed`: `engaged()` stays true after the break until the next stage, so the break's own earn would wait a whole stage otherwise), and queued toasts show in order.

## Per-world terrain and the director's stage row (alpha R11, W2)

`Terrain.maps` is still one map object per table row, made at `_init`. The ground and props are built per global stage and lazily by world: `build()` builds the first world's stages (every stage of a one-world table, so boot is unchanged), and any read that needs a stage's ground (`use`, `geometry_root`, `height`, `spawn_point`, `beacon_point`, `vault_point`, `ground_callable`, `rescue_point` through `height`) builds that stage's whole world first (`_ensure`). Two rows that share a map id still get their own ground. A root built late is parked and takes the process mode the others have (disabled after `park_all`, else inherit). `is_built(stage)` reads without building. A stage number outside the table is reported once (`push_error`, or `error_sink` when a test sets one; `range_errors` counts every occurrence) and then clamped as before.

`world.gd` adds contraptions through `_add_contraptions()`: setup covers built stages, and each stage entry covers the world `use()` just built, so setup no longer forces world two.

Stage rows gain `unlock_base` (int) and `ancient` (a Roster kind id): 2, 4, 6 with `reactor_relic`, `swarm_remnant`, `mind_hand` on the default table, 2 and `reactor_relic` on the slice. The two-world double reads 2, 4, 6 then 3, 5, 7 with `mind_hand`, `reactor_relic`, `swarm_remnant`, so a test can tell a row read from a guess. `Director.use_row(row)` takes them; `world.gd` calls it on stage entry after `_author_director()`. A row without both keys (or no call) leaves the threat guess exactly as it was.

- **Tests.** `terrain_worlds_test` (standalone terrain) and `director_rows_test`. Red: with `use_row` not called on entry, stages 4, 5 and 6 read the guess and the unlock and ancient checks fail; restored, they pass.
- **Timing (headless, ms, three runs, a loaded shared machine, two-world double).** Before: building all six stages at setup 2568, 2558, 2556. After: world one at setup 2022, 1817, 2700; world two on first read 1320, 1312, 2120. World two's first use is over 250 ms, a hitch for a later unit to hide (build it behind a loading beat or ahead of the world's last stage).
- **Follow-up.** `game.gd:152-154` calls `geometry_root` for every Spillway row at setup, which builds world two at boot for the two-world double; left alone because two other writers are in that file.

## Music from the stage row and the boss row (R12 W5)

Each stage row gains `"music": {"theme": int, "ace": String}`. Theme stays 0 to 2 (the engine's tables are three long). `ace` names a package `audio.gd` can load (`ACE_PACKAGES`, today `"spillway"`); `""` is the synth band, silently; any other id plays the synth and warns once per id.

| Table | Stage | theme | ace |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| stage_defs | 1 | 0 | spillway |
| stage_defs | 2 | 1 | "" |
| stage_defs | 3 | 2 | "" |
| slice_defs | 1 | 0 | spillway |
| two_world_defs | 1-3 | 0, 1, 2 | spillway, "", "" |
| two_world_defs | 4-6 | 0, 1, 2 | spillway, "", "" |

- **Boss track.** `audio.gd` reads `flow.boss_row(world).music` for the world being fought (`_boss_track`). A track not in `MusicEngine.BOSS_TRACKS` (`["brindle"]`) plays `"brindle"` and warns once per id, so `waking` and `mind` sound as Brindle until their music is written. `_follow_brindle` still dispatches on the engine's track, which is always `"brindle"` today.
- **`Boss.music_state()`** returns `{in_phase, held, phase_seconds}` (empty outside phases 1 to 3). Audio calls it when the boss module has it and keeps the Rules reflection for a module without it.
- **Left alone.** The stage-entry stinger argument `clampi(stage, 1, 4)`; the engine's own theme clamps.
- **Tests.** `audio_worlds_test`. Red: with the slice row's `ace` set to `""`, its slice check fails; restored, it passes.

## The settings page (alpha R12, S3)

- **One page, two hosts.** `src/ui/settings_pages.gd` holds the rows; the pause menu shows it in place of its column from a new `settings` row (key O, directly above `quit`), and the title shows it from a Settings button on S. The first page keeps its rows and order (17 words with the new row, budget 40); the page reads 22 words (budget 70). Back, Esc, P or a pad's Start return to the page it came from; on the title Esc only goes back. Opening it never pauses, captures the mouse or quits.
- **Rows.** Fullscreen, Quality (High or Low), a rebind row each for jump, fire, harpoon, haul, boost, special and interact, Reset keys, then roll and look as fixed rows, then Back. `target` stays a launch-flag probe. Keys W, S, the arrows and Enter work, as do a pad's `ui_up`, `ui_down`, `ui_accept` and `ui_cancel`.
- **Rebinding.** A row waits for the next key or mouse button (left, right, middle, side buttons); Esc cancels. A press any other verb's action already holds in the InputMap (roll's keys included) is refused and the row reads "Taken". A new binding goes to `SettingsStore.set_binding` and to `Game.apply_binding`, the same function `apply_saved_bindings` now calls at boot. Pad buttons are not rebindable here. `Game.reset_bindings` clears every saved override and re-registers the table.
- **One store.** `Game.settings_store()` is the one `SettingsStore` for the pause menu and the page; two copies would save over each other's volumes and bindings.
- **Display.** Two comfort numbers, `fullscreen` (0 or 1) and `quality` (0 High, 1 Low), default 0, so an older file loads as today. `Game.apply_saved_display()` runs after the presentation is built: the saved quality applies unless a `--preset=` flag was given, and fullscreen (borderless, `WINDOW_MODE_FULLSCREEN`) only when `_windowed()` is true, and at boot only when saved on.
- **Test.** `tests/ui_settings_pages_test.gd` (44 checks). Red: with the `game.apply_binding` call removed from the rebind, the "G is on cl_jump at once" check fails (plus three that read the InputMap after it); restored, all pass.
- **Follow-up.** The cap on a rebind row reads the InputMap; the HUD and ability bar glyphs still read the table default until the glyph unit lands.

## On-screen caps follow the device and the bound keys (alpha R12, S4)

`src/ui/glyphs.gd` is the one lookup. `Glyphs.device` is `"kbm"` or `"pad"`, set by `Game.note_device`. `Glyphs.of(verb)` reads the verb's main action from the InputMap for that device (a key gives `key_<name>` from `OS.get_keycode_string`, so Space is `key_space` and Ctrl `key_ctrl`; left and right mouse give `key_lmb` and `key_rmb`). Roll and look keep `key_w` and `key_mouse` on the keyboard. `Glyphs.of_action(action, fallback)` covers actions that are no verb (`cl_retry`). With no such action in the InputMap, the table glyph answers.

- **Pad ids.** `pad_a`, `pad_b`, `pad_x`, `pad_y`, `pad_lb`, `pad_rb`, `pad_lt`, `pad_rt`, `pad_ls` (roll), `pad_rs` (look), `pad_start`. Jump A, haul X, boost B, special LB, interact Y, fire RT, harpoon LT, retry RB. `Keycap` draws them with today's cap colours: face buttons and sticks as round caps, the rest as rounded caps; label and words are A, LB, RT, L and so on.
- **Surfaces.** Prompts (`KeyGlyphs.glyph_of`, and the glyph id is part of `_placements()`, so a device switch or a rebinding redraws), the ability bar (`AbilityState.slot_key`: the table label while the keyboard keeps its default key, else the glyph id; `AbilitySlots.normalized` passes `pad_` ids through) and the restart meter.
- **Not yet.** Field notes, the controls diagram, the class card and the tree and shop cards still read `Bindings.glyph`; `AbilityState.KEYS` stays a label table for them.
- **Test.** `tests/glyph_device_test.gd`. Red: with `note_device` not setting `Glyphs.device`, the S4-4 checks fail; restored, they pass.

## Asset registry and budget classes (alpha R12, AB)

Every `.glb` under `assets/` and every texture under `assets/textures/` is listed in `assets/registry.json` with a class, whether it ships, its loader (`file:line` or the format string) and any exception. The caps live in `assets/asset_budgets.json`: a triangle, a vertex and a draw (surface) cap per class, and 2,048 px on the longest side for a texture. `tests/asset_registry_test.gd` fails on a model or texture that is unlisted or listed but absent, and on any shipped model over its class or exception; it prints one table of every asset against its caps. New models for worlds two and three register a class first.

- **Counts.** Taken after Godot import over the whole file, as the per-asset tests take them (index count / 3, vertex count, one draw per surface). A fresh checkout needs `godot --import` first; `sketch:smoke` does it.
- **Rule.** Cap = smallest round number (100 from 1,000 up, 10 below) at or above the largest member, never above the tightest existing per-asset ceiling of any member. A member over its class carries an exception (`exception` plus `exception_why`) instead of raising the class. The old per-asset tests are untouched and still own their finer checks; where a file holds several variants (chunks, gate), the old band is per variant and the file total is the registry's number.
- **Shipped.** 29 models (14 at the top of `assets/models/`, 15 under `world_one/`), 6 textures. The three top-level `shell_*.glb` have no loader in `src/` (`hero_assets.gd:38-40` loads the `world_one/` shells), so they are `ships: false` and not counted.

| Class | Triangles | Vertices | Draws | Set by |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| enemy | 1,100 | 2,100 | 3 | plated brute (1,044 / 2,063); 3-draw ceiling of five enemy tests |

| warden_kit | 1,500 | 2,800 | 18 | reactor (T), mind (V), hive and mind (D) |

- **Exceptions.** enemy_chaser and enemy_lobber 5 draws, enemy_winch_drone 4, enemy_mag_trapper 6; world_one/shell_kest 16 draws; dam 2,400 T / 4,600 V / 3 D; moss_workshop 4,400 T / 7,900 V / 3 D.
- **Test.** Red: an unregistered copy of `dam.glb` under `assets/models/` fails naming it; removed, it passes. Enemy triangle cap lowered to 800 fails naming the lobber (and deflector, brute); restored, it passes. `SCRAPKEEP_AB_MEASURE=1` prints the table without cap checks, for deriving a cap.
- **Follow-up.** `scenery` mixes a 120-triangle strip with a 2,400-triangle landmark, so it is two sizes under one name; split it when world two adds scenery.

## A gamepad through every menu (alpha R13, S5)

The engine's `ui_accept` held Enter, Kp Enter and Space and `ui_cancel` held Escape, so a pad moved focus (d-pad and stick are in `ui_up/down/left/right`) but could not press or go back. `Bindings.UI_PAD` holds the two rows (`ui_accept` gets pad A, `ui_cancel` pad B); `game._register_actions` adds them once at boot and keeps the engine's key events.

| Screen | Pad | Does |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Title | d-pad or stick | focuses the first button (`screens._pad_enters_title`, pad events only) |
| Title | A | presses the focused button; with nothing focused, starts the run as before; with help or achievements open, nothing |
| Help, achievements | B | closes the page |
| Class select | d-pad, A | moves between cards, picks (the same gate as keys and clicks; a locked card shakes) |
| Class select | X | cycles the open card's paint (`cycle_paint`, as P) |
| Dead | A, B, Y, X | retry, new run, class select (C), replay (T) |
| Summary | A, B | continue, new run (N) |
| Workshop counter | Y | talks (as T) while the counter is open; at the hatch with it closed, Y stays the interact verb and does not also talk |
| Upgrade pick | A | the first A after the pick opens picks nothing (the pick already focuses card 0); the second picks; a d-pad move counts as the first (`TreePick.confirm_pad`, `_pad_ready`); keys keep `confirm` |
| Field notes | Back held, d-pad left/right | shows them (as Tab), turns pages (as Q/E) |

- **Focus out of play.** `game._on_flow_changed` releases GUI focus when play starts, so A (jump) can never press a button left focused. Menus that close inside play (pick, counter) hide their controls, which drops focus.
- **`ui_accept` / `ui_cancel` readers, all gated by an open menu.** `pause_menu.gd:373,376` inside `_menu_key`, reached only when `_open` (line 309). `workshop.gd:432,441` inside `_pick_key`, reached only when `pick.is_open()`; `workshop.gd:471,478` inside `_counter_key`, only when `counter.is_open()`; both after `armed`. `class_card.gd:452` in `_unhandled_input`, only for the visible card 0 with no focus owner, so it needs the class-select screen on. `settings_pages.gd:245,247` inside `handle`, only while the page is visible. Plus the GUI's own button press, which needs a focused Control.
- **Not changed.** The play pad table, keyboard and mouse behaviour, the pause menu, settings pages, reward ceremony and discovery cards (a discovery card still passes its dismissing A through, as Space does).
- **Test.** `tests/input_pad_menus_test.gd`. Red: with `UI_PAD` empty, 16 checks fail (S5-1, the title press, class select A, the screens that follow); restored, 44 pass.

## The hook chain, state and rule (alpha full-send wave 1, HC1)

- **Call.** A chain is harpoon bites with no ground contact between them; an enemy bite is a link, a surface bite a pivot, a smash pays 1 + 0.3 per link beyond the first, capped at six (x2.5). Combo 1 is today's game: the ceiling is new, the floor is not lower. Principle: momentum is sacred, opt-in layers. Spec `design-v3/HOOK-CHAIN.md`.
- **Rulings from the gate.** The combo pays the chained enemy only, never the shockwave or bystanders, so a combo cannot break an activity wall a plain smash would not. A rewind press is not a miss (Andrew keeps the spam). Chain rows ride `line_state_changed` but every presentation listener skips `chain_*` until HC2 draws them.
- **Test.** `tests/hook_chain_test.gd` (14 sequences; cap mutant red), `tests/chain_listeners_test.gd` (guard mutant red).

## Activity signposting (alpha full-send wave 1, AS)

- **Call.** One amber edge marker per unfinished activity site from drop-in, coloured by the site's own activity row, hidden while the Warden fight is engaged; the plate says "Find an activity" (glyph `obj_event`) while the beacon is sealed, the fight is not engaged and a site is left, then the Warden rows as before. Since 10 Nov 2026 the Warden is the plate's goal from the drop and a site takes the row only while the ball is at one it has started, by its own verb (`Objectives.ACTIVITY_WORDS`, `activities.at_hand`; the rule is in [LEGIBILITY](design-v3/LEGIBILITY.md), the objective line), which answers the veto-list item below. The markers are unchanged. Principle: readability first, one meaning per layer.
- **Debt.** The marker is chevron-only; the site row's shape is not drawn. On Andrew's veto list: the plate pointing at activities before the Warden.
- **Test.** `tests/activity_signpost_test.gd` (done-filter mutant red).

## The hook chain, readout, log and card (alpha full-send wave 2, HC2 and HC3)

- **Call.** The combo reads from one source, `player.chain`, in every layer: a counter right of the ability bar from combo 2 that swells on each link and falls on the end; one collar ring per link up to six; the link bite a semitone up per link past the first, the smash at the root; the chain end heard only when it had a combo of 2 or more; one dry tick per miss. The run record carries `chains`, `best_combo`, `misses`, arena included. The codex card "Chain" opens at the first combo of 3. Principle: one meaning across every abstraction, the floor is today's game. Spec `design-v3/HOOK-CHAIN.md`.
- **Debt.** No still of combo 4 yet; the digit pulse runs on its own timer rather than the hit-stop clock; combo knock and shake feel is not built. The gate found and fixed a zip at combo 1 before merge.
- **Test.** `tests/chain_readout_test.gd` (links-1 suppression and combo-2 zip mutants red), `tests/chain_log_test.gd` (codex threshold mutant red).

## Brindle on the posture contract (alpha full-send wave 4, F2a)

- **Call.** Brindle's hull is a posture body on the frozen boss contract: contacts fill the bar, a full bar staggers him and opens the cockpit, a contact in the opening cracks and a later one shatters, a smash shatters at once, guns press the bar and never crack. Four chunks replace 22000 HP and the phase floors: chunks one to three end the three phases (minimum seconds kept, a chunk broken early holds the phase), the fourth is the scooter catch. Weak points (magnets, caps, the stunned or charging bow) keep their verbs and press the hull's one bar. Numbers are the slice Warden's (`curve` 2.0, `ram_shatters` false): his size makes contacts easy to land, which argues against an easier shatter, and the phase minimums already pace the fight. Principle: one fight grammar, readability first.
- **Debt.** The plate, ring and music read is F2b's; Brindle publishes no `posture_changed` yet and `music_state` follows the bar, so it can fall as the bar drains. A stagger does not cancel a charge in flight (the Warden's does). On Andrew's veto list: no shred on the hull (decision row 107 over the 2026-09-27 shred rule; coordinator ruling 2026-10-04).
- **Test.** `tests/boss_posture_test.gd` (gun-cracks mutant red), `tests/boss_contract_test.gd` (Brindle live). Repointed from the HP pool to chunks and posture: `boss_scaling_test`, `boss_flow_test`, `boss_fixwave_test`, `boss_prompt_test`, `combat_delivered_damage_test`, `combat_shred_test`, `audio_worlds_test` (W5-5).

## The cog wallet (alpha wave 4, CG1)

- **Call.** Scrap is mass and cogs are money (ROADMAP "Cogs are the currency"). Shop, fabricator, sell and refund spend or pay `run.cogs`; a scrap piece raises mass only, a brass gear raises cogs only, and damage, theft, wear and `recover_mass` never touch cogs. Provisional prices are today's scrap prices in one table, `Fabricators.COG_PRICES`. Drop mix: a kill drops one 1-cog gear per scrap unit beside its scrap, and a scrap line lays a 2-cog gear beside every second piece, so lines and kills pay about the cogs they paid as wallet scrap. Gears are placed without the rng, so the loot stream is unchanged.
- **Debt.** Discovery bursts, activities, the boss spill trail, the stampede, ballast and the scrap-burst item still pay scrap only, so a run earns fewer cogs than it earned wallet scrap. The HUD, shop tiles, fabricator label, summary and Moss's "scrap" lines still show scrap as money (CG2). No world device charges `spend_scrap` (the Wake device never charges). No `cogs_changed` signal yet.
- **Test.** `tests/cog_wallet_test.gd` (negative-spend mutant red).

## The run store (alpha wave 4, RS2)

- **Call.** `src/core/run_store.gd` keeps one run document on disk through SafeStore, with a generation counter and a merge that never lowers retries spent, `ended` or cleared worlds. After the gate review, `clear()` over a readable file writes a tombstone `{schema, generation: N+1, ended: true}` instead of deleting it; `load` reads a tombstone as no run, and a second store still holding an older generation has its `save` refused with `run_ended`, so it cannot resurrect an ended run.
- **Test.** `tests/run_store_test.gd` (stale-save mutant red).

## World-one Warden tests (alpha wave 4, F4)

- **Call.** Warden fights in world one are tested on real maps. Measured medians over seeds 1-3: Spillway 66.5 s, Reach 42.0 s, completionist 26.2 s.

## Smash damage per fight (alpha wave 4, B1)

- **Call.** The run log records `smash_damage` (total) and `smash_damage_by_fight` (one slot per Warden engagement), read from combat's delivered-damage ledger when a smash lands. A smash outside a fight counts in the total only. `stage_entered` closes the open fight slot, so a retry that skips death cannot credit early smashes to the previous attempt's fight.
- **Test.** `tests/chain_log_test.gd`.

- **Call.** Test-time zone slots validate world-one maps, adapt Spillway launch benches and Reach workshop gate slots as bay data, and seed absent optional positions from each map's own sites without changing authored data.

## Lore wiring (alpha wave 4, C5)

- **Call.** Notes reads saved codex lore ids in row order with empty locked slots. Zone-entry Moss and crew exchanges play once per zone per run and stop at Warden engagement. Ruling after the gate review (momentum is sacred, reading is optional): an exchange advances only on the interact key (E, pad Y); it never reads Escape or `ui_accept` (Space and pad A are jump), so Escape still pauses. It dismisses itself silently, counted as seen, when the ball exceeds cruise speed or the player fires or hooks.
- **Test.** `tests/ui_lore_wiring_test.gd`, `tests/ui_pause_menu_test.gd`.

- **Call.** The explorer bot can send an A-to-B chain through apex release and grace. Controlled input proves the hand; the short slice probe reaches combo 2 in 5 of 16 seeds (median 1), which leaves the planned median band unqualified.

- **Call.** SPIL-9 escape opens a two-second window after a survived hit at or below 25% hull, once per zone, never revives.

- **Call.** Four saved look settings are applied at boot and again after Reset keys; invert affects stick pitch only.

## Overpowered pays (Andrew, 2026-10-17 21:55Z)

- **Call.** A strong build or a lucky run is never punished; a nutty setup always earns something.
- **Warden ceiling.** Andrew's tap "Lift the ceiling" (22:04Z): a monster build's hit counts up to 3x the plating it meets (`boss_posture.gd` MULT_MAX, was 1.5) and one set piece fills up to 70 on a closed guard (`ultimate.gd` `warden_cap`, was 45). Coordinator ruling (B) on #222 (21 Oct): monster = run heft strictly above the fight's library top-bucket maximum heft (`data/challenge/monster_heft.json`, generated from lib-base by `tools/monster_heft_gen.gd`, drift-tested by `tests/monster_heft_test.gd`; park Wardens take the next stage's entrance, w1 1.445 to w5 12.105, and the stage 6 park Warden, which has no s7, gets none and stays ordinary; the Waking takes its own s4 entrance 6.539 and the Mind its own s6 12.105; each maximum is rounded up to 0.001, so the top-bucket build itself is never above its own threshold). Not a heft class, no constant, no hand tuning. It fails closed: a rule never told, a missing threshold (INF) and a Warden with no heft source all keep 1.5 and 45. The hull bosses keep 1.5 (`boss_rules.gd` POSTURE). The fleet-less arena Warden gets its heft from the stub boss's spec `heft` callable. The sheet-ram change in `warden_reactor.gd` is reverted (it reads `ordinary_ceiling()`, the stage base's 1.5). Measured on the hive (U1): the global lift sped the small arm (seed 2 140.16 -> 86.01 s) and dropped small/grown 1.95 -> 1.79.
- **One ordinary hit.** A posture row may set `hit_cap`: one hit at a ratio up to 1.5 adds at most that, and the cap climbs to a full bar at mult_max, so a plain ram never fills the bar but a monster build's still can. The Waking sets 15 until its first stagger (`hit_cap_until_stagger`), so an ordinary brawler's ram cannot fill its bar in one hit but the later chunks do not drag. The reactor Warden subclasses (`warden_reactor.gd`) rewrite gain after judge and do not apply it.
- **Dead offers go.** A linear or capped part held at its stack cap is swapped out of every roll for one of the same kit side, bucket, hitter-ness and shelf-ness (`Parts.stacked_out`). Two offer bars moved with it (coordinator ruling (a), 22:54Z): greedy kit share floor 55%, alpha top-10 overlap 0.50.
- **Overkill scrap, tried and dropped (18 Oct).** Paying scrap for damage past a kill helped small pilots more than strong ones: they one-shot chaff too. On main + #134 the hive's small median fell 99.54 -> 85.19 s while grown held at about 44 s, breaking the hives' 2x rule. Damage past a kill pays nothing again; the big damage number is its payoff.
- **Big hits draw big.** A damage number grows 6 pt per doubling past 40, to 60 (`damage_numbers.gd` font_size).

## The victory lap at Meteor Crater (design answer Q16, 2026-10-17)

- **Call.** After the Mind, the crew's fly-over plays, then the ball drops over Meteor Crater's rim for a 60 s free roll, then the credits roll over the bowl; any key skips the credits. The win is recorded when the ending begins, so quitting mid-ending keeps it.
- **Call.** The lap has no enemies, hazards or fail state: the arena and every fight group are cleared first, and flow ignores a death in the lap.
- **Call.** The bowl's middle is a vent that throws the ball up harder the faster it arrives, and new records (top speed, best air, wall laps) get a drawn word and a flare. Principle 5, overpowered pays: a strong build gets bigger numbers, and they appear in the credits.
- **Call.** If the bowl cannot be placed, the run ends with the old fly-over and credits.

## Reduce flashes toggle (design question 7, 17 Oct 2026)

- **Call.** Andrew picked "Reduce flashes toggle" as the alpha's comfort setting. It is a Display-page row beside reduced motion, saved as `reduce_flashes` in settings.json, and every bright flash, white pop and strobe reads `FlashGuard` when it fires (rules in VISUAL-LANGUAGE.md section 8). With both toggles on, the hit edge flash takes the lower share, so the hit still reads. The boss finales' Eurodance sections and comic words drive no visuals today, so they need no guard yet; a visual added to either must read FlashGuard. `tools/flash_capture.gd` renders each site off and on, and `still_compare.gd --peak` measures the brightness it adds.

## Spawned bodies enter where they stand; one fuzz seed per process (wave 17 item-fuzz major, 20 Oct 2026)

- **Call.** `spawn_enemy` places an enemy before it joins the tree, and a body that `setup` settles (the vault onto the ground, the Warden onto its landmark anchor) leaves the physics space and re-enters it at the settled transform. Jolt reads a kinematic body moved after it entered the space as a velocity over the next step, so every enemy used to cross from the world origin to its spawn point in one step and push whatever stood at the origin along the way. The 19 Oct speedrunner persona's tarn seed 2751 caught it: a miniboss spawned 24 m ahead flung a ball rolling 1 m from the origin out at 500 m/s (243.9 m/s planar). It was a physics shove, not a part stacking past its ceiling. `spawn_physics_place_test` pins all 19 kinds on the server where their nodes stand.
- **Call.** `tools/item_fuzz.gd` runs every seed in its own child Godot with its own user data, as `traversal_line` does. Seed 2751 drifted from tick 568 when 2750 ran first in the same process (physics-space float drift, not a script static), so a batch finding didn't replay alone. Now a red team replay line reproduces its batch row. Each child adds about 5 s of boot, so `item_fuzz_smoke_test` declares 300 s.

## Growth choices: the held shell, the hook's cobalt, the set banner (world 1 design pass, 9 Nov 2026)

- **Call.** Fitting the Riveted Shell changes the ball. The H1 slice (29 Sep, above) made the ball behave "as if the part is fitted" whenever `--hotwire-shell` is on, the flag ships on, and Fit / Recycle arrived later without moving the two numbers onto the held part. So every run rolled at 5 scrap per absorbed HP and air steering x0.75, Fit changed nothing, and Recycle's 20 cogs always won. Every band, pin and played build since was fit on those numbers, so they stay the baseline (`Build.SHELL_SCRAP_PER_HP`, `SHELL_AIR_FACTOR`), and the held part (`Build.shell_held()`, `RIVETED_ARMOUR` 2.0, `RIVETED_AIR_FACTOR` 0.75) pays what its card says from there: 2.5 scrap per HP and air steering x0.5625. A run that recycles plays exactly as before. The S4, R6 and K5 inversions still lift the factor to their own value, so they are worth more to a pilot who fits it. The other reading, BUILD-SYSTEMS.md's 10 scrap per HP and full air steering for a ball without the part, halves every run's armour until its first Warden; it is recorded as rejected for now and is a difficulty question for after the sitting.
- **Call.** The shop's top hook draws a cobalt once the park table's relics are held (`Shop.TOP_FALLBACK`). The Spare Frame is that table's only relic and stays the hook until held (24 Oct). After that the hook fell back to the shelf's rarities, so Moss kept a common behind the heft lock and called it too strong for you. Cobalt is the top ordinary rung of the alpha (ruling bn); the hook shows that table's odds, never repeats a shelf part, and is still stocked once per stage (ruling as). A table that lists no relic (alpha, slice, the default) has nothing to run out of, so its hook draws as before and its runs replay.
- **Call.** The third distinct part of a set raises one "Set complete" banner (`Build.set_completed`, `presentation._on_set_completed`): the set's name and its one line, edged in the colour of the studs `set_tell.gd` puts on the ball, once per run, behind the same calm gate as every find. In cards mode the first showing ever is the card and later runs get the small banner. A resume or a retry that restores a held set announces nothing.

## Frame smoothing (pre-sitting technical pass, 9 Nov 2026)

- **Call.** The sim runs 90 physics ticks a second and nothing drew between ticks, so on a display that is not 90 Hz the ball stood still in some frames and jumped in others: 216 of 576 frames at 144 Hz (`tools/judder_probe.gd`). Frame smoothing is Godot's physics interpolation, opt-in per body. The Game root is `PHYSICS_INTERPOLATION_MODE_OFF`, so a node that says nothing is drawn where it is, as before; only the ball's rigid body asks, and what follows the ball in `_process` (the lens, the HUD) reads `PlayerBall.shown_position()`. The probe then reads 0 frozen frames and an even step at 60, 120, 144 and 165 Hz. The cost is that the ball is drawn up to one tick (11 ms) behind the sim.
- **Call.** It is a Display-page row, saved as `frame_smoothing` in settings.json, on by default. Only a game on a window takes it. The sim reads the lens for the aim origin and the lens follows the drawn ball, so a headless bot or a test with smoothing on would not play the run the gate measures; a skipper trace over 9000 ticks is byte-identical before and after this change. Self-play on a real window does smooth. `--smoothing=0` or `--smoothing=1` on the launch line beats the setting and that rule, for a capture or to replay an older windowed seed; F3 under `--dev` flips it for the session. The engine's physics jitter fix rests while smoothing is on, as Godot's docs advise.
- **Call.** A ball that is placed (stage entry, a boss intro, a rescue, the camp) resets its interpolation in `RigidBall.teleport`, deferred to the end of the frame; until that reset has run `shown_position()` answers with the raw position (`RigidBall.placing`), so a lens snapped in the same breath outside a physics tick (the camp walk, from a key press) lands on the new spot instead of the old one and sweeping across; without it the ball was drawn up to 76 m off for a frame, sliding in from the old spot (`frame_smoothing_test` FS-5).
- **Call (DECISIONS cb, Andrew 9 Nov).** The game starts fullscreen and lets the monitor's variable refresh follow it; there is no frame cap. Variable refresh only follows a fullscreen window, and Andrew's monitors already had it set that way, but the game ran as a tiled window on the compositor's fixed 144 Hz clock, so it never engaged. `SettingsStore.fullscreen()` now defaults to on, the Display page's Fullscreen row turns it off, and `--fullscreen=0` asks for a window once without touching the setting (`Game.launches_fullscreen`; `--fullscreen=1` does the reverse). Godot's own `--windowed` cannot do that job: the engine keeps the flags it handles out of the list the game reads, so the game goes fullscreen after it. A game with no player's save folder (a test, `--self-play`) keeps the window its command gave it, so captures and timing runs keep their size, unless the command gives `--fullscreen=1`. The two fix different halves: variable refresh shows each frame when it is ready, and smoothing makes the frame worth showing, because without it a frame between two of the sim's 90 ticks repeats the last tick's ball at any display rate. The picture keeps its 16:9 shape, so a 21:9 monitor shows bars at the sides; filling it is a look call that waits on a HUD pass.
- **Rule for the next body.** A node that asks for interpolation must be moved only in `_physics_process`; a node animated in `_process` stays off; a pooled or teleported node calls `reset_physics_interpolation()` when it jumps; and a `_process` follower of an interpolated body reads its shown position. `tools/interp_audit.gd` plays the game with a bot and lists every node that breaks one of these (MOVER, STREAK) and every physics mover still drawn at the tick rate (STEP). Enemies, shots, scrap and the bosses are on that STEP list and are the second step.
- **Rule for drawing code.** Under `src/presentation` and `src/ui` the ball's place is `shown_position()`, with no choice to make per call: in a drawn frame it is where the ball is drawn, and at a tick it is the tick's place, so it is also right in a handler the sim calls (`frame_smoothing_test` FS-6 holds both halves). `ball_position()` stays the sim's question. Lint rule L8 fails any mention of `ball_position` in those folders, and the same read by another door, `body().global_position`; `SIM_READS` in `scripts/sketch-lint.mjs` lists the four reads that are not a picture of the ball, and why. Three are rules, which read the tick's place because they decide or mirror what the sim does: the pull-up to Moss that opens the counter, the reward ceremony's `calm()` that lets a ceremony stop the world, and the landing-sound guard in `audio.gd` that repeats the director's clearance check. The fourth asks a `Sight`, not the ball: the Warden's lens, which gets the last seen place while the ball is cloaked. The test for a new read: if the answer changes what the game does, it is a rule and takes the tick; if it only changes where something is drawn or how it sounds, it takes the drawn place.
- **The player node has no place of its own.** `PlayerBall` is a `Node3D` that never leaves the origin; its body and its visuals move under it. So `player.global_position` is the map's origin for the whole run, and nothing fails loudly: a rule measured from it never fires and a picture placed from it sits at the origin. Two shipped that way until 9 November 2026: the mist's clear zone and the midges around the ball (`world_fx.gd`), and the reach of every Signal cache (`radio_cache.gd`), which no roll could collect. Both passed their tests because the tests handed in a plain moving `Node3D`, or called `collect()` directly. Lint rule L9 fails the read anywhere under `src`; a caller that may be handed a plain node guards with `has_method` on the same line. A test of anything that follows the ball hands it a stand-in whose own place stays at the origin (`world_fx_test` `FakeBall`), or the game's own player (`radio_test`).
- **One call places the ball.** `PlayerBall.reset_to(point, velocity)` is the whole placement: `RigidBall.teleport` gives the body its new place and speed in the call, so the ball reads at the new spot at once, a lens snapped in the same breath looks at it, and a ride that catches the ball on that tick reads the speed it was placed with; the tick after sets the same point and speed again (and the rolling spin, which only that tick sets), so the ball leaves the spot on the tick it always did. A frozen ball takes the place now and the speed at the thaw. Until 9 November 2026 a rolling ball's node moved only on the next tick, and nine callers (the title park, stage entry and retry, both boss arena placements, both arena rescues, both camp placements, the victory lap) set `body().global_position` by hand after the call, as the ball's own setup did for its spawn point. A caller that forgot read the old spot for a tick; a caller that remembered left the ball at the new spot for a tick with the speed it had before, and `warden_reactor.gd` reads the body's speed, so a pulse sheet that caught the ball on that tick would have carried it off at the old one. A rescue now takes hold on the tick the ball leaves the map, so a test that parks the ball off the map to stage something finds it back at once. Lint rule L10 fails a write to the ball's body under `src` when it is spelled through `body()`, `active_body()`, `_ball_body()` or the ball's own `_rigid` (a body held in a local variable is not seen); `HAND_MOVED` lists the one write that is not a placement (an ultimate's flight leg in `kest_kit.gd`, which must keep the kit and the speed a reset clears).

## Death debris leaves by size, not by fading (pre-sitting technical pass, 10 Nov 2026)

- **Found.** On the 4080 the frame after the dam's first kill took 43 to 48 ms on every run of two seeds (stage ffe474c6, frames 541 and 623), with three pipelines built while the frame waited. A killed machine plays its death clip (0.53 s) and then breaks into pieces (`EnemyMotion.scatter`), and each piece faded through its instance's `transparency`. In Godot's Forward+ renderer an opaque surface whose instance fades is drawn in the blended pass, with pipelines of its own that are only built when a surface first needs them, so the first fading piece of every look stalled its frame. The frame before it cost 21 to 22 ms for a second reason: the debris boxes' looks (`BakeLook.unbaked` twins) were meant to be built at stage entry by `BakeWarm.death_warm`, but the warm freed its holder in the call that added the boxes, so the renderer never saw one.
- **Call.** A piece never fades. It flies at full size for the first half of its life and shrinks to nothing over the second half (`enemy_motion.gd` `SHRINK`), so a piece of the model is drawn with the pipelines the living machine already used, for every kind in every world, with no list to keep in step with the roster. Its path, spin and lifetime are unchanged. The box pieces are a look the body never drew (below). Serves 7 (fast without debt).
- **Call.** The warm's holder now stands a second drawn frame with the debris boxes in it, and with the body's own break-up built as a death builds it (`bake_warm_test` reads both from the tree each drawn frame had; on the stage before this change the boxes are in none). That covers the kinds the warm stands (the next section: what the stage can send). A kind that never stands and whose box look matches none of theirs still builds it on the frame its first debris appears (about 11 ms at the time of #587); nothing has measured which kinds those are.
- **Assumption (technicals thread, Andrew can overturn).** Shrinking reads as well as fading for half a second of flying debris. The other fix, kept in reserve, is to draw each kind's fading pieces once at stage entry, which only covers the kinds on a warm list. It is on the watch list for the wave's film.
- **Rule for the next effect.** Do not fade an opaque thing through `GeometryInstance3D.transparency` in play: move it, shrink it, or give it a material that is blended from the start and is drawn once ahead. `ambient_life.gd` (creatures fading in), `radio_cache.gd` (the ring's pulse) and the cosmetic trail cards still do; their first use is owed a read on the PC.

## The warm stands what the stage can send (pre-sitting technical pass, 10 Nov 2026)

- **Found.** With the debris fixed, the PC's longest frame in the dam's first minute was 77 to 81 ms, in three runs of three on seed 40317 (frame 4345, draw-bound, pipelines built that frame). The tick before it the dropship lands the Spillway's elite: a hooker, a jolt layer and a plated brute. The warm stood seven kinds out of sight at stage entry, a list kept by hand of the dam's ordinary packs; the elite was left out, and the plated brute, the one skinned body of the three, was first drawn on the frame it landed. Every dam run meets that elite. That the brute is the cost is read from a replay of the seed (the landing tick and the three kinds match the PC's frame), not measured: the PC has not yet run this change.
- **Call.** The list is no longer kept by hand. `Director.kinds_sent(row, table)` names every kind the stage's packs and its elite hold, read the same way the director is handed them (`Director.author`, which now owns that rule; `world.gd` calls it), and `BakeWarm` stands each one once a session at the first stage that can send it: eight on the dam, then the foreman and the dispatcher on the flume. A roster change moves the warm with it. Each kind costs two frames at stage entry, so the dam's warm is two frames longer and the flume's four. Serves 7 (fast without debt).
- **Found and call.** The run's briefing pauses the game a few frames after the dam is entered, and the warm stopped with it: 55 one-a-frame items on the dam (22 kits, 8 kinds standing, 4 rig scenes, 21 first moments), most of which then ran in the first half second the player rolled. The warm now goes on under a paused game, behind the briefing's veil (`bake_warm_test` drains it paused). A bot run has no briefing, so its frames are as they were.
- **Not covered.** A kind no pack or elite names never stands: the relic, the vault, a camp's miniboss, a Warden, Brindle. The Warden's engage frame costs 28 to 32 ms on the PC with three looks first drawn there, and one telegraph's first use costs 25 ms (seed 40317 frame 1146); both are open. Worlds 2 and 3 have no kit list, so nothing stands there (ruling at).
